# seekdb → Rust feasibility: verified evidence (brief)


## AREA: sql-engine
**What it is:** The execution engine that runs physical plans. It has pull-style operators with a batch interface (get_next_batch), an expression evaluator that writes results into per-query byte frames, parallel execution (PX) with in-process data transfer (DTL), and the data access service (DAS), which hands scans and DML to storage. Many core types (ObOperator, ObOpSpec, ObExpr, ObEvalCtx, ObPushdownFilter) are declared in src/query/api/query/engine, and 38 headers under src/sql/engine are one-line forwards to them.

**Size split:** Commands: `git -C $R ls-files <dir> | grep -E '\.(h|hpp|cpp|cc|c|ipp|y|l|inc)$' | xargs wc -l`, plus separate `git ls-files` passes for .map, .py and other extensions.

The counted source (C/C++ extensions only):
- src/sql/engine: 1,379 files, 351,853 lines. This matches the brief. By subdirectory:
  - expr: 921 files, 170,218 lines
  - px: 121 files, 41,158 lines
  - basic: 78 files, 34,043 lines
  - aggregate: 18,536 lines
  - cmd: 18,401 lines
  - dml: 14,141 lines
  - join: 13,153 lines
  - top-level files: 12,336 lines
  - table: 6,337 lines
  - sort: 5,676 lines
  - window_function: 4,973 lines
  - set: 3,478 lines
  - pdml: 2,885 lines
  - everything else: under 2,100 lines each
- src/sql/das: 103 files, 46,252 lines (iter/: 54 files, 29,185 lines)
- src/sql/dtl: 42 files, 9,995 lines

Related interface headers outside the area: src/query/api/query/engine and src/query/api/query/das hold 55 files and 14,439 lines. The area contains 38 stub headers (689 lines) that do nothing but include them.

Hand-written code: about 406,100 lines.
- engine: 351,853 − 1,528 (data) − 425 (generated) = 349,900
- das: 46,252
- dtl: 9,995

Generated code, checked in: 9 files, 425 lines. They are src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_str_cmp_func_compile/ob_expr_str_cmp_func_{all,part_0..7}.cpp, written by gen_expr_str_cmp_func.py (325 lines of Python, also tracked).

Data:
- 15 `.map` type-promotion tables under src/sql/engine/expr, 16,512 lines. These are C++ array initializers included by ob_expr_result_type_util.cpp and are not in the 351,853 count.
- src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_to_pinyin_tab.cpp, 1,528 lines (pinyin lookup table).

Vendored third-party code: none. `git grep -L 'Copyright (c) 20.. OceanBase'` over the area returned no C/C++ file, and no MySQL, Google, ClickHouse or similar copyright lines were found.

**Summary:** 1. The operator layer is a plain pull model. ObOpSpec is the immutable plan node, ObOperator holds runtime state and ObOpInput holds per-task input. There are 61 operator types registered through a macro-plus-template table; 45 are marked vectorized and the other 16 go through a one-row adapter. Operator bodies (limit, join, aggregation) would map to a Rust trait with open/get_next_batch/rescan/close without much trouble.

2. The expression layer is the hard core. ObExpr holds raw C function pointers (eval_func_, eval_batch_func_), which each expression's cg_expr chooses at code-generation time. Results live in untyped per-query byte frames, reached by `reinterpret_cast<ObDatum*>(frames_[frame_idx_] + datum_off_)` at offsets computed by the code generator. String results often point back into reserved space in the same frame.

3. Vectorization means batches of 12-byte ObDatum {ptr, len/null} values plus skip and evaluated-flag bitmaps. The default batch is 256 rows, and loops handle 16 rows per 16-bit bitmap word. The columnar "rich format" engine 2.0 was removed in cbcea2b91 (2026-07-30) and row-at-a-time mode in 8f96ded56 (2026-08-12). Hand-written SIMD is small: AVX2/AVX-512 in 5 files, one of which depends on sizeof(ObDatum)==12.

4. The engine-to-storage boundary is not clean. DAS calls ObITabletScan::table_scan through server_service<>. Storage then writes batches directly into the engine's expression frames (ObVectorStore is a friend of ObEvalCtx) and runs the engine's pushdown filter executors inside its block scanners. That is 419 references in 46 storage files, plus 209 references to the frame API in 32 storage files. So the engine and storage cannot be translated independently without first fixing a shared column-batch type.

5. PX runs in one process but still serializes the whole spec tree, the ObExpr array and the frames, and deserializes them in the worker threads. ObExpr pointers are encoded as indexes into a thread-local array. Function pointers are encoded as raw addresses, because the ObFuncSerialization registry was removed in 71937e637. A Rust design could share an immutable plan instead. The existing index encoding also shows that the expression graph is already one contiguous array, which fits Rust arena indexes.

6. Memory is arena-based and skips destructors. Plan specs are freed by allocator clear with no destructor calls, per the README and ObPhysicalPlan::destroy. Operators are torn down with a hand-written destroy() rather than the destructor (108 overrides). ObExecContext holds three arenas plus MemoryContexts, and spilling operators go through ObSqlMemMgrProcessor (20 files). Row stores swizzle pointers to offsets and back (94 hits in 13 files), and hash tables chain rows through next pointers stored inside the rows.

7. Expression semantics are the bulk of the code. There are 528 registered expression types in about 451 files. Each file combines compile-time type deduction (called by the resolver), code generation and runtime evaluation. They lean on 48 global function-pointer tables filled by template instantiation over type × type × collation, a 13,621-line cast file and 16,512 lines of type-promotion tables.

8. There are no C++ unit tests for this area at HEAD: 27 unittest/sql/engine files were deleted in 45cbbe9e1. The surviving tests are all public-surface mysqltests (283 .test / 305 .result in tools/deploy/mysql_test), so they survive a rewrite. Their expression coverage is thin, though: only 141 of 414 function names appear as calls in them. Verification of expressions will therefore need differential runs against the C++ build.

9. There is concrete pain that Rust would remove:
   - a use-after-free of an SQC buffer captured in an async closure (bc31e03f4)
   - a sort comparator that returned an inconsistent order in its error state, so std::sort read out of bounds (5aa611c00, b1587c030)
   - a crash from a wrong static_cast (a44b2526f)
   - crashes from reading uninitialized frame memory (1e4f589b8)
   - a latent null dereference in ObLimitOp, found by reading

**Structure call (surveyor):** Split verdict.

The operator bodies can be translated close to one-to-one. They pull batches from their children, keep small state and call expr->eval_batch, so a Rust trait with open, get_next_batch, rescan and close, plus Vec children, would carry them.

Individual expression files can also be translated one per file. Each one is a uniform set of three parts: type deduction, cg_expr choosing a function, and eval functions.

What they both run on does not map to safe idiomatic Rust:
- Untyped char** frames are addressed by offsets fixed at code generation.
- ObDatum pointers point back into those frames.
- Every operator shares mutable frames and arenas through const references.
- Raw function pointers are chosen at code generation and serialized as addresses.
- Specs are arena-freed with no destructors, and operators use a hand-written destroy().
- Row stores swizzle pointers, and hash chains are stored inside the rows.
- Storage writes directly into engine frames and runs engine-owned filter executors.

Keeping that layout means a large unsafe Rust core that copies C++ memory rules. Doing it properly means a redesign: typed column batches, expression ids into an arena (the existing index-based ObExpr serialization already points that way), an Arc-shared plan instead of in-process serialization, and Drop-based lifetimes. That redesign reaches into src/sql/code_generator, which lays out the frames, and src/storage/access, which fills them. So the core must be redesigned, and the ~61 operators and ~528 expressions are then translated file by file against the new API.

Files that force the call: src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h, src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_frame_info.cpp, src/query/api/query/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.h, src/sql/engine/basic/ob_chunk_datum_store.h, src/storage/access/ob_vector_store.cpp, src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the main call: redesign the core first, then translate the operators and expressions one by one against the new API. The files that force it are the right ones. ob_expr.h:367/436 has untyped frame offsets with datum pointers back into the frame. ob_expr_frame_info.cpp:231-280 allocates the frames. ob_vector_store.cpp:109-112 and ob_pushdown_filter.h show storage filling engine frames and running engine filters. ob_chunk_datum_store.h does pointer swizzling. ob_operator_factory.cpp does placement new with no destructors. The frame layout is computed in src/sql/code_generator/ob_static_engine_expr_cg.cpp (create_tmp_frameinfo at :712, arrange_datums_data at :904), so the code generator has to move together with the core. I disagree on three points. (1) The recommended 'Arc-shared plan instead of in-process serialization' is mostly in place already. ObPhysicalPlan is a ref-counted ObPlanCacheObject (ob_physical_plan.h:60) that concurrent executions share, and const frames live in the plan. PX workers share the SQC's plan by default (_px_worker_share_plan_enabled=True; ob_dfo.cpp:579-615). Only the one SQC-level serialization remains. So the real constraint is that the plan must be Send+Sync and immutable, and a few runtime const_casts into shared specs break that today (ob_groupby_op.cpp:61, ob_hash_join_op.cpp:431-433, ob_px_util.h:508/540). (2) 'Operators translate close to one-to-one' holds for control flow only: 66 of 156 operator files touch frame internals directly. (3) Expressions cannot be translated one file at a time against an engine-only API. The resolver calls their compile-time half (ob_raw_expr_deduce_type.cpp). 183 expr files read session state, 23 include sql/pl headers, and most depend on geo, json, number, time and cast libraries outside the area. So the expression unit of work also depends on the session, PL and value-library boundaries being settled first.

**Size split check (verifier):** The size split reproduces exactly. With cat|wc -l over git ls-files, which is a different method from the survey's xargs wc: src/sql/engine is 1,379 files / 351,853 lines, src/sql/das 103 / 46,252, src/sql/dtl 42 / 9,995, and src/query/api/query/{engine,das} 55 / 14,439 (51/14,150 + 4/289). Every engine subdirectory figure matches: expr 921/170,218, px 121/41,158, basic 78/34,043, aggregate 18,536, cmd 18,401, dml 14,141, join 13,153, top level 12,336, table 6,337, sort 5,676, window_function 4,973, set 3,478, pdml 2,885, and the rest at or under 2,041. Non-C/C++ tracked files in the area are only the README, 15 .map files (16,512 lines), gen_expr_str_cmp_func.py and prepare/BUILD.bazel. The generated code is 9 files / 425 lines and the pinyin table is 1,528 lines, both correct. The hand-written total of about 406,100 is right (349,900 + 46,252 + 9,995 = 406,147). Small corrections: ob_expr_str_cmp_func_common.ipp (27 lines) in the generated directory is hand-written; one .map (ob_expr_compatiable_promotion.map) is dead; and two .map files are included by ob_expr_promotion_util.cpp, not ob_expr_result_type_util.cpp. The split leaves out value libraries that the expressions cannot work without, all outside the area: src/share/geo 47K, oblib json_type 26.6K, ObNumber 10.1K, ob_obj_cast 14.8K, ObTimeConverter 5.7K and charset 11.5K. Whether they are counted elsewhere should be checked. No vendored code was found.

**Units of work:** 1. Framework core, done first because it fixes the API everything else uses. About 30 files:
   - ObOperator/ObOpSpec/ObOpInput with the factory and registration (src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator*.h, src/sql/engine/ob_operator*.cpp)
   - ObExpr, ObEvalCtx and ObExprFrameInfo
   - ob_exec_context, ob_physical_plan(_ctx)
   - batch rows and bit vectors
   - serialization helpers
   This group must be designed together with src/sql/code_generator (20,521 lines) and the storage fill path in src/storage/access.

2. Operators: one unit per registered operator (Spec/Op[/Input] as an .h/.cpp pair). There are 61 registered types in 78 *_op.cpp files across basic, aggregate, join, sort, set, window_function, recursive_cte, subquery, table, dml, pdml and px/exchange. Large shared helpers form their own units: ob_hash_partitioning_infrastructure_op.h (3,085 lines), ob_aggregate_processor.cpp (7,937), ob_hash_join_op.cpp (5,499), ob_sort_op_impl.cpp (3,365).

3. Expressions: one unit per ob_expr_xxx.{h,cpp} pair. There are 528 registered types in about 451 ob_expr_*.cpp files (465 .cpp in engine/expr). Shared helper groups:
   - cast: ob_datum_cast.cpp and friends, 9 files / 17,305 lines
   - compare-function tables: ob_expr_cmp_func*.ipp/.cpp plus the two *_compile directories, 21 files
   - result-type utilities plus the 15 .map tables
   - json utilities (71 files), geometry utilities (102), array utilities (66), ai functions (14)

4. Row stores and spilling: about 30 files in engine/basic (the *_store.* files and chunk_store/), plus ob_sql_memory_manager and ob_sql_mem_mgr_processor.

5. PX: 121 files / 41,158 lines. Natural groups are scheduler and coordinator (top-level px), exchange (24 files), datahub (21) and p2p_datahub runtime filters (12).

6. DTL: 42 files / 9,995 lines, as one group.

7. DAS:
   - core: 49 top-level files / 17,067 lines (task, ref, ctdef/rtdef, DML ops, tablet mapper)
   - iterators: 54 files / 29,185 lines. These split into lookup/merge iterators, vector-index HNSW/IVF (which talk to vsag through src/query/api/query/vector) and full-text/sparse retrieval.

8. engine/cmd: 64 files / 18,401 lines of command executors (DDL, load data, variable set, user commands). These belong with the command/DDL layer rather than the execution engine.

**Public-surface tests:** Every one of the 283 .test / 305 .result files under tools/deploy/mysql_test (95,255 test lines) runs through this engine, because any SELECT or DML goes through operators, expressions and DAS. They are all public-surface: SQL in, text out. No internal C++ tests remain for engine, DAS or DTL (unittest/ holds only two .gitignore files, and 27 unittest/sql/engine files were deleted in 45cbbe9e1), so no test depends on internals and all of them survive a rewrite.

Suites that target this area:
- test_suite/expr: 11 tests (collation_expr, expr_ceil, expr_floor, expr_instr, expr_locate, func_regexp, mul, ...)
- test_suite/executor: 3 tests (basic, full_join, trx)
- test_suite/groupby: 3
- test_suite/window_function: 2
- test_suite/subquery: 11
- test_suite/array: 2
- test_suite/vector_index: 22 (DAS HNSW/IVF iterators)
- test_suite/fts_index: 15 (DAS text retrieval)
- test_suite/geometry: 42
- top-level t/: 93, including join_basic, join_many_table, join_null, distinct, limit, topk, intersect, except, count, func_group_*, two_order_by, parallel_insert

Feature counts over all 283 tests:
- 22 use parallel( hints or PX settings
- 61 contain JOIN
- 39 contain GROUP BY
- 123 contain ORDER BY
- 12 use MATCH(
- 30 use vector distance or vector index features
- 66 .result files contain plan tables. That output comes from the optimizer, but it fixes operator names in the expected results.

Coverage gap: only 141 of 414 function names appear in call form. addtime, atan2, aes_encrypt, bin_to_uuid, bit_length, array_sort and array_contains appear in no test at all. So expression and cast parity needs differential runs against the C++ binary, beyond these suites.

A further 500 .test files sit under tools/obtest, mostly distributed-era suites: primary_standby 145, storage 60, rootservice 41, sql 15, tsc 11, join 5. Whether they still run against seekdb is unverified.

**Hard parts:**
- Untyped per-query frames with offsets fixed at code generation, and ObDatum.ptr_ pointing back into reserved space in the same frame: src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h:367,436; src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_frame_info.cpp:231-300; the frame layout is computed in src/sql/code_generator/ob_static_engine_expr_cg.cpp.
- Shared mutable data behind shared references: every operator's ObEvalCtx points at the same frames, temporary arena and flag (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr.cpp:47-58), and storage writes into them too (src/storage/access/ob_vector_store.cpp:109-112,261). In Rust this needs UnsafeCell/raw pointers or a redesign around ownership of each batch.
- Raw C function pointers chosen in cg_expr and serialized as raw addresses (src/query/api/query/engine/ob_serializable_function.h), plus 48 global function-pointer tables filled by template recursion over ObObjType × ObObjType × collation (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_cmp_func.ipp:900-1010, src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_cmp_func_compile/, OB_DATUM_CAST_MYSQL_IMPLICIT in src/sql/engine/expr/ob_datum_cast.cpp). Rust would need enum ids or generic-generated tables in their place.
- Operator registration macro framework: REGISTER_OPERATOR with preprocessor argument counting and CONCAT tricks, template trait specializations, a PHY_END-sized function table built by ObArrayConstIniter, and dynamic_cast to optimizer ObLogXxx classes (src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator_reg.h, src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp:137-330). DAS has the same pattern (src/sql/das/ob_das_def_reg.h).
- Plan trees serialized inside one process for PX, with ObExpr* encoded as indexes through a thread-local array (src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h:577,1323-1360; src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_util.h:280 ObPxTreeSerializer; src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_local_sqc_launcher.cpp:428-490). ObExprFrameInfo::assign rebases parent pointers by pointer arithmetic (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_frame_info.cpp:150-165).
- Lifetimes managed by hand: specs freed by arena clear without destructors (src/sql/engine/README, src/sql/engine/ob_physical_plan.cpp:196); operators torn down with destroy(), which every subclass must chain (ob_operator.h:433, 108 overrides in 90 files); 288 reset()/reuse() definitions in 127 files; placement new of an object after its child-pointer array (ob_operator_factory.cpp AllocSpecHelper/AllocOpHelper).
- Custom allocators everywhere: ObArenaAllocator (456 hits), MemoryContext (107), 3 arenas in ObExecContext (src/sql/engine/ob_exec_context.h:794-892), auto memory management and spill via ObSqlMemMgrProcessor/ObSqlMemoryManager (src/sql/engine/ob_sql_memory_manager.cpp, 20 operator files).
- Row stores with pointer swizzling and variable-length packed rows: src/sql/engine/basic/ob_chunk_datum_store.h (StoredRow::build with its unswizzling flag), ob_ra_datum_store, ob_temp_block_store, the chunk_store/ directory, and dtl block unswizzling (src/sql/dtl/ob_dtl_basic_channel.cpp:1106,1186); 94 swizzl hits in 13 files.
- Linked structures stored inside the objects themselves: hash join bucket chains through set_next on stored rows (src/sql/engine/join/ob_hash_join_op.h:468-557), ObHashPartCols::next_ reinterpreted as a pointer (src/sql/engine/basic/ob_hash_partitioning_infrastructure_op.h:66), and ObExprOperator derived from ObDLinkBase (src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr_operator.h:304).
- The pushdown filter executor tree is defined in the engine but run inside storage's micro-block scanners (src/query/api/query/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.h, 14 executor/node classes; src/sql/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.cpp, 3,017 lines; 419 references in 46 src/storage files). It cannot move to Rust separately from storage's read path.
- SIMD tied to the C++ ObDatum byte layout (static_assert(12 == sizeof(ObDatum)) in src/sql/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter_simd.cpp), AVX2/AVX-512 function versions via OB_DECLARE_*_SPECIFIC_CODE (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_like.cpp, ob_expr_trim.cpp, src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_bloom_filter.h), and batch loops that rely on the compiler auto-vectorizing them (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_batch_eval_util.h).
- PX/DTL concurrency: worker threads, channel flow control and atomics (98 hits in 25 px files, 80 in 14 dtl files); buffer ownership passed by nulling a pointer reference, with unchecked reinterpret_cast downcasts (src/sql/dtl/ob_dtl_local_channel.cpp:57-120).
- Unions and bit-fields in hot structs: ObExpr flag_/extra_ unions, ObEvalInfo bit-fields, ObDatumMeta precision/length_semantics union (src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h), and the ObOperator/ObOpSpec child_/left_ union (src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator.h:323,582).
- A large body of MySQL semantics that has to match exactly: the cast matrix src/sql/engine/expr/ob_datum_cast.cpp (13,621 lines), 15 type-promotion tables (16,512 lines), json (71 files / 15,582 lines), geometry (102 files / 14,441 lines), datetime (66 / 10,907) and array (66 / 10,444) expressions. The hard part is proving the Rust version behaves the same, not writing it.
- Errors are int return codes (17,167 OB_FAIL). Callbacks that cannot return errors keep them in a member, for example the sort comparator ret_ in src/sql/engine/sort/ob_sort_op_impl.cpp:395-420. A Rust port has to decide whether comparator failures become panics, pre-validation or a Result-aware sort.

**Pain instances:**
- bc31e03f4 (2026-06-22) 'fix(px): own SQC serialize buffer to avoid UAF'. An async closure in src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp captured a raw pointer into the exec_ctx arena, and the old comment argued this was safe. The fix uses std::shared_ptr<char[]>. Rust's 'static + Send bound on spawned closures rejects the original code.
- 5aa611c00 (2026-04-09) and b1587c030 (2026-04-17). In its error state the sort comparator returned false for every pair, so introsort read past the array bounds and aborted on macOS. src/sql/engine/sort/ob_sort_op_impl.cpp:401-408 still carries a pointer-compare fallback. Rust's sort_by stays memory-safe even with an inconsistent comparator.
- a44b2526f (2025-12-19) 'Fix core due to wrong static type cast'. src/sql/das/iter/ob_das_iter_utils.cpp cast rowkey_scan_ctdef to ObDASScanCtDef with static_cast. An enum or a checked downcast would reject this.
- c68c6749a (2025-11-06) 'Fix core due to nullptr in ObDASTRMergeIter'. It adds OB_NOT_NULL guards before destroy() on four parameter arrays in src/sql/das/iter/sparse_retrieval/ob_das_tr_merge_iter.cpp inner_release.
- 1e4f589b8 (2026-04-20) 'fix: crash caused by uninitialized discrete vector ptrs on Windows'. Frame memory was deliberately not zeroed, and readers read garbage ptr/len. The workaround was a MEMSET under #ifdef _WIN32 in src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr.cpp, whose comment says the real bad operator was never found. That code path was later removed with vec 2.0.
- 38054020d (2026-06-15) 'pl function has memory leak issue in long running sql'. A per-call ParamStore was allocated on the query-lifetime arena, so memory grew with every PL call. The fix added a context-owned arena in src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_udf.h. It is a lifetime mismatch that ownership rules make explicit.
- Latent, found by reading and not reported in git: src/sql/engine/basic/ob_limit_op.cpp:231-236 reads child_brs->end_ even when child_->get_next_batch failed. src/sql/engine/ob_operator.cpp:1021-1030 leaves batch_rows as nullptr when check_stack_once() fails, and stack size was cut to 256K in a5b53834c, which makes that failure more likely. With Result and ? the output could not be used on failure.
- Defensive code: 4,712 OB_ISNULL( checks across the area. The ENABLE_DEBUG_LOG dummy_mem_context_ in ObOperator exists to catch operators that are not closed or destroyed (src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator.h:644,693). src/sql/dtl/ob_dtl_local_channel.cpp:57 warns 'Each return path must set on_finish otherwise subsequent operations will hang'. A Drop guard would cover all three.

**Open questions:**
- Is any serialized spec, ObExpr or frame form stored anywhere that outlives the process, such as the plan cache, disk or an upgrade path? Raw function-pointer encoding (C5) suggests not, but I did not trace every caller of the serialize methods.
- How much of the batch evaluation relies on the compiler auto-vectorizing the 16-row bitmap loops? A Rust rewrite needs a performance baseline, and that requires a build and benchmark run, which a read-only survey cannot do.
- What is the real line and branch coverage of operators and expressions from the 283 mysqltests? I could only estimate expression coverage from function names (C17). No coverage run is possible read-only.
- Does ObStaticEngineExprCG (src/sql/code_generator/ob_static_engine_expr_cg.cpp) have to move to Rust together with the engine? It computes the frame layout, so changing the frame model forces changes there.
- Do the 16 non-vectorized operators (DML, values, topk, function table, json table) really need a separate row path, or can they be vectorized as part of the rewrite?
- Are the 500 .test files under tools/obtest still runnable against seekdb? If so, they add public-surface coverage for tsc, join and sql. Their suite names (primary_standby, rootservice) suggest most are dead.
- Is the vsag coupling in the DAS HNSW iterator shallow enough to call through C++ FFI? src/sql/das/iter/ob_das_hnsw_scan_iter.cpp has 10 vsag references, mostly behind src/query/api/query/vector/ob_vector_index_adaptor.h, which I did not read.
- About 42 of the 104 PHY_OP_DEF types are no longer registered. I did not check whether other code (optimizer, plan monitor, explain) still produces them, or whether they can be dropped rather than carried into Rust.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The compiled plan is already a shared, read-only, ref-counted object used by many sessions at once. A Rust design has to keep it Send+Sync, and a few places in the current code write into it at runtime. (evidence: src/sql/engine/ob_physical_plan.h:60 `class ObPhysicalPlan : public ObPlanCacheObject`. src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_frame_info.cpp:225-248: const frame memory 'comes from shared memory in plan' (const_frame_ptrs_). src/share/parameter/ob_parameter_seed.ipp:1148 _px_worker_share_plan_enabled default "True", and src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo.cpp:579-615 skips the plan and spec tree for workers. Writes through const: src/sql/engine/aggregate/ob_groupby_op.cpp:61, src/sql/engine/join/ob_hash_join_op.cpp:431-433, src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_util.h:508,540 (16 const_casts to ObExpr, specs, plan or frame info in 11 engine/das/storage files).)
- Expressions are tied to the SQL session and the PL engine, not only to frames and storage. They cannot move to Rust until a session and PL interface exists on the Rust side. (evidence: 183 files in src/sql/engine/expr reference GET_MY_SESSION, get_my_session() or ObSQLSessionInfo; 549 such lines in 191 engine+das files. Across the area, 83 files include sql/session, 72 sql/resolver, 40 sql/pl and 15 sql/optimizer headers. In engine/expr, 70 include sql/session and 23 include sql/pl. 17 expr files use PL exec types (ob_expr_udf.cpp, ob_expr_pl_*.cpp, ob_expr_obj_access.cpp, ob_expr_get_package_var.cpp).)
- Expression semantics rest on large value libraries outside the surveyed area and not in its 406K-line count, so the survey's size understates the cost of porting expressions. (evidence: src/share/geo is 47,252 lines, included 111 times from engine/expr. src/oblib/common/json_type is 26,565 lines (34 includes). src/oblib/common/number/ob_number_v2.{h,cpp} is 10,103 lines, used in 88 expr files. src/oblib/common/timezone/ob_time_convert.{h,cpp} is 5,749 lines (33 expr files). src/share/object/ob_obj_cast.{h,cpp} is 14,797 lines, a second cast matrix beside ob_datum_cast.cpp. src/oblib/lib/charset is 11,460 lines.)
- Vectorization is shallow, and the row-at-a-time path is still there after 8f96ded56. A Rust engine has to keep both a row and a batch evaluation API, and the current performance baseline is mostly one indirect call per row per expression. (evidence: 577 `eval_func_ = ` against 92 `eval_batch_func_ = `. expr_default_eval_batch_func (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr.cpp:711-751) loops calling expr.eval_func_ per row. 58 engine files still define Ob*Op::inner_get_next_row, and the 16 non-vectorized registered operators run through get_next_batch_with_onlyone_row (src/sql/engine/ob_operator.cpp:1136-1141). About 511 scalar `->eval(ctx...)` call sites in engine/das/storage. ObExpr::eval in ob_expr.h:1040-1068 is the scalar entry point.)
- The engine reads process-global and thread-local state instead of taking it as arguments. A Rust port needs accessors for these across FFI, or a context object passed down. (evidence: Over the five directories: GCTX 131 lines in 41 files, GCONF 82/56, share::server_service<> 72/41 (for example ob_das_scan_iter.cpp:38-40, ob_dtl_local_channel.cpp:71), THIS_WORKER 62/33, CURRENT_CONTEXT 49/43, lib::ContextParam 45/40, and RLOCAL g_expr_ser_array (ob_expr.h:577). The DTL channel registry is also global (DTL.get_channel in ob_dtl_local_channel.cpp:74).)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey can be trusted for sizes, commit citations and the behavior claims at the lines it cites. Every size figure reproduced exactly with a different counting method. All nine commit citations match their diffs. The behavior claims I checked (frames, ObEvalCtx sharing, raw function-pointer serialization, ObVectorStore filling frames, the ObLimitOp null dereference, the no-destructor teardown, DTL ownership) describe what the code does. The errors are in derived counts and in one behavior claim. The SIMD line sum is 67, not 63. The 104 PHY_OP_DEF figure includes a #define line: there are 101 real types and 40 unregistered, not 42. The class-hierarchy counts are low because the grep matched single-line class headers only (about 574 expression classes, not 544; 74 operator classes, not 72). The 48 function tables is a lower bound tied to a narrow type list (at least 61). C14 overstates in-process serialization: workers share the plan by default, and only the per-SQC step serializes the full tree. The biggest weakness is what the survey leaves out, and it bears on the decision. It frames the engine as coupled mainly to storage. It misses that expressions depend on the session, PL and about 115K lines of value libraries outside the area, that the plan is already a shared immutable cache object, and that most 'vectorized' evaluation is a per-row function-pointer loop. The structure call points the right way, but it underestimates how much of the operator and expression code must be rewritten rather than translated. Process note: I wrote one scratch file, /tmp/_x_all, by mistake while counting. I deleted it right away and confirmed it was gone. Nothing in the worktree or build_release/ was touched.


## AREA: sql-optimizer
**What it is:** This is the middle of the SQL compiler. src/sql/rewrite applies 34 query transformations to the resolver's statement objects (ObDMLStmt, ObRawExpr) and also holds the code that extracts query ranges. src/sql/optimizer enumerates join orders and access paths, costs them with a selectivity and cost model, and builds a tree of ObLogicalOperator nodes that the code generator turns into a physical plan; it also contains the DBMS_STATS statistics subsystem (src/sql/optimizer/stat).

**Size split:** Hand-written: 100% of the counted lines. Generated: 0. Vendored: 0. Data: 1 file.
- src/sql/optimizer: 205 .h/.cpp files (105 .h, 100 .cpp), 155,780 lines. Split by subsystem:
  - stat/ (DBMS_STATS, stat caches, gather tasks): 64 files, 26,638 lines
  - plan classes *_log_plan.*: 14 files, 28,678 lines
  - operator files ob_log_* plus the two factories: 69 files, 22,938 lines
  - ob_logical_operator.*: 2 files, 7,742 lines
  - join order and conflict detector: 4 files, 22,448 lines
  - cost, selectivity and dynamic sampling: 19 files, 21,736 lines
  - table location, route and physical location: 4 files, 6,711 lines
  - driver, context, util and misc: 29 files, 18,889 lines
  Plus the data table ob_join_property.map (363 lines, a hand-written C bool array included by ob_conflict_detector.cpp:26), BUILD.bazel and README.
- src/sql/rewrite: 95 .h/.cpp files (48 .h, 47 .cpp), 97,268 lines:
  - ob_transform_* (rules, rule base, transformer driver, utils): 74 files, 79,986 lines
  - query range (ob_query_range_*, ob_range_*, ob_expr_range_converter, ob_key_part): 11 files, 12,364 lines
  - other helpers (stmt comparer, predicate deduce, equal analysis, union find, expand aggregate): 10 files, 4,918 lines
  Plus README.
Commands:
- `git ls-files src/sql/optimizer src/sql/rewrite | awk -F. '{print $NF}' | sort | uniq -c`
- `git ls-files <dir> | grep -E '\.(h|cpp)$' | grep -E <pattern> | xargs cat | wc -l` for each group; the groups sum to 155,780 and 97,268.
- `git grep -liE 'do not edit|auto-?generated|generated by'` matched only prose comments, so no generated files.
- A license grep found no third-party code.

**Summary:** Almost everything here is hand-written C++: about 253K lines in 300 files, with no generated or vendored code. The algorithms are layered clearly: 34 rules applied in a fixed order, 17 named plan-tree passes, 30 direct ObLogicalOperator subclasses and 8 Path subclasses. The hierarchies are shallow (depth 3 at most) and use no multiple inheritance, so they map naturally onto Rust enums or traits.

What resists a one-to-one port is the data model, not the class shapes:
- Plans, paths and expressions are raw-pointer graphs placed by placement new into per-query arenas, and some destructors are only run by hand.
- Operators hold child_, parent_ and my_plan_ back-pointers, and candidate plans share child subtrees.
- ObRawExpr nodes are shared across statements, operators and the runtime (rt_expr_). They are identified by address in hash maps (34 reinterpret_cast<uint64_t> key sites) and replaced in place through ObRawExpr*& parameters (488) and ObRawExprPointer, which stores ObRawExpr** slot addresses.
- Statements and expressions point at each other (ObQueryRefRawExpr::ref_stmt_, ObExecParamRawExpr::outer_expr_, TableItem::ref_query_).

Rewrite and the optimizer depend on each other. Eight rule files call accept_transform (13 calls; the base rule's evaluate_cost is in a ninth file), and that path runs the full optimizer on deep copies of statements and then restores shared counters by hand. So the two directories, and the resolver's ObRawExpr and ObDMLStmt types they share, have to move together.

Deep recursion depends on SMART_CALL stack switching (617 call sites), which matters because worker stacks were cut to 256K.

The test surface is mysqltest only; no C++ unit tests exist for this area. CI runs 272 configured cases. 40 of them pin 658 EXPLAIN tables, and 568 of those tables in 33 files pin EST.ROWS and EST.TIME, computed as ceil() of doubles. The masking in the tests covers only table ids and plan signatures, never the cost columns. So a port must reproduce the cost arithmetic exactly (to the ceil boundary). It must also reproduce the tie order of std::sort at 34 unstable-sort sites and the iteration order of OB's own hash maps at 3 sites.

History already shows platform-dependent plan output and aliasing bugs. Commit 0dca2a5d3 fixed a NaN sort key that ordered filters differently on macOS. Commit 71e2b595b fixed a shared-subtree copy bug that caused error 4016. Only a redesign of expression ownership (immutable or copy-on-write expressions with IDs) would remove that second class of bug; a straight Rc<RefCell> translation would keep it.

**Structure call (surveyor):** Split verdict. The algorithm layer maps closely onto Rust:
- 34 rule classes applied in a fixed order (ob_transformer_impl.cpp:455-487)
- 17 named plan-tree passes (ob_optimizer.h TraverseOp)
- 30 operator subclasses that could become an enum or trait
- shallow, single-inheritance hierarchies, few templates (55 lines) and no macro framework beyond X-macros and OB_FAIL chains

These could be ported one file stem at a time while keeping names, pass order and cost formulas, and pass order and cost formulas have to stay the same anyway to keep the pinned EXPLAIN output. The data model does not translate:
- per-query arenas with placement new and destructors run by hand
- operators with parent_, my_plan_ and void* back-pointers, and candidate plans sharing child subtrees
- expressions shared across stmts, operators and the runtime, identified by address and rewritten in place through ObRawExpr*& and ObRawExpr** slots
- stmt and expression cycles
- rewrite running the optimizer on deep copies and then undoing global counters by hand

In idiomatic Rust this becomes typed-ID arenas (ExprId, StmtId, OpId, PathId) with explicit copy and replace operations. That is a redesign of ObRawExpr and ObDMLStmt, which live in src/sql/resolver and are read by the code generator, so it cannot be scoped to the optimizer alone. A literal port on raw pointers would compile only as wall-to-wall unsafe code and would bring nothing Rust offers.

Files that force the redesign: src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h, src/sql/resolver/dml/ob_dml_stmt.h, src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.h, src/sql/optimizer/ob_join_order.h, src/sql/optimizer/ob_join_order.cpp, src/sql/rewrite/ob_transform_rule.cpp.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the split verdict. Porting the algorithms file by file is feasible: the rule list is really fixed in code (ob_transformer_impl.cpp:455-487, 33 APPLY_RULE_IF_NEEDED plus pre/post process), there really are 17 TraverseOp passes (ob_optimizer.h:38-90), and the area's own classes are single inheritance and at most 3 deep. The data model does force a redesign that crosses areas: ObRawExpr/ObDMLStmt in src/sql/resolver (ob_raw_expr.h:2226-2234, 2786, 2892, 3128; ob_dml_stmt.h:278), address-keyed copier and replacer in resolver/expr, and the code generator reading ObLog* nodes (ob_static_engine_cg.h, 95 ObLog*& parameters). Three things make the redesign bigger than the survey says. (1) Identity-by-address is not limited to 34 hash-map sites. ObOptimizerUtil::find_item (ob_optimizer_util.h:265-287) is documented as 'based on pointer comparison to determine if two expressions are the same'. It and the set helpers built on it (append_array_no_dup, remove_item, intersect, is_subset) appear at about 580 call lines in the area, and there is a whole pass, ADJUST_SHARED_EXPR, for shared expressions. Any ExprId design has to reproduce these equality semantics exactly, or plans change. (2) 'File by file, keeping names' and 'typed-ID arenas' conflict. With 488 ObRawExpr*& parameters and 18,618 OB_FAIL lines, the redesign changes the signature of nearly every function, so this is a rewrite of every file against a new model, not a translation. (3) Rewrite builds ObOptimizer at 5 sites in 3 files, not one, so the requirement to switch rewrite and optimizer over together is firmer than stated. A literal raw-pointer port would indeed be mostly unsafe code.

**Size split check (verifier):** Reproduced. The optimizer has 205 .h/.cpp files and 155,780 lines; rewrite has 95 files and 97,268 lines. I recounted five of the eight optimizer groups directly with grep plus cat | wc -l: stat/ 64 files/26,638; *_log_plan.* 14/28,678; ob_log_* operators plus the two factories 69/22,938; ob_logical_operator.* 7,742; join order plus conflict detector 4/22,448 (ob_join_order.cpp alone is 17,680). The other three groups add up to 47,336, which matches the survey's 21,736 + 6,711 + 18,889. All three rewrite groups (79,986 / 12,364 / 4,918) reproduce exactly. The hand-written, not-generated, not-vendored call holds: the only 'generated' hits are prose comments, and all 300 files carry the same OceanBase 2025 header. The split has two gaps. First, 7 of the 105 optimizer headers (ob_opt_default_stat.h, stat/ob_stat_define.h, ob_opt_column_stat.h, ob_opt_table_stat.h, ob_opt_ds_stat.h, ob_opt_osg_column_stat.h, ob_stat_item.h) are 18-19-line forwarders to src/query/api/query/optimizer. Second, that directory holds 3,195 more lines of optimizer type definitions that the count leaves out (DistAlgo and other algorithm enums, location defs, the stat structs, a 1,090-line ob_stat_define.h). The real optimizer footprint is therefore about 159K lines, and the stat/ group is bigger than 26.6K.

**Units of work:** The natural unit is a file stem (.h plus .cpp pair). There are 153 stems: 105 in src/sql/optimizer (73 outside stat/, 32 in stat/) and 48 in src/sql/rewrite. They fall into about 10 groups:
1. Logical operators: 33 ob_log_* operator stems plus the operator and plan factories, about 22.9K lines.
2. Plan classes: 7 *_log_plan stems, 28.7K lines.
3. ObLogicalOperator base: 1 stem, 7.7K lines.
4. Join order and conflict detector: 2 stems plus the .map file, 22.4K lines. ob_join_order.cpp alone is 17.7K lines and has to be split internally.
5. Cost, selectivity and dynamic sampling: about 10 stems, 21.7K lines.
6. Location, sharding and partition-wise join: about 5 stems, shared with the executor.
7. Driver, context and utils: about 15 stems, 18.9K lines.
8. stat/ DBMS_STATS subsystem: 32 stems, 26.6K lines. It is loosely coupled: inner SQL, KV cache and timer tasks.
9. Transform rules: 34 rule stems plus rule base, transformer driver and ob_transform_utils (14.2K lines), 80K lines in total.
10. Query range: 6 stems, 12.4K lines, shared with the executor and plan cache; plus 5 helper stems.

Ordering constraint: groups 1-7 and 9 cannot start until the ObRawExpr, ObDMLStmt and ObRawExprFactory data model in src/sql/resolver has been redesigned (46K lines in resolver/dml, 35K in resolver/expr, another area). Rewrite and the optimizer then have to switch over in the same step because of evaluate_cost. The stat/ group and parts of cost and selectivity can go earlier behind a narrow interface.

**Public-surface tests:** All tests for this area go through the public SQL surface; there are no C++ unit tests (unittest/ holds only .gitignore files). CI discovers only tools/deploy/mysql_test (.github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py:246-300) and runs the 272 cases listed in tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml. Every one of those queries goes through rewrite and the optimizer.

The cases that pin optimizer output directly:
- 42 .result files under tools/deploy/mysql_test contain 820 plan tables. 40 of the files are CI-configured and hold 658 tables.
- 568 tables in 33 CI files pin '|ID|OPERATOR|NAME|EST.ROWS|EST.TIME(us)|'; 90 are basic tables with only ID, OPERATOR and NAME.
- The 'Outputs & filters' blocks also pin expression order, filters and rowset=16 (3,598 rowset= lines in 33 files).
- Masking covers only table ids and plan signatures, in 18 of the 40 test files that contain EXPLAIN.

Largest examples:
- test_suite/executor/r/mysql/basic.result (152 tables)
- test_suite/subquery/r/mysql/subquery.result (42)
- test_suite/vector_index/r/mysql/sparse_vector_index_vsag_query.result (41)
- test_suite/geometry/r/mysql/geometry_filter_mysql.result (39)
- test_suite/fts_index/r/mysql/simple_query.result (39)
- r/mysql/dist_nest_loop_simple.result (38)
- test_suite/executor/r/mysql/full_join.result (36)
- r/mysql/topk.result (28)
- test_suite/subquery/r/mysql/optimizer_subquery_bug.result (27)
- test_suite/global_index/r/mysql/global_index_lookup_{1,2,3,5}.result (20-23 each)

Not run by CI:
- test_suite/update/r/mysql/update2.result (128 tables) and test_suite/geometry/r/mysql/geometry_index2_mysql.result (34) are not in the config.
- tools/obtest (386 EST. ROWS|COST tables and 112 basic tables) is not discovered by the runner.

Regression cases for rewrite correctness without plans: t/sqlancer_optimizer_regressions.test (configured, 37 lines).

These tests survive a port unchanged because they touch no internals. They only pass if the Rust optimizer reproduces plan shape, expression order, and ceil() of the cost and row estimates exactly.

**Hard parts:**
- Shared, mutable expression graph with pointer identity. Hash maps are keyed on reinterpret_cast<uint64_t>(expr) at 34 lines, e.g. ObAllocExprContext in src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp:255-313. ObRawExprPointer stores ObRawExpr** slot addresses (src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h:5137-5150). There are 488 ObRawExpr*& in-out parameters, and ObRawExprCopier and ObRawExprReplacer run everywhere: 264 replace_expr calls and 85 ObRawExprCopier references in the area.
- Cycles between stmts and expressions, and expressions pointing into the runtime: ObQueryRefRawExpr::ref_stmt_, ObExecParamRawExpr::outer_expr_ and ObColumnRefRawExpr::dependant_expr_ (src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h), TableItem::ref_query_ (src/sql/resolver/dml/ob_dml_stmt.h:278), and ObRawExpr::rt_expr_, which the code generator fills in.
- Operator graph with back-pointers. ObLogicalOperator has child_, parent_, my_plan_ and a void* traverse_ctx_ that is cast back to OpCtx* (ob_logical_operator.h:1674, 1742, 1863, 1871; ob_log_exchange.cpp:589). Candidate plans share child subtrees (ob_select_log_plan.cpp:830-855), and set_child overwrites parent_.
- Arena lifetimes with manual destruction. Placement new is used in ob_log_operator_factory.cpp and ob_log_plan_factory.cpp. ObLogPlan::destory is not the destructor (ob_log_plan.cpp:135). The ~ObRawExprFactory cleanup depends on the thread type (ob_raw_expr.h:5018-5025). JoinPath objects are recycled with placement new over reused memory (ob_join_order.cpp:5435-5446, 11037-11050, 11077), relying on a hand-written reuse() that resets every field (ob_join_order.cpp:8153).
- Rewrite and the optimizer call each other. evaluate_cost deep-copies stmts, runs ObOptimizer inside a temporary memory context, then restores query_ctx and transformer counters by hand (src/sql/rewrite/ob_transform_rule.cpp:346-420; ObEvalCostHelper and ObTryTransHelper in ob_transform_rule.h:237-290). There are 42 and 23 cross-includes between the two directories.
- Deep recursion over stmt, expression and plan trees relies on SMART_CALL stack switching: 617 sites, implemented in src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_smart_call.h:100-138 with jump_call onto a heap stack, under 256K worker stacks (commit a5b53834c). Rust needs stacker-style stack growth or explicit work stacks.
- Exact numeric reproduction for the pinned EXPLAIN. EST.ROWS and EST.TIME are ceil() of doubles (src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp:1189-1192). Libm calls appear in ob_opt_est_cost_model.cpp, ob_join_order.cpp and ob_opt_selectivity.cpp (about 35 call lines). Summation order and possible clang FMA contraction on arm64 must be matched.
- Tie order of unstable sorts. There are 34 lib::ob_sort sites wrapping std::sort (src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_sort.h:28-41), e.g. ob_log_join.cpp:794 (sort by table name) and ob_join_order.cpp:4553 (sort by index_). Rust's sort_unstable uses a different algorithm, so every comparator needs a full tie-breaker, and that alone may change the pinned plans.
- Iteration order of OB's own hash containers leaks into output at src/sql/optimizer/ob_join_order.cpp:16662 and :16727 (full-text tokens) and src/sql/rewrite/ob_range_generator.cpp:1799 (GIS cell ids). A Rust HashMap would change that order.
- Structures shared with the executor. ObTableLocation (src/sql/optimizer/ob_table_location.cpp, 30 serialization macro uses) and the query-range graph (src/sql/rewrite/ob_query_range_define.*, ob_key_part.*) are stored in ObPhysicalPlan, ObPlanSet and ObTableScanOp, so their layout and serialization cross the optimizer boundary.
- Volume of the error-code style: 18,618 OB_FAIL( and 7,713 OB_ISNULL in the area. The change to Result and ? is mechanical but touches almost every line, and most OB_ISNULL checks exist only because every link is a nullable raw pointer.

**Pain instances:**
- Commit 0dca2a5d3 ("fix gis and sort problem in macos"). reorder_filters_exprs ranked security filters with -NAN, which breaks the strict weak ordering std::sort requires, so filter order differed between platforms. The fix swapped in -infinity and an original_pos tie-breaker (src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.h ObExprRankPair / ObExprRankPairCompare). In Rust f64 is not Ord, so NaN handling must be written out explicitly.
- Commit 71e2b595b (SEEK-545). The ObRawExprCopier cache left a CASE subtree shared between the index-lookup filter and other parents; later virtual-column expansion then pulled an unavailable column into the index filter, giving error 4016. This bug class comes from shared mutable expressions. A redesign with immutable or copy-on-write expressions would remove it; a literal Rc<RefCell> port would not.
- src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp:394-408. set_child() returns void and throws away a failed child_.push_back, but still calls child_op->set_parent(this), leaving a half-linked tree after an allocation failure. With Rust's ? or an aborting Vec push this cannot go unnoticed.
- src/sql/optimizer/ob_log_operator_factory.h:119. The bound check is `idx <= sizeof(dist_algo_str) / sizeof(ObString)` on a 25-entry array, which is off by one. It is latent today: the highest DistAlgo bit is 1<<23, so idx is at most 24. Rust indexing would panic instead of reading past the array.
- src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h:5018-5025. ~ObRawExprFactory calls destory() only when THIS_WORKER has no req flag, and the comment says this avoids ObSEArray leaks. Whether cleanup runs depends on the thread type, which Drop plus arenas would make unnecessary.
- src/sql/optimizer/ob_log_plan_factory.cpp:34-37. The destructor has '//destroy();' commented out, and ObLogPlanFactory::destroy calls ObLogPlan::destory() (ob_log_plan.cpp:135-146) instead of ~ObLogPlan. So the ObLogOperatorFactory member destructor, the one that runs the operator destructors, is not reached on that path. Whether this leaks depends on memory-context reclamation; see open questions.
- src/sql/rewrite/ob_transform_rule.cpp:377 and src/sql/rewrite/ob_transform_late_materialization.cpp:436. Both carry the comment 'jinmao TODO: defensive code, remove it later' on a formalize_stmt re-run.
- src/sql/optimizer/ob_join_order.cpp:8153 JoinPath::reuse(). Recycled join paths are reset field by field by hand before placement new; any field added later and left out of this list carries state across recycled paths.

**Open questions:**
- Do the 568 pinned EST.ROWS and EST.TIME tables pass unchanged on both x86-64 Linux and arm64 macOS builds today, given clang's floating-point contraction? This needs running mysqltest on both platforms.
- For the small test tables, where do the EST.ROWS values come from: storage-layer row estimation (ob_storage_estimator.cpp calls storage estimate_row_count), default statistics, or dynamic sampling (inner SQL)? The answer decides how strongly the optimizer port's pinned output depends on the storage port.
- Are ~ObLogPlan and the ObLogicalOperator destructors ever reached on the normal path, or is ObSEArray heap memory (ModulePageAllocator falls back to ob_malloc) reclaimed by an enclosing memory context? This needs a runtime memory trace.
- Is each shared child subtree's parent_ pointer fixed up after the final candidate plan is chosen and before code generation, or do later passes rely on get_parent(root, ...) searches? Tracing the plan finalisation passes in ob_log_plan.cpp would settle it.
- How much of the optimizer and rewrite code do the 272 CI cases actually reach? No coverage data exists in the tree, so it is unknown how many of the 34 rules and 30 operator types have pinned plan coverage.
- Is tools/obtest (886 more pinned plan tables in 'EST. ROWS|COST' and basic formats) still run anywhere, or is it dead OceanBase history?
- Could a differential oracle work: running the C++ and Rust optimizers on the same resolved stmt and comparing plan-item info or the EXPLAIN text? That needs a shared, serialisable stmt and expression representation, which does not exist today.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- Expression identity is pointer equality almost everywhere, not only in the 34 address-keyed hash maps. Array membership, dedup, intersection and subset checks all compare raw pointers. So an ID or copy-on-write redesign must preserve exactly which nodes are 'the same object', or plan shapes and the pinned EXPLAIN text will change. (evidence: src/sql/optimizer/ob_optimizer_util.h:265-287 (find_item compares items.at(i) == item; its doc says 'Based on pointer comparison to determine if two expressions are the same'); git grep counts 243 find_item( call lines and 339 lines of append_array_no_dup/remove_item/intersect/is_subset in optimizer plus rewrite; the ADJUST_SHARED_EXPR plan pass in ob_optimizer.h's TraverseOp enum; address-keyed maps in src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr_copier.cpp:112-234 and ob_raw_expr_replacer.cpp:335-338.)
- Part of the optimizer already lives outside the surveyed directories, in the src/query/api layer. sql/optimizer headers are now forwarders to it. This moves the size count and also offers a boundary a Rust port could reuse (or has to respect). (evidence: src/query/api/query/optimizer holds 10 headers and 3,195 lines: ob_optimizer_algo_defs.h (the DistAlgo enum used throughout join order and the operator factory), ob_optimizer_location_defs.h, stat/ob_stat_define.h (1,090 lines), ob_opt_column_stat.h, ob_opt_table_stat.h and others. 7 headers under src/sql/optimizer are 18-19-line '#pragma once / #include "query/optimizer/..."' forwarders. src/query/api/query/resolver/ob_raw_expr_traits.h is a similar layer for expressions.)
- The transformer is a fixed-point loop, not one pass in a fixed order. It repeats the 33-rule sequence up to max_iteration_count_ times and changes the enabled-rule set after rules fire, to avoid rules undoing each other. A port has to reproduce the iteration count, the convergence test and the disable logic exactly, because the final stmt shape decides the pinned plan. (evidence: src/sql/rewrite/ob_transformer_impl.cpp:213, 320-362 (loop over iteration_count, and 'transformer ends without convergence' at 362); 447-450 (the comment says cur_enable_types may be updated after each rule to avoid rewrite ping-pong between PREDICATE_MOVE_AROUND and CONST_PROPAGATE / SIMPLIFY_EXPR); commit dcf7be667 'Fix the non-convergence issue in the transformation' added 115 lines to ob_transformer_impl.*.)
- The expression classes that the optimizer downcasts into are neither shallow nor single-inheritance. This weakens the 'maps naturally onto Rust enums' framing for the part of the model that matters most. (evidence: src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h:4675 'class ObWinFunRawExpr : public ObRawExpr, public ObWindow' (multiple inheritance); depth 5 through ObRawExpr > ObNonTerminalRawExpr (3270) > ObOpRawExpr (3298) > ObSysFunRawExpr (3868) > ObUDFRawExpr / ObCollectionConstructRawExpr (4087, 3934); the optimizer and rewrite hold 493 static_casts to Ob*RawExpr subclasses (C4).)
- The pinned numbers are checked on only one platform, but the C++ build targets five toolchain and libm combinations. Differences in libm results, not only FMA, can move a value across a ceil() boundary. There is also evidence that macOS already diverges. (evidence: cmake/Env.cmake:143-144 (clang++-17 on Linux), 159 (Android NDK clang), 188 (Apple clang), 206-207 (clang-cl on Windows); .github/workflows/seekdb.yml runs mysqltest only on arc-runner-k8s-ci-org (Linux); commit 0dca2a5d3 changed geometry plan tests for macOS; about 27 lines of log/exp/pow/sqrt calls in the area plus 22 ceil( in the optimizer; EST.ROWS also comes from storage estimation (src/sql/optimizer/ob_storage_estimator.cpp:87-148), and several mysql_test includes force major or minor freezes (include/majorfreeze.inc, minor_merge_tenant.inc), which change what storage reports.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey can mostly be trusted. Every size count and nearly every code citation I opened reproduced exactly: the file and line totals, the group splits, the inheritance table, the 488 ObRawExpr*& parameters, SMART_CALL 617, ob_sort 34, the ceil() in get_plan_item_info, the placement-new factories and ObLogPlan::destory, the shared child subtrees in hash group-by, and the 820/658/568 EXPLAIN table counts. The errors are in secondary counts and in scope. The accept_transform count is wrong (10 calls in 6 files, not 13 in 8), and the survey missed the 4 other ObOptimizer sites in late materialization and temp table. 4 of the 34 'address-keyed' lines are really pointer values. ob_transform_utils.h is included by 18 files, not 20. Masking is in 12 of the 40 CI plan tests, not 18. The churn counts include the bulk-import commit. The hash-iteration list misses ob_key_part.cpp:289/305. The open questions quote 886 obtest tables where the survey's own claim gives 498. The '264 replace_expr calls' in hard_parts is really 264 lines mentioning replace_expr* names, which include declarations. None of these errors changes the conclusion. The bigger omissions push the same way the survey already points. Pointer-equality identity through find_item and the set helpers is much more widespread than the 34 map sites. The transformer is a fixed-point loop. The expression hierarchy has multiple inheritance and is 5 deep. And 3.2K lines of optimizer types already sit in src/query/api. So the survey's main verdict stands: the algorithms port cleanly, but the expression and stmt data model needs a redesign that crosses areas, and exact EXPLAIN output makes the cost arithmetic and tie orders part of the specification. I would treat its figures as accurate within a few percent and its list of hard parts as incomplete rather than wrong.


## AREA: sql-frontend
**What it is:** The SQL front end: a bison/flex MySQL grammar that builds an untyped C ParseNode tree, a hand-written fast parser that turns literals into parameters for the plan cache, the resolver that turns parse trees into ObStmt objects plus the ObRawExpr expression graph, the code generator that turns optimizer plans into engine operator specs, the plan/PS cache, the session object with its roughly 720 system-variable classes (generated), the SQL printers, and the ObSql driver (ob_sql.cpp, ob_spi.cpp, ...). Every statement, whether from a client or from internal inner SQL, passes through this code.

**Size split:** Scope: git-tracked .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.y/.l files only. Command: `git -C $R ls-files <dir> | grep -E '\.(h|hpp|cpp|cc|c|ipp|y|l)$' | xargs wc -l`. Loose files: `git ls-files src/sql | grep -E '^src/sql/[^/]+\.(...)$'`.
TOTAL: 548 files, 305,554 lines.
- parser: 37 files, 31,537 lines
- resolver: 336 files, 151,357 lines (ddl 40,489 / dml 46,346 / expr 35,318 / cmd 10,428 / dcl 5,262 / tcl 766 / prepare 582 / top-level 12,166)
- code_generator: 15 files, 20,519 lines
- plan_cache: 54 files, 19,890 lines
- session: 22 files, 33,062 lines
- printer: 14 files, 11,079 lines
- pl: 34 files, 10,478 lines (33 headers plus 1 .cpp; the implementations are in src/pl, and src/pl/ob_pl.h is a 20-line include of sql/pl/ob_pl.h)
- loose files directly under src/sql: 36 files, 27,632 lines
Split by kind:
- Generated, checked in: src/sql/session/ob_system_variable_factory.cpp (10,705) plus .h (5,235) = 15,940 lines. Written by src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py (1,200 lines) from ob_system_variable_init.json (10,979 lines, 776 entries). The same script writes 6 more files in src/share/system_variable (14,006 lines, outside this area).
- Grammar sources that are inputs to generators (hand-written): 20,911 lines. That is sql_parser_mysql_mode.y 18,723 + .l 1,853 + ftsparser.y 250 + ftsblex.l 85.
- Data: non_reserved_keywords_mysql_mode.c, 1,096 lines (a keyword table with 984 quoted strings). reserved.py (2,137 lines, not in the extension set) is a keyword reference list; `git grep reserved.py` finds nothing that uses it.
- Hand-written C/C++: 305,554 - 15,940 - 20,911 - 1,096 = 267,607 lines.
- Vendored third-party code: 0.
- Generated at build time, git-ignored, not counted above (wc on files the running build left in src/sql/parser): sql_parser_mysql_mode_tab.c 81,297 + _lex.c 75,028 + ftsparser_tab.c 1,916 + ftsblex_lex.c 2,344 + type_name.c 2,030 = 162,615 lines. The lex header adds 3.6 MB.
- Closely related code outside the area: src/query/api/query/parser/ob_item_type.h (2,477, the node/expression type enum) and parse_node.h (485, the ParseNode C struct); src/pl/parser, 4,642 lines (PL grammar .y 2,976, .l 698).

**Summary:** The front end is about 305.6K tracked lines. About 268K are hand-written C/C++, 20.9K are grammar sources and 15.9K are a generated system-variable factory. There are no vendored files. The build also generates another 162.6K lines of bison/flex C that is not tracked. The parser is an LALR(1) grammar for bison 2.4.1 (1,186 tokens, 3,605 rules, 6,550 states, zero conflicts enforced). Its 2,039 C action calls build an untyped C struct tree (ParseNode). Because that tree is already a C ABI type, keeping bison output behind FFI is the cheapest parser option. A Rust LR generator could reuse the grammar's rule structure, but every action body and the flex lexer (9 exclusive start states, fast-parse hooks, callbacks into the C++ PL symbol table) would need rewriting.

The hardest coupling is not the grammar itself. First, the grammar hand-maintains per-node constant counts (param_num_, is_hidden_const_) so they match a separate hand-written fast parser. Second, the fast parser's parameterized text becomes the plan-cache key, and its MD5 becomes the sql_id that looks up persisted outlines. A replacement parser therefore has to reproduce constant recognition exactly.

The resolver (151K) is about 93 stmt-resolver classes dispatched on ObItemType, and it reads positional children_[N] in 2,309 lines. Its output IR, ObRawExpr (29 subclasses, a DAG with cycles that is edited in place through ObRawExpr*&), is shared with the optimizer, rewrite and the code generator, and with 791 engine files whose cg_expr takes a const ObRawExpr&. Resolver type deduction in turn calls engine operators. So the resolver/expr unit cannot move alone.

The plan cache is a generic library cache: a hash map, then rwlocked nodes with an intrusive refcount, then intrusive lists of values and plan sets, then atomically refcounted objects. It also holds PL functions and packages. It has had real lifetime and concurrency bugs within the last year (e66776e2d, 8b956047c).

The session is a multiple-inheritance class of about 206 fields with an intrusive hash-map refcount. It is referenced by 594 files, 140 of them outside src/sql. Its system variables come from checked-in output of a hand-run Python generator (720 virtual classes); in Rust these would naturally be a generated table.

There are no C++ unit tests for this area. All coverage comes from the 272 mysqltest cases run in CI, and every statement in them goes through this code. Those tests survive a rewrite only if EXPLAIN, SHOW CREATE and error text stay byte-identical. Outline/sql_id behavior is not covered in CI.

**Structure call (surveyor):** Most of this area does not map one-to-one onto idiomatic Rust, and the parts that force a redesign also bind neighbouring areas.

The parser is the exception. It can stay as generated C behind FFI with no redesign, because ParseNode is already a C ABI struct. Rewriting it in Rust is a separate choice, and the fast-parser constant-count rule limits that choice.

The resolver could be translated class by class, keeping the ParseNode children_[N] access (2,309 lines), the 93-class resolver hierarchy and the 90 stmt classes. That produces non-idiomatic Rust (arena handles, downcasts, a context-pointer struct) unless the parse tree becomes a typed AST, and a typed AST means rewriting every positional access.

The real redesign point is the ObRawExpr IR. It is a pointer-identity graph with cycles and in-place mutation through ObRawExpr*&, with back-pointers to the factory and to engine ObExpr objects. It is shared with optimizer, rewrite, the code generator and 791 engine files (cg_expr), and it calls engine operators for type deduction. In Rust it needs an arena with index handles (ExprId) or unsafe pointers, and any choice here spreads beyond this area.

The plan cache (intrusive lists, manual atomic refcounts, rwlocked nodes, guard with hand-written swap) and the session (multiple inheritance, intrusive link-hash refcount, raw exec-ctx back-pointer) also need a redesign around Arc and a concurrent map. Their behavior stays the same.

Printer, code generator and sysvar code translate structurally. The sysvar code is better regenerated from the JSON as a data table than as 720 classes. The code generator must move together with the optimizer and engine types.

Files that force the call: src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h (plus the 791 src/sql/engine files whose cg_expr takes ObRawExpr), src/query/api/query/parser/parse_node.h, src/sql/resolver/ob_resolver_define.h (ObResolverParams), src/sql/plan_cache/ob_i_lib_cache_node.h, src/sql/plan_cache/ob_cache_object_factory.h, src/sql/session/ob_sql_session_info.h.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the main call, with two corrections.

The parts that hold:
- The parser can stay generated C behind FFI. ParseNode is an extern "C" struct (src/query/api/query/parser/parse_node.h:36,129).
- ObRawExpr is where the redesign is forced. It is an identity graph edited in place (740 ObRawExpr*& lines), the cycles through ref_stmt_/outer_expr_ are real (ob_raw_expr.h:2786,2892), and it is shared with the 791 cg_expr files in src/sql/engine/expr.
- The plan cache and the session need an Arc/concurrent-map redesign (ob_i_lib_cache_node.h:214-218, ob_i_lib_cache_object.cpp:98-112, ob_sql_session_info.h:183).

Correction 1: the "resolver can be translated class by class" option really only covers DDL, DCL and CMD. The DML stmt classes are the IR that rewrite and the optimizer mutate, just like ObRawExpr. ObDMLStmt and ObSelectStmt (src/sql/resolver/dml/ob_dml_stmt.h, 1,258 lines; ob_select_stmt.h, 596 lines) are referenced by 79 rewrite files and 56 optimizer files, 4,357 lines between them. So resolver/dml (46K) belongs in the same indivisible unit as resolver/expr (35K). The unit is about 81K lines, not 35K.

Correction 2: two points about keeping the parser behind FFI.
- It keeps a C++ support layer: parse_malloc.cpp, ob_parser_charset_utils.cpp and the lookup_pl_symbol callback in src/pl/ob_pl_stmt.cpp:3376.
- The consumer needs mutable access to the tree. ob_sql_parameterization.cpp:583 rewrites nodes in place, and there are 177 field assignments in src/sql/resolver.

Smaller points:
- The session redesign must keep, or replace, the OB_UNIS encoding that is used in-process today (see missed item 3). It is not only a lifetime problem.
- The "SIMD fast path" hard part is wrong. src/sql/parser/ob_parse_simd.cpp (218 lines) is built into the parser object library (src/sql/parser/CMakeLists.txt:39; SQL_PARSER_SOURCES in sql_source_inventory.bzl), not ob_sql_simd. It uses OB_DECLARE_AVX512_SPECIFIC_CODE per function, with runtime is_arch_supported dispatch and a scalar fallback. That is easy to express in Rust with #[target_feature] and is_x86_feature_detected!, so it is not a hard part.
- ObResolverParams has 20 raw pointer members (17 non-const, ob_resolver_define.h:318-458), not about 14.

**Size split check (verifier):** Every per-directory figure reproduces exactly with an independent command (cat | wc -l instead of wc per file): 548 files and 305,554 lines. The subtraction to 267,607 hand-written lines is correct. Generated: 15,940 (factory .h/.cpp) and 14,006 for the six share files. Grammar sources: 20,911. Build-time generated, untracked: 162,615 lines, plus a 3.66 MB lex header. Small corrections:
(1) The keyword data file non_reserved_keywords_mysql_mode.c has 991 keyword entries (998 quoted strings) in two tables, Mysql_none_reserved_keywords and Mysql_sql_keywords_in_pl, not 984.
(2) The extension filter leaves out src/sql/resolver/cmd/ob_sql_topic.def (2,262) and ob_sql_category.def (38). These are help-topic data files that nothing includes; they appear only in sql_header_inventory.bzl, so they are dead data and should not be ported.
(3) Parser generation exists in three copies, not two: gen_parser.sh, gen_parser_win.ps1 and tools/bazel_migration/generate_sql_parser.sh (153 lines, used by the BUILD.bazel genrule).
(4) There are 319 distinct basenames, not 320. Trivial.
The bucket split (about 88% hand-written C/C++, 7% grammar input, 5% generated sysvar code, 0 vendored) stands.

**Units of work:** The natural unit is a class pair: an .h/.cpp pair, or a stmt resolver plus its stmt class. By basename there are 320 units: 299 in the subdirectories plus 21 loose ones.

Grouped by what must move together:
- Parser: 1 unit. Grammar 20,911 lines plus parse-node, keyword and ObParser C/C++ code of about 9.5K. It is 0 units if kept as bison C behind FFI. The fast parser (ob_fast_parser.{h,cpp}, 3,198 lines) and ob_sql_parameterization.cpp must move with any parser change. The PL grammar in src/pl/parser (4,642 lines) re-enters the SQL parser and should be decided with it.
- Resolver: about 80 stmt resolver/stmt file groups (77 *_resolver.cpp files, 69 *_stmt.h files) in ddl (40.5K), dml (46.3K), cmd (10.4K), dcl (5.3K), tcl and prepare (1.3K), plus 12.2K of top-level utilities.
- resolver/expr: 32 files, 35.3K lines. This is one indivisible unit: the ObRawExpr IR, which must be planned together with optimizer, rewrite, the code generator and the engine expression library.
- code_generator: 1 unit, 15 files. It moves in step with the optimizer's ObLog* types and the engine's *Spec types.
- plan_cache: 2 units, plan cache/library cache and PS cache (54 files).
- session: 1 hand-written unit (about 17K lines without the generated factory), plus 1 generator unit that regenerates the sysvar table from ob_system_variable_init.json instead of translating 15,940 generated lines.
- printer: 1 unit, 14 files.
- Loose driver files: 21 pairs. The large ones are ob_sql.cpp (4,113), ob_spi.cpp (6,218, the PL bridge), ob_sql_utils.cpp (3,879), ob_result_set.cpp (1,517), ob_sql_trans_control.cpp (1,277), ob_query_retry_ctrl.cpp (1,053) and ob_optimizer_trace_impl.cpp (1,055).

**Public-surface tests:** Every mysqltest case exercises this area, because each statement goes through ObSql::stmt_query: fast parse, plan-cache lookup, parse, resolve, code generation.
- CI (.github/workflows/seekdb.yml, via .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py) runs the 272 cases listed in tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml.
- tools/deploy/mysql_test holds 283 .test and 305 .result files (933,203 expected-output lines).
- Cases touching specific front-end features (git grep -liE over mysql_test .test files): SET @@/GLOBAL/SESSION, 164 files (sysvars, session); /*+ hints, 56; EXPLAIN, 55 (these pin printer and plan text); SHOW CREATE, 36 (ObSchemaPrinter); plan_cache, 11; PREPARE, 8 (PS cache).
- Examples: t/non_reserved_keyword.test, t/explain.test, t/index_14_hint.test, t/identifier_name_length.test, t/empty_input.test, t/escape.test, t/special_stmt.test, t/view.test, t/view_2.test, test_suite/sys_vars/t/tx_isolation_basic.test, test_suite/system_variable/t/autocommit.test.
- No tests import internals: unittest/ contains only .gitignore files. All existing coverage therefore survives a rewrite, as long as client-visible text (EXPLAIN output, SHOW CREATE, error messages, warnings) stays byte-identical.
- Gaps: CREATE OUTLINE and sql_id stability appear only in 4 tools/obtest cases. The 500 obtest .test files are not referenced by any .github workflow, so outline matching through sql_id has no CI coverage.

**Hard parts:**
- The ObRawExpr graph (src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.h). Expressions are shared by pointer identity (ObLogicalOperator::extract_shared_exprs in src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp:1726-1850). They form cycles through ObQueryRefRawExpr::ref_stmt_ and ObExecParamRawExpr::outer_expr_, are replaced in place through ObRawExpr*& (740 lines in the area), are copied by ObRawExprCopier (145 references), and cache the engine pointer rt_expr_. This does not fit Rust ownership without an arena of index handles.
- Inheritance hierarchies. 29 ObRawExpr subclasses, including multiple inheritance in ObWinFunRawExpr (ob_raw_expr.h:4675). 93 resolver classes up to four levels deep (ObSelectResolver -> ObDMLResolver -> ObStmtResolver) and 90 stmt classes. 673 static_cast downcasts in the area. ObSQLSessionInfo multiply inherits three bases (ob_sql_session_info.h:183).
- The resolver context: ObResolverParams (src/sql/resolver/ob_resolver_define.h:318) holds about 14 raw mutable pointers (allocator, schema checker, session, plan cache, PL engine, expr/stmt factories, query ctx). Nested resolvers copy it, and ObDMLResolver keeps a parent_namespace_resolver_ back-pointer (ob_dml_resolver.h:852) for correlated subqueries. This is shared mutable aliasing that &mut cannot express directly.
- The bison/flex parser (src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.y, .l, sql_parser_base.h with 95 #defines). It exits through setjmp/longjmp on a thread-local jmp_buf (sql_parser_base.c:104). Actions call into C++ PL mid-parse (lookup_pl_symbol). The lexer has 9 exclusive start conditions and charset-dependent escape handling. Constant-count bookkeeping must match the hand-written fast parser, src/sql/parser/ob_fast_parser.cpp.
- The SIMD fast path: AVX-512 and AVX2 hex scanning in src/sql/parser/ob_parse_simd.cpp, compiled into ob_sql_simd with -mavx512vl -mavx512bw (src/sql/CMakeLists.txt:10-15).
- Plan cache concurrency (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp, ob_i_lib_cache_node.h, ob_i_lib_cache_object.cpp, ob_ps_cache.cpp). It combines hand-written ATOMIC_* refcounts, a CAS try_inc_ref_count, TCRWLock and SpinRWLock, intrusive ObDList chains, raw ObILibCacheKey* map keys, eviction by a timer task, and memory accounting under CAS. The same cache also stores PL objects (src/share/ob_lib_cache_namespace.def).
- Session lifetime (src/sql/session/ob_sql_session_info.h, ob_sql_session_mgr.h). The intrusive LinkHashValue refcount with manual get_session/revert_session, the raw cur_exec_ctx_ back-pointer that is never cleared (ob_des_exec_context.cpp:112-114), OB_UNIS serialization, and about 206 member lines. The type is used by 594 files.
- Segmented stack growth: SMART_CALL (src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_smart_call.h) switches to a heap-allocated stack through jump_call. It has 352 uses in the area, and HEAP_VAR/SMART_VAR has 97. Rust would need a stacker-like crate or an explicit work stack (assumption about the crate).
- Byte-exact text output. The printers (src/sql/printer/ob_raw_expr_printer.cpp 3,835 lines, ob_schema_printer.cpp 3,495 lines) produce stored view definitions (ob_create_view_resolver.cpp:585-620) and SHOW CREATE and EXPLAIN text that .result files pin. The fast parser's parameterized text drives sql_id and outline matching (ob_sql.cpp:3848-3880).
- Coupling across areas in the code generator (src/sql/code_generator/ob_static_engine_cg.{h,cpp}): 63 generate_spec overloads, the GenSpecHelper<TYPE> template, and the REGISTER_OPERATOR macro table in src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator_reg.h. Input types come from the optimizer and output types from the engine.
- DDL output types owned by other areas: stmt classes embed 57 obcall::Ob*Arg structs from src/share/ob_rpc_struct.h, which rootserver consumes.
- Inner SQL: internal metadata access runs SQL through ObSql (src/observer/ob_inner_sql_connection.cpp:57,197,245). 337 files across share, rootserver, observer and storage use ObMySQLProxy/ObISQLClient, so the front end can never be taken offline during a migration.

**Pain instances:**
- e66776e2d (2026-08-20), plan/PS cache coredumps. ObPlanCache::add_cache_obj read cache-object memory accounting after releasing the node lock, so concurrent eviction could drop the last reference first (use-after-free). The PS cache statistics callback dereferenced ps_item_ after the item could be destroyed (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp, ob_ps_cache_callback.h). Arc ownership or borrow checking would reject both.
- 8b956047c (2026-08-13, also ad4b27d9d and 1b9c722db). The CAS loops in ObPlanCache::dec_managed_used (ob_plan_cache.h:321-330) and ObPsCache::release_managed_memory (ob_ps_cache.cpp:196-203) loaded old_value once, outside the loop, so a failed CAS spun forever. Rust's AtomicI64::fetch_update, or compare_exchange returning Err(actual), makes this pattern hard to write. That makes it less likely, but does not rule it out completely.
- Defensive code: the ObRawExpr destructor detects double free with a magic number (0x13572468 on construction, 0x86427531 after destruction) and logs 'ObRawExpr maybe double free!' (src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr.cpp:190-196, ob_raw_expr.h:1797,2217).
- Hazard: get_param_expr returns ObRawExpr*& and, for an out-of-range index, returns a reference to the global mutable ObRawExpr *USELESS_POINTER (ob_raw_expr.cpp:42 and :831; ob_raw_expr.h:3393-3399, 3573). A caller that writes through it would corrupt a process-wide global, and callers compare addresses (&expr == &USELESS_POINTER, ob_raw_expr.cpp:3157). Option<&mut> removes this.
- Hazard: ObCacheObjGuard releases a cache-object reference in its destructor but does not delete its copy constructor. Its hand-written swap() carries the comment 'this function may be somewhat dangerous and may cause some memory leak... PLEASE CONCAT @Shengle' (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_cache_object_factory.h:71-127). Rust move semantics would make it safe by construction.
- 5c5cece34 (2026-04-22): the resolver's type-demotion pass dereferenced exec_ctx->get_sql_ctx() and get_physical_plan_ctx(), reached through the session's raw cur_exec_ctx_ back-pointer, and these can be null in MV scenarios. The fix added null checks (src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr_type_demotion.cpp:184-190). Option types would force the check.
- 5c2637685 (2026-07-08) '[seekdb][pl] Avoid static SQL buffer leak': SPI signatures changed from const char* to ObString (src/sql/ob_spi.h:466-652, ob_spi.cpp).
- Volume of defensive code in the area: 7,851 OB_ISNULL/OB_NOT_NULL lines and 5,082 OB_ERR_UNEXPECTED lines. 342 of these check context pointers that are always set, for example OB_ISNULL(session_info_) and OB_ISNULL(schema_checker_) in resolvers (git grep over the area).
- Build-pipeline defect, observed in this worktree's running build. On macOS, the sed post-processing in gen_parser.sh fails with BSD sed ('extra characters at the end of d command', 'bad flag in substitute command') and the script ignores the exit code. The generated lexer is therefore used unpatched: yylex_init still has 'return 1' at sql_parser_mysql_mode_lex.c:73578/73585 and the memcpy patch appears 0 times (src/sql/parser/_gen_parser.error). Moving parser generation into a Rust build step would remove this, but only if the parser leaves bison/flex.

**Open questions:**
- Whether grmtools/lrpar (or another Rust LR generator) accepts this grammar with only mechanical edits (bison-specific %define api.pure, %name-prefix, %destructor, %locations, %error-verbose, ival[2] union tricks). This needs the tools to be run, which was not allowed here.
- Whether any code copies ObCacheObjGuard, which has an implicit copy constructor and releases in its destructor, and so would double-decrement. Deleting the copy constructor and compiling would settle it.
