- How much optimizer and rewrite logic depends on ObRawExpr pointer identity (== on pointers, shared-expr producer/consumer tracking) as opposed to structural equality. This decides whether an ExprId arena keeps semantics for free.
- Whether any deployment relies on persisted outlines keyed by sql_id. If so, a new parser's parameterized text must be byte-identical to today's output, and no CI test checks that.
- Whether the unpatched macOS lexer (yylex_init returns 1 instead of errno; the sed patches fail silently) changes any runtime behavior on macOS or embedded builds.
- What an FFI crossing per statement would cost if the parser stays in C while the resolver moves to Rust, or the reverse. Parser and plan-cache hit-path latency were not measured.
- Whether the src/query/api facades (ObSessionAccess, ObIPlanCacheAccessService, ob_operator_reg.h) are meant to become the module seam. ObSessionAccess currently has 7 users against 140 direct ObSQLSessionInfo users outside src/sql.
- How much of ObBasicSessionInfo's OB_UNIS serialization is still exercised in single-node seekdb (ob_des_exec_context.cpp still deserializes sessions). If it is dead code, the session redesign gets smaller.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The DML stmt classes are a second shared IR, alongside ObRawExpr. ObDMLStmt, ObSelectStmt, TableItem and the related classes live in resolver/dml, and rewrite and the optimizer mutate them. So the DML resolver (46K lines) cannot be translated class by class independently. It must move with resolver/expr, rewrite and the optimizer. (evidence: git grep -lE 'ObDMLStmt|ObSelectStmt' -- src: sql/rewrite 79 files, sql/optimizer 56, sql/printer 11, code_generator 5, observer/virtual_table 4. There are 4,357 referencing lines in rewrite+optimizer. src/sql/resolver/dml/ob_dml_stmt.h is 1,258 lines and ob_select_stmt.h is 596.)
- The repo already has a Rust crate directly in front of this area, and a working C++-to-Rust FFI call in the statement path. The survey never mentions it. It sets the toolchain, the cbindgen header convention and CI gates that any front-end plan inherits. It is also the obvious boundary to extend: the MySQL protocol already lives in Rust, and ObSql is called from observer/mysql. (evidence: rust/Cargo.toml, rust/sql-nio (22 .rs files, 8,420 lines: reactor, request/response, stmt_execute.rs, row_encode.rs), rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h (cbindgen), .github/workflows/rust-checks.yml (clippy -D warnings, header-drift check, RustSec audit). src/observer/mysql/obmp_stmt_execute.cpp:1029 calls the Rust nio_parse_mysql_execute_params (stmt_execute.rs:437).)
- Session serialization is live code in single-node seekdb, which answers the survey's open question. ObExecContext serializes *my_session_ with OB_UNIS, and local parallel execution (PX) decodes that buffer in the same process. A Rust session must reproduce the OB_UNIS encoding of ObBasicSessionInfo and ObSQLSessionInfo, or PX must be changed at the same time. (evidence: src/sql/engine/ob_exec_context.cpp:1091-1129 (DEFINE_SERIALIZE with OB_UNIS_ENCODE(*my_session_), and OB_UNIS_ADD_LEN). src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_local_sqc_launcher.h:50-60 ('in-process entry: decode the serialized args buffer', holding an ObDesExecContext). ob_px_sqc_handler.cpp:180-238 (do_deserialize). ob_des_exec_context.cpp:74-114 creates the shadow session.)
- When a new parser disagrees with the fast parser about constants, nothing fails. ObSql re-parses the statement and skips the plan cache, so mysqltest results stay green while every affected statement loses plan caching. The rewrite needs a check that plan-cache hit rates and constant counts stay the same; byte-identical output alone will not catch this. (evidence: src/sql/ob_sql.cpp:3327-3350 (parameterize_syntax_tree fails, need_retry_param, then reset_datum_param_store, is_enable_transform_tree=false and a new parser_and_check, with the comment 'mark this query not to go into plan cache'). ob_sql_parameterization.cpp:189-197 returns OB_NOT_SUPPORTED only at TRACE log level. Only 11 mysqltest .test files mention plan_cache.)
- The resolver depends heavily on the schema layer in src/share/schema, which the survey does not measure. A Rust resolver needs Rust-side access to ObTableSchema, ObColumnSchemaV2 and ObSchemaGetterGuard, or an FFI facade over them, before any stmt resolver can move. (evidence: git grep over src/sql/resolver: 2,419 lines in 93 of 336 files reference ObTableSchema|ObColumnSchemaV2|ObSchemaGetterGuard|ObSchemaChecker|schema_checker_. 43 resolver files include share/schema headers. src/sql/resolver/ob_schema_checker.{h,cpp} is 1,428 lines. ObResolverParams holds ObSchemaChecker *schema_checker_ (ob_resolver_define.h).)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey is largely reliable. Every size figure and most counts reproduce exactly with independent commands: 22 of 25 claims confirmed or needing only small corrections. The cited file:line locations also check out, and the build-pipeline defect on macOS is real (_gen_parser.error shows the BSD sed failures, and there are 0 memcpy patches in the generated lexer). The errors fall into three groups. (1) Some counts came from loose regexes: 891 grammar T_ tags is really 778, 2,309 resolver children_[ lines is really 2,672, and 206 session members does not reproduce. (2) A few behavior claims are one-sided. The session's exec-ctx pointer is normally a restored stack via LinkExecCtxGuard, not never-cleared. op_ lives in ObNonTerminalRawExpr. A fast/normal parse mismatch falls back silently instead of failing. (3) One hard part, the AVX-512 build flags, is simply wrong. The bigger risk for the migration decision is what the survey left out, not what it got wrong. It treats ObRawExpr as the only shared IR and misses that the DML stmt classes are equally shared with rewrite and the optimizer. That understates the smallest unit that can move by about 46K lines. It also does not mention the existing rust/sql-nio crate and its FFI precedent, it misses that session serialization is live through in-process PX, and it does not measure the resolver's schema coupling. Use its numbers as given, after the corrections above. Its structure call is broadly right, but plan with a larger minimum unit than it proposes.


## AREA: storage-core
**What it is:** The single-node LSM storage engine behind seekdb's SQL layer. It holds the MVCC memtable (a B-tree index over intrusive version chains), sstables stored as 2MB macro blocks made of micro blocks (flat or column-encoded) with an index-block tree, per-tablet metadata that is copy-on-write and ref-counted and persisted through slog and checkpoints, compaction and merge, DDL and fork data paths, LOB, temp files and the dag scheduler. It sits on oblib and share for allocators, IO and the KV cache, and on the query layer's pushdown-filter and expression types.

**Size split:** Command: git -C <wt> ls-files src/storage | grep -Ev '^src/storage/(fts|tx|tablelock|multi_data_source)/' | grep -E '\.(h|hpp|cpp|cc|c|ipp|y|l|inc)$' | xargs wc -l.
TOTAL: 1,206 files (631 .h, 569 .cpp, 6 .ipp), 405,768 lines.
- Hand-written: all 405,768 lines. The same grep outside code files finds only 9 build/doc files (BUILD.bazel, CMakeLists.txt, 6 .bzl, memtable/README).
- Generated: 0. Grepping for 'do not edit|auto-generated|generated by|this file is generated' matched only prose comments in 3 files.
- Data: 0. An awk scan found no file with more than 100 lines of numeric literals. The 276K-line IK dictionary sits in fts, which is out of scope.
- Vendored third-party: 0. 1,205 of 1,206 files carry 'Copyright (c) 2025/2026 OceanBase'. The one without a header (tablet/ob_i_tablet_mds_interface.ipp) is OceanBase code. No MIT/BSD/Google/Facebook headers turned up.
- Dead but tracked (hand-written, not compiled): 4,028 lines in 7 files. They are access/ob_advance_scanner.{h,cpp}, meta_mem/ob_tablet_meta_mem_mgr.{h,cpp}, meta_mem/ob_meta_obj_pool.{h,cpp} and src/storage/ob_micro_block_handle_mgr.h. The exclusion list at src/storage/BUILD.bazel:45-53 marks them, and the storage-root header has zero includers.
Per directory (lines): blocksstable 87,627 (encoding 25,062, index_block 21,197, rest 41,368); tablet 34,645; ddl 34,344; compaction 32,965; access 32,058; root files 27,420; memtable 27,023; tmp_file 20,893; ls 15,425; lob 14,210; meta_mem 11,731; retrieval 10,674; tx_table 9,956; tx_storage 8,243; scheduler 6,446; slog_ckpt 5,822; slog 4,416; deadlock 4,380; truncate_info 3,531; checkpoint 2,123; allocator 2,098; vector_index 1,951; throttle 1,811; vector_type 1,768; meta_store 1,697; blockstore 1,527; concurrency_control 801; api 135; testing 48.
Borderline parts another researcher may also count: the tx-adjacent dirs (tx_table + deadlock + concurrency_control = 15,137, plus tx_storage 8,243) and search/vector (retrieval + vector_type + vector_index = 14,393).

**Summary:** Storage-core is 405,768 lines in 1,206 files, all hand-written. There is no generated, data or vendored code; about 4K lines are dead twins left out of the build. The on-disk format is not one codec but at least four styles mixed together:
- raw memory images of packed C++ structs with bitfields and anonymous unions (the micro block header even unions a pointer with an int64 and writes nullptr into that slot);
- big-endian fixed-width encode_iN;
- OB unis varint envelopes (61 OB_UNIS_VERSION, 46 OB_SERIALIZE_MEMBER, 155 LST_DO_CODE);
- per-field MEMCPY.
Cell values are written as raw ObDatum payload bytes, and headers MEMCPY oblib types such as ObObjMeta and a 1-byte ObObjType. Byte compatibility therefore reaches outside storage into oblib's value representations.
Byte compatibility with old data directories does not appear to be a constraint the project keeps. After release v1.1.0, commits renumbered the persisted TableType enum (1a6192cf6, MDS_MINI_SSTABLE 26->17) and the persisted ObCompressorType (4238c88e9, ZSTD_1_3_8 6->2), with no mapping code found. 3e877d774 also deleted the tablet V1-V3 deserializers.
The memory model is allocator-first C++. There is no plain new: 320 placement-news, 432 explicit destructor calls, 1,308 ObIAllocator parameters and 750 reinterpret_casts. Ref-counted handles (ObMetaObjGuard, ObTabletHandle, ObTableHandleV2) are hand-rolled and carry runtime leak detectors.
The hardest structures are these. ObTablet deserializes into its own fixed-size pool buffer and places sub-objects after itself, with a header in front found by pointer arithmetic. Cold tablets are 'washed' under pool pressure, chosen by std::type_info. The memtable index is a B-tree with a hand-built reader/writer lock and QClock/retire-station deferred reclamation over intrusive version chains. Index blocks swizzle raw pointers into the KV cache.
The read path and encoders are tied to the SQL expression engine: 1,069 sql:: lines in 98 files, mostly pushdown filter executors, ObBitVector, ObEvalCtx and ObExpr in blocksstable and access. Outside callers come only from observer (115 files), rootserver (41) and standby (25); sql has 1 and share/query/logservice have 0.
SIMD is small: 57 intrinsic lines in 9 files (AVX-512/NEON raw and dict decoder filters, vector add/div) built on template-generated N-dimensional function-pointer tables.
Test cover for a rewrite is thin. No C++ unit tests are tracked. CI runs 283 mysqltests; only 11 force a freeze (the sstable write, merge and compaction paths), none restart the server (so persisted-format read-back and slog/clog replay go untested), and the 500 obtest tests are cluster-era scripts that CI does not run.
The area is shrinking fast: 203 commits since the initial import, +193,747/-407,415 lines, including removal of column store, backup and direct load (158,874 lines deleted in three commits).

**Structure call (surveyor):** Split. The upper half translates one-to-one into ordinary Rust once shared types exist: compaction policy and merge tasks, ddl/lob/tmp_file control flow, ls services, the ObIDag/ObITask families, and the int-ret/OB_FAIL error style, which maps directly onto Result and `?`. It is plain C-with-classes code with shallow inheritance (591 of 2,019 classes derive, 12 use multiple inheritance). The memory and format core does not translate one-to-one into safe, idiomatic Rust; it forces a redesign:
- tablets deserialized into pooled buffers with pointers into themselves, plus type_info-driven wash;
- hand-rolled refcount guards and handles (1,122 handle-type lines in 186 files);
- a memtable B-tree with a union-packed spin lock and QClock reclamation over intrusive version chains;
- on-disk headers that are packed memory images, one with a pointer/int union;
- raw pointers swizzled into the KV cache;
- decoders whose signatures take sql:: pushdown executor types.
A literal port of this core would be mostly unsafe Rust and would keep every lifetime rule implicit. The idiomatic version needs index/offset-based tablet layout, Arc or epoch reclamation (crossbeam-epoch style), explicit little-endian codecs with bit positions spelled out, and a storage-owned filter trait in place of sql types. Byte compatibility need not constrain this design: the project itself broke compatibility with v1.1.0 twice (C6).
Files that force the call: src/storage/tablet/ob_tablet.cpp, src/storage/meta_mem/ob_storage_meta_mem_mgr.cpp, src/storage/meta_mem/ob_storage_meta_obj_pool.h, src/storage/meta_mem/ob_meta_obj_struct.h, src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_keybtree.cpp, src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_keybtree_deps.h, src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_mvcc_row.h, src/storage/blocksstable/ob_micro_block_header.cpp, src/storage/blocksstable/ob_kvcache_pointer_swizzle.h, src/storage/blocksstable/encoding/ob_dict_decoder.cpp.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with 'Split', and the redesign-core file list is right: ob_tablet.cpp:1670-1815 (self-referencing pooled buffer), ob_storage_meta_mem_mgr.cpp:2137-2220 (wash chosen by type_info), ob_meta_obj_struct.h (hand-rolled guards), the keybtree files and ob_micro_block_header.cpp all show code that safe Rust cannot express one-to-one. Four caveats:
(1) The 'upper half translates one-to-one' side is overstated. The compaction, ddl, lob and ls code holds and passes the same ref-counted handles (ObTabletHandle/ObTableHandleV2/ObLSHandle on about 960 lines in 175 files), so it cannot be ported before the handle design is settled. It also reaches global state through server_service<T>() on 551 lines in 149 files.
(2) The error style does not map purely onto Result and `?`. There are 638 `ret = OB_SUCCESS;` resets that swallow errors, 586 OB_ITER_END comparisons (end of iteration signalled as an error code), and 1,162 tmp_ret/OB_TMP_FAIL lines (continue after an error). Each of these needs a per-site decision.
(3) The redesign core does not sit inside this area. 70 storage-core files include storage/tx and 30 include multi_data_source (27 of them in tablet, 19 in memtable), so groups 2 and 3 cannot move without the excluded tx/MDS code (354K lines together with tablelock and fts).
(4) 'Byte compatibility need not constrain the design' is supported by 1a6192cf6 and 4238c88e9 (which also deleted the LZ4 codec that v1.1.0 used by default), but not by 3e877d774. And the argument covers data files only: storage-core also defines clog (redo log) payloads, some as memory images, which matter for any staged mixed C++/Rust run.

**Size split check (verifier):** Reproduced exactly with my own list (git ls-files, the same exclusions, summed with cat|wc -l): 1,206 files (631 .h, 569 .cpp, 6 .ipp) and 405,768 lines. The 9 non-code files are BUILD.bazel, CMakeLists.txt, memtable/README and 6 .bzl. Every per-directory figure matches, including blocksstable 87,627 = encoding 25,062 + index_block 21,197 + rest 41,368. Dead code is 4,028 lines in 7 files, confirmed by wc -l, the BUILD.bazel:45-53 exclusions and zero includers; the dead meta_obj_pool.cpp duplicates function definitions in the live ob_storage_meta_obj_pool.cpp. Generated, data and vendored are all 0, confirmed. The 12 unit-of-work groups sum to 405,585, plus api 135 and testing 48 = 405,768. One arithmetic error: open_questions gives tx_table + tx_storage + deadlock + concurrency_control as 24,380 lines. The correct sum is 9,956 + 8,243 + 4,380 + 801 = 23,380, which matches the size_split field's own 15,137 + 8,243.

**Units of work:** File level: 649 .h/.cpp basenames (553 pairs, 94 header-only, 2 triples) in 37 leaf directories. Because of the coupling, the units that can realistically be translated and tested on their own are 12 directory groups (lines in brackets):
1. On-disk block formats and readers/writers: blocksstable including encoding and index_block, 235 files [87,627].
2. Tablet metadata and persistence: tablet + meta_mem + meta_store + slog + slog_ckpt + blockstore + truncate_info [63,369].
3. Memtable/MVCC with its allocators: memtable + allocator + throttle [30,932].
4. Read path: access [32,058], blocked on the query-layer pushdown types.
5. Compaction plus dag scheduler: compaction + scheduler [39,411].
6. DDL data paths: ddl [34,344].
7. LOB: lob [14,210].
8. Temp files: tmp_file [20,893].
9. LS container and services: ls + tx_storage + checkpoint [25,791].
10. Tx-adjacent: tx_table + deadlock + concurrency_control [15,137].
11. Search/vector: retrieval + vector_type + vector_index [14,393].
12. Root files: ob_i_table, ob_storage_schema, ob_storage_struct, DML context and similar [27,420].
Groups 1-3 are the redesign core and must come first; groups 4-9 depend on them. Dead files (4,028 lines) drop out.

**Public-surface tests:** CI runs 283 mysqltest .test files from tools/deploy/mysql_test: 93 in t/ and 190 in test_suite/*/t, selected by .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py:248-272. Every DML and SELECT test goes through storage-core (ObAccessService -> ObLSTabletService -> memtable or access), but most tests keep their data in the memtable.

Tests that reach deeper paths:
- 11 run 'alter system major/minor freeze' (13 statements of each), which drive the sstable writer, compaction and the merge read path. Examples: t/bulk_insert.test, test_suite/fork_table/t/fork_table_merge.test, fork_table_sstable.test, fork_table_cow.test, test_suite/ddl/t/dump_ddl_mem.test, test_suite/histogram/t/stats_farm.test, test_suite/geometry/t/geometry_index2_mysql.test.
- The fork_table suite (18 tests) exercises ddl/ob_tablet_fork_task.cpp.
- test_suite/storage has 3 tests (rowkey_is_char, rowkey_is_int, rowkey_is_null).
- 26 tests use longtext, longblob, mediumtext or json (LOB paths); 59 create indexes (DDL build path); 13 truncate.

Gaps:
- 0 tests restart or shut down the server, so reading persisted files back (super block, slog replay, tablet deserialize, sstable decode after restart) is not covered by any public-surface test.
- Only 1 storage-core fault-injection point is used by a public test (BEFORE_TABLET_FULL_DIRECT_LOAD_MGR_CLOSE in ddl/ob_ddl_merge_task_v2.cpp, used by dump_ddl_mem.test). Storage-core has 407 lines of DEBUG_SYNC/ERRSIM/EN_ points that only internal tooling would reach.
- No C++ unit tests that import internals are tracked (unittest/ is empty), so no internal tests would break or need porting.
- tools/obtest holds 500 more .test files (storage 63, minor_freeze 26, errsim_storage 15, ...). They are cluster-era OBI scripts outside CI and are not usable as a public surface without rework.

All the public-surface tests above talk to the server over SQL, so they survive a rewrite unchanged. They just cover the storage format and recovery paths poorly.

**Hard parts:**
- Tablet objects hold pointers into themselves. ObTablet::deserialize deep-copies read info, table store, the ddl_kvs array and macro info into the bytes after sizeof(ObTablet) inside a fixed pool buffer (3,824 B or 65,480 B), with a bitfield header before the object found by pointer arithmetic. Paths: src/storage/tablet/ob_tablet.cpp:1663-1815; src/storage/meta_mem/ob_storage_meta_obj_pool.h:41-110.
- Tablet wash under memory pressure. The pool asks the manager for a free buffer by std::type_info; the manager evicts a cold tablet whose refcount is 0 and hands the raw buffer over, while ObTabletPointer keeps the disk address so the tablet can be loaded again. Path: src/storage/meta_mem/ob_storage_meta_mem_mgr.cpp:2137-2200.
- Hand-rolled ref-counted guards and handles: ObMetaObjGuard<T> copy/assign with inc_ref/dec_ref, freed either to an object pool or through allocator plus explicit destructor, with runtime leak and threading checks. ObTabletHandle, ObTableHandleV2 and ObLSHandle appear on 1,122 lines in 186 files. Path: src/storage/meta_mem/ob_meta_obj_struct.h:160-385.
- The memtable B-tree ObKeyBtree: templates over key/value, a union-packed spin RWLock, retire lists and QClock deferred reclamation from oblib, and a .cpp included textually into its header. Paths: src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_keybtree.cpp, ob_keybtree.h:328, ob_keybtree_deps.h:61-105 and 407-432.
- Intrusive MVCC structures: ObMvccTransNode has raw prev_/next_ pointers, a zero-length trailing buffer and CAS-set flag bits. Nodes come from the memstore arena (ObFifoArena: bump pages with atomic ref and pos) and are linked into both the row chain and the transaction callback list. Paths: src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_mvcc_row.h:63-95, src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_tx_callback_list.cpp, src/storage/allocator/ob_fifo_arena.h:43-190.
- On-disk headers that are packed memory images with bitfields and unions:
- ObMicroBlockHeader: a 64-byte image whose pointer shares a union with an int64;
- ObMacroBlockCommonHeader, ObSSTableMacroBlockHeader::FixedHeader and 13 encoder meta headers.
A byte-exact Rust codec must reproduce GCC/Clang bitfield ordering explicitly. Paths: src/storage/blocksstable/ob_micro_block_header.h, ob_sstable_macro_block_header.cpp:162-230, encoding/*_encoder.h.
- Encoding styles differ within the same file set: little-endian memory images, big-endian encode_iN (oblib serialization.h:212), unis varint with version/length envelopes, and SERIALIZE_MEMBER_WITH_MEMCPY. The stored cell bytes are ObDatum payloads (ob_row_writer.cpp:666-688), so a Rust engine must also reproduce oblib's ObNumber, decimal and datetime byte layouts.
- KV-cache pointer swizzling: the index block row scanner keeps raw pointers into cache memory blocks, checked by version and hazard version. Paths: src/storage/blocksstable/ob_kvcache_pointer_swizzle.{h,cpp}; src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.h.
- Encoders and decoders are dispatched through template-generated N-dimensional function-pointer tables filled at static-init time. SIMD kernels are template specializations behind #if __AVX512BW__ and NEON variants, and they take sql::PushdownFilterInfo and sql::ObBitVector. Paths: src/storage/blocksstable/encoding/ob_dict_decoder.cpp:128-182, ob_encoding_query_util.h:225-260, ob_raw_decoder_simd.cpp, neon/.
- The read path is tied to the SQL expression engine: access and blocksstable take sql::ObPushdownFilterExecutor, ObEvalCtx, ObExpr and ObBitVector (1,069 sql:: lines). The read path cannot be moved without either keeping those C++ types across FFI or moving the pushdown and expression layer too. Paths: src/storage/access/ob_pushdown_aggregate.cpp, src/storage/blocksstable/encoding/*_decoder.cpp.
- Borrowed row views flow through deep iterator chains. ObDatum and ObStorageDatum point into micro-block buffers pinned by cache handles, through ObMultipleMerge -> sstable row scanner -> micro block row scanner -> decoder. The pin/view lifetime is an unwritten rule that Rust would have to state as lifetimes or turn into copies. Paths: src/storage/access/ob_multiple_merge.cpp, src/storage/blocksstable/ob_micro_block_row_scanner.cpp.
- Two-phase init and error-code style: is_inited_ on 2,841 lines in 551 files, and OB_FAIL on 16,267 lines. Mechanically this maps to constructors and Result, but the sheer volume makes it the bulk of the translation effort (all directories).

**Pain instances:**
- 9e2b6ba16 (2026-01-22), src/storage/ddl/ob_tablet_fork_task.cpp. A stack-local ObTableStoreIterator pinned the sstables, but the raw sstable pointers collected from it (src_sstables) were used after it went out of scope. The fix moved the iterator into the long-lived context. A borrow checker rejects a reference that outlives the value it borrows from.
- 62b9d1a81 (2025-11-04), src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_independent_dag.cpp: 'fix mem leak due to parallel using mem'. A single ObArenaAllocator (not thread-safe) was shared by parallel merge tasks. The fix swapped in a FIFO allocator and added manual tracking with destructor-then-free for each merge helper. Rust's Send/Sync would have refused the sharing, and Drop would handle the cleanup.
- 9bb15300f (2025-12-04), src/storage/ddl/ob_hnsw_embedmgr.cpp: '[CP] core fix'. The slot ring called ~ObEmbeddingTask() and ob_free while the embedding handler thread could still hold the task. The fix added manual retain_if_managed/release_if_managed refcounting, which is what an Arc gives by construction.
- 37b91155c (2025-11-10), src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_tablet_context.{h,cpp} and ob_ddl_merge_task_v2.cpp: 'fix bug, fix core'. The merge helper's owner was unclear: it was created per task parameter inside a dag arena. The fix moved ownership into MergeCtx with an explicit destructor call and an is_inited_ guard.
- b7abb6324 (2026-04-21), src/storage/memtable/ob_nop_bitmap.h: 'fix hung and core when drop column'. `~0LU`, `1LU << pos` and __builtin_ctzl were 32-bit under Windows LLP64 and corrupted the upper 32 bits of a 64-bit bitmap. Rust's fixed-width u64 and trailing_zeros remove that whole class of bug.
- b8eafbae8 (2026-01-29) and 3dc21147b (2026-01-26), src/storage/access/ob_multiple_merge.cpp. A hash map that was not yet created (extra_access_ctx_) was queried, returning OB_NOT_INIT, and the refresh code built it in the wrong order. This is the two-phase-init state problem that an Option<HashMap> would force the code to handle.
- Defensive code that exists only because ownership is unchecked:
- ObMetaObjGuard warns on handles held for over 2 hours, logs 'obj ref cnt may be leaked' on negative counts, logs 'may be accessed by multiple threads' and calls ob_abort() (src/storage/meta_mem/ob_meta_obj_struct.h:184, 325, 355-385);
- 71 'leak' lines in 23 files;
- 83 ob_abort() calls in 31 files;
- 1,838 OB_NOT_INIT checks.

**Open questions:**
- Must a Rust engine read existing seekdb data directories at all? Two persisted enums were renumbered after v1.1.0 (1a6192cf6, 4238c88e9) with no mapping code, and no upgrade or format-compatibility policy was found in docs/. The project owners have to answer this; it decides whether the four encoding styles must be reproduced byte for byte.
- Are data files byte-identical between Linux (clang, Itanium ABI) and Windows (clang-cl, MSVC ABI) builds, given the packed bitfield headers? This needs a cross-platform dump comparison and cannot be settled read-only.
- What does it cost to keep the storage/SQL seam as FFI? data_plane/api (16,157 lines, another area) exports C++ value types such as ObDatumRow, ObStorageDatum and ObTableScanParam carrying sql::ObExpr pointers, not just abstract interfaces. The size of the actual call surface was not measured.
- How often does tablet wash or GC actually run in embedded mode, and how much of the pooled-buffer design is needed for single-node sizes? This needs a running instance with metrics.
- Is there any performance baseline for the AVX-512/NEON decoder filters and the keybtree? No storage benchmarks are tracked, so a rewrite has no reference numbers to match.
- Can any of the 500 cluster-era obtest scripts (storage, minor_freeze, errsim_storage) be adapted to single-node seekdb to cover restart/replay and compaction? This was not tried.
- Whether tx_table, tx_storage, deadlock and concurrency_control (24,380 lines together, tx-adjacent) and retrieval/vector (14,393) should count here or with the tx and fts researchers. This report counts them in storage-core per the literal area definition.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- CI does not run all 283 mysqltests. It runs only the 272 cases listed in a config file, and the dropped ones include 3 of the 11 freeze tests and the only test that uses a storage-core fault-injection point. Public-surface coverage of the sstable write/merge path is thinner than the survey reports. (evidence: mysqltest_for_seekdb.py:245-305 (discover_cases -> load_configured_case_names on tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml, returns only the configured names); the config has 272 active entries and 4 commented out, including '# - fork_table.fork_table_sstable'. Freeze tests that CI does not run: test_suite/ddl/t/dump_ddl_mem.test (the only user of BEFORE_TABLET_FULL_DIRECT_LOAD_MGR_CLOSE), fork_table/t/fork_table_sstable.test, geometry/t/geometry_index2_mysql.test.)
- Storage-core relies on process-wide mutable singletons found through a typed service registry and global macros. A Rust port, and especially a piece-by-piece port across FFI, has to replace this with explicit context passing or global statics that both languages can reach. (evidence: server_service<T>() on 551 lines in 149 files (ObLSService 108, ObStorageMetaMemMgr 60, ObTransService 25, ObLobManager 21, ObDagScheduler 20, ...), defined in src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h:126-145 and included by 178 storage-core files; OB_STORAGE_OBJECT_MGR on 97 lines, OB_SERVER_BLOCK_MGR 35, SERVER_STORAGE_META_SERVICE 13, plus ::get_instance() singletons (ObStorageCacheSuite 16, ObTmpPageCache 12, ObSLogWriteRunner 9).)
- The tablet and memtable 'redesign core' groups are tied to the excluded tx and multi_data_source areas, so their order and scope cannot be planned inside storage-core alone. (evidence: 70 storage-core files include storage/tx/ headers and 30 include storage/multi_data_source/ (tablet 27 files, memtable 19, tx_table 12, ls 9); 17 include tablelock. The excluded tx+mds+tablelock+fts code is 354,481 lines. tablet/ob_i_tablet_mds_interface.ipp is compiled into ObTablet.)
- Storage-core also defines write-ahead (clog) payload formats, not just data-file formats, and some are raw memory images. Dropping data-directory compatibility does not remove the need to match these if C++ and Rust components must replay each other's logs during a staged migration. (evidence: memtable/ob_memtable_mutator.cpp:102-137 (ObMemtableMutatorMeta::serialize/deserialize do MEMCPY of `this`), :229-262 (ObMutatorRowHeader via encode_i32/encode_i8), :343-362 (mutator row: rowkey, dml_flag, new_row); also ddl/ob_ddl_clog.cpp, ob_sync_tablet_seq_clog.cpp, ls/ob_ls_storage_clog_handler.cpp and tablet/ob_tablet_service_clog_replay_executor.cpp.)
- The OB_FAIL error style is not a clean fit for Result and `?`. A large share of call sites reset, swallow or reinterpret error codes, so the error translation needs per-site decisions, not a mechanical rewrite. (evidence: Over the 1,206 files: 638 lines of `^\s*ret = OB_SUCCESS;` (errors cleared), 586 OB_ITER_END comparisons (end of iteration as an error code), 1,162 tmp_ret/OB_TMP_FAIL lines (best-effort paths that keep going), and 393 OB_ENTRY_NOT_EXIST/OB_HASH_NOT_EXIST checks used as normal control flow.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's counts and code citations are reliable. The size split reproduces to the line, every file:line I opened matched the claim, and all the cited commits say what the survey says they say. Its errors are in a few places. First, some counts depend on the regex (lock guards and bucket locks are undercounted about 2x, and multiple inheritance about 2x). Second, a few conclusions go further than the evidence. 3e877d774 does not break v1.1.0 compatibility, although the LZ4 removal makes the compatibility break stronger than stated. 'Only three outside callers' ignores the data_plane/api seam that SQL actually uses. And CI runs 272 configured tests, not all 283, which drops 3 freeze tests and the only storage fault-injection test. There is also one arithmetic slip (23,380, not 24,380). The main technical claims survive checking: the packed-image on-disk headers, the tablets that hold pointers into their own buffers, the hand-rolled guards, the keybtree reclamation (two mechanisms, not one), and the SQL-type coupling in the read path. The 'Split' structure call is sound, but it understates how far the handles, the global service registry and the tx/MDS dependencies reach into the 'easy' half. Use its numbers as given; treat its compatibility and test-coverage conclusions as needing the corrections above.


## AREA: tx-log
**What it is:** This area is seekdb's single-node transaction engine and its write-ahead log. It covers transaction contexts and one-phase local commit (tx), table and object locks (tablelock), the multi-data-source framework that makes non-row metadata commit together with a transaction (MDS), palf-lite, which is the OceanBase Paxos log with replication removed, plus apply and replay services, a physical standby that ships clog over gRPC (standby), and a header-only API layer between SQL and storage (data_plane).

**Size split:** Command: git -C <wt> ls-files -- <dir> | grep -E '\.(h|hpp|cpp|cc|c|ipp|y|l|inc)$' | xargs cat | wc -l, with per-extension splits from git ls-files '<dir>/*.<ext>'.
- src/storage/tx: 104 files, 37,512 lines (.h 13,117 / .cpp 24,395)
- src/storage/tablelock: 35 files, 16,157 lines (.h 4,452 / .cpp 11,590 / .ipp 115)
- src/storage/multi_data_source: 65 files, 14,243 lines (.h 5,404 / .cpp 3,705 / .ipp 5,134)
- src/logservice: 124 files, 32,705 lines (.h 10,326 / .cpp 22,379). palf is about 23,057 lines; apply, replay and glue code are 9,648.
- src/standby: 51 files, 14,899 lines (.h 3,137 / .cpp 11,762)
- src/data_plane: 114 files, 16,157 lines (112 headers with 16,029 lines, 2 .cpp with 128). Only transaction/ (1,345), tablelock/ (289) and memtable/ (167) relate to transactions; the rest is vector, SIMD, access and scheduler interfaces for other areas.
- Total: 493 source files, 131,673 lines.

By kind:
- Hand-written: 131,673 lines, which is everything. 492 files carry 'Copyright (c) 2025 OceanBase' and 1 carries 2026 (git grep 'Copyright').
- Dead code inside that total: about 1,100 lines in tx. These are ob_trans_log.h (412), ob_tx_log_operator.h (473) and ob_trans_version_mgr.* (215).
- Generated: 0. git grep for 'DO NOT EDIT|@generated' finds nothing real. Standby does link 12,345 lines of tracked generated protobuf/gRPC code in src/oblib/grpc/standbyservice*.pb.* (wc -l), which is counted under oblib.
- Data: 0.
- Vendored: 0.
- Build files, not counted above: BUILD.bazel, CMakeLists.txt and .bzl, totalling 304 (logservice), 179 (standby) and 515 (data_plane) lines.
- Tightly coupled directories outside the area: storage/memtable 27,023; storage/tx_table 9,956; storage/tx_storage 8,243; storage/ls 15,425; storage/checkpoint 2,123; share/log 1,674 (holds ObLogBaseType, LSN and AccessMode).

**Summary:** 1. This area is 131,673 hand-written lines in 493 files, with no generated or vendored code. It is shrinking fast as distributed features are pruned: tx went from 75,472 lines at v1.0.0 to 37,512 at HEAD, and logservice from 84,064 to 32,705.
2. The transaction model is a single-node, one-phase commit. commit_version = max(local gts, max_read_ts+1) is passed to the log append, then overwritten with the SCN (commit timestamp) of the TX_COMMIT_LOG itself.
3. Only REDO_COMPLETE, COMMIT and ABORT are ever set as durable states. MVCC visibility comes from ObTxData (state, commit_version, undo ranges) held in the tx table, and from memtable version backfill.
4. palf-lite is a local write-ahead log where commit means the local flush finished. Election, reconfiguration and network code are gone.
5. Recovery starts palf in a RECOVERING state, then replays from base_lsn. Replay is split into queues by replay_hint − tx_id, into 32 replay queues and 64 apply queues.
6. A checkpoint takes the minimum rec_scn (recovery start SCN) reported by roughly 10 checkpoint handlers, then advances base_lsn.
7. The core is hard to port as a one-to-one translation. ObTxCtx is a 6,113-line .cpp with 174 methods and three latches, and it holds the memtable context by value. Its log callbacks form an intrusive, reference-counted cycle with the apply service, with ob_abort() enforcing the order in which callbacks complete.
8. MDS is compile-time metaprogramming: an X-macro header included at 11 sites, including inside an enum body, plus template tuple-index recursion.
9. On-disk compatibility is weaker than assumed. The project itself broke clog and ctx-table compatibility after v1.1.0: LogGroupEntryHeader now accepts only version 3, and the tx log and exec-info field lists were pruned. HEAD's format definitions match v1.4.0 (tagged 2026-08-26), so v1.4.0 is the realistic compatibility baseline, if one is required at all.
10. Standby makes the palf group-entry bytes a wire format as well.
11. For verification, the in-tree unit tests for this area were deleted in 45cbbe9e1. CI runs 272 mysqltest cases, about 21 of them transaction-focused, and none restarts the server, so replay and recovery have no in-repo CI coverage.
12. Recent transaction bug fixes are mostly protocol, ordering or logic errors that Rust would not have prevented. The defensive code, though (manual refcount aborts, spin-waits for references to drop, two-phase init checks), is the kind Rust ownership removes.

**Structure call (surveyor):** This area splits into two parts.

**Parts that can be translated keeping their structure:**
- The byte formats: palf block, group and entry headers, LogMeta, ObLogBaseHeader, the tx log block and bodies, ObTxExecInfo and ObTxData rows, ObPresistIDLog, and the UNIS encoding with positional fields and placeholder fields that must stay in place.
- palf-lite's single-writer pipeline (LSN allocator, then group buffer, then one IO thread, then flush callback), and the apply and replay services.
- These are either byte contracts or local pipelines with simple ownership. They map to Rust structs with derived encode/decode code. The 128-bit CAS needs a small unsafe or portable-atomic island.

**Parts where idiomatic Rust forces a redesign:**
- The transaction core. ObTxCtx inherits from both ObTransCtx and ObLink. It holds the memtable context by value and keeps an intrusive list of ObTxLogCb objects that hold raw ObTxCtx* pointers. The apply service reaches those callbacks through AppendCb intrusive links (CONTAINER_OF). Lifetimes are enforced by manual reference counts, and the lock protocol hands locks off through before_unlock/after_unlock.
- MDS, which is X-macro and template metaprogramming with no one-to-one Rust form.
- The tablelock object-lock map with intrusive lists and priority queues, a moderate redesign.
- A literal translation would be raw pointers and unsafe code throughout. The natural Rust design is an Arc'd context with state split across separate locks, completion events delivered in submit order through a channel or sequence number, and an enum plus trait registry for MDS types.
- The transaction core cannot be cut out alone: memtable (27K lines) and tx_table (10K lines) move with it.
- data_plane (ObITransactionService, 26 methods) is the ready-made trait or FFI boundary on the SQL side.

**Committed line:** redesign is forced by src/storage/tx/ob_tx_ctx.{h,cpp}, src/storage/tx/ob_trans_submit_log_cb.h and src/logservice/ob_append_callback.{h,cpp} (the ownership cycle between the transaction context, its log callbacks and the memtable, held together by manual reference counts), and by src/storage/multi_data_source/compile_utility/{mds_register.h,compile_mapper.h} and src/storage/multi_data_source/mds_node.h (X-macro and template metaprogramming). The formats in src/storage/tx/ob_tx_log.{h,cpp} and src/logservice/palf/log_*_header.cpp can be translated keeping their structure.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I partly agree. The redesign verdict for the transaction core and for MDS is well supported:
- ObTxCtx : ObTransCtx (ObLightHashLink refcount) + ObLink holds mt_ctx_ by value (ob_tx_ctx.h:103, 598).
- It keeps busy_cbs_ as an ObDList<ObTxLogCb> (633), and each callback holds a raw ObTxCtx* (ob_trans_submit_log_cb.h:121, 155).
- The apply service reaches those callbacks through CONTAINER_OF (ob_append_callback.cpp:25).
- on_success enforces submit order with ob_abort (ob_tx_ctx.cpp:1586-1636).
- MDS is an X-macro registry expanded inside an enum body (ob_multi_data_source.h:50-68).

I disagree on four points:
1. palf's pipeline is filed under 'keep structure', yet the survey's own hard parts list its 128-bit CAS allocator (lsn_allocator.h:91-99) and the placement-new, slot-refcounted FixedSlidingWindow. Both exist to serve concurrent Paxos appends. Now that palf-lite commits on local flush (log_sliding_window.cpp:1700-1704), only palf's byte formats need to survive. The 23K-line pipeline is itself a redesign candidate, not a structure-preserving port.
2. The formats are not a clean 'derive encode/decode' job. Fields are serialized big-endian (serialization.h:212-220), but header checksums are computed over the raw in-memory C++ object image, and HEADER_SER_SIZE = sizeof(struct):
   - log_entry_header.cpp:27, 83 (xxhash_16 over this)
   - log_block_header.cpp:101-102
   - log_meta_entry_header.cpp:180-181
   - log_group_entry_header.cpp:29

   A compatible Rust port needs #[repr(C)] mirror structs with identical padding and host byte order, plus a bit-exact ob_crc64 (a CRC32C built on SSE4.2/ARM instructions, ob_crc64.cpp:344-364).
3. data_plane is the ready-made boundary only on the SQL side. 159 files outside the area include tx, tablelock or MDS headers directly: storage/tablet 27, memtable 21, observer/virtual_table 14, tx_table 12, ls 9, ddl 5, rootserver and others. 51 files outside logservice include logservice headers.
4. MDS cannot be cut out along with tx alone: mds_register.h:24-38 pulls in payload and helper types from storage/tablet, compaction and truncate_info.

The committed line is right about which files force a redesign. It is too optimistic about what can be ported as-is and about how narrow the boundary is.

**Size split check (verifier):** All of it reproduces. I used a different counting pipeline (git ls-files -z | xargs -0 sh -c case-filter | wc -l) and got exactly 37,512 / 16,157 / 14,243 / 32,705 / 14,899 / 16,157 = 131,673 lines in 493 files. The per-extension splits match. palf is 23,057 lines, so apply, replay and glue are 9,648. Build files are 304 / 179 / 515 lines. The coupled directories match: memtable 27,023, tx_table 9,956, tx_storage 8,243, ls 15,425, checkpoint 2,123, share/log 1,674. The standby .pb files total 12,345 (12,480 with the .proto). The dead-code files total exactly 1,100. The only tracked non-source file in the area is src/storage/tx/README (123 lines). tx, tablelock and MDS have no build files of their own; they are built from src/storage/CMakeLists.txt and storage_*_inventory.bzl. I agree with the classification of 0 generated, 0 vendored and 0 data. The dead-code carve-out is right in size, but two of the three 'dead' headers are still #included (see C21).

**Units of work:** The natural unit is a file group built around one class family, usually a .h/.cpp pair plus its helpers. The area has 320 file stems in all (tx 57, tablelock 19, MDS 36, palf 51, apply and replay 4, logservice glue 11, standby 28, data_plane 114 headers). That gives about 27 translation groups.

**tx: 9 groups** (group sizes are wc -l on the named files)
1. Context core: ob_tx_ctx*, ob_trans_ctx*, ob_trans_ctx_lock*, ob_trans_submit_log_cb*, ob_tx_redo_submitter.h, ob_tx_ctx_mds*. 9,625 lines.
2. Service, API and ObTxDesc: ob_trans_service*, ob_tx_api*, ob_trans_define_v4*. 7,165 lines.
3. Log format and definitions: ob_tx_log*, ob_tx_big_segment_buf*, ob_trans_define*. 4,276 lines.
4. Context manager and functors: ob_trans_ctx_mgr_v4*, ob_trans_functor.h. 3,163 lines.
5. Tx data: ob_tx_data_define/op/functor*, ob_ctx_tx_data*. 3,170 lines.
6. Replay: ob_tx_replay_executor*. 874 lines.
7. ID and timestamp services, including ob_ts_mgr, the timestamp waiter and the version manager. 1,707 lines.
8. MDS notifier bridge: ob_multi_data_source*. 1,110 lines.
9. Deadlock adapter, timers, loop worker, keep-alive, ELR and the end-transaction callback. About 5.3K lines.
- About 1,100 of these lines are dead and can be dropped.

**tablelock: 4 groups**
- Object lock and map, lock memtable/table/manager, lock service with RPC structs and executor, and live detector/deadlock/callback.

**MDS: 3 groups**
- Template core (.ipp files and table/unit/row/node headers, about 9K lines), runtime utility (factory, service, list helper), and adapters, dump and merge DAG.

**logservice: 6 groups**
- palf formats (block, group and entry headers, meta, checksum): 2,771 lines.
- palf IO and storage (block manager, IO worker, storage, reader, iterators, cache, GC): 10,355 lines.
- palf core (sliding window, LSN allocator, group buffer, handle and env, state and mode managers, threads): 10,187 lines.
- Apply service: 1,631 lines.
- Replay service: 3,354 lines.
- Handler, log-service and block-manager glue: about 4.6K lines.

**standby: 2 groups**
- Log sync, gRPC and control: about 5.5K lines.
- restore/: about 9.4K lines.

**data_plane: 1 group** for this area
- Transaction, tablelock and memtable interface headers, 1,801 lines. They become the Rust trait and FFI boundary.

**Dependency constraint:** groups tx-1 through tx-6 cannot be landed without the matching parts of storage/memtable (27,023 lines) and storage/tx_table (9,956 lines). A working order is format groups first (golden-byte testable), then ID and timestamp services and palf, then the transaction core together with memtable and tx_table.

**Public-surface tests:** **CI (in-repo, public surface):**
- .github/workflows/seekdb.yml runs mysqltest through .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py over the 272-entry test-set in tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml.
- About 21 of those cases are transaction-focused by name:
  - big_trans_with_mutil_redo, ddlrollback, ms_lose_rollback, sfu, sfu_norow_alias, trans_ac
  - trx_4, trx_5, trx_6, trx_timeout, trx_timeout_bug
  - deadlock_detector.trans_deadlock_basic, executor.trx, fork_table.fork_table_lock, histogram.stats_lock_and_unlock, system_variable.autocommit
  - trx.bug_different_tranid_intrx, trx.repeatable_read_transaction, trx.serializable_constrains, trx.serializable_transaction, trx.trans_weak_read
- 43 of the 283 tools/deploy/mysql_test .test files mention savepoint, rollback, begin/start transaction, lock table, for update or autocommit.
- Every DML test implicitly exercises commit, redo logging, palf append and the apply path.
- No CI mysqltest restarts the server. The config_reboot_value.inc include is empty. Replay, recovery, checkpoint and base_lsn recycling therefore have no in-repo CI coverage.

**Tests that import internals:**
- None at HEAD. Commit 45cbbe9e1 deleted all unittest/ and mittest/ sources, 34 of which (6,642 lines) were unittests for tx, tablelock, MDS, tx_table and logservice.
- A port can therefore be verified only through SQL-level tests and whatever new harness it adds.

**obtest:**
- tools/obtest/t holds 500 .test files, including suites trans (32), trx_succ (10), logservice (4), clog (2) and primary_standby (145).
- 162 of those files stop or restart servers (force_stop or nstart).
- obtest is not wired into CI (git grep obtest -- .github finds nothing), and it is OceanBase-era: 173 cases use CREATE TENANT or multi-zone clusters. For example, tools/obtest/t/logservice/1001_c1_restart_check_data_loss.test queries __all_tenant.

**Outside this repo:**
- Commit messages for 61a371a65 and 370ce4fff point to transaction regression tests in an external repo, seekdb_test (MRs !67, !71, !74).
- Those tests drive debug-sync injection points such as DEBUG_SYNC(BEFORE_TX_ABORT_DECISION) at ob_tx_ctx.cpp:1599. The area has 64 debug-sync or errsim mentions, 19 of them ERRSIM_POINT_DEF.
- A port would have to keep these named hooks for those tests to keep working.

**Hard parts:**
- Ownership cycle between transaction contexts and log callbacks. ObTxCtx (src/storage/tx/ob_tx_ctx.h:103) inherits ObTransCtx (an ObLightHashLink reference count, src/storage/tx/ob_trans_ctx.h:85) and ObLink. It owns common::ObDList<ObTxLogCb> busy_cbs_ (ob_tx_ctx.h:633). Each ObTxLogCb holds a raw ObTxCtx* (src/storage/tx/ob_trans_submit_log_cb.h:83-130) and is also queued in the apply service through AppendCb's intrusive __next_ link, recovered with CONTAINER_OF (src/logservice/ob_append_callback.cpp:25).
- ObTxCtx is one very large class: ob_tx_ctx.cpp has 6,113 lines and 174 method definitions, plus 5 more in ob_tx_ctx_log_cb_helper.cpp. It holds memtable::ObMemtableCtx by value (ob_tx_ctx.h:598), so transactions and the memtable must be ported together.
- Three-latch lock protocol with bitmask modes and lock handoff. CtxLock and CtxLockGuard are in src/storage/tx/ob_trans_ctx_lock.h:62-142. before_unlock/after_unlock with CtxLockArg (ob_trans_ctx.h:131-132) run the commit callback outside the lock. on_success chooses the lock mode dynamically (ob_tx_ctx.cpp:1550).
- The ObTransService class body is spread across three headers through #include inside the class (src/storage/tx/ob_trans_service.h:169 and ob_trans_service_v4.h:226).
- MDS metaprogramming. The X-macro registry src/storage/multi_data_source/compile_utility/mds_register.h is included at 11 sites, one inside an enum body (src/storage/tx/ob_multi_data_source.h:62-66). compile_mapper.h does type-to-ID mapping and constexpr recursion over tuple indexes. mds_register.h documents detection of optional methods (on_redo, on_commit and others). UserMdsNode uses multiple inheritance whose order must not change (src/storage/multi_data_source/mds_node.h:181, 'this order can not be changed!!!'). MDS has 501 template lines.
- Lock-free LSN and SCN allocation: a 128-bit CAS on a bitfield union (src/logservice/palf/lsn_allocator.h:91-99, CAS128 at 8 sites in lsn_allocator.cpp). Rust has no stable AtomicU128, so this needs portable-atomic or inline assembly, or a redesign now that there is a single writer.
- FixedSlidingWindow allocates a raw array through ge_alloc, constructs slots with placement new, and gives each slot a reference count and an ObByteLock (src/logservice/palf/fixed_sliding_window.h).
- Custom slice allocator: ObTxData and ObUndoStatusNode live in fixed 128-byte slices with embedded hash and sort links, a static_assert on size, and a _WIN32 ABI branch (src/storage/tx/ob_tx_data_define.h:55-112, 369).
- Byte-exact positional serialization through OB_SERIALIZE_MEMBER, OB_UNIS_VERSION and DEFINE_SERIALIZE (258 macro lines in the area). Placeholder fields must keep their position: checksum_[0] and checksum_scn_[0] marked 'FARM COMPAT WHITELIST' (src/storage/tx/ob_trans_define.cpp:407-408) and obsolete_trace_payload_ (ob_tx_log.cpp:218). A header field is fixed-size and back-patched through FixSizeTrait_int64_t (ob_tx_log.h:644-692). The redo body is the memtable mutator format, which belongs to storage/memtable.
- Type-erased callback arguments: ObTxCbArg holds const void *arg_ (src/storage/tx/ob_tx_log.h, ObTxCbArg). The area has 334 lines mentioning void*, 70 reinterpret_cast and 107 static_cast to pointer types.
- Hand-written reference counting in 65 call sites, with guard classes and ob_abort on bad counts (src/storage/tx/ob_tx_data_define.h:375-393, src/logservice/replayservice/ob_replay_status.h:523-637, src/logservice/applyservice/ob_log_apply_service.cpp).
- Error-code control flow: 2,248 functions start with 'int ret = OB_SUCCESS', there are 3,628 OB_FAIL/MDS_FAIL, and long if/else-if chains with FALSE_IT side effects (for example ob_tx_ctx.cpp:5687-5712). OB_EAGAIN-driven retry loops appear 239 times.
- Direct IO and a pool of preallocated log block files, with platform branches: 125 hits for O_DIRECT, fsync, fallocate, pwrite or pread; 25 _WIN32 and 9 __APPLE__ ifdefs (src/logservice/ob_server_log_block_mgr.cpp, palf/log_block_handler.cpp, palf/log_io_adapter.cpp).
- Standby's gRPC service is built on 12,345 lines of generated C++ protobuf code (src/oblib/grpc/standbyservice*.pb.*). It sends serialized ObLSMetaPackage and ObCopyTabletInfo inside protobuf bytes fields (standbyservice.proto:26-31).

**Pain instances:**
- src/storage/tx/ob_tx_api.cpp:159-198: ObTransService::reuse_tx waits in a spin loop, with usleep(2000000) per iteration past 2,000 spins, for ObTxDesc's reference count to fall to 1 or 2 before reinitializing it, and calls ob_abort in debug builds after 2,300 spins. This waits out references at run time; unique ownership (Arc::try_unwrap or moving the value) would make it a compile-time property.
- src/storage/tx/ob_tx_data_define.cpp:393-394 ('TODO : @gengli remove ob_abort'), 476 and 751, and ob_tx_data_define.h:389: ObTxData and ObTxDataOp crash the process on an invalid reference count or on reset while still referenced. This is defensive code for manual reference counting that Arc/Drop removes.
- src/storage/tx/ob_tx_ctx.cpp:2008-2013: on_failure calls ob_abort() when a log callback's cb_arg_array is empty. src/storage/tx/ob_tx_ctx.cpp:3143-3144 calls ob_abort() when log_cb->set_callbacks fails, which is an allocation failure.
- src/storage/tx/ob_tx_ctx_mds.cpp:177-195: ObTxMDSCache::is_mem_leak keeps a memory-statistics hash in debug builds to catch leaked MDS nodes. src/storage/multi_data_source/mds_table_mgr.h:37 has RemovedMdsTableRecorder ('tablet leak will resulting mds table leak, we must prove it'). a87fd9832 changed MDS recycle timers from static TG ids to per-instance ids in a service that also kept a memory_leak_debug_map_, which is no longer in HEAD.
- src/logservice/palf/palf_handle.h:45-48: the copy-assignment operator is documented to leak PalfHandleImpl and Callback when the target is valid. That is an ownership rule left to comments.
- 3175abf03 (src/storage/multi_data_source/runtime_utility/mds_factory.cpp): during template recursion over the tuple, deep_copy_buffer_ctx compared the tuple index IDX with old_ctx.get_binding_type_id(), mixing two ID spaces, so the matching context was never copied and new_ctx stayed null. A Rust enum match makes this mistake impossible.
- 7bef5aa47 (src/logservice/ob_log_service.cpp): a crash in embedded mode caused by a service being skipped at start() yet used later. The area has 1,074 IS_NOT_INIT, OB_INIT_TWICE or is_inited_ checks guarding the two-phase init/start/stop/wait/destroy lifecycle that constructor-based Rust types avoid.
- src/storage/multi_data_source/runtime_utility/common_define.h:45-53: MDS_ASSERT calls ob_abort() in release builds, with 62 uses. The area has 34 direct ob_abort() calls in all, many guarding pointer or ordering invariants.

**Open questions:**
- Does seekdb promise in-place upgrade from v1.4.0 data directories (clog block_N files, ctx-table and tx-data sstable rows)? If yes, a port must match v1.4.0 byte for byte. If not, as the project's own break after v1.1.0 suggests (51cb986b9), the format can be redesigned. The repo has no upgrade or compatibility test or policy.
- Are mixed-version primary/standby pairs supported? Standby ships raw palf group entries (FetchLogRes.buf), which would freeze the group-entry format as a wire format.
- Was the deletion of all unittest/mittest sources in 45cbbe9e1 intentional? The commit subject is about a decimal aggregate fix. Do those tests live on in the external seekdb_test repo? That repo, and how many transaction or recovery cases it holds, could not be inspected read-only.
- Are enable_early_lock_release (default True, ob_parameter_seed.ipp:555) and _enable_parallel_redo_logging (default True, :562) actually exercised in embedded and server deployments? Each adds a state (ELR_COMMIT, serial_final or parallel replay queues) that a port must reproduce or drop explicitly.
- Is there a commit-latency or log-throughput baseline a port must meet? No in-repo benchmark for palf or transactions was located (not searched exhaustively).
- How much of the removed-type space in ObLogBaseType (36 values, many with no registered handler) and ObTxLogType (the TX_CLEAR_LOG decode-only path) can appear in logs written by v1.4.0? Answering this needs a real v1.4.0 clog to decode, and no clog dump tool (ob_admin) is tracked in the repo.
- Will the area keep shrinking? Commits such as 1cbe30f00 (2026-09-18) and the removals of XA, dup-table and multi-log-stream suggest yes. That affects whether translation should wait for the pruning to settle.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- On-disk header checksums hash the in-memory C++ struct bytes, not the serialized bytes. So format compatibility depends on struct layout, padding and host byte order, and on oblib's ob_crc64, which is really CRC32C with hardware and table variants. A Rust port that 'derives encode/decode' would write logs whose checksums do not match. (evidence: log_entry_header.cpp:83 xxhash_16(checksum, reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(this), sizeof(LogEntryHeader)); log_block_header.cpp:101-102 ob_crc64(this, sizeof(*this)-sizeof(checksum_)); log_meta_entry_header.cpp:180-181; HEADER_SER_SIZE = sizeof(...) at log_entry_header.cpp:27, log_group_entry_header.cpp:29, log_meta_entry_header.cpp:26; encode_i64 is big-endian (src/oblib/lib/utility/serialization.h:212-220); ob_crc64.cpp:344-364 uses _mm_crc32/__crc32cd.)
- The WAL, replay and checkpoint framework is shared with log producers outside transactions: DDL, storage schema, tablet sequence sync, medium compaction, reserved snapshot, keep-alive, vector index, and rootserver major freeze. Porting palf and the replay service therefore means keeping a cross-language ObIReplaySubHandler / ObICheckpointSubHandler interface for those modules, or porting them too. (evidence: src/storage/ls/ob_ls.cpp:700-706 (7 REGISTER_TO_LOGSERVICE calls), ob_ls.cpp:640-656 and 740 (vector index log handler, VEC_INDEX_LOG_BASE_TYPE), src/share/log/ob_log_base_type.h:262-300 (register macros); implementers include src/rootserver/freeze/ob_major_freeze_service.h:38, storage/ls/ob_ls_ddl_log_handler.h:62, ob_ls_tablet_service.h:97, ob_ls_sync_tablet_seq_handler.h:31; 51 files outside src/logservice include logservice headers.)
- On the storage side, the boundary of the transaction layer is wide, not the 26-method data_plane interface. storage/tablet, memtable, tx_table, ls, ddl, compaction, access, lob, deadlock, observer virtual tables and rootserver all include tx, tablelock or MDS headers directly and use internal types such as ObTxDesc, ObTxCtx stats and lock ops. (evidence: git grep -lE '#include [<"](src/)?storage/(tx|tablelock|multi_data_source)/' -- src minus the area gives 159 files (storage/tablet 27, storage/memtable 21, observer/virtual_table 14, storage/tx_table 12, storage/ls 9, storage/ddl 5, observer/vector_index 5, observer/change_stream 5, rootserver/fork_table 4, ...). Examples: observer/virtual_table/ob_all_virtual_tx_stat.cpp and storage/access/ob_dml_param.h include ob_trans_define*.h.)
- The MDS registry pulls in payload types owned by other modules, and the IDs it generates are persisted in logs. So MDS cannot move without the tablet, compaction and truncate-info helpers, or a cross-language callback layer for them, and its IDs are frozen. (evidence: src/storage/multi_data_source/compile_utility/mds_register.h:24-38 includes storage/tablet/ob_tablet_create_mds_helper.h, ob_tablet_binding_helper.h, ob_tablet_fork_mds_helper.h, compaction/ob_medium_compaction_info.h and truncate_info/ob_truncate_info_mds_helper.h; lines 63-92 register 8 helper types with fixed IDs; ob_multi_data_source.h:58 '5 was DDL_BARRIER. Reserved; do not reuse.')
- The proposed compatibility baseline v1.4.0 sits on a release branch that HEAD does not contain, so the formats can drift apart on the two lines. Separately, the 'release-only log' branch of MDS_ASSERT is never compiled: OB_BUILD_PACKAGE is only a CMake variable and never a compiler define, so MDS_ASSERT aborts in every build. (evidence: git merge-base --is-ancestor v1.4.0 HEAD fails; v1.4.0 (de5be277d) is contained only in remotes/origin/release/1.4.0, one commit past merge-base 977053bf1, and HEAD has 692 commits since. MDS_ASSERT: common_define.h:35-53 (#ifdef OB_BUILD_PACKAGE logs, #else ob_abort). OB_BUILD_PACKAGE appears only in CMakeLists.txt:128-161 (checks), build.sh:422 and build.ps1:358 as a -D CMake option, never in add_compile_definitions or any .bzl file.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey can be trusted for sizes and for most facts at the level of individual lines. Every count reproduces exactly with an independent method: directory totals, per-extension splits, history deltas, commit counts, include sites, CI test-set size and deleted unit tests. Most cited line numbers point at the code described: the one-phase commit chain, the on_success ordering aborts, the three-latch guard, the class body split across headers, local-flush commit, the 128-bit CAS, the header version break and the standby wire path.

The errors are overstatements rather than fabrications:
- PREPARE, PRE_COMMIT and CLEAR are still read in branches that can no longer run (C5).
- The two queue concepts in replay routing are merged into one (C9).
- 'Unreferenced' dead headers are in fact still #included (C21).
- One ob_abort is misattributed to a bad refcount when it fires on a null allocator (C22).
- ob_abort is counted as 34 lines rather than 27 calls (C16).
- The MDS_ASSERT 'release builds' reasoning is wrong, although the conclusion holds.

The structure call is sound where it matters most, the transaction-context ownership cycle and the MDS metaprogramming. It is too optimistic in three places: how easily the byte formats can be kept compatible (checksums over raw struct memory), how narrow the boundary is (data_plane covers SQL only; 159 storage-side includers and a WAL shared with DDL, compaction and vector index), and whether palf's concurrent pipeline is worth keeping. Use its numbers as given and its hard-parts list as a floor, not a ceiling.


## AREA: search-vector-gis
**What it is:** Full-text search (five built-in tokenizers including the dictionary-driven IK Chinese tokenizer, a BM25/sparse-vector retrieval engine, and the boolean-mode query parser), vector search (HNSW/HGraph/SINDI through the vsag C++ library, a seekdb-own IVF with k-means, SIMD distance kernels, and the index lifecycle and background tasks), hybrid search (Elasticsearch-style JSON translated to SQL), and MySQL-compatible GIS (src/share/geo on boost.geometry and s2geometry, plus about 50 ST_ expressions). All four sit on top of ordinary seekdb tables; the only native libraries are vsag, s2geometry, boost.geometry and CRoaring.

**Size split:** Scope is a curated list of 649 git-tracked .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.y/.l/.inc files with 461,657 lines. Command: sz(){ f=$(git -C $R ls-files src | grep -E '\.(h|hpp|cpp|cc|c|ipp|y|l|inc)$' | grep -E "$pattern"); echo "$f" | grep -c .; echo "$f" | sed "s|^|$R/|" | xargs cat | wc -l; }, run once per sub-area regex. The sub-area patterns were checked by printing their file lists.
- DATA: 1 file, 276,043 lines. src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp is 5,272,001 bytes and holds three static const char* arrays (awk line ranges + grep -o '"[^"]*"' | wc -l): the main dictionary with 275,908 words, a quantifier list with 316 words, and a stop list with 33 words.
- GENERATED: 2 files, 651 lines. src/share/geo/ob_vector_tile.pb-c.{c,h} say "Generated by the protocol buffer compiler" and come from the tracked ob_vector_tile.proto. The bison/flex output for the FTS query parser (ftsparser_tab.c, ftsblex_lex.c) is on disk but not tracked, so it is not counted.
- VENDORED THIRD-PARTY: 0 lines in git. vsag 1.1.0, s2geometry 0.10.0, roaringbitmap-croaring 3.0.0, boost 1.74.0, abseil, icu 69.1 and libcurl arrive as prebuilt packages listed in deps/init/oceanbase.*.deps.
- HAND-WRITTEN: 646 files, 184,963 lines, split as follows:
  - FTS and retrieval, 37,622 lines: src/storage/fts without the dictionary is 71 files and 10,526 lines; the FTS API headers (data_plane/fts, share/text_analysis, query/das) are 2,093; FTS SQL/DDL glue (sql/parser/fts*, resolver ob_fts_*, rootserver fts tasks, tokenize/bm25/doc_id exprs, das text_retrieval iterators) is 11,981; storage/retrieval with data_plane/retrieval and das/iter/sparse_retrieval is 13,022.
  - Vector, 74,519 lines: the vsag adaptor in src/oblib/lib/vector is 1,991; src/observer/vector_index is 25,210; other vector code (query/api/query/vector, query/vector, share/vector, storage/vector_index, storage/vector_type, data_plane/vector, storage/api/storage/vector) is 13,618; vector SQL/DDL glue (das hnsw/ivf/spiv iterators, 42 ob_expr_vec*/spiv/embedded/semantic files, ob_vec_index_builder_util, rootserver vec tasks, dbms_vector packages, virtual tables, ob_hnsw_embedmgr, ob_vector_allocator) is 89 files and 33,407 lines; the share/roaringbitmap memory hook is 293.
  - GIS, 62,551 lines: src/share/geo is 179 files and 47,033 hand-written lines; 104 GIS expression files in sql/engine/expr are 14,674; ob_geo_wkb_define.h, ob_srs_service and ob_das_spatial_scan_iter are 844.
  - Hybrid search: src/sql/hybrid_search, 6,060 lines.
  - Shared domain-index DAS code (ob_domain_id, ob_das_domain_utils, ob_das_domain_id_merge_iter): 4,211 lines.
Inside share/geo, 38 files (16,691 lines) touch boost (git grep -l 'bg::\|boost::geometry\|boost/') and 6 files (5,210 lines) touch S2 (git grep -lE 'S2[A-Z]|s2/'). The rest is seekdb's own geometry code: WKB/WKT/GeoJSON handling, 29 visitors, prepared-geometry caches, MVT and SRS.

**Summary:** This area is 184,963 hand-written lines plus one 276,043-line data file. No third-party code is vendored in git: vsag, s2geometry, boost, CRoaring and ICU all arrive as prebuilt packages. Most of the code is seekdb's own logic, not thin glue.
- **FTS** calls no native library. The IK tokenizer is a port of the Java IK Analyzer and uses seekdb's charset library, not ICU.
- **IVF** is seekdb's own code, including its k-means.
- **The vsag boundary is narrow.** vsag is reached only through a roughly 2,000-line adaptor that already passes void* handles and raw arrays, plus 88 references in 8 files.
- **boost.geometry and S2 are not thin glue.** seekdb registers its own packed WKB view classes as boost.geometry types through template traits. It dispatches 22 functions across coordinate system × 7 × 7 geometry-type combinations using 716 macro-generated template specializations. It also maps boost exceptions to user-visible OB_ERR_BOOST_GEOMETRY_* errors, which the geometry tests check.
- **Three things are effectively on-disk formats and decide between keeping the library behind FFI and rewriting it:**
  - S2 cell ids are written into spatial index rows, and an update recomputes them from the old WKB.
  - The vsag snapshot serialization format is pinned to the legacy format (commit 2a27b3f11).
  - IK/ngram tokenizer output must reproduce the tokens already stored in FTS index rows.

So Rust should keep vsag and S2 behind a C shim. GIS should either stay in C++ behind a C ABI at the ST_-function level or be redesigned on georust with a high verification cost. FTS, retrieval, hybrid search, IVF and the vector distance kernels can be translated largely one-to-one.

The dictionary carries over cleanly. The main array is byte-sorted, has no duplicates and no escape sequences, so it can become a 2.78 MB newline-separated UTF-8 file loaded with include_str!. The table-based loading path is dead code and can be dropped.

The case for Rust here rests on concrete bugs:
- a data race added to parallel dictionary building and fixed 8 days later (87ab54c34, then b5d96efaf);
- a null dereference on the release path (c68c6749a);
- a leak on an error path (b5e98215e);
- a bad static_cast that crashed (a44b2526f);
- a lifetime bug in the embedding task that was fixed with a hand-written reference count ending in `delete this` (9bb15300f).

Public-surface coverage is good for GIS (42 tests, 85K lines, many ported from MySQL) and moderate for vector search (22 tests) and FTS (15 tests). It is thin for the IK tokenizer (4 tests) and zero for the hybrid-search DSL. No C++ unit tests are tracked, so every existing test survives the migration, but the IK tokenizer needs a differential corpus.

**Structure call (surveyor):** The area splits in three.

**Translates close to one-to-one into idiomatic Rust (about 68K lines).** This covers the FTS tokenizers and dictionary code, storage/retrieval, the FTS/vector SQL and DDL glue, hybrid search, IVF/k-means and the SIMD kernels. These are shallow interface-plus-final-class hierarchies, OB_FAIL error chains that become Result and ?, arena-allocated lists that become Vec or an arena, and borrowed-pointer tokens that become &str lifetimes. The SIMD kernels map onto std::arch with #[target_feature] and runtime detection.

**Needs an ownership redesign but not an algorithm redesign.** The vector-index runtime in src/observer/vector_index and src/query/api/query/vector uses hand-written reference counts, five locks per adaptor, raw roaring64 pointers and self-deleting tasks. These become Arc plus Mutex or RwLock plus owned bitmaps.

**Cannot be translated structurally.**
- vsag and S2 stay native behind a small extern "C" shim written in C++. The shim keeps the std::streambuf bridge, the vsag Allocator, Logger and Filter subclasses, and the S2RegionCoverer call, because their outputs are persisted.
- boost.geometry is a template library instantiated over seekdb's own packed WKB view classes. It cannot be called over FFI and has no drop-in Rust equivalent. Either share/geo stays C++ behind a C ABI at the ST_-function level, or it is redesigned on georust `geo`, `proj4rs` and a hand-written WKT parser, accepting drift from MySQL-identical results and error codes.

Files that force the call: src/share/geo/ob_geo_bin_traits.h, ob_geo_tree_traits.h, ob_geo_dispatcher.h, ob_geo_bin.h, the 9 ob_geo_func_*_helper.ipp, ob_srs_wkt_parser.cpp and ob_geo_func_transform.cpp force redesign or a C++ island. src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp, src/query/api/query/vector/ob_vector_index_serialize.h and src/share/geo/ob_s2adapter.cpp / ob_geo_to_s2_visitor.cpp force a C++ FFI shim. Everything else translates one-to-one.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I partly agree. Keeping vsag behind a small C++ shim is right. The C++-only bridges are real: std::streambuf subclasses in ob_vector_index_serialize.h:32-109, vsag::Allocator/Logger/FilterInterface subclasses in ob_vector_index_adaptor.h:147,237 and ob_vector_util.h:35. The snapshot format is pinned (2a27b3f11). Keeping S2 native is also right, and easier than the survey suggests: all SQL-side use goes through seekdb's own ObS2Adapter (ob_range_generator.cpp:1617-1642, ob_table_scan_op.cpp:3511, ob_das_domain_utils.cpp:166), which is already a narrow wrapper. Calling boost.geometry the hardest part is right (ob_geo_bin_traits.h, ob_geo_bin.h pack(1) views, 710 dispatcher specializations). I disagree on two points. (1) The 'C ABI at the ST_-function level' boundary for GIS is too narrow. share/geo is also called from casts (ob_datum_cast.cpp has 103 geo references; share/object/ob_obj_cast.cpp), the range generator (sql/rewrite, 5 files), the SRS service (observer/omt/ob_srs_service.cpp), dml_service, the aggregate processor and table_scan_op. ObSpatialMBR is an OB_UNIS-serialized type in the scan params (ob_s2adapter.h:60, ob_tablet_scan.h:346). boost.geometry reaches 25 expression files through ob_geo_func_register.h -> ob_geo_dispatcher.h -> ob_geo_func_common.h:30. A C++ GIS island therefore needs cast, range and SRS entry points as well, not only ST_ functions. (2) The 'translates close to one-to-one' group is not a unit that can move on its own. Its vector and FTS SQL glue depends on plan code in src/sql/optimizer (ob_log_table_scan.cpp, ob_join_order.cpp, ob_log_plan.cpp), in src/sql/code_generator/ob_tsc_cg_service.cpp, and in the DDL pipeline (storage/ddl/ob_ddl_pipeline.cpp, ob_vector_index_ddl_pipeline.h). The translation is easy line by line, but the order is decided by when the optimizer, DAS and DDL move. The three-way split itself (easy translation, ownership redesign for the vector-index runtime, keep native for vsag/S2/boost.geometry) is a sound way to divide the area.

**Size split check (verifier):** The line numbers hold up. I recounted the big sub-areas with awk NR and Python instead of cat|wc, and they match exactly: FTS 286,569 (10,526 without the dictionary, 71 files), observer/vector_index 25,210, oblib/lib/vector 1,991, other vector dirs 13,618, retrieval 13,022, share/geo 47,684 (47,033 + 651 generated), hybrid_search 6,060, distance kernels 5,444 in 44 files. The 14-term sum of 461,657 and the hand-written total of 184,963 are correct. The glue buckets (FTS SQL glue 11,981, vector SQL glue 33,407) come from curated regexes I could not reproduce exactly. My spot checks are in the same range: 10,813 lines in the das/iter vector files, 44 vector expression files with 4,469 lines, 5,833 lines of rootserver vec code. Problems: (1) share/geo has 179 source files including the 2 pb-c files, so 177 are hand-written, not 179. (2) The FTS API headers come to 2,051 lines from data_plane/fts + share/text_analysis; the other ~42 lines are from part of query/das (289 lines in 4 files). (3) The structure call's 'about 68K lines translate one-to-one' does not add up from the survey's own numbers: the listed pieces (FTS lib, retrieval, FTS and vector SQL/DDL glue, hybrid, IVF, kernels) come to roughly 89K, or roughly 50K without the vector glue. (4) The scope leaves out vector and FTS code that lives in core SQL: 271 files outside the counted vector dirs mention hnsw/ivf/vsag/vec_index. The optimizer has about 944 vector-index tokens and 280 FTS tokens, ob_tsc_cg_service.cpp has 362 matching lines, and storage/ddl/ob_ddl_pipeline.cpp has 180. None of this appears in any bucket, so the hand-written total understates how much code this feature area touches.

**Units of work:** Natural units are directory-sized crates or modules. There are 14:
1. IK dictionary data: 1 file becomes 3 UTF-8 resources. The main list is 275,908 sorted, unique lines (2.78 MB), loaded with include_str!, or the trie can be pre-built in build.rs. This is mechanical and done by a script.
2. FTS tokenizer library, src/storage/fts minus the data file: 71 files, 10,526 lines, in 4 groups:
   - the 5 parsers with their descriptors;
   - ik/ processors, token chain and arbitrator (13 files);
   - dict/ trie, cache and hub (27 files, of which roughly 800 lines of table-loading code are dead);
   - utils/ ngram and unicode (5 files).
   It depends on a Rust charset module and a KV-cache binding.
3. FTS boolean-mode query parser: 6 tracked files (ftsparser.y, ftsblex.l, fts_base.c/h, fts_parse.c/h), a rewrite with a hand-written parser or a parser generator.
4. Sparse retrieval engine: storage/retrieval, data_plane/retrieval and das/iter/sparse_retrieval, 41 files and 13,022 lines. The iterator interfaces become traits.
5. FTS SQL/DDL glue: resolver, rootserver tasks, tokenize/bm25/doc_id expressions and das text_retrieval iterators, 28 files and 11,981 lines. It moves with the SQL and DDL units.
6. vsag shim: src/oblib/lib/vector, 4 files and 1,991 lines. It stays C++ and becomes the extern "C" boundary. The streambuf and allocator bridges from ob_vector_index_serialize.h and ObVsagSearchAlloc move into it.
7. Vector-index runtime: src/observer/vector_index (33 files, 25,210 lines) plus query/api/query/vector and query/vector (9 files, 5,126 lines). It needs the ownership redesign.
8. Distance kernels: data_plane/vector and storage/vector_type, 44 files and 5,444 lines.
9. IVF: storage/vector_index k-means (2 files, 1,951 lines), plus ob_das_ivf_scan_iter and the ivf expressions inside unit 10.
10. Vector SQL/DDL glue: 89 files and 33,407 lines, including the hnsw/ivf/spiv DAS iterators (about 9.6K lines), 42 expression files, ob_vec_index_builder_util (5.6K lines) and the rootserver vec tasks.
11. Hybrid search: 10 files and 6,060 lines, self-contained, translating JSON to SQL.
12. GIS core, src/share/geo: 179 files and 47,033 hand-written lines, plus 651 generated lines to regenerate with prost. It is either one C++ island behind a C ABI, or on a rewrite about 7 groups: representations, 22 function types, visitors, SRS/WKT, S2 adapter, MVT, and utils/cast.
13. GIS expressions: 104 files and 14,674 lines, about 50 ST_ expressions (48 ST_ .cpp files).
14. Shared domain-index DAS code: 6 files and 4,211 lines.
The roaring glue (2 files, 293 lines) goes with unit 7.

**Public-surface tests:** All tracked tests for this area run through SQL (mysqltest or obtest). No tracked test uses C++ internals: unittest/ holds only .gitignore files, and deps/oblib/unittest has 2 jemalloc tests. So every test survives a translation unchanged.
- tools/deploy/mysql_test/test_suite/geometry: 42 .test and 42 .result files, 85,270 lines together. 39 of them call ST_ functions. They include tests ported from MySQL, such as st_buffer_mysql (MySQL Bug #24947868 on boost::geometry buffer), st_transform_mysql (expects ER_BOOST_GEOMETRY_UNKNOWN_EXCEPTION), create_spatial_index_mysql and geometry_index{,1,2}_mysql. There are 2,834 ERROR/--error lines, so error codes are part of the contract. This is strong coverage if boost/S2 are kept and a large diff risk if they are rewritten.
- tools/deploy/mysql_test/test_suite/vector_index: 22 .test and 22 .result files, 20,404 lines. Examples: vector_index_basic, vector_index_ivfflat_{basic,dml,scan,post_create}, sparse_vector_index_vsag_{ddl,query}, create_table_with_hybrid_vector_index, vector_similarity, vector_calc_{gbk,utf8}_mysql, rebuild_vector_index, all_virtual_vector_index_info.
- tools/deploy/mysql_test/test_suite/fts_index: 15 .test and 15 .result files, 16,581 lines. Examples: basic_dml (4,060-line result), simple_query, partitioned_simple_query, cn_word, tokenize_function, parser_properties, offline/online DDL. Only 4 tests use the ik parser (cn_word.test has 9 uses) and tokenize() appears in 3 tests, so the 275,908-word IK dictionary is exercised by a handful of sentences.
- FTS or vector index DDL also appears in fork_table (2 fulltext, 3 vector), array (2), histogram (2) and geometry (1).
- tools/obtest/t/storage: dic_loader.test, create_fts_index_mid_switch_rs.test, data_tablet_autoinc_column_sync_for_fts.test, dml_and_fts_ddl_parallel.test. tools/obtest/t/stanby/standby_restart.test and switchover_roundtrip.test also use fulltext and vector.

Gaps:
- There is no tracked test for DBMS_HYBRID_VECTOR.SEARCH/GET_SQL. psmalltest.py names hybrid_search.dbms_hybrid_search_rrf_mysql and _es_mode, but those files are not in git. GET_SQL returns the generated SQL text, so it would make an ideal golden test.
- The IK tokenizer needs differential testing, running the C++ and Rust tokenizers side by side over a large Chinese corpus, because mysqltest coverage cannot prove token-for-token equality.

**Hard parts:**
- boost.geometry over seekdb types: boost traits specialized for seekdb's packed WKB view classes, which overlay raw bytes and use a const operator= hack. The boost algorithms are instantiated per (function, type1, type2). A Rust port needs a C++ island or a rewrite on georust with different numerical results. Paths: src/share/geo/ob_geo_bin_traits.h, src/share/geo/ob_geo_tree_traits.h, src/share/geo/ob_geo_bin.h.
