The code assumes raw pointers into allocator-owned memory throughout: ObIAllocator in about 38% of files, 2,741 placement-new sites, 5,938 raw-pointer fields, ObString/ObDatum pointers without lifetimes, and allocation failure as a recoverable error. It also relies on intrusive links (ObDLinkBase under 739 classes), atomics applied to ordinary fields, self-referential byte-copied rows, and function-pointer unions serialized as raw addresses. A structure-preserving translation therefore yields unsafe-heavy Rust. A safe result needs a redesign of memory and ownership (arenas with lifetimes or index handles, fallible collections, Atomic* field types, enum-based expression dispatch), done first in the oblib foundation (alloc, allocator, container, hash, list, queue, lock, atomic, utility, oblog, thread and others: about 91.8K lines) and then propagated to every caller. The files that force the call: src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_iallocator.h, src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h, src/query/api/query/engine/ob_serializable_function.h, src/oblib/lib/list/ob_dlink_node.h, src/oblib/lib/atomic/ob_atomic.h, src/sql/engine/basic/ob_chunk_datum_store.cpp, src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_unify_serialize.h.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the main call: file and function structure can mostly be kept, but safe Rust needs the ownership and memory layer redesigned first, and everything else follows it. The evidence holds up. ObIAllocator appears in 2,498 files (ob_iallocator.h:66). There are 2,741 placement-new lines and 5,938 raw-pointer fields. Rows point into themselves (ob_chunk_datum_store.cpp:90-100 with point2pointer at :61). ObExpr keeps raw args_/parents_ and function-pointer unions that are serialized as addresses (ob_expr.h:643-678, ob_serializable_function.h:52-83, sql/engine/expr/ob_expr.cpp:114). Four corrections change how the plan is framed, but not the verdict. (1) The intrusive-link burden is overstated. ObDLinkBase is a CRTP template (ob_dlink_node.h:33), inherited directly by about 58 classes. The 573 ObExprOperator descendants inherit a link that is never used in a list (ob_expr_operator.h:304, and :992 only resets it), so that part is a deletion, not a redesign. (2) The ATOMIC_* retype covers about 823 distinct names, and the compiler finds every leftover plain access once a field is Atomic*, so it does not need clang whole-program analysis. (3) Stable Rust has try_reserve on std collections. The real blocker is arena-parameterized fallible containers (allocator_api is unstable), not fallibility itself. (4) The foundation list leaves out two pieces that are just as foundational: lib/rc (thread-local memory contexts, context.h:46-67, which ObArray binds to on first allocation at ob_array.h:486-490) and share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h:121-160 (per-type global service slots, used on 1,222 lines). The allocator and ownership design has to cover the implicit CURRENT_CONTEXT binding, and the global-state design has to replace the generic static slot, which Rust cannot express as written.

**Size split check (verifier):** Reproduced with a python line count over `git ls-files -- src/*.{h,hpp,cpp,cc,c,ipp,inc,y,l}`: 6,646 files and 2,774,622 lines, exact. Data: 276,043 + 39,989 + 1,481 + 4,472 = 321,985 in 4 files, exact. Generated: the 21 listed files (errno, system_variable plus ob_system_variable_factory.*, grpc pb, pb-c, codec ob_generated_*) sum to 86,172 lines, exact. Their generator outputs were confirmed at gen_ob_sys_variables.py:1171-1197. A header scan for DO NOT EDIT / Generated by / auto-generated finds no other generated file of size; inner_table schema files are generated at build time and are not tracked. Vendored recounts to 70 files / 29,531 lines (zstd_src 47 / 25,154, oblib/easy 19 / 3,017, xxhash 2 / 1,214, murmur_hash 2 / 146), against the survey's 72 / 29,597, so hand-written is about 6,551 files / 2,336,934 lines. Borderline code that stays in 'hand-written': mysql_errno.h (1,044 lines, a MySQL errno list with no OceanBase header), tbsys-derived Time.h/Time.cpp/config.cpp (790), wyhash.h (113), and MySQL-derived charset/dtoa code (about 12K lines of logic in lib/charset under OceanBase headers). None of this moves the split by more than 1%. Grammar .y/.l = 24,585 lines in 6 files, confirmed; no .inc files are tracked in src.

**Units of work:** Idioms are cross-cutting, so there are two kinds of unit. (a) Rulebook entries, one per idiom family, written and stress-tested before any bulk translation. This census gives about 19 families: error convention, defensive checks and two-phase init, allocators and ownership, intrusive and lock-free structures, templates and specialization, macro frameworks (serialization / to_string / log / X-macro / registration), virtual hierarchies and downcasts, multiple inheritance, casts/unions/bitfields/overlays, memcpy serialization, goto, setjmp, exceptions, RTTI, thread-locals and globals, atomics, asm and SMART_CALL, SIMD, and static-init registration. (b) Translation units: a header plus its implementation/.ipp file, i.e. 3,824 basename groups (2,817 with 2 or more files, 1,007 single files) in 325 directories. The oblib foundation that fixes the idioms (lib/alloc, allocator, container, hash, list, queue, lock, atomic, utility, oblog, thread_local, thread, objectpool, function, signal, stat: 16 directories, about 91,767 lines) is a prerequisite group. It has to be designed, not translated, because every other group depends on its ownership and error types. Generated code (21 files) is best handled by porting the 5 generator scripts (gen_ob_sys_variables.py, the errno generator from ob_errno.def, generate_scalar_bitpakcing.py, generate_simd_bitpakcing.py, and protobuf regenerated from .proto) rather than translating their output. Vendored zstd/xxhash/libev (72 files) is replaced by crates.

**Public-surface tests:** Mostly n/a for idioms: C++ idioms are not visible at the SQL surface, and all 793 tracked .test files (283 in tools/deploy/mysql_test, 509 in tools/obtest) are SQL scripts that survive any translation language. There are no tracked C++ unit tests that import internals: unittest/ holds only 2 .gitignore files after 45cbbe9e1 deleted 678 unittest files. Two idiom families do couple tests to internals and must be kept by name in the Rust code. (1) DEBUG_SYNC hooks: 202 .test files (193 obtest, 9 mysql_test) set debug_sync points such as FORK_TABLE_BUILD_DATA (48 uses), TABLE_REDEFINITION_REPLICA_BUILD (24) and DELAY_INDEX_WRITE (11); about 127 distinct point names; these match 174 DEBUG_SYNC( call lines in 74 src files and the 414-line OB_DEBUG_SYNC_POINT_DEF table in src/share/ob_debug_sync_point.h. (2) errsim tracepoints: 12 .test files (4 mysql_test, 8 obtest; e.g. tools/obtest/t/errsim_storage/test_add_sstable_merge_leak.test, tools/deploy/mysql_test/test_suite/update/t/update2.test) set tracepoints by tp_no (e.g. 60, 61, 368) or tp_name, which bind to the numbering in src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_tracepoint_def.h (322 GLOBAL_ERRSIM_POINT_DEF lines).

**Hard parts:**
- Arena/allocator ownership: objects are placement-new'd into ObIAllocator memory and destroyed by explicit destructor calls, and ObString, ObDatum::ptr_ and raw char* members (469 fields) point into those arenas with no lifetime. Safe Rust needs lifetimes on nearly every struct or index handles. src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_iallocator.h, src/oblib/lib/allocator/ (8,320 lines), src/oblib/lib/alloc/ (8,436 lines)
- Fallible allocation: 4,975 lines return OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED and the server is expected to survive memory limits. Rust std collections abort on OOM and allocator_api is unstable, so the project needs its own fallible containers to replace ObSEArray (5,602 lines), ObArray (2,429) and ObHashMap/ObHashSet (697). src/oblib/lib/container/, src/oblib/lib/hash/
- Expression DAG and dispatch: ObExpr holds raw ObExpr** args_/parents_, a union of eval function pointers with serializable_function, a type-erased void** inner_functions_, and bitfield unions; function pointers are serialized as raw addresses. src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h:640-680, src/query/api/query/engine/ob_serializable_function.h
- Deep, data-carrying class hierarchies with downcasts: ObExprOperator (573 descendants, and itself an intrusive list node), ObDDL pipelines 6 levels deep, the JSON node tree 5 levels deep, 7,392 static_cast<T*> lines and 263 dynamic_cast lines. src/query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr_operator.h:304, src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_macro_block_write_task.h:114, src/oblib/common/json_type/ob_json_tree.h:753
- Intrusive and lock-free structures with manual memory reclamation: ObDLinkBase/ObDList, ObLink queues, hazard-version and QSync reclamation, the memtable ObKeyBtree. src/oblib/lib/list/ob_dlink_node.h, src/oblib/lib/queue/ob_link_queue.h, src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.cpp, src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_keybtree.cpp
- Atomics on plain fields: 2,950 ATOMIC_* calls operate on ordinary int64/pointer fields that may also be read non-atomically elsewhere. Every such field has to be found and retyped as Atomic*. src/oblib/lib/atomic/ob_atomic.h
- Self-referential and byte-overlay layouts: variable-length rows memcpy'd into this and then pointer-fixed, structs overlaid on buffers with reinterpret_cast, packed structs, zero-length trailing arrays. src/sql/engine/basic/ob_chunk_datum_store.cpp:93, src/sql/engine/basic/ob_chunk_row_store.cpp:93, src/oblib/common/number/ob_number_v2.h:2156, src/oblib/common/datum/ob_datum.h:131
- Macro frameworks that must become derive/proc macros or build scripts: the OB_UNIS versioned, length-prefixed serialization (byte-exact if on-disk compatibility matters), TO_STRING_KV/J_* printing, the logging modules, and X-macro tables included several times. src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_unify_serialize.h, src/oblib/lib/oblog/ob_log_module.h (598 function-like macros), src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_macro_utils.h (411), src/share/ob_debug_sync_point.h (414-line macro), src/oblib/lib/trace/ob_trace_def.h
- Template metaprogramming with no direct Rust equivalent: tuple-index registration in MDS, the trait-detection macros, and boost::geometry concept adaptation with exception-based errors. src/storage/multi_data_source/compile_utility/map_type_index_in_tuple.h, src/storage/multi_data_source/compile_utility/mds_register.h, src/oblib/common/meta_programming/ob_type_traits.h, src/share/geo/ob_geo_bin_traits.h, src/share/geo/ob_geo_dispatcher.h
- Static-initialization registration: function tables built by template recursion and filled before main, constructor attributes with init priorities (malloc hook, latch table), and a compile-time operator registry built by template specialization. src/storage/blocksstable/encoding/ob_dict_decoder.cpp:128-160, src/oblib/lib/alloc/malloc_hook.cpp:150, src/oblib/lib/stat/ob_latch_define.cpp:27, src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator_reg.h:68-95
- Stack switching and non-local exits: SMART_CALL moves execution onto a fresh stack with inline asm (1,138 call lines), and the bison/flex parsers bail out with longjmp from a static __thread jmp_buf. src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_smart_call.cpp, src/sql/parser/sql_parser_base.c:104-106, src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.l:1852
- SIMD kernels and a generated SIMD codec. The generator script, not its 12,705-line output, has to be ported. src/data_plane/api/data_plane/vector/ob_vector_l2_distance.h, src/sql/parser/ob_parse_simd.cpp, src/oblib/lib/codec/generate_simd_bitpakcing.py, src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_sse_to_neon.h
- Error-convention corner cases that `?` does not cover: record-first-error-and-continue (tmp_ret 928 lines, OB_TMP_FAIL 631), and error state kept in a member of bool comparators passed to std::sort. src/sql/engine/sort/ob_sort_compare_vec_op.ipp

**Pain instances:**
- 1e4f589b8 (2026-04-20): ObExpr::init_vector read uninitialized arena memory as discrete-vector pointers. The comment says Linux arenas are 'frequently zero by accident', while Windows returns garbage and crashes. The fix is a Windows-only MEMSET workaround and the offending operator is still unidentified (src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr.cpp:1056). Rust does not allow reading uninitialized memory as a pointer outside unsafe code.
- e66776e2d / 6da784ac1 (2026-08-19/20): coredumps in the plan cache (an object used after its last node reference was released) and the PS cache (ObPsStmtInfo outlives ObPsStmtItem and dereferenced a dangling ps_item_). src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp, src/sql/plan_cache/ob_ps_cache_callback.h. Arc/Weak or lifetimes would have made the dangling pointer impossible to write.
- 65997991e (2026-08-13): MdsFactory::deep_copy_buffer_ctx compared a tuple index with a binding type id and returned OB_SUCCESS without allocating, leaving copied_ctx == nullptr and causing a SIGSEGV in log replay. The error code and the out-pointer result could disagree. A Result<Box<..>> return cannot express success-with-null.
- 40fcfa901 (2026-05-18): worker use-after-free plus a missing lock in th_worker (src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp and ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp, the latter under deps/oblib at the time). A Rust Mutex<T> ties the data to its lock.
- 6bd81a07b (2026-02-03): rpc mempool use-after-free (ob_local_procedure_call.h, under deps/oblib at the time).
- 5aa611c00 (2026-04-09): sort comparators keep an error code in a member (`int &ret = ret_`). Once an error was set they returned false for every pair, breaking strict weak ordering, so introsort's scan ran past the array bounds and aborted on macOS (src/sql/engine/sort/ob_sort_compare_vec_op.ipp). Rust's sort does not read out of bounds when given an inconsistent comparator.
- 54123f13e (2026-06-10): the kvcache hazard-version retire condition matched every node (tenant_id 0 == 0) and freed nodes that concurrent readers still held, a use-after-free (src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.cpp). Rust alone would not catch a logic error inside hand-written unsafe reclamation; it would only help if this is replaced with a vetted epoch crate.
- Defensive code Rust types would remove: 17,805 OB_ISNULL checks that raise OB_ERR_UNEXPECTED (pointer invariants), 4,718 OB_NOT_INIT lines guarding 1,664 two-phase init() methods, and 1,019 OB_INIT_TWICE lines. Examples: src/data_plane/api/data_plane/meta/ob_fixed_meta_obj_array.h:127 and :256, src/data_plane/api/data_plane/scheduler/ob_dag_scheduler.h:1463.

**Open questions:**
- Must the OB_UNIS byte format (slog, clog, tablet meta, sstable meta) stay readable from existing data directories? If yes, the serialization derive has to be byte-exact, including the version and fixed-width length prefix. If no, a new format is possible.
- Will the translation rulebook allow unsafe raw pointers into arenas (structure-preserving, unsafe-heavy), or does it require safe Rust (lifetimes or index handles, i.e. a redesign)? The census cannot settle this; it is a policy call.
- What should happen on out-of-memory in Rust? Options include a custom fallible container set, allocator-api2, or accepting abort, which would change behavior against the 4,975 OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED paths.
- Which fields touched by ATOMIC_* are also read or written non-atomically? Retyping them needs a clang-based whole-program analysis; grep cannot tell a field access from a local.
- Exact counts of virtual methods, class-template instantiations and C-style casts, and how much of the code is compiled at all under the seekdb build (Bazel/CMake, per platform: NEON versus x86, _WIN32 branches). This needs a compile database and an AST pass, which the read-only, no-build rules ruled out.
- Do the PL catch(...)/rethrow blocks in src/pl/ob_pl.cpp (5 sites) and the Windows SEH path noted in src/sql/pl/ob_pl.h:1014 depend on unwinding across C frames? This decides how PL error propagation maps to Rust panics or Results.
- Is raw-address serialization of ObExpr function pointers (ob_serializable_function.h) still used anywhere that crosses process boundaries in single-node seekdb (plan cache persistence, PX)? If not, it may be removable instead of translated.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The per-tenant MTL accessor was replaced by a process-wide typed service slot, and the survey does not count it. server_service<T>() / bind_server_service<T>() appear on 1,222 lines in 382 files, more than GCTX (858) and GCONF (679) together. get_instance() singletons add 713 lines in 316 files. ObServerServiceSlot<Service>::service_ is a generic static data member (an inline static template member). Rust has no generic statics, so this needs a TypeId-keyed registry or an explicit context struct passed through calls. The slots are also bound and unbound at runtime as raw pointers, which is a lifetime hazard. (evidence: src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h:121-160 (ObServerServiceSlot, bind/unbind/server_service, borrow_server_object); /tmp/gg.sh '\bserver_service\s*<' = 1,222 lines / 382 files; '\bget_instance\s*\(\s*\)' = 713 / 316; the 65997991e unittest binds `reinterpret_cast<ObMemstoreFreezer*>(this)` into a slot.)
- Error codes are used as values, not just propagated, so `?` alone is not the mapping. There are 4,189 lines comparing ret with a specific code (`OB_X == ret`), 2,497 lines with `!=`, and 2,905 standalone `ret = OB_SUCCESS;` resets that swallow errors. 17,767 lines use `OB_SUCC(ret) &&` as a loop guard, after which code in the function keeps running. The Rust error type therefore has to carry the exact OB code (codes are also returned to MySQL clients from ob_errno.def), and the rulebook needs entries for matching and swallowing codes and for loop-then-cleanup. This goes beyond the tmp_ret/comparator cases the survey lists. (evidence: /tmp/gg.sh '\bOB_\w+\s*==\s*ret\b|\bret\s*==\s*OB_(?!SUCCESS)\w+' = 4,189 / 1,028 files; the != variant 2,497 / 772; '^\s*ret\s*=\s*(common::)?OB_SUCCESS\s*;' = 2,905 / 846; '\bOB_SUCC\s*\(\s*ret\s*\)\s*&&' = 17,767 / 1,727.)
- The brief asked for casts, and the survey reports only subsets. const_cast appears on 1,733 lines in 512 files. Mutating through a const view is the pattern that collides most directly with Rust's aliasing rules and needs Cell/RefCell/UnsafeCell decisions. reinterpret_cast totals 3,824 lines in 768 files; the survey reports only the 103 buffer-overlay lines. C-style casts to primitive pointer types are about 1,978 lines and to Ob* class pointers about 299. About 1,193 of the 7,392 'downcast' static_cast lines are really char*/void*/int* buffer casts. (evidence: /tmp/gg.sh '\bconst_cast\s*<' = 1,733 / 512; '\breinterpret_cast\s*<' = 3,824 / 768; '\(\s*(const\s+)?(unsigned\s+)?(char|void|u?int(8|16|32|64)_t|...)\s*\*+\s*\)\s*[\w(&*]' = 1,978 / 382; the static_cast primitive-target split = 1,193 lines.)
- The survey does not treat manual reference counting and handle types as an idiom family, although they drive the lifetime bugs it cites (e66776e2d: plan-cache object used after its last reference was released). inc_ref/dec_ref-style calls appear on 441 lines in 127 files, with 130 `class *Handle` definitions and 593 `Ob*Handle x;` members or locals. ref_cnt_ and ref_count_ are the most common ATOMIC_* targets (98 and 34 uses). Many of these release objects back to a pool or allocator (op_alloc/op_free/OB_NEWx: 642 lines), so a plain Arc does not fit, and the rulebook needs an Arc-with-custom-drop or handle rule. (evidence: /tmp/gg.sh '\b(inc_ref|dec_ref)\w*\s*\(' = 441 / 127; 'class \w+Handle\b' = 130; '\bOb\w*Handle\s+\w+;' = 593 / 230; the ATOMIC_* argument-name histogram (ref_cnt_ 98, ref_count_ 34); git show --stat e66776e2d (ob_plan_cache.cpp, ob_ps_cache_callback.h).)
- Allocation is often tied implicitly to a thread-local memory context, not to an allocator passed as an argument. ObArray with auto_free binds to CURRENT_CONTEXT (lib::Flow::current_ctx()) on its first allocation. WITH_CONTEXT and CREATE_WITH_TEMP_CONTEXT are scope macros built from for-loops that also read the enclosing `ret`. 547 lines in 227 files use the context macros explicitly, and every default-constructed ObArray uses the context implicitly. A Rust design with arenas and lifetimes must decide how a container learns its arena when no allocator parameter exists. lib/rc (1,005 lines) is missing from the survey's list of 16 foundation directories. (evidence: src/oblib/lib/rc/context.h:46-67 (CURRENT_CONTEXT, WITH_CONTEXT_P → CONTEXT_P for-loop using OB_SUCC(ret)); src/oblib/lib/container/ob_array.h:484-492 (mem_context_ = CURRENT_CONTEXT on first alloc); /tmp/gg.sh '\b(CURRENT_CONTEXT|WITH_CONTEXT|CREATE_WITH_TEMP_CONTEXT|MemoryContext|ROOT_CONTEXT|DESTROY_CONTEXT)\b' = 547 / 227.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's numbers can mostly be trusted as orders of magnitude. I independently reproduced the file and line totals, the data and generated splits, the error-convention counts, the allocator, virtual, cast, template, atomic, thread-local, SIMD and goto counts, and the exact inheritance-depth distribution. Most matched to the line, and the rest were within regex noise of about 5-30%. The weak spots are interpretation and staleness, not arithmetic. The ObDLinkBase '739 classes' figure merges unrelated CRTP instantiations, and most of it comes from a link on ObExprOperator that the code never uses. The 7,392 'downcasts' include about 1,200 buffer casts. The ob_array.h typeid example is dead code. Two cited locations no longer exist at HEAD: ob_sort_compare_vec_op.ipp and the ob_expr.cpp:1056 workaround, both removed by cbcea2b91, and src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp is gone too. That shows how fast this tree changes. Some Rust-side claims are too strong: stable Rust does have try_reserve, the compiler would catch non-atomic accesses once fields are retyped, and Rust's sort can panic on a bad comparator. The survey also misses several idiom families that matter for the rulebook: the server_service<T> slots, error codes used as values, const_cast, manual refcounting and handles, and implicit thread-local memory contexts. Use the survey for sizing and for its overall structure verdict, which I agree with. Do not use its specific examples or the redesign-versus-rule split without the corrections above.


## AREA: tests-judge
**What it is:** This area covers the repo's test suites and the CI that runs them, i.e. the tool that could judge old and new code through the public surface. It also lists the contract a new implementation must reproduce. The only suite CI runs is 272 mysqltest cases, driven by .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py against a real seekdb process over the MySQL protocol. Everything else is either a legacy cluster harness (tools/obtest) that CI never runs, or a handful of C++/Rust unit tests.

**Size split:** Hand-written test inputs:
- tools/deploy/mysql_test: 283 .test files (95,255 lines) and 46 .inc files (2,924 lines).
- tools/obtest: 509 .test files (48,015 lines) and .inc files (3,215 lines).
- Harness and CI: .github holds 23 files (3,263 lines), including mysqltest_for_seekdb.py (696 lines) and sdb.py (560 lines).
- obtest scripts: 61 text files, 3,625 lines.
- tools/deploy/mysql_test/psmalltest.py: a 2,806-line list of case names.

Recorded expected output (produced by running the C++ server):
- mysql_test: 305 .result files, 933,203 lines. Four arithmetic matrices (datatype/div, t/add, datatype/minus, expr/mul) make up 770,711 of those lines.
- obtest: 555 .result files, 64,317 lines.

Data:
- tools/obtest/sql: 49 files, 193,824 lines.
- test_suite/geometry/t/default_srs_data_mysql.sql.

Vendored binaries:
- tools/obtest/jar/mytest.jar: 92,847,851 bytes.
- tools/obtest/lib/libmysqlclient.so.18: 8,159,644 bytes.
- tools/mysql_tzinfo_to_sql: 5,095,239 bytes.
- obtest/obsqlclient/obsqltest: 131 KB.
- obtest/tools/iof and libhook.so.1: about 24 KB each.
- The mysqltest/obclient client itself is fetched as an obclient 2.2.x package listed in deps/init/*.deps. It is not in the repo.

Tests of C++ internals: 3 gtest files, 313 lines. Rust tests: 3 #[test] in rust/sql-nio.

Architecture checkers that depend on C++ layout: tools/module_check, about 4,260 lines of .py plus module_layers.conf; bazel/probes has 4 .cpp probes (113 lines).

Commands used:
- `git ls-files '<glob>' | while read f; do wc -l < $R/$f; done | awk '{s+=$1}'`
- `git diff --numstat $(git hash-object -t tree /dev/null) HEAD -- tools` (the '-' rows mark binaries)
- `stat -f%z` for byte sizes

**Summary:** 1. The only judge CI actually runs is 272 mysqltest cases: all 93 top-level t/ cases plus 179 cases from 27 of the 31 test_suite directories. They run in 4 slices, and each case goes through obclient's `mysqltest` against a freshly started seekdb binary. The harness depends on no C++ code, so it can judge a Rust server unchanged, provided the new server keeps the same process contract: `--base-dir/--port` flags, run/seekdb.pid pointing to the same executable, readiness via `select 1`, and acceptance of tools/deploy/init.sql and init_user.sql.

2. The corpus pins much more than MySQL semantics:
   - EXPLAIN text: 820 plan tables with EST.ROWS/EST.TIME cost numbers in 42 result files, 40 of them in the CI set.
   - OB internal tables: 39 distinct __all_* names; fork_table cases poll __all_ddl_task_status for task type 10009.
   - OB error numbers: 23 of the 126 distinct codes are in OB's own range (>=4000).
   - OB-specific SHOW CREATE TABLE options.
   - Tracepoint IDs: CI init switches on 10 numbered and 2 named tracepoints through `alter system set_tp`, plus hidden `_` parameters.

3. 153 of the 283 cases touch at least one of those internals, or use sleep or ALTER SYSTEM. 130 cases (48,301 lines) are plain SQL.

4. Tests that import C++ internals barely exist, so almost nothing dies with a language change:
   - 3 gtest files (313 lines); two of them are wired into no build file.
   - unittest/ holds only .gitignore files, although the Bazel and CMake rules expect an internal unittest/<module> tree.

5. The public corpus is a small slice of what upstream runs, and it has been shrinking. tools/deploy/mysql_test/psmalltest.py lists 2,784 cases, and 2,535 of them are not in the repo (267 optimizer, 178 PL, 34 JSON). Commit 706253423 alone deleted 201 cases, including all PL tests, even though its message is about packaging.

6. Coverage gaps are large:
   - About 50k lines of JSON source are exercised by only 11 json_* calls; XML (16,200 lines) and hybrid search (6,060 lines) have 0 tests.
   - src/pl (42,811 lines) has 9 procedure test files.
   - No CI case uses the binary prepared-statement protocol or TLS, or restarts the server. So redo replay/recovery and standby (src/standby, 14,899 lines) are never exercised by CI; standby lives only in 9 obtest cases.

7. tools/obtest (509 cases) is not wired into CI and needs internal infrastructure:
   - a 92.8 MB prebuilt Java harness;
   - x86-64 ELF helpers;
   - multi-host configuration and a download from an internal IP.
   Even so, 152 of its cases define only single-server topologies and it copies seekdb in as bin/observer, so part of it could in principle run against single-node seekdb.

8. On-disk compatibility is not something today's C++ offers across versions: the server refuses any persisted data version other than its own (1.4.0.0 since 91bb9a6cf). The embedded C API lives in a separate repo (seekdb-bindings/lib/include/seekdb.h) and reaches the server only through a process contract: the `--embedded` flag, the run/sql.sock MySQL-protocol socket and a flock on run/seekdb.clients.

9. There is no wasm/browser build at this HEAD.

10. Differences to expect by design:
   - Plan text and cost numbers, which were re-recorded in 24 commits over the past year.
   - `rowset=16` batch-size text.
   - 70 cases that use sleep.
   - Platform-dependent ordering (6628b4ec0).
   - The runner itself retries each failing case 3 times and ignores trailing whitespace.

**Structure call (surveyor):** The judge itself needs no translation. The Python harness and the obclient mysqltest client are independent of the server's language and can run the same 272 cases against a C++ or a Rust binary. What forces the call is what the corpus pins. Only 130 of 283 cases are plain SQL. The rest depend on:
- byte-exact EXPLAIN text with cost-model floats;
- OB internal catalog tables polled by include files;
- OB-range error numbers;
- numbered tracepoints and hidden parameters set at CI init.

A structure-preserving translation keeps all of that working: the same errno.def, inner-table definitions, sysvar JSON, parameter seed, tracepoint numbers, plan printer and cost model. A redesign of the optimizer, catalog or error layer would invalidate up to 153 cases (40 CI cases with plan text alone), and the judge would have to be rewritten before it could judge anything. Four generators (gen_errno.pl, gen_ob_sys_variables.py, generate_inner_table_schema.py, syspack_codegen.py) already produce the contract data from declarative sources, so pointing them at Rust output is cheap.

Files that force the call: tools/deploy/init_user.sql, src/sql/monitor/ob_sql_plan.cpp with src/sql/optimizer/ob_opt_est_cost_model.cpp (pinned by the 42 plan-bearing .result files, e.g. test_suite/executor/r/mysql/basic.result), test_suite/fork_table/include/wait_fork_table.inc, src/share/ob_errno.def, src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py and src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_tracepoint_def.h.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the main call. The judge itself is independent of language: mysqltest_for_seekdb.py and sdb.py import nothing from C++, and obclient's mysqltest is an external binary. Keeping the contract data sources is cheap and correct, because they are already declarative and turned into code by generators: src/share/ob_errno.def with gen_errno.pl, src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py, ob_system_variable_init.json and ob_parameter_seed.ipp. So is keeping the plan printer and the cost model (src/sql/monitor/ob_sql_plan.cpp and src/sql/optimizer/ob_opt_est_cost_model.cpp, pinned by 40 CI result files).

Two corrections to the reasoning.

(1) 'Up to 153 cases invalidated by a redesign' is an upper bound from a broad grep. That grep includes 70 sleep-only cases, and those do not depend on internal structure. The cases that do depend on redesign are narrower: 40 with plan text, 40 that read __all_*/__tenant_ tables, and 47 that use ALTER SYSTEM. These overlap.

(2) The survey misses the strongest argument for structure-preserving, in-place work: the judge's entry gate. Before any case runs, tools/deploy/init.sql (not init_user.sql) must run with no errors. It creates a PL procedure with a CONTINUE HANDLER, GET DIAGNOSTICS and PREPARE/EXECUTE. It issues 12 'alter system set_tp' statements and sets 5 hidden parameters. init_user.sql then runs ANALYZE TABLE on OCEANBASE.__ALL_VIRTUAL_CORE_ALL_TABLE. execute_init_sql() raises on any nonzero exit, which fails the whole slice. So a clean-room Rust server gets zero signal from this judge until it has PL, the set_tp syntax, the hidden parameters and those virtual tables. A module-by-module replacement inside the running process, as rust/sql-nio already does behind rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h, can be judged from the first day.

Small overstatement in hard_parts: the 22 test_suite/inner_table result files are orphans with no .test. The CI never runs them, so they are a useful spec but not part of the gate.

**Size split check (verifier):** Most of the split reproduces exactly:
- mysql_test: 283 .test (95,255 lines), 46 .inc (2,924), 305 .result (933,203). The four arithmetic results sum to exactly 770,711 (div 198,622, add 192,731, minus 191,019, mul 188,339).
- obtest: 509 .test (48,015), 97 .inc (3,215), 555 .result (64,317); sql/ has 49 files and 193,824 lines.
- .github: 23 files, 3,263 lines; mysqltest_for_seekdb.py is 696 lines and sdb.py 560.
- psmalltest.py: 2,806 lines.
- Binary sizes: mytest.jar 92,847,851; libmysqlclient.so.18 8,159,644; mysql_tzinfo_to_sql 5,095,239; obsqltest 131,273; iof 24,703; libhook.so.1 24,445.
- gtest: 313 lines. module_check .py: 4,260 lines. bazel/probes: 4 .cpp files, 113 lines.

The one figure I could not match is '61 text files, 3,625 lines' of obtest scripts. A looser filter gives 98 text files, so that number depends on the filter. It is immaterial.

The split leaves out one fact that changes how to read it: 429 of the mysql_test cases date from the initial import, and the tracked corpus has shrunk to 283 since then. In the result bytes, 83% of the lines are the four arithmetic matrices. So the 'recorded expected output' mass mostly tests number formatting, not broad features.

**Units of work:** For this area the unit is a mysqltest case group used as an acceptance gate, not a translated file. There are 32 groups: t/ plus 31 test_suite directories, holding 283 cases, 272 of them in CI. Supporting work:

1. The harness: 2 Python files, 1,256 lines (mysqltest_for_seekdb.py and sdb.py), reused as is. It needs at most a change to the pid-file/argv check, and only if the Rust binary's process layout differs.
2. Contract generators to point at Rust output: 4 of them (src/share/gen_errno.pl, src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py, src/share/inner_table/generate_inner_table_schema.py, src/share/inner_table/sys_package/syspack_codegen.py). Their declarative inputs are ob_errno.def, ob_system_variable_init.json, ob_inner_table_schema_def.py and 26 sys_package .sql files.
3. Hand-written contract files to port with care: ob_command_line_parser.cpp (17 flags), ob_parameter_seed.ipp (289 parameters), ob_tracepoint_def.h (322 points) and ob_sql_plan.cpp.
4. Test rewrite backlog, only if internals are redesigned: up to 153 cases.
5. Internal tests to drop or rewrite: 3 gtest files, the module_check scripts and 4 Bazel probes.
6. Optional second judge: 152 single-server obtest cases, which would need their harness moved off mytest.jar before they could gate anything.

**Public-surface tests:** Suites that survive a language change: they are SQL over the MySQL protocol, run by obclient mysqltest.

Case counts (the 283 tracked cases, of which 272 run in CI):
- t/ (top level): 93 cases, all in CI. Examples: t/join_basic.test (69 lines, no internal references); t/add.test (1,747 lines, 192,731-line result).
- geometry: 42 cases, 39 in CI, e.g. geometry/t/st_distance_mysql.test (2,483 lines, no internal references).
- vector_index: 22 cases, 21 in CI.
- fork_table: 18 cases, 16 in CI.
- fts_index: 15 cases.
- subquery: 11 cases.
- expr: 11 cases.
- type_date: 10 cases.
- global_index: 7 cases.
- datatype: 6 cases.
- ai_function and trx: 5 cases each.
- delete and histogram: 4 cases each.
- executor, groupby, storage and update: 3 cases each.
- array, table_redefinition, window_function and yonyou_test: 2 cases each.
- 1 case each: config_test, ddl, deadlock_detector, foreign_key, hierarchical_query, information_schema, sys_vars, system_variable, view.
- inner_table: 22 .result files with no .test.

Split by portability (C7): 130 cases are plain SQL. 153 depend on OB internals (plan text, __all_* tables, ALTER SYSTEM, freeze, sleep). Examples of the second kind:
- test_suite/executor/t/basic.test (--explain_protocol 2, 152 pinned plans);
- test_suite/fork_table/t/*.test (poll __all_ddl_task_status through wait_fork_table.inc);
- the test_suite/inner_table result files.

Other public-surface tests:
- tools/obtest: 509 SQL-level cases. They need the mytest.jar cluster harness and CI does not run them; 152 are single-server only. Examples: tools/obtest/t/stanby/basic.test (two cluster=1 instances, SWITCHOVER/FAILOVER) and tools/obtest/t/quick/1628_c33332_sync_schema_version.test (multi-zone).
- tools/ob_error/test/test.sh: a CLI output comparison against expect_result.result.
- Outside this repo: seekdb-bindings/lib/tests (4 .cc tests and a Python test) drive the embedded API against the server binary.

Tests that die with the language change:
- deps/oblib/unittest/lib/alloc/test_jemalloc_hook.cpp
- deps/oblib/unittest/lib/allocator/test_malloc_backend.cpp
- tools/ob_error/test/test_ob_error.cpp
- tools/module_check/unittest_module_check_test.py and the roughly 4,260 lines of module_check scripts, plus the 4 bazel/probes/*.cpp probes, which enforce C++ header and module layering.

**Hard parts:**
- Reproducing EXPLAIN output byte for byte, including EST.ROWS and EST.TIME(us) floats: any change to the order of floating-point operations changes the text. Involves src/sql/monitor/ob_sql_plan.cpp and src/sql/optimizer/ob_opt_est_cost_model.cpp (2,330 lines); 820 plan tables in 42 result files.
- Matching numeric formatting across the type matrix: datatype/div, t/add, datatype/minus and expr/mul pin 770,711 result lines of arithmetic output. Involves src/oblib/common/number/ob_number_v2.cpp and the C double-to-string code declared in src/oblib/lib/charset/ob_dtoa.h.
- Keeping a numbered, SQL-reachable tracepoint registry: CI init runs `alter system set_tp tp_no=...` against a registry built from macro-defined globals (322 GLOBAL_ERRSIM_POINT_DEF in src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_tracepoint_def.h, 98 ERRSIM_POINT_DEF sites, 111 files under #ifdef ERRSIM).
- Reproducing internal catalog contents that tests poll, e.g. __all_ddl_task_status with task type 10009 in test_suite/fork_table/include/wait_fork_table.inc (sourced 87 times), __all_table (375 references), and 22 inner_table result files pinning __all_virtual_* rows. Source: src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py.
- Keeping the error catalog: 1,546 entries in src/share/ob_errno.def with OB-range numbers returned to clients. It is generated by src/share/gen_errno.pl, so retargeting is mechanical, but every message text is pinned (991 distinct ERROR lines).
- Data compatibility, if the product wants it: formats defined by 909 OB_UNIS_VERSION and 654 OB_SERIALIZE_MEMBER macro sites, plus the SQLite meta store at store/sstable/meta.db (src/share/storage/ob_sqlite_connection.cpp). Today's code does not read other data versions (src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:50-68).
- The judge relies on OB's mysqltest fork (explain_protocol, replace_numeric_round, enable_sorted_result), shipped as a prebuilt obclient 2.2.x package (deps/init/*.deps). No source is in the repo, and no package is listed for Windows or older macOS.
- The embedded-mode lifetime contract: shared/exclusive flock on run/seekdb.clients, LockFileEx on Windows, and exit through _Exit(0) (src/observer/ob_server.cpp:1635-1680). It is simple, but the flock semantics must match across platforms.

**Pain instances:**
- 6628b4ec0 (src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp:2467): `rank = -NAN` inside an ob_sort comparator made the ordering invalid, and plan text differed between macOS and Linux. In Rust, f64 has no built-in total order, so a sort has to pick total_cmp or handle NaN explicitly, and std sort_by is stable. That removes this class of bug.
- e66776e2d (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp:1059-1105, src/sql/plan_cache/ob_ps_cache_callback.h, src/observer/virtual_table/ob_table_columns.cpp:603): the plan cache read an object after unlocking the node while eviction could free it, a PS-cache callback dereferenced a destroyed item, and databuff_printf was called with the wrong buffer length. An internal sanity stress run found these, not the mysqltest judge. Borrow-checked lock guards and bounds-checked slices would have rejected or trapped them.
- 40fcfa901 (src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp:1148, ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp:151): a worker was added to an intrusive list without holding workers_lock_, and could be reaped before it started (use-after-free). Rust's Mutex-owned data and Send/Sync checks rule out the unlocked list mutation. The fix added no public-surface test.
- Crash fixes land without regression cases in the judge: 33bec38dc (src/rootserver/ob_ddl_service.cpp), dd899675c (src/share/schema/ob_multi_version_schema_service.cpp), e66776e2d and 40fcfa901 changed only src files. For memory errors, the public judge is not what currently catches them.

**Open questions:**
- Do all 272 CI cases pass at HEAD 076eb309b, and how often do the 3-retry and trailing-whitespace allowances fire? That flake rate is in CI logs on the internal NFS, which I could not reach.
- Where do the 2,535 psmall cases missing from the repo live (optimizer, PL, JSON, window functions), and could they be published or shared to widen the judge? Similarly, where are the internal 'Sanity Lite' and farm jobs (9787855fc, e66776e2d)?
- Why did commit 706253423 (a packaging commit) delete 201 cases, including every PL test, while src/pl (42,811 lines) remains? Were the cases failing, moved to the internal farm, or were the features dropped?
- Do the tracepoints and hidden parameters in tools/deploy/init_user.sql (random PX shuffle, forced new sort, simulated DAS group size) change any pinned result text? If they do, a Rust server must reproduce them; if not, they can be dropped from the judge's setup.
- Does anyone run tools/obtest (mytest.jar) against seekdb today? The stanby cases were edited in 2026-08. On what infrastructure, and could the single-server subset move to the Python harness?
- Is reading data files written by released seekdb 1.4 a product requirement? The C++ code itself refuses any other data version, so compatibility is a decision to make, not a constraint that already exists.
- Is the source of OB's mysqltest fork (obclient 2.2.x, with explain_protocol and replace_numeric_round) available, so the judge can run on platforms without a published package (Windows, older macOS)?
- Does the seekdb-bindings test suite, the only tests of the embedded C API, run in any CI? That repo is outside this worktree.
- How much of the planned rewrite would keep the current cost model? That decides whether the 40 plan-pinning CI cases stay valid or must be re-recorded or masked.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The judge has a hard entry gate that the survey does not name. Every slice first runs tools/deploy/init.sql and init_user.sql, and a single failed statement aborts the slice. That makes PL stored procedures, 'alter system set_tp', hidden '_' parameters, recyclebin and OB virtual tables preconditions for getting any pass/fail signal at all, not just features that a few cases test. (evidence: tools/deploy/init.sql: 'create procedure exec_sql' with 'declare continue handler for sqlexception', GET DIAGNOSTICS, prepare/execute; 12 'alter system set_tp' lines; 'set global recyclebin = on' (18 result files mention recyclebin). tools/deploy/init_user.sql: 'ANALYZE TABLE OCEANBASE.__ALL_VIRTUAL_CORE_ALL_TABLE'. mysqltest_for_seekdb.py execute_init_sql() raises RunnerError on a nonzero obclient exit, and command_run() turns that into a slice error. No test calls exec_sql (git grep finds 0), so the procedure exists only to be created.)
- Cases do not run on fresh instances. Each slice starts one server and runs its cases one after another against it, picked round-robin (all_cases[slice_index::slice_count]). A restart happens only for a retry or after a failure. A case's result can therefore depend on state left by earlier cases in the same slice, and on the slice count (MYSQLTEST_SLICES=4). For comparing old and new code, the case order and slice count must be fixed. A case that passes alone may still fail in sequence. (evidence: .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py command_run(): prepare_instance() is called once before the loop, and again only inside the retry branch and after a failed case. .github/workflows/seekdb.yml sets MYSQLTEST_SLICES: "4". This contradicts summary item 1 ('each case ... against a freshly started seekdb binary').)
- The CI judge runs only on OceanBase-internal infrastructure, only on Linux, and it is new. A migration effort outside that infrastructure has to host the judge itself. macOS result differences are found outside CI. (evidence: seekdb.yml: every job has the condition github.repository_owner == 'oceanbase', runs-on arc-runner-k8s-ci-org, uses the container harbor.oceanbase-dev.com/obrde-test/ob-actions-runner-worker, and passes data through NFS at /data/storage/jenkins_on_k8s. sdb.py spawn_detached() raises 'Windows is not supported yet'. The Python judge and mysqltest_config.yaml arrived in b4c1b0655 (2026-08-28, 'ci: enable OBD-free seekdb mysqltest on master'). 6628b4ec0 fixed a macOS-only plan-order difference. tools/deploy/obd.sh is a second, OBD-based way to run mysqltest locally.)
- The 'single-server obtest subset' the survey offers as a possible second judge is mostly legacy OceanBase and is much smaller than 152. Of the 152 cases that declare only cluster=1, 54 use CREATE RESOURCE UNIT/POOL/TENANT, which the seekdb MySQL grammar no longer accepts, and 80 start more than one OBI instance (for example primary plus standby). At most about 90 cases are single-instance and use no tenant DDL. (evidence: A per-file loop over tools/obtest/*.test (the set of cluster= values equals 'cluster=1' → 152; of those, grep -i 'create (resource unit|resource pool|tenant)' matches 54, and 80 have more than one 'OBI('). src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.y:15616 says 'TENANT is intentionally no longer a global keyword in seekdb', and the grammar has no RESOURCE UNIT rule. tools/obtest/t/primary_standby/14000_c111_p1_delete_server.test uses cluster=1:1:1, CREATE RESOURCE UNIT and CREATE TENANT.)
- There is no performance judge in the repo, and the browser build lives on a branch. The mysqltest judge checks only result text. The only benchmark workflow in the tree just posts a report produced by a 'VLDB CI' workflow that is not in the repo. Separately, a WebAssembly build with its own public surface (a SQL shell with OPFS storage) is being actively developed on feature/webassembly-shell, so a Rust rewrite may have to cover a wasm target that this HEAD does not show. (evidence: .github/workflows/vldb-benchmark-comment.yml triggers on workflow_run of workflows: [VLDB CI] and downloads the artifact 'vldb-fts-large-benchmark'. No workflow named 'VLDB CI' is tracked (ls .github/workflows). git for-each-ref --contains cd41da17b shows feature/webassembly-shell and origin/feature/webassembly-shell; 20d9ceb43 is 'Add WebAssembly SQL shell with OPFS storage', and cd41da17b (2026-09-22) is 'Terminate discarded WebAssembly instances and fix Safari Worker hangs'. Neither is an ancestor of HEAD.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's numbers can be trusted. I recounted about 60 of them with my own commands, and nearly all match exactly. These include the test, result and include counts, the 272-case CI set and the 11 cases it leaves out, the 820 plan tables in 42 files, the error and ERROR-line counts, the catalog, sysvar and parameter counts, the psmall gap, the 201 deletions in 706253423, and the binary sizes. The rest differ by 1-5% because of how the grep was written.

Its reading of the code is less reliable, and several factual framings are wrong:
- The tracepoints, hidden parameters and PL procedure are in init.sql, not init_user.sql. The survey repeats this wrong file in five places.
- Cases share one server instance per slice; they do not each get a freshly started server.
- config_test does not restart the server.
- '603 added' mostly counts the initial import of 429 cases.
- There are 8 obtest stanby cases, not 9.
- The claim that no wasm build exists ignores an active branch.
- The idea that 152 obtest cases could judge seekdb ignores that a third of them use tenant DDL the grammar no longer accepts.

It also underplays the entry gate in init.sql, which is the fact that matters most for how to structure the migration. Use the survey's inventory and counts as they are. Treat its judgments about what survives a rewrite and what could serve as a second judge as a starting point, and correct them with the points above.


## AREA: build-platforms
**What it is:** The CMake and Bazel build graph, the code generators that run at configure/build time or whose outputs are checked in, the per-platform dependency sets and platform-specific C++ (Linux el7/8/9/al8 x86_64+aarch64, macOS arm64, Windows x64, Android arm64, and WebAssembly on a side branch), the Rust integration (rust/sql-nio, deps/external jemalloc crate), and the CI that verifies the result. Production builds use CMake in Unity mode, but CMake reads its source lists and Unity groups from Bazel .bzl inventories; GitHub CI builds only Linux and runs mysqltest against the binary.

**Size split:** Tracked .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.y/.l: 2,777,368 lines (2,774,622 under src/, 2,746 elsewhere: bazel/probes, deps/oblib, include/, tools/ob_error, tools/codestyle). Commands: `git ls-files -z -- '*.h' '*.hpp' '*.cpp' '*.cc' '*.c' '*.ipp' '*.y' '*.l' | xargs -0 cat | wc -l`, the same with 'src/*' and ':!src'.
- Checked-in generated C/C++: 87,220 lines. errno 18,824 (src/share/ob_errno.cpp, src/share/ob_errno.h, src/oblib/lib/ob_errno.h, generated by src/share/gen_errno.pl from ob_errno.def); system variables 29,946 (8 files generated by src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py from a 10,979-line JSON file, including src/sql/session/ob_system_variable_factory.{h,cpp}); bit-packing 24,406 (src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_*); grpc/protobuf/protobuf-c stubs 12,996 (src/oblib/grpc/*.pb.*, src/share/geo/ob_vector_tile.pb-c.*); split compile parts for comparison/datum functions 1,048.
- Data tables: 329,166 lines in 5 files where at least 1,500 lines are literal-only (heuristic scan): ob_ik_dic.cpp 276,044, ob_timezone_info.cpp 39,990, ob_ctype_uca.cc 7,130, ob_ctype_utf8_tab.h 4,473, ob_expr_to_pinyin_tab.cpp 1,529.
- Vendored third-party: 30,317 lines. zstd 1.3.8 is 25,827 (src/oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8); xxhash/wyhash/murmur is 1,473; libeasy remnants in src/oblib/easy are 3,017 lines in 19 files (libeasy came from outside the project but is maintained here, so it is borderline).
- Grammar DSL (.y/.l): 24,585.
- Hand-written C/C++ (the remainder): about 2,306,080.
- Generated at build time and not tracked (measured read-only in the main checkout /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/seekdb): bison/flex output is 174,926 lines (sql_parser_mysql_mode_tab.c 81,297, _lex.c 75,028, PL tab.c 8,409 + lex.c 3,902, plus FTS and type_name.c); inner-table schema is 103,354 lines in 51 files under build_release/generated; syspack_source.cpp is 62,827 bytes.
- Build system: Bazel is 93 files (.bzl 27,468 + BUILD.bazel 10,200 = 37,668 lines). CMake is 24 CMakeLists.txt plus cmake/*.cmake at 1,507 lines. Also build.sh 529, build.ps1 450, bazel.py 670, tools/cmake/emit_bazel_source_inventory.py 431, and 3,378 lines in .github (workflows plus scripts).
- Rust: rust/sql-nio has 8,448 lines of .rs; deps/external/src/lib.rs is an empty anchor.

**Summary:** Production seekdb is built by CMake in Unity mode (RelWithDebInfo only, via build.sh and build.ps1). Its source lists and the exact Unity groups come from Bazel .bzl inventories (37,668 lines of Bazel). Bazel itself is not run by any GitHub workflow. The build compiles 216 Unity groups covering 2,789 files plus about 135 standalone files, which is 364 compile commands instead of about 2,936 separate .c/.cpp files. It links one 193 MB binary (91.5 MB of machine code) from a 523 MB static archive.

The most important finding for the migration: the C++ module layering holds only at the #include level (5 include lines break bazel/architecture/module_policy.bzl). At link level it does not hold. nm over the built objects shows 12 cross-module edges against the policy, carrying 743 symbols (sql to storage 271, sql to pl 156, storage to sql 100, storage to observer 33). src/data_plane and src/query work as header-only modules whose functions are defined in storage, sql and observer: 264 of 265 data_plane symbols and all 145 query symbols are defined outside their own directories. The executable link holds this together with --start-group/--end-group on Linux and /FORCE:MULTIPLE on Windows. Cargo crates cannot form cycles, so the module graph must be redesigned (traits or registration at startup) rather than copied one module to one crate.

About 87k lines of generated C++ are checked in, and about 279k more (bison/flex parsers and inner-table schemas) are generated at configure time, some of them into the source tree. Every generator needs a Rust back end, or its output has to stay in C behind an FFI. The largest is an 18,723-line bison 2.4.1 SQL grammar with C actions. About 329k lines are data tables, 276k of them the IK dictionary, which in Rust become data files.

Platform code is spread thin, not isolated. There are 577 _WIN32 lines in 248 files, about 6.3k lines under Windows conditionals, 152 __APPLE__ lines in 67 files, and 41 __ANDROID__ lines in 19 files. WebAssembly exists only on the feature/webassembly-shell branch, and it already needs nightly Rust with -Zbuild-std for sql-nio. Rust support tiers cover the matrix: Linux, macOS arm64 and Windows are Tier 1; Android and wasm32-unknown-emscripten are Tier 2 without host tools. Each platform also has its own set of 19 to 31 prebuilt C/C++ dependencies, including vsag, s2geometry, boost, ICU and grpc, which have no drop-in Rust versions.

Verification is cheap per run but narrow. GitHub CI compiles in 2 to 3 minutes with a warm ccache, or about 14 minutes when the cache restore was near-instant (probably a miss). It then runs 272 mysqltest cases in 4 slices of 6 to 10 minutes, all on Linux x86_64. macOS, Windows, Android and wasm have no CI. The Windows packaging path in build.ps1 has been dead since a merge on 2026-08-27. The C++ unit-test tree (330 files, 107k lines) was deleted on 2026-08-21, so the only remaining tests are public-surface SQL tests plus 3 Rust #[test]s.

The current Rust integration is one 8.4k-line crate built by a CMake custom command with lto=thin and codegen-units=1. Its unit tests are not run by any active workflow, and rust/Cargo.lock is gitignored even though CI passes --locked. The C++ side uses clang 17 while rustc 1.98.1 uses LLVM 22, so cross-language LTO and the custom Sanity LLVM pass cannot cover Rust code during a mixed period.

**Structure call (surveyor):** The build graph cannot be translated one-to-one. It forces a redesign. Unity groups are compile batches, not meaningful units. The CMake/Bazel module list looks like a clean DAG at the #include level, but the linked program depends on cycles and on functions declared in one module and defined in another. src/data_plane and src/query declare functions and classes whose bodies live in storage, sql and observer (264 of 265 data_plane symbols and all 145 query symbols); PL headers sit in src/sql/pl while their bodies are in src/pl; sql and storage call each other both ways. The final link holds all this together with --start-group on Linux and /FORCE:MULTIPLE on Windows. Cargo crates must form a DAG, so each of these links has to become a trait object or function registered at startup, or the modules have to merge into crates too large to compile in parallel.

The platform layer maps onto Rust fairly directly: #[cfg] instead of #ifdef, std/mio/socket2 instead of ob_platform_utils.h, and every target is Tier 1 or Tier 2. The cost there lies in the prebuilt C++ dependencies (vsag, s2geometry, boost, ICU, grpc), and in wasm needing nightly Rust. The code generators all need new Rust back ends, or their C output kept behind an FFI.

Files that force the call: src/data_plane/api/data_plane/report/ob_tablet_report.h + src/observer/ob_tablet_runtime_meta_updater.cpp, src/sql/pl/ob_pl.h + src/pl/ob_pl.cpp, src/observer/CMakeLists.txt (--start-group and /FORCE:MULTIPLE), bazel/architecture/module_policy.bzl, src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.y.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree the build graph cannot be copied one-to-one, and the main evidence holds up. My independent nm run gave the same 49 edges and 12 policy violations (743 symbols), with two-way pairs sql<->storage (271/100), pl<->sql (382/156) and observer<->sql (427/131). data_plane and query are almost entirely interfaces whose code is defined elsewhere, e.g. src/data_plane/api/data_plane/report/ob_tablet_report.h:66 is defined at src/observer/ob_tablet_runtime_meta_updater.cpp:760. I would change three things. (1) The Windows side of the 'link holds the cycles together' point is wrong. The cycles are resolved on Linux by --start-group (src/observer/CMakeLists.txt:90-100) and need nothing on macOS. /FORCE:MULTIPLE (line 76) handles duplicate definitions, which is a separate ODR problem. (2) 'Must be redesigned' is too strong. Rust can declare a function in a lower crate (an extern block) and define it with #[no_mangle] in a higher crate, which resolves at link time just as the C++ does now. That is an unsafe stopgap for the mixed period rather than a target design, so traits or registration at startup remain the right end state. (3) The layering is not enforced anywhere in CI. bazel/architecture/module_dependency_aspect.bzl and tools/bazel_pilot_verify.sh run only by hand, so the policy file describes an intent, not the current graph. The platform assessment (#[cfg], Tier 1/2 targets) matches rustc's platform-support doc and the macro counts. The generator and dependency costs are fairly stated.

**Size split check (verifier):** Reproduced. The total is 2,777,368 lines by `git ls-files ... | xargs -0 cat | wc -l` and again by a Python newline count. src/ is 2,774,622 and the rest is 2,746. The survey's list of the rest leaves out rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h (534 lines, a checked-in cbindgen-generated header, which should also count as generated) and bazel/sanity_abort.cpp (22). Generated files match: errno 18,824, the 8 sysvar files 29,946, bit-packing 24,406, protobuf 12,996. The 1,048 split parts sum correctly but could not be pinned to an exact file set. Other figures: data tables 329,161 by wc (the survey's 329,166 counts final lines without a newline); zstd 25,827; libeasy 19 headers and 3,017 lines, all headers; grammars 24,585; Bazel 93 files, 27,468 + 10,200 lines; cmake/*.cmake 1,507, plus 24 CMakeLists.txt at 1,512 lines, which the survey omits; Rust 8,448. One figure is wrong: .github is 3,263 lines in total and 3,145 in workflows, scripts and actions, not 3,378. The build-time generated sizes reproduce: parser .c files 174,926, inner tables 103,354 in 51 files, syspack 62,827 bytes. The hand-written remainder of about 2.306M follows from the parts. The split holds, with minor corrections.

**Units of work:** For the build and platform work:
(1) Crate-level units: the 12 module roots in bazel/architecture/module_policy.bzl (oblib, share, query, data_plane, objit, sql, pl, storage, logservice, standby, rootserver, observer). The graph is 7 levels deep, but sql and storage each need roughly 5-15 sub-crates along their top-level subdirectories. For example, sql/engine is 351,853 lines, optimizer 155,780, resolver 151,357, rewrite 97,268. storage/fts is 286,569, most of it the dictionary data, and blocksstable is 87,627.
(2) File-group units: the 216 Unity groups (2,789 files, median 10 files per group) plus about 135 standalone or parser files, or roughly 350 groups. This matches the 364 compile commands of the current build.
(3) Generator units, about 10: SQL, PL and FTS bison/flex; inner tables; syspack; errno; system variables; bit-packing (SIMD and scalar); comparison/datum split parts; protobuf/grpc; plus the cbindgen interface during the mixed period.
(4) Platform units: 5 platform families (Linux 8 dependency flavors, macOS, Windows, Android, wasm) across 13 deps/init/*.deps files, and the platform layer (ob_platform_utils.h/.cpp, ob_win_service, ob_statvfs_win32.h, android_compat.h, plus the conditional blocks spread over about 250 files).
(5) Build drivers: build.sh, build.ps1, cmake/Rust.cmake, deps/external, and the CI workflows.

**Public-surface tests:** This area has no tests of its own, but the build output is verified only through public-surface SQL tests against the seekdb binary. Those tests use the MySQL protocol and survive a change of implementation language. GitHub CI (.github/workflows/seekdb.yml) runs 272 mysqltest cases from the runtime_configs.psmall.test-set in tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml, in 4 slices of 6-10 minutes on a Linux x86_64 container. The largest groups are 93 top-level cases, geometry 39, vector_index 21, fork_table 16, fts_index 15, expr 11, subquery 11 and type_date 10. tools/deploy/mysql_test holds 283 .test files (93 in t/, the rest in test_suite/*/t). tools/obtest holds 579 t and 556 r files for the internal farm, which .gitlab-ci.yml triggers through templates that are not tracked. No tests exercise the macOS, Windows, Android or wasm builds in CI; the wasm branch has its own bridge, tools/wasm/mysqltest-bridge.mjs, on feature/webassembly-shell. Tests that import internals are nearly gone: the C++ unittest tree (330 files, 107,446 lines) was deleted in 45cbbe9e1, and sql-nio has 3 Rust #[test]s that no active workflow runs.

**Hard parts:**
- Link-time cycles and functions declared in one module but defined in another. data_plane and query APIs are defined in storage, sql and observer (src/data_plane/api/data_plane/report/ob_tablet_report.h:66 is defined in src/observer/ob_tablet_runtime_meta_updater.cpp:760); PL headers are in src/sql/pl/*.h, bodies in src/pl/*.cpp; sql and storage call each other both ways (271 and 100 symbols). Rust crates cannot form cycles, so each link needs a trait or a registration point, or a merged crate.
- Crate sizing and compile parallelism. src/sql is 983,706 lines and src/sql/engine alone 351,853 lines in 1,379 files; src/storage is 760,249. They need a split much finer than the 12 module roots in bazel/architecture/module_policy.bzl, or type checking becomes a serial bottleneck.
- The SQL grammar: src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.y (18,723 lines) and .l (1,853) are pinned to bison 2.4.1 (src/sql/parser/gen_parser.sh:31-37), with C actions that build ParseNode trees (src/sql/parser/parse_node.h). Also src/pl/parser/pl_parser_mysql_mode.y (2,976) and the FTS grammar. Rust has no drop-in bison-compatible generator at this size, so the options are a rewrite or keeping the C parser behind an FFI.
- SIMD with per-target ISA flags and generated intrinsics: src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_unalign_simd_bp_func.cpp (7,200 intrinsic lines, generated by generate_simd_bitpakcing.py); AVX-512 flags on ob_sql_simd and ob_storage_simd (src/sql/CMakeLists.txt:13-14, src/storage/CMakeLists.txt:21-22); the hand-made src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_sse_to_neon.h and src/storage/blocksstable/encoding/neon/. These become #[target_feature] functions with runtime dispatch, and the generators need Rust output.
- Prebuilt C++ dependencies with no drop-in Rust version: vsag, s2geometry, boost, abseil, ICU, grpc, plus OpenMP/openblas/diskann on Android (deps/init/*.deps; src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt:55-70). Their template-heavy C++ APIs need handwritten C shims or cxx bridges on every platform.
- The allocator and memory-checking stack: ob_malloc + jemalloc from the Cargo crate seekdb-jemalloc-sys (deps/external), malloc_hook on Linux only (src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt:24-40), and the Sanity build, which adds a custom LLVM pass through -fpass-plugin (src/oblib/CMakeLists.txt:177-185) plus 17 -Wl,--wrap libc wrappers (CMakeLists.txt:264-281). Rust code built with LLVM 22 is not instrumented by a clang 17 pass plugin.
- The mixed-language period: panic=abort is required at the FFI (rust/Cargo.toml profile.release and profile.cmake-debug); build.rs writes the cbindgen header rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h (534 lines) into the source tree; clang 17 and rustc's LLVM 22.1.8 differ, so FFI calls cannot be inlined across the language boundary through cross-language LTO.
- Configure-time and checked-in generators that emit C++: src/share/inner_table/generate_inner_table_schema.py (103k generated lines), src/share/inner_table/sys_package/syspack_codegen.py, src/share/gen_errno.pl, and src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py, which also writes into src/sql/session/. Each needs a Rust emitter (build.rs or checked-in output) and a plan for the mixed period, when both languages consume the same definitions.
- The platform matrix with no CI beyond Linux: Windows (clang-cl, vcpkg, /FORCE:MULTIPLE, 57 files including windows.h, build.ps1 already rotting), Android NDK API 28 cross-builds (build.sh configure_cmake, cmake/Rust.cmake:55-100), and wasm, which requires nightly Rust with -Zbuild-std and +atomics (feature/webassembly-shell tools/wasm/rust-build-env.sh). Every Rust crate must build for all of them, and only Linux is checked automatically.

**Pain instances:**
- e66776e2d (2026-08-20): the Sanity memory-checking build found use-after-free in Plan Cache/PS Cache (an object accessed after its last node reference was released; a destroyed ps item dereferenced after the schema expired) and a wrong buffer length in skip-index output. Ownership and bounds checks in Rust remove this class of bug. The Sanity build mode itself (CMakeLists.txt:215-290, with a custom LLVM pass) exists to catch these.
- src/observer/CMakeLists.txt:71: /FORCE:MULTIPLE on the Windows link (added in 34e0af1af) accepts duplicate symbol definitions. That is an ODR hazard, and Rust's per-crate symbol mangling does not allow it.
- 22b7c24db ('Fix compilation in non-unity build (#932)') and e45b8ae5c ('fix compile in non-unity'): Unity batching hid missing includes and declarations, so the non-Unity build broke without anyone noticing. Rust modules compile only one way.
- cmake/Utils.cmake unity_after: the Unity build must #undef USING_LOG_PREFIX after every included .cpp because 2,324 .c/.cpp files define it, and the macro leaks between files batched into one Unity translation unit. Rust has no preprocessor-level leak of this kind.
- src/oblib/CMakeLists.txt:110-172: every C++ target is compiled with -fno-strict-aliasing and with -Wno-class-memaccess, -Wno-invalid-offsetof, -Wno-maybe-uninitialized and -Wno-reinterpret-base-class (52 warnings disabled on clang, 141 on the Windows clang-cl path). These are build-level defenses against memory patterns that are undefined behavior or depend on how compilers handle aliasing; safe Rust does not allow those patterns.
- e2b7c7a8f: duplicate template instantiations across translation units made one Unity translation unit take 455 s, fixed by hand with extern template and ALONE splits. Rust removes the header-driven duplication, but generic instantiation across crates has a similar cost, so Rust only partly removes this.

**Open questions:**
- Does the internal farm pipeline build with Bazel, run the tools/obtest suites, or build and test macOS, Windows or Android? .gitlab-ci.yml includes test/ci/templates/*.yml, which is not in this repository.
- Clean-build time and the slowest Unity groups at HEAD on the reference machine. This is left to the separate timing run in build_release/; the only per-group numbers found are 455 s and 342 s in the e2b7c7a8f message, which come from a coverage build.
- The link-edge and symbol counts (C6, C7) come from main-checkout objects at 35eb2c3a3, 4 commits behind HEAD with 20 src files different. A re-run at HEAD should confirm the numbers.
- Whether the macOS, Windows and Android builds succeed at HEAD. No CI covers them, and build.ps1's package path is already dead code.
- Were the C++ unit tests deleted in 45cbbe9e1 moved elsewhere (another repo or farm-only), or dropped on purpose? This decides whether any tests that import internals exist to port.
- Would `cargo test --locked -p sql-nio` in buildbase fail without the gitignored rust/Cargo.lock? Not run, per the read-only rule.
- How much of the 7,200 generated x86 intrinsic lines and the NEON paths could become portable Rust (std::simd is still unstable, and auto-vectorization is another option) instead of per-ISA #[target_feature] code?
- Compile time, peak memory and link time of a Rust build at 2M lines depend on the crate split and profile settings; C24 and C25 are assumptions, not measurements.
- Whether the vsag, s2geometry, ICU and grpc dependencies are to be kept through FFI or replaced. That decides how much of the per-platform deps/init matrix survives.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- Packaging is broken for every format, not only the Windows path in build.ps1. Commit 45cbbe9e1 (titled as a SQL precision fix) also deleted tools/systemd/profile/*, tools/timezone_V1.log, tools/windows/seekdbConfigurator and tools/windows/seekdb_manage.ps1, and the packaging code still references all of them. CI builds only `make seekdb`, so nothing notices. A migration cannot use package builds as a baseline or a check. (evidence: `git show --numstat 45cbbe9e1` lists these deletions. The references that remain: cmake/RPM.cmake:51-66 and cmake/DEB.cmake:22-34 (configure_file on the deleted templates); cmake/Pack.cmake:130,152,208,225-251,264; build.ps1:209; package/deb/seekdb-build.sh:128; tools/deploy/copy.sh:69. CMakeLists.txt:323-329 includes these files whenever a package format is selected. origin/master has the same state.)
- Nothing in CI enforces the module layering. The only checks are a Bazel aspect and a script run by hand, and GitHub never runs Bazel. That is why link-level cycles and include-level violations have piled up, and nothing will check the C++ side during a mixed migration. (evidence: bazel/architecture/module_dependency_aspect.bzl enforces ALLOWED_MODULE_DEPS on BUILD edges. tools/bazel_pilot_verify.sh:13-24 runs tools/module_check/*.py. `git grep -l module_check` finds no reference in .github, build.sh or CMake.)
- Only one Rust staticlib can go into seekdb. cmake/Rust.cmake builds only `--package sql-nio` into libsql_nio.a, which is already 88 MB for 8.4k lines (debug=true, bundling std, rustls and ring). Each Rust staticlib carries its own copy of std, so linking two into one binary causes duplicate symbols, and on Windows /FORCE:MULTIPLE would hide them. A crate-per-module port therefore needs one umbrella staticlib that re-exports every migrated crate, or cargo taking over the final link. The duplicate-std behavior is general knowledge and was not tested here. (evidence: cmake/Rust.cmake add_custom_command (`--package sql-nio`); rust/Cargo.toml `members = ["sql-nio"]`; rust/sql-nio/Cargo.toml crate-type staticlib+rlib; main checkout build_release/rust-target/release/libsql_nio.a is 88,176,272 bytes; src/observer/CMakeLists.txt:76 /FORCE:MULTIPLE.)
- Rust dependency versions are not locked in the production build. rust/Cargo.lock is gitignored, and cmake/Rust.cmake runs cargo build without --locked. So every fresh CI, Android or Windows build picks the newest semver-compatible mio, rustls, ring and x509-parser. The CI cargo cache key hashes a file that does not exist. deps/external, by contrast, tracks its Cargo.lock and uses --locked. At 2M lines with many crates, reproducible builds and audits need a lockfile policy. (evidence: rust/.gitignore:5; cmake/Rust.cmake cargo build with no --locked; .github/actions/setup-rust/action.yml:102 key hashFiles('rust/Cargo.lock', ...); deps/external/Cargo.lock is tracked; CargoExternal.cmake:74 uses --locked.)
- The measured artifacts come from a macOS arm64 build, and CI never compiles aarch64 code. The binary size, __text size and nm link-graph numbers all describe the Darwin build: no --start-group, no x86 AVX-512 flags, NEON paths compiled. The Linux x86_64 binary that CI tests was not measured. The NEON decoders and ob_sse_to_neon.h (Linux aarch64 and macOS) are compiled only on developer machines. (evidence: `file build_release/src/observer/seekdb` gives 'Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64'. src/observer/CMakeLists.txt:90 excludes Darwin from the grouping. src/storage/CMakeLists.txt:20-22 applies AVX-512 on x86_64 only. The only CI runners are arc-runner-k8s-ci-org and ubuntu-*. Code lives in src/storage/blocksstable/encoding/neon/ and src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_sse_to_neon.h.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey is largely trustworthy. Most counts reproduced exactly with independent methods: total and src lines, Unity groups by module, compile commands, link edges and policy violations, platform macro counts, grammar and generated file sizes, CI compile times, and the 272 test cases. The behavior claims about CMake reading Bazel inventories, the Rust integration, the dead buildbase and cargo test path, and the dead build.ps1 package path all match the code. The errors are small and mostly about framing. /FORCE:MULTIPLE handles duplicate definitions, not cycles. Two of the 'disabled warnings' apply only to the unused GCC branch. The link reads about 3.1 GB of archives, not one 523 MB archive. The .github and wasm line counts are too high. CI slices sometimes take about 24 minutes and whole runs up to 43. The AutoFDO and hot-function profiles are empty placeholders. The data_plane and query symbol counts depend on how symbols are matched, but the conclusion holds. The biggest gaps are three things for the verification-cost part of the decision. Packaging is broken for every format. The layering policy is not enforced anywhere. The measured binary is a macOS build, so nothing about the Linux x86_64 artifact that CI tests was measured. The Rust-side constraints (a single staticlib and unlocked dependencies) are also missing. None of this overturns the structure call.


## AREA: deps
**What it is:** seekdb links about 19 prebuilt third-party libraries, listed in deps/init/*.deps (13 platform files) and wired in mainly through src/oblib/CMakeLists.txt, src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt and src/oblib/grpc/CMakeLists.txt. It also carries about 34k lines of copied third-party C/C++ source (zstd 1.3.8, xxHash, IANA tzcode, libeasy/libev headers, CRC code) plus about 13k lines of checked-in protobuf/gRPC output. Most libraries are called from a small number of wrapper files. The exceptions are boost::geometry, which runs through src/share/geo, and vsag, whose C++ interfaces seekdb subclasses.

**Size split:** Library code itself is outside the tree (prebuilt packages under deps/3rd, not git-tracked). The in-tree lines that belong to this area break down as follows.

VENDORED THIRD-PARTY SOURCE, 33,789 lines, verified:
- zstd 1.3.8, src/oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8/zstd_src: 47 .c/.h files, 25,154 lines.
- xxHash, src/oblib/lib/hash_func/xxhash.{c,h}: 1,214 lines.
- murmur_hash.{cpp,h} and wyhash.h: 259 lines.
- IANA tzcode, src/oblib/common/timezone/{localtime.c,private.h,tzfile.h}: 2,859 lines.
- libeasy and libev header remnants, src/oblib/easy: 19 .h files, 3,017 lines.
- CRC code, src/oblib/lib/checksum/ob_crc64.{cpp,h}: 1,286 lines.
Commands: `git ls-files <dir> | grep -E '\.(c|h)$' | xargs cat | wc -l` and `wc -l`.

PROBABLY PORTED, NOT VERIFIED:
- src/oblib/lib/charset: 20 files, 24,815 lines, MySQL strings/ ctype-uca and dtoa style. The provenance headers were replaced by the OceanBase Apache header.
- The SHA1 code in src/oblib/lib/encrypt/ob_encrypted_helper.cpp uses the RFC 3174 names SHA1ProcessMessageBlock and Intermediate_Hash.

GENERATED, 12,996 lines:
- src/oblib/grpc/standbyservice.{pb,grpc.pb}.{cc,h}: 12,345 lines.
- src/share/geo/ob_vector_tile.pb-c.{c,h}: 651 lines.
Command: `wc -l`.

DATA, about 38,963 lines:
- src/oblib/common/timezone/ob_timezone_info.cpp lines 26-38988 are the embedded tz tables TIME_ZONE_NAMES and TIME_ZONE_TRANS, out of 39,989 lines. Command: `awk '/^};/'` to find where the tables end.

GRAMMARS FED TO BISON/FLEX, 24,585 lines:
- 3 .y files and 3 .l files. Command: `git ls-files '*.y' '*.l' | xargs wc -l`.

HAND-WRITTEN WRAPPERS THAT CALL THE LIBRARIES DIRECTLY, about 13.5k lines:
- vsag: src/oblib/lib/vector, 4 files, 1,991 lines.
- S2: ob_s2adapter.* and ob_geo_to_s2_visitor.*, 1,343 lines.
- sqlite: ob_sqlite_*, 1,145 lines.
- libxml2: SAX handler plus XML parser, 2,039 lines.
- ICU regex: ob_expr_regexp_context.*, 954 lines.
- relaxed-rapidjson: ob_json_parse.*, 853 lines.
- zstd: ob_zstd_* wrapper, 608 lines.
- gRPC: ob_grpc_*, 675 lines.
- CRoaring: ob_rb_memory_mgr.*, 293 lines.
- curl: the 3 curl-using .cpp files, 3,627 lines in total, not all of it curl code.

BOOST::GEOMETRY-DEPENDENT CODE:
- 59 files and 16,383 lines in src/share/geo include boost/geometry or go through ob_geo_dispatcher.h / ob_geo_func_common.h.
- The whole of src/share/geo is 179 files and 47,684 lines.
Command: `git grep -l -E 'boost/geometry|boost::geometry|ob_geo_func_common.h|ob_geo_dispatcher.h' -- src/share/geo | xargs cat | wc -l`.

ORPHANS:
- deps/oblib: 3 files, 308 lines. No build file references them.

**Summary:** 1. The dependency surface is smaller than the manifests suggest, and it keeps shrinking. LLVM is now only the compiler toolchain: the PL JIT was removed in 3f1fbb84f (-62,423 lines), and src/objit is a single enum header. The compression libraries were cut down to none/zstd138 in 4238c88e9 (-53,912 lines). abseil, xz and gtest have no direct users.

2. ICU is not used for charsets or collations. Those are 24,815 lines of in-tree, MySQL-style code in src/oblib/lib/charset. ICU is only the regex engine behind REGEXP (the uregex_* C API in ob_expr_regexp_context.cpp) plus one URL-codec call. So ICU stays a plain C-FFI dependency, while the charset code is translation work that belongs to oblib.

3. Most C dependencies are called from 1-8 wrapper files each and have mature Rust answers:
   - openssl → RustCrypto crates plus rustls, which sql-nio already uses.
   - libcurl → reqwest or the curl crate.
   - sqlite → rusqlite, which reads the same meta.db format.
   - zlib → flate2.
   - libaio → io_uring or libc syscalls.
   - jemalloc → tikv-jemallocator. It is already built through Cargo.
   - protobuf-c → prost.
   - gRPC/protobuf → tonic/prost. That removes 12,345 checked-in generated lines.

4. Three dependencies decide the structure:
   - boost::geometry is header-only C++ templates. It is specialized over seekdb's own geometry types (ob_geo_bin_traits.h, ob_geo_tree_traits.h) and uses ellipsoid ("geographic") strategies. Its exception types leak into user-visible error codes (OB_ERR_BOOST_GEOMETRY_*).
   - vsag is C++ with virtual Allocator, Logger and Filter interfaces that seekdb subclasses in 4 places. vsag's own C API does not cover allocators, loggers, extra-info, iterators or sparse vectors.
   - relaxed-rapidjson is a header-only fork with non-standard parse flags.
   None of the three can be called over FFI without a C++ shim, and no Rust crate matches their behavior.

5. Several dependencies are tied to on-disk formats, so replacing them is a compatibility question and not just a code question. S2 cell ids are written into spatial-index rows; vsag index snapshots are persisted (see the legacy-format fix 50306fbfd); zstd 1.3.8 compresses blocks; the CRC-32C-based ob_crc64 produces checksums; and sqlite stores meta.db.

6. About 34k lines of copied third-party code can be deleted and replaced by crates: zstd, twox-hash/xxhash-rust, chrono-tz/tz-rs, crc32c, and removing the vestigial libeasy headers. The ~39k-line embedded tz table becomes a crate dependency.

7. The strongest case-for-leaving evidence from this area is the in-house memory-safety toolchain: the ob-sanity LLVM pass plugin plus jemalloc-level shadow memory. Commit 6da784ac1 fixed a wrong buffer length, a read after unlock and a dangling pointer, all found with it.

8. Public-surface tests cover boost/S2 heavily: 42 geometry test files, whose 42 result files total 55,904 lines and include results carried over from MySQL. vsag has 31 tests. There are 6 REGEXP tests. libxml2, protobuf-c (ST_AsMVT) and the zlib COMPRESS() function have none.
