**Structure call (surveyor):** This area does not translate one-to-one. Each dependency needs its own decision, and they fall into four kinds.

(a) C libraries with a mature Rust crate or plain C FFI can be swapped behind their current wrappers without changing structure:
- OpenSSL → RustCrypto crates (md-5, sha1, sha2, aes, des, hmac) plus rustls.
- libcurl → reqwest, or the curl crate.
- sqlite → rusqlite, same meta.db file format.
- zlib → flate2.
- libaio → io-uring or libc syscalls.
- jemalloc → tikv-jemallocator.
- libunwind → keep over FFI, because backtraces taken inside signal handlers need it.
- CRoaring → croaring (the same C library) or roaring.
- protobuf-c → prost.
- gRPC/protobuf → tonic + prost.
- ICU regex → keep ICU4C over its C API, since no pure-Rust regex matches ICU semantics.
- fast_float → Rust std float parsing.

(b) Copied third-party source is deleted and replaced by crates:
- zstd → zstd or ruzstd.
- xxHash → xxhash-rust.
- tzcode and the tz data → chrono-tz or tz-rs.
- CRC → crc32c / crc64fast.
- libeasy/libev headers → std::net types.
Each replacement must give bit-exact output wherever the result is persisted.

(c) Three dependencies force a redesign, because they are header-only or virtual-interface C++ and no Rust crate behaves the same:
- boost::geometry, including ellipsoid ("geographic") strategies and user-visible exception-to-error mapping. Options: keep it behind a hand-written C++ shim that instantiates the templates for seekdb's geometry types, or move to geo + geographiclib-rs + proj and accept different numeric results.
- vsag. Needs a C shim that turns its Allocator, Logger and Filter subclasses and its std::istream/ostream serialization into function-pointer callbacks.
- relaxed-rapidjson. Needs a hand-written Rust SAX parser that reproduces the fork's flags.

(d) S2 is C++ as well, but its surface is small (1,343 lines). It needs either a small C++ shim or the partial pure-Rust s2 crate, and in either case the cell coverings must stay identical, because cell ids are stored in index rows.

Files that force the redesign: src/share/geo/ob_geo_dispatcher.h, src/share/geo/ob_geo_bin_traits.h, src/share/geo/ob_geo_tree_traits.h, src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp, src/storage/allocator/ob_vector_allocator.h, src/oblib/common/json_type/ob_json_parse.cpp.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I mostly agree. This area needs one decision per dependency, not a line-by-line translation. boost::geometry and vsag are the two real redesign points. The evidence: bg:: and boost:: are used in 39 files, with traits in ob_geo_bin_traits.h and ob_geo_tree_traits.h and the dispatcher in ob_geo_dispatcher.h:1314-1363. vsag is reached through C++ virtual subclasses in ob_vector_util.h:35, ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp:87, ob_vector_allocator.h:71 and ob_vector_index_adaptor.h:237, and through std::istream/ostream serialization, while vsag_c_api.h offers only 'bool (*)(int64_t)' as a callback. Five adjustments. (a) relaxed-rapidjson does not belong in the same bucket as boost and vsag. The fork's full reader.h (2,417 lines) is installed and readable, it is used from 7 files through one SAX wrapper (ob_json_parse.cpp), and Windows already ships without the relaxed flags. So it is a bounded parser rewrite, not a redesign. (b) Keeping S2 behind a C++ shim also keeps OpenSSL: libs2.a has 26 undefined BN_* symbols. libcurl.a and libgrpc.a also reference SSL_*. The plan to replace OpenSSL with RustCrypto plus rustls only works after S2, curl and gRPC are all replaced. (c) tzcode needs deleting, not replacing with a crate. (d) The ICU decision covers ob_expr_regexp_context.cpp alone; the URL codec does not call ICU. (e) The malloc-hook and label hard part applies only to Linux (src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt:33-40). The CRoaring out-of-memory path is simpler than it looks, because seekdb never serializes roaring64 bitmaps: the only roaring64 calls are add, remove, contains, set operations and cardinality, in 4 vector-index files.

**Size split check (verifier):** Most of the counts reproduce exactly: zstd_src 47 .c/.h files and 25,154 lines, xxHash 1,214, murmur plus wyhash 259, tzcode 2,859, easy 3,017, CRC 1,286, charset 20 files and 24,815 lines, gRPC stubs 12,345, pb-c 651, grammars 24,585, boost-dependent geo 59 files and 16,383 lines, deps/oblib 308. src/share/geo is 180 files and 47,763 lines including ob_vector_tile.proto (79 lines); leaving out the .proto gives the survey's 179 and 47,684. Some of the wrapper sizes are a little off, for example the zstd wrapper is 592 lines, or 608 with ob_empty.c. The classification needs changes. (1) Of the '33,789 vendored' lines, tzcode (2,859) is dead: it is in neither build inventory and nothing includes it. libeasy (3,017) is Alibaba's own code; only ev.h (838) is outside code. Live copied third-party code is about 27.9k lines (zstd, xxHash, CRC, wyhash). (2) Of the 24,815 charset lines, ob_ctype_uca.cc (7,129) is not compiled and cannot compile, because it includes ob_ctype_uca_tab.h, which is not in the tree. ob_charset.cpp:2080-2088 registers only utf8mb4_general_ci, utf8mb4_bin and binary. So about 17.7k lines of charset code are live, and that includes big tables such as ob_ctype_utf8_tab.h (4,472). (3) The split leaves out two large blocks that are not hand-written code. src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp is 276,043 lines of embedded IK Analyzer dictionary words and is the largest file in src by far. src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_{unalign_simd,scalar}_bp_func.cpp are 24,299 lines of bit-packing code produced by the in-tree Python generators generate_*_bitpakcing.py, which carry an Alibaba 2014-2018 header; ob_fast_delta.h cites Lemire's SIMD compression work. Both belong with the data and generated lines, and together they are larger than every block the survey counted.

**Units of work:** The natural unit is one dependency boundary: the wrapper file group plus its direct callers. There are 18:
1. boost::geometry: src/share/geo, 59 files / 16,383 lines use boost or the dispatcher.
2. S2: 4 adapter files plus 3 callers.
3. vsag: src/oblib/lib/vector (4 files) plus the 3 files that subclass vsag interfaces, plus src/observer/vector_index callers; 11 files call it.
4. ICU regex: ob_expr_regexp_context.{h,cpp} and ob_expr_url_codec.cpp.
5. relaxed-rapidjson: src/oblib/common/json_type/ob_json_parse.*, ob_json_tree.h, and the admin dump helper.
6. libxml2: ob_libxml2_sax_handler.* and ob_xml_parser.*.
7. libcurl: 5 files.
8. OpenSSL: 9 including files, plus the in-tree SHA1 in ob_encrypted_helper.
9. sqlite: src/share/storage, 17 files.
10. gRPC/protobuf: src/oblib/grpc plus 8 files in src/standby.
11. protobuf-c: MVT, 3 files.
12. CRoaring: 2 files plus 4 users.
13. zstd: copied source plus wrapper, plus 4 users.
14. zlib: 6 files.
15. jemalloc and malloc_hook: 3 files plus deps/external.
16. libaio and libunwind: 8 files plus 1 file.
17. bison/flex grammars: 3 .y and 3 .l files.
18. The other copied code, each replaced by a crate: tzcode and tz data, xxHash/murmur/wyhash, CRC, libeasy/libev headers.

fast_float (2 call sites) and the dependencies with no direct users (abseil, xz, gtest) need no work unit of their own.

**Public-surface tests:** All of these are black-box SQL tests run by mysqltest through obclient (tools/deploy/obd.sh:66-71), so they survive a rewrite unchanged. Counts come from git grep over 'tools/deploy/mysql_test/*.test' and 'tools/obtest/*.test', out of 793 in total.

- boost::geometry and S2: test_suite/geometry has 42 .test files, e.g. geometry_bugfix_mysql.test, create_spatial_index_mysql.test and st_buffer_mysql. Its 42 .result files total 55,904 lines. Across all suites, 49 tests call ST_* functions, 14 create SPATIAL INDEX, and 4 use ST_Transform (boost srs).
- vsag and CRoaring: 31 tests mention vector index or distance functions, 22 of them in test_suite/vector_index, e.g. vector_index_basic.test and sparse_vector_index_vsag_query.test.
- ICU: 6 tests use REGEXP/RLIKE, e.g. test_suite/expr/t/func_regexp.test and collation_expr.test.
- libcurl: 8 tests in test_suite/ai_function. They need model endpoints.
- relaxed-rapidjson: 5 tests use JSON_ functions or ->>.
- OpenSSL: 2 tests use MD5/SHA/AES/PASSWORD.
- sqlite (indirectly, through parameter persistence): 472 tests use 'alter system set'.
- gRPC: the 145 tests in tools/obtest/t/primary_standby, 8 in stanby and those in errsim_primary_standby mention standby. It is not established whether they run against seekdb's gRPC standby.
- Not covered by any test: libxml2 (0 tests use ExtractValue/UpdateXML/xmltype), protobuf-c (0 use ST_AsMVT) and the URL codec (0).
- There are no gtest unit tests. The only 2 gtest files, in deps/oblib/unittest, are not built.

**Hard parts:**
- boost::geometry is header-only C++ templates, specialized over seekdb's own geometry binary and tree types through traits (src/share/geo/ob_geo_bin_traits.h has 56 template lines; ob_geo_tree_traits.h has 46). src/share/geo/ob_geo_dispatcher.h (256 template lines) sends each geometry-type pair and coordinate system to the right function. None of this can be called from Rust without a C++ layer that instantiates every combination.
- Geographic-coordinate results must match MySQL. src/share/geo uses srs::spheroid, geographic_winding, geographic_segments and andoyer strategies. The Rust geo crate is mostly planar, and geographiclib-rs covers geodesic distance and area but not intersection, within or difference on an ellipsoid. The expected results in tools/deploy/mysql_test/test_suite/geometry/r (55,904 lines, partly from MySQL, e.g. r/mysql/st_buffer_mysql.result) pin the numbers.
- Error codes follow boost's exception types: src/share/geo/ob_geo_dispatcher.h:1316-1359 maps them to OB_ERR_BOOST_GEOMETRY_* (src/share/ob_errno.h:2533-2541). Any replacement must reproduce when these user-visible errors are raised.
- S2 cell coverings are persisted as spatial-index keys (src/sql/das/ob_das_domain_utils.cpp:168-193). A pure-Rust S2 must produce the same S2RegionCoverer output (src/share/geo/ob_s2adapter.cpp) as S2 0.10.0, or existing spatial indexes become invalid.
- The vsag boundary calls back into seekdb code. vsag::Allocator is implemented in src/storage/allocator/ob_vector_allocator.h:71 and src/query/api/query/vector/ob_vector_index_adaptor.h:237; vsag::Filter in src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp:87; vsag::Logger in src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vector_util.h:35. Serialization goes through std::ostream/istream (fserialize/fdeserialize). vsag's own C API has no hooks for any of these, so a seekdb-owned C++ shim is required.
- The C++-stream boundary is where a real out-of-bounds read happened: d08a76bce (2026-08-20) worked around vsag's BufferStreamReader treating one shard length as the full stream length when reading seekdb's ObIStreamBuf. The Rust-to-C shim would sit on exactly this seam.
- vsag snapshots are persisted, and the format is locked (commit 50306fbfd, 'use legacy VSAG serialization format', in src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp). Replacing vsag with a Rust ANN crate such as hnsw_rs or instant-distance would need a way to read existing indexes.
- CRoaring out-of-memory handling depends on C++ exceptions unwinding through C frames (src/share/roaringbitmap/ob_rb_memory_mgr.cpp: `throw std::bad_alloc()` inside roaring_malloc; 44 CROARING_TRY_CATCH sites). Rust aborts when a panic crosses extern "C", so the Rust design needs a different error path, for example fallible allocation in the pure-Rust roaring crate.
- Process-wide malloc interposition with memory attributed by thread-local labels (src/oblib/lib/alloc/malloc_hook.cpp:339-342; ObMallocHookAttrGuard used 103 times in 36 files). This is how memory that C libraries allocate gets charged to modules, and Rust's #[global_allocator] does not cover allocations made inside C libraries.
- relaxed-rapidjson is a header-only template fork whose non-standard flags (src/oblib/common/json_type/ob_json_parse.h:28-30) are implemented in a package whose source is not in the tree. A Rust parser must reproduce case-insensitive keywords, unquoted keys, relaxed numbers and the syntax-error message text.
- libxml2 is used as a SAX tokenizer that reads parser internals (ctxt->input, 7 uses, through libxml/parserInternals.h in src/oblib/common/xml/ob_libxml2_sax_handler.cpp). Moving to quick-xml means re-deriving the XML declaration and entity handling that is currently read out of libxml2's internal state.
- Persisted checksums and hashes must be bit-exact: ob_crc64() calls ob_crc64_sse42 (src/oblib/lib/checksum/ob_crc64.cpp:292), which uses the CRC-32C instruction (_mm_crc32_u64, line 365). murmurhash appears in 152 files, and XXH64 in 14 files including blocksstable bloom filters. zstd 1.3.8 compresses storage blocks through ObZstdWrapper with custom memory callbacks (src/oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8/ob_zstd_wrapper.cpp).
- Two TLS/crypto stacks in one binary: OpenSSL 1.1.1u for gRPC TLS (src/oblib/grpc/ob_grpc_context.cpp), telemetry HMAC and the SQL encrypt functions, and rustls+ring in rust/sql-nio. Converging on one is extra work.
- ICU regex has no pure-Rust equivalent. The regex crate lacks backreferences and lookaround; fancy-regex differs in syntax and behavior. ob_expr_regexp_context.cpp also relies on uregex_setStackLimit, uregex_setTimeLimit and uregex_appendReplacement semantics, so ICU4C stays behind C FFI.

**Pain instances:**
- Commit 6da784ac1 (2026-08-19) fixed three memory-safety defects found with the in-house Sanity LLVM pass (the ob-sanity dependency). (1) src/observer/virtual_table/ob_table_columns.cpp:603 passed buf_len instead of extra_print_buf_size to databuff_printf, so the write could overflow the buffer. (2) src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp read a cache object's allocator totals after unlocking the node, while a concurrent eviction could free it. (3) src/sql/plan_cache/ob_ps_cache_callback.h dereferenced ps_item_ after the item could already be erased. Rust's bounds checks, lock guards that own the data, and borrow rules rule out all three kinds of mistake.
- The existence of ob-sanity itself (deps/init/*.deps, CMakeLists.txt:223-263) and commit a0d8e61ca (2026-09-01, 'support Sanity with jemalloc': shadow memory, jemalloc extent hooks, --wrap=memcpy and friends) are standing costs paid to find memory errors that safe Rust would not produce.
- Commit ae6edddba (2026-07-13, 'Fix sanity startup crash in pre-main THP init') removed an __attribute__((constructor(101))) from src/observer/main.cpp because it ran before the runtime was ready. 9 __attribute__((constructor)) sites remain in src, e.g. ob_global_init_crc64_table in src/oblib/lib/checksum/ob_crc64.cpp. src/observer/CMakeLists.txt:17 notes that malloc_hook 'conflicts with LLVM's early global constructors on macOS'. Rust has no code that runs before main, so this whole class of problem goes away.
- src/share/roaringbitmap/ob_rb_memory_mgr.cpp contains defensive code: seekdb's allocator callback throws std::bad_alloc through CRoaring's C frames, and it is caught at 44 CROARING_TRY_CATCH call sites. roaring_free also picks the allocator from the thread-local label at free time, so an allocation made under one label and freed under another goes to the wrong allocator (a hazard; I found no bug report for it). With a Rust bitmap crate that uses the Rust allocator, the wrappers disappear. Caveat: Rust's default reaction to running out of memory is to abort, not to return an error.
- src/oblib/lib/json/ob_json.h:22-35 replaces POSIX regcomp/regexec on Windows with stubs that always return -1, so RegexFilter silently never matches on that platform. A portable Rust regex crate removes the need for such a stub. The Windows manifest also pulls in the dirent and pthreads compatibility libraries (deps/init/oceanbase.windows.x86_64.deps), used by 9 files that include dirent.h and 39 that include pthread.h. Rust's std covers both.

**Open questions:**
- Must zstd-compressed storage blocks be byte-identical to zstd 1.3.8's output, for example if checksums of compressed blocks are compared in backup, standby or restore? Or is being able to decode old blocks enough? If decoding is enough, the zstd crate, which bundles a newer libzstd, is a drop-in replacement.
- Are roaring64 bitmaps persisted anywhere, for example vector-index valid-id bitmaps? If so, does the pure-Rust roaring crate's 64-bit serialization match CRoaring's portable roaring64 format?
- Which ICU regex features actually work with libicustubdata linked, and therefore what a replacement has to match? This needs a runtime test.
- Are the 145+8 primary_standby/stanby tests in tools/obtest run against seekdb's gRPC StandbyService, or are they legacy OceanBase cases? They are not wired into the documented obd.sh mysqltest flow.
- What third-party set does the embedded form link? I found no embedded-library target in this worktree's CMake, only the imported/shared 'oceanbase' target in src/observer/CMakeLists.txt:27-43.
- Is exact numeric agreement with MySQL on geographic results a product requirement? If yes, boost::geometry has to stay behind a C++ shim; if no, geo, geographiclib-rs and proj become viable.
- The source of the relaxed-rapidjson fork (which implements the flags 1024, 2048 and 4096) and of the vsag build are outside the tree, so the exact behavior a Rust port must match cannot be read here.
- What are the provenance and license of src/oblib/lib/charset (24,815 lines) and the SHA1 in ob_encrypted_helper.cpp? They look like ports of MySQL strings/ and RFC 3174 code, but the original headers were replaced with the OceanBase Apache header.
- Are grpc 1.46.7 and libcurl 8.12.1 built against the end-of-life OpenSSL 1.1.1u package or against their own TLS? That decides whether dropping OpenSSL is possible before those two are replaced.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- Dead code counted as translation or replacement work: IANA tzcode (2,859 lines) and ob_ctype_uca.cc (7,129 lines) are not compiled. The live charset surface is only the three utf8mb4_general_ci, utf8mb4_bin and binary collations. (evidence: src/oblib/oblib_source_inventory.bzl, which CMake reads via CMakeLists.txt:65 and tools/cmake/emit_bazel_source_inventory.py, lists only 7 charset files and no localtime.c. No file includes localtime.c, tzfile.h or ob_ctype_uca.cc. ob_ctype_uca.cc:24 includes lib/charset/ob_ctype_uca_tab.h, which git ls-files does not track. ob_charset.cpp:2080-2088 returns only ob_charset_utf8mb4_general_ci, ob_charset_utf8mb4_bin or ob_charset_bin.)
- About 300k lines of embedded data and generated code in dependency-adjacent directories are missing from the size split: the IK Analyzer dictionary and the generated SIMD/scalar bit-packing kernels. The dictionary should become a data file; the kernels should be regenerated or replaced (for example by the bitpacking crate), and the format they write to disk must stay bit-exact. (evidence: src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp is 276,043 lines of string literals (static const char *main_dic[] = {"一一列举", ...}). src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_unalign_simd_bp_func.cpp has 12,705 lines and ob_generated_scalar_bp_func.cpp has 11,594, generated by src/oblib/lib/codec/generate_simd_bitpakcing.py and generate_scalar_bitpakcing.py. ob_fast_delta.h:20 cites Lemire et al.)
- OpenSSL is needed by S2, libcurl and gRPC as well as by seekdb's own 9 files. This answers the survey's open question: none of these libraries brings its own TLS or bignum code, so OpenSSL 1.1.1u cannot be dropped while any of them stays. (evidence: On the installed deps (read-only): nm deps/3rd/.../lib64/libs2.a shows 26 undefined BN_* symbols (BN_add, BN_CTX_new, BN_exp, ...). nm libcurl.a shows undefined SSL_CTX_* and SSL_*. nm lib/grpc/libgrpc.a shows SSL_CTX_new undefined (1 U, 0 T), so there is no bundled BoringSSL. src/oblib/CMakeLists.txt:290-291 links libssl.a and libcrypto.a for all of these.)
- Dependency versions and behavior already differ by platform, so the 'bit-exact / behavior-exact' targets the survey sets are per platform today. Windows ignores the relaxed JSON flags and has full ICU data; Android runs an older vsag. (evidence: deps/init/oceanbase.android.arm64.deps lists vsag 0.18.0 (Linux uses 1.1.0), libcurl 8.2.1 and libxml2 2.10.4. deps/init/oceanbase.windows.x86_64.deps lists upstream devdeps-rapidjson, and ob_json_parse.h:26-32 defines the fork flags only under _WIN32. src/oblib/CMakeLists.txt:276-278 links icudt.lib on Windows, while line 299 links libicustubdata.a elsewhere. malloc_hook is Linux-only (src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt:33-40).)
- Third-party libraries are wired into two build systems, and CMake takes its source lists from Bazel files. Every crate swap or C++ shim has to be added to Bazel as well as CMake, and Bazel has no rules_rust. (evidence: MODULE.bazel:10-18 declares only rules_cc and platforms, plus the seekdb_3rd_headers repository. src/observer/BUILD.bazel:529 references @seekdb_3rd_headers//:lib/liblzma.a, and 23 files reference seekdb_3rd_headers. CMakeLists.txt:65 reads src/oblib/oblib_source_inventory.bzl. deps/external/bazel/jemalloc.bzl and rust/BUILD.bazel drive Cargo from Bazel. On top of this, the Windows build takes its libraries from vcpkg (VCPKG_LIB_DIR in src/oblib/CMakeLists.txt).)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's facts can mostly be trusted. Almost every count I re-ran with a different method came out the same or within pattern noise: manifests, vendored line counts, gRPC stubs, grammars, geo files, sqlite and vsag wrapper sizes, the test-suite size, and the commit hashes and dates. Every cited path and line range I opened showed what the survey says it shows. The errors are in classification and in scope rather than in fabrication, and some of them matter for the decision. The survey counts 10k lines of dead code (tzcode, ob_ctype_uca.cc) as work. It treats libeasy as third-party. It misses about 300k lines of embedded dictionary data and generated codec kernels. It overstates the URL codec's use of ICU, the gRPC streaming count, and how widely Boost is used (the bg:: alias adds 308 uses). It presents ob-sanity and the malloc hook as always on, when they are optional and Linux-only. It reads the Windows-only flag definitions as the fork's. It leaves the OpenSSL question open, although the installed archives answer it: S2, curl and gRPC all need OpenSSL. The structure call (boost::geometry and vsag force C++ shims or a redesign; most C libraries can be swapped behind their wrappers) holds up. relaxed-rapidjson should drop to a bounded parser rewrite, and the OpenSSL retirement has to wait for S2, curl and gRPC. The survey is good enough to use for which libraries are used and where. Its size figures should be recomputed with the dead code removed and the data and generated blocks moved to their own category before anyone uses them to estimate effort.


## AREA: pain
**What it is:** This area is the evidence that C++ is costing seekdb something concrete, not a single module: memory-safety and concurrency bugs in this repo's history and in the upstream issue tracker, the defensive code the source carries because of C++, and build pain. It also covers the counter-evidence, meaning the things C++ does well here that a Rust version would have to match.

**Size split:** This area cuts across the whole tree, so the split below is for the whole tree. It is followed by the size of the defensive code that exists because of C++.

Whole tree: 2,777,368 lines of git-tracked .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.y/.l. I re-summed this with `git ls-files -- '*.h' '*.hpp' '*.cpp' '*.cc' '*.c' '*.ipp' '*.y' '*.l' | xargs wc -l`.
- **Data (about 317.6K lines):**
  - src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp: 276,043
  - src/oblib/common/timezone/ob_timezone_info.cpp: 39,989 (38,956 of those lines are `{...}` table rows)
  - src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_to_pinyin_tab.cpp: 1,528
- **Generated but tracked (about 83.6K lines):**
  - ob_errno.cpp/.h (share and lib): 18,824
  - src/share/system_variable/ob_system_variable_init.cpp: 10,048
  - src/sql/session/ob_system_variable_factory.cpp/.h: 15,940
  - ob_sys_var_class_type.h: 750
  - src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_{unalign_simd,scalar}_bp_func.*: 24,406
  - src/oblib/grpc/standbyservice*.pb*: 12,345
  - src/share/geo/ob_vector_tile.pb-c.*: 651
  - tools/ob_error/src/os_errno.*: 640
  - These were found by scanning the first 3 KB of each tracked file for 'generated by / DO NOT EDIT' and checking the hits by hand.
- **Grammar inputs (hand-written, turned into C at build time):** .y/.l files, 24,585 lines.
- **Vendored third-party (about 29.7K lines):**
  - src/oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8/zstd_src: 25,184
  - xxhash/wyhash/murmur in src/oblib/lib/hash_func: 1,473
  - libeasy remnants in src/oblib/easy: 3,053
- **Hand-written:** what is left, about 2.35M lines.

Defensive code that exists because of C++ (sizes from wc -l on tracked files):
- signal handlers, backtrace and abort (src/oblib/lib/signal/*, src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_backtrace.*, src/oblib/lib/ob_abort.*, src/observer/ob_signal_handle.*): 1,419
- Sanity memory instrumentation (src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_jemalloc_sanity.cpp, ob_memory_sanity.h, ob_sanity_libc_wrap.cpp, bazel/sanity_abort.cpp): 932
- leak checkers (src/share/leak_checker/ob_leak_checker.h, src/storage/tx_storage/ob_tx_leak_checker.h): 416
- memory dump (src/oblib/lib/alloc/memory_dump.*): 1,041
- the two allocator directories src/oblib/lib/alloc and src/oblib/lib/allocator: 69 files, 16,756 lines. These include the magic-code checks and leak printing, but mostly they are real allocator code.
- lock-free memory reclamation that a Rust version would still need:
  - src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_*: 1,862
  - ob_hazard_ref.*: 397
  - qsync files: 859

**Summary:** - **Pain shows up much more in the tracker than in the commit log.** Only about 20 distinct commits in the last year are memory-safety fixes that safe Rust would rule out: 10 use-after-free or lifetime bugs, 1 double free, 4 out-of-bounds or wrong-type casts, 2 uninitialized reads and 3 null dereferences. That is roughly 27 commits once the mirrored obdev + PR pairs are counted. The upstream tracker shows more: 92 of 840 issues have crash, core or SIGSEGV titles (18 still open), 11 name memory_sanity_abort, and 54 come from Sanity-instrumented builds.
- **Most of the real memory bugs are lifetime bugs across an async boundary.** Memory allocated from a request- or statement-lifetime arena (an ObIAllocator), or a borrowed ObString, is handed to a task that runs later on another thread. Examples are the index schema passed to an async DDL task, the serialized buffer passed to the async PX SQC, and ObStrings copied shallowly into an embedding task. Issue #920 is an open SIGSEGV on the same PX dispatch_sqcs path.
- **The code has checks that look defensive but do not protect:**
  - ObArray::operator[] logs an out-of-range index and returns data_[idx] anyway (ob_array.h:323-335, commented 'dangerous').
  - ObIArrayWrap::at() relies on OB_ASSERT, which is `(void)(x)` under NDEBUG (ob_macro_utils.h:707-708).
  - Sort comparators carry an error code in ret_. After an error they must still return a consistent ordering, or std::sort runs past the end of the array. Commit 5aa611c00 fixed this, and issue #528 is still open. There are 31 such comparators in 18 files.
- **The defensive code is large:**
  - Null checks: OB_ISNULL 35,376 and OB_NOT_NULL 7,345.
  - 'Cannot happen' branches: `ret = OB_ERR_UNEXPECTED` appears 27,379 times.
  - Checks on two-phase init state: OB_NOT_INIT 4,392 and OB_INIT_TWICE 943.
  - A whole Linux-only Sanity build mode (build.sh sanity, ENABLE_SANITY) built on an external LLVM pass that adds shadow-memory checks.
  - Magic codes in allocator headers to catch double frees and corruption (439 mentions).
  - Leak printers in the slice allocators, plus signal and coredump handlers.
- **The error-code style maps directly onto Rust.** Code like `int ret = OB_SUCCESS` (37,286) and `OB_FAIL` (99,998) turns into Result and `?` mechanically, so it helps rather than blocks.
- **Several classes of bugs here Rust would not remove:**
  - Deadlocks: the PX-budget hold-and-wait and the PX fast-SQC self-deadlock.
  - Stack overflows.
  - Logical leaks into long-lived arenas, such as the PL expression contexts.
  - Engine hangs: 47 issue titles match hang, deadlock, stall or stuck, though some of those are installer UI hangs.
- **Build pain is real but moderate.** One commit cut a unity translation unit from 455 s to 53 s using extern templates. 47 commits fix compile errors (18 of them platform ports), and breakage that only appears in non-unity builds recurs.
- **Counter-evidence that Rust must match:**
  - 10,581 SSE/AVX intrinsic calls in 22 files, part of them in 24K lines of generated bit-packing code.
  - 97 allocator classes, with ObIAllocator passed around 8,577 times and per-label memory accounting.
  - Hazard-pointer and qsync memory reclamation in the KV cache.
  - Zero-copy ObString views (17,716 mentions).
- **The existing Rust crate shows where unsafe gathers.** rust/sql-nio already has 186 `unsafe` in 8,448 lines, concentrated at the C ABI. So during a mixed-language period the boundary gives back much of the safety.
- **Overall:** the case for leaving C++ rests on a steady stream of crash reports and a large amount of defensive code, not on a flood of memory-bug commits. The memory-bug commits alone are thin, about 4% of fix commits.

**Structure call (surveyor):** Most of the bugs Rust would remove sit in how memory is owned and handed off. Objects and strings are allocated from an ObIAllocator arena (request, exec_ctx or schema arena), or held as a borrowed ObString, and then passed to another thread, an async task or a cache that outlives the arena. The fixes in this history patch that one place at a time: shared_ptr, a copy into a longer-lived allocator, a new ref_cnt_, or a lock around arena rotation.

A one-to-one translation that keeps ObIAllocator&, raw ObString views, placement new and the hand-counted Guard/Handle classes would compile only with unsafe or raw pointers everywhere. That keeps exactly the bugs this area lists, so it gains little.

The error-code style (int ret / OB_FAIL / OB_ISNULL) does translate mechanically into Result, `?` and Option. That part is a plain one-to-one win.

The KV-cache hazard pointers, qsync, the intrusive lists and the SIMD codecs stay unsafe-heavy under either approach. They can be translated one-to-one as a small, audited block of unsafe code.

So getting the safety payoff forces a redesign of arena ownership and of how data is passed to async tasks. Everything else can be translated directly.

The files that force the call: src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_iallocator.h, src/oblib/lib/allocator/page_arena.h, src/oblib/lib/string/ob_string.h, src/sql/engine/ob_exec_context.h, src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp, src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.cpp, src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the main call. The bugs Rust would remove sit where arena or borrowed memory is handed to later or other-thread work, so a translation that keeps ObIAllocator&, raw ObString views and hand-counted guards buys little. The code confirms it. In ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp dispatch_sqcs, a lambda passed to ex_rpc::async_call captured a pointer into exec_ctx's arena (fixed with std::shared_ptr in b4ad89151). In Rust, a Send + 'static bound on that closure would reject this at compile time. eda242c9a is the same shape: a request-arena index_schema_ used by an async DDL task. I have three qualifications. (1) 'Everything else can be translated directly' is too strong. ObString (about 17.7K uses, src/oblib/lib/string/ob_string.h) and ObIAllocator& parameters (8,577) are everywhere, not only at async boundaries, so choosing between lifetimes, owned buffers and Arc touches most signatures in the codebase, as the survey's own hard_parts admits. Arena-scoped borrows (&'arena, bumpalo-style) are idiomatic Rust; what must change is the handoff, not the arenas. (2) Shared fields are plain types changed through ATOMIC_* macros over __atomic builtins (src/oblib/lib/atomic/ob_atomic.h:41-51; 3,051 uses vs 26 std::atomic). Rust needs Atomic* field types, so a field-by-field retyping is needed even in 'direct' code. eda242c9a's non-atomic done_ flag shows the risk. (3) Two cited files are stale. src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp was deleted in 71937e637 (2026-07-27); the intrusive worker list now lives at src/oblib/lib/thread/ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp:162. src/share/vector_index no longer exists; the embedding and refresh tasks are in src/query/vector and src/observer/vector_index. The unsafe islands the survey names (src/share/cache hazard code, ob_qsync.h, SIMD) are correct, and 54123f13e shows that bugs inside those islands would remain in Rust.

**Size split check (verifier):** I re-summed the tree with git ls-files -z ... | xargs -0 cat | wc -l and got 2,777,368 lines, the same as the survey. The biggest files match: ob_ik_dic.cpp 276,043, ob_timezone_info.cpp 39,989, ob_expr_to_pinyin_tab.cpp 1,528. .y/.l files are 24,585. The generated-but-tracked files add up to 83,604: ob_errno 18,824, sys var init 10,048, factory 15,940, class_type 750, codec generated 24,406, grpc 12,345, vector_tile pb-c 651, os_errno .cpp/.h 640. A header scan for 'generated by / DO NOT EDIT' found nothing large that is missing; inner_table schema .cpp files are produced at build time and are not tracked. Vendored code: the whole zstd_1_3_8 directory is 25,827 lines (the survey counted only the zstd_src subdir, 25,184), easy is 3,053, and hash_func is 1,546 in total (1,473 for the three hashes). So about 2.35M hand-written lines holds. The defensive-code sizes also reproduce: signal 1,419, sanity 932, leak checkers 416 (130+286), memory_dump 1,041, the two alloc dirs 16,756, hazard 1,862, ob_hazard_ref 397, qsync 859. One classification is wrong. memory_dump.{h,cpp} feeds the per-label memory virtual table (friend observer::ObAllVirtualMemoryInfo), so it is operational memory accounting that a Rust version needs too, not C++-only defense. The signal and coredump handlers are also still needed in Rust for panics, aborts and unsafe code. So units_of_work's plan to drop or shrink about 3,800 lines is too optimistic.

**Units of work:** Pain is not a translation unit in itself. It points at file groups where a rewrite pays off most:
1. The arena-to-async handoff sites: src/sql/engine/px (dfo scheduler, rpc processors), the DDL async task args in src/share/ob_rpc_struct.h, the embedding and vector-index tasks under src/query/vector and src/share/vector_index, and schema-manager arena rotation in src/share/schema. That is about 6-8 file groups.
2. Ref-counted caches: src/share/cache (22 files, 8,356 lines) and src/sql/plan_cache.
3. Thread pools and intrusive worker lists: src/oblib/lib/thread and src/observer/omt.
4. The container accessors in src/oblib/lib/container, which should become bounds-checked slices.

The defensive infrastructure is about 3,800 lines (signal 1,419, sanity 932, leak checkers 416, memory_dump 1,041). It would be dropped or shrunk rather than translated.

The error-code code style (OB_FAIL/OB_ISNULL across about 2.35M hand-written lines) is a mechanical conversion rule applied inside every file, not a unit of its own.

**Public-surface tests:** The public tests that exist today are the mysqltest-style suites:
- tools/deploy/mysql_test: 283 .test and 305 .result files (190 of the .test files are under test_suite/, 93 under t/). The biggest suites are geometry (42), vector_index (22), fork_table (18) and fts_index (15).
- tools/obtest: 509 .test files.
- The C++ unit tests were all deleted in 45cbbe9e1 on 2026-08-21, so none of the regression unit tests added by crash fixes survive (65997991e's test_tablet_create_mds_ctx is one example).

Only 8 tracked .test files mention crash/core/SIGSEGV, among them tools/deploy/mysql_test/t/range.test, test_suite/delete/t/delete_from_mysql.test and tools/obtest/t/stanby/failover_switchover_reentry.test. The suites that found most of the upstream crashes (array3_mysql_sqlqa, array_func2_mysql_sqlqa, gb2312_cp932_sqlqa, mysql8_t_1, split_part_keyvalue_sqlqa, window_function_mysql and so on) are not tracked in this repo (git ls-files | grep gave 0 for each), so the public tree does not guard against those crashes coming back.

Some tests reach into internals, and they would break unless a Rust version keeps the same hooks:
- tools/obtest: 193 tests use debug_sync injection points, 340 read __all_virtual_* tables and 16 use errsim.
- tools/deploy/mysql_test: 9 tests use debug_sync and 9 read __all_virtual tables.

A crash-free Rust core would still need Sanity-like stress runs (54 issues came from Sanity builds) plus the internal sqlqa suites to show that it actually removed these bugs.

**Hard parts:**
- Memory owned by an arena and borrowed across threads. Code passes ObIAllocator& 8,577 times, and objects are built with placement new (2,910) inside request, exec_ctx or schema arenas. Rust would need lifetimes tied to each arena, or owned or Arc'd buffers wherever a value crosses into async work. See src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_iallocator.h, src/oblib/lib/allocator/page_arena.h and src/sql/engine/ob_exec_context.h; the bugs at those boundaries are eda242c9a, b4ad89151 and 752f5950d.
- ObString is a pointer plus a length with no owner, and it appears 17,716 times. Deciding for each use whether it becomes &'a [u8], an owned Vec or an Arc slice is a whole-codebase decision; 9d24b8807 is a dangling-copy bug from this. See src/oblib/lib/string/ob_string.h.
- Per-label memory accounting and custom allocators: 97 Ob*Alloc* classes, ObMemAttr/ObModIds labels (872 + 791 uses), and memory limits enforced per label or per context (src/oblib/lib/alloc/*, src/oblib/lib/allocator/*, 16,756 lines). Stable Rust has no per-collection allocator parameter (allocator_api is still unstable, as far as I know), so labels would have to be threaded through by hand or via a global allocator with thread-local labels.
- Lock-free memory reclamation: the KV-cache hazard pointer and version domains (src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_{domain,pointer,version}.*, 1,862 lines), ob_hazard_ref.* (397 lines) and qsync (src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_qsync.h, src/oblib/lib/lock/ob_qsync_lock.*, ob_bucket_qsync_lock.*). These need unsafe code or crates like crossbeam-epoch, and the UAF in 54123f13e came from exactly this code.
- Intrusive linked lists and queues (ObDLink/ObDList 335 mentions, ObLink/ObSpLinkQueue 268), for example workers_.add_last(&w->worker_node_) in src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp. The thread-pool UAF and double free were in this code (40fcfa901, 328253969 in ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp).
- Hand-counted references and RAII guards: 145 Handle classes, 158 Guard classes, and 215 inc_ref / 306 dec_ref or revert calls, for example the plan cache nodes in src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp and the tablet handles under src/storage/meta_mem/. Moving them to Arc or Drop changes when objects get freed, and the plan cache and PS cache lifetime fixes in e66776e2d depend on that timing.
- SIMD intrinsics: 10,581 _mm*_ call sites in 22 files (src/oblib/lib/codec, src/data_plane/api/data_plane/vector, src/storage/blocksstable/encoding, src/sql/engine/expr). They map onto std::arch in unsafe #[target_feature] functions, and the 24K lines of generated bit-packing code in src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_*_bp_func.cpp would need their generator ported.
- Macro systems that generate code: 2,941 function-like macros and 15,450 #defines, including the OB_UNIS serialization macros (1,991), TO_STRING_KV (3,377) and the log macros. They would become derive or proc macros, which is a rewrite rather than a line-by-line translation.
- Comparators that carry an error state (31 sites in 18 files, for example src/sql/engine/sort/ob_sort_op_impl.cpp:401-408). In Rust, sort_by needs a total order and an error cannot leave the closure, so these become a sort that can fail, or a pre-pass that validates the data first.

**Pain instances:**
- 54123f13e (2026-06-10): the KV-cache hazard-version retire check skipped version protection, so nodes still held by readers were freed. A use-after-free crash in src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.cpp.
- eda242c9a (2026-06-22): ObCreateIndexArg.index_schema_ was allocated from the request arena, which was freed while the async DDL task still ran (UAF). The same commit fixed async_call dropping its result and a non-atomic done_ flag in src/share/ob_rpc_struct.h and src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp.
- b4ad89151 / bc31e03f4 (2026-06-22/23): the PX SQC serialize buffer outlived its owner and was moved to std::shared_ptr (src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp). Issue #920, still open, is a SIGSEGV on the same dispatch_sqcs async callback.
- 40fcfa901 (2026-05-18): a worker use-after-free and a missing lock when adding a worker to an intrusive list (ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp, src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp).
- 328253969 (2026-05-26): a worker double free in the simple thread pool and resource manager.
- 6bd81a07b (2026-02-03): a use-after-free in the RPC memory pool, in the local procedure call path (that file has since been removed).
- e66776e2d / 6da784ac1 (2026-08-19/20), found by Sanity: a plan cache object was used after its last node reference was released, a PS cache stats callback dereferenced a destroyed ps item, and the skip-index output used the wrong buffer length (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp, ob_ps_cache_callback.h, src/observer/virtual_table/ob_table_columns.cpp).
- 9d24b8807 (2025-12-04): ObString fields were copied shallowly into an async embedding task and later dangled. The crash was in databuff_print_key_obj.
- 9bb15300f and ea6657904 (2025-12-04): an embedding task could be freed while still queued, fixed by adding ref_cnt_ with retain/release. A refresh task was freed before its ctx_ was used.
- dd899675c (2026-08-23): a schema-manager arena was rotated or reset while a fallback schema was being built from it. Fixed with the refresh mutex in src/share/schema/ob_multi_version_schema_service.cpp.
- 5aa611c00 (2026-04-09): the comparator's error state broke strict weak ordering, so std::sort ran past the array bounds. Issue #528, still open, is a memory_sanity_abort in the same comparator.
- fe79a0dd6 (2026-08-06, issue #1261): a negative primary key gave a negative modulo index, and ObSEArray access went out of bounds (SIGSEGV) in src/observer/change_stream/ob_change_stream_dispatcher.cpp.
- a44b2526f (2025-12-19): a crash from a static_cast to the wrong type in src/sql/das/iter/ob_das_iter_utils.cpp.
- 1e4f589b8 (2026-04-20): uninitialized vector pointers crashed on Windows. b8eafbae8 (2026-01-29): an extra context map was used while uninitialized.
- c68c6749a, a76f9168b, 65997991e: null-pointer cores. In 65997991e, a template tuple-index versus binding-id mismatch made MDS deep_copy_buffer_ctx return success with a null copy, and log replay hit SIGSEGV.
- Defensive code: 35,376 OB_ISNULL, 27,379 'ret = OB_ERR_UNEXPECTED' and 4,392 OB_NOT_INIT plus 943 OB_INIT_TWICE state checks.
- Allocator magic-code and double-free checks in src/oblib/lib/alloc/alloc_struct.h:277-286, malloc_hook.cpp and ob_iallocator.h.
- The Sanity build mode (build.sh sanity; CMakeLists.txt:223-260; 932 lines of sanity source).
- Leak printers in ob_slice_alloc.cpp, ob_vslice_alloc.h:244 and ob_hashutils.h, plus ObBaseLeakChecker and ob_tx_leak_checker.h.
- Signal and coredump handlers in src/oblib/lib/signal/ob_signal_handlers.cpp.
- ObArray::operator[] in src/oblib/lib/container/ob_array.h:323-335 logs an out-of-range index and then reads anyway.
- Leak fixes, partly Rust-relevant: 93bcb851d (sparse vector), 2f10d62cf (ai service guard), beeb04d0d (ServerObjectPool), 62b9d1a81 (parallel mem), c4742e663 (static SQL buffer). Issue #938 is a vslice ref_=400 that was never decremented. Leaks in safe Rust would still be possible through Arc cycles or long-lived arenas, as in 752f5950d.

**Open questions:**
- Whether RelWithDebInfo release builds really define NDEBUG, so that OB_ASSERT bounds checks are off in production. The build_release/ flags were deliberately not inspected.
- How many of the 92 upstream crash issues were fixed through commits whose messages give no memory keyword. Most obdev commits mirror an internal tracker (dima) and use short messages, so the ~20 memory-safety fixes counted here are probably an undercount. Linking issue numbers to commits would need the internal tracker or PR metadata.
- The root causes of the open crashes: #920 (PX dispatch_sqcs SIGSEGV), #528 (sort comparator), #1268 (null pointer in replay), #1163, #836, #992 and #976. Their bodies show sanity or SIGSEGV stacks but not whether the cause is memory safety or logic.
- Whether the removal of all unittest/ C++ tests in 45cbbe9e1 (2026-08-21) was intended or an accident of a squash. The commit message describes only a decimal-precision fix.
- How long a clean full build takes now. The timing build is running in build_release/, and only the per-unit numbers from e2b7c7a8f were available.
- How much of the Sanity pass (libsanitypass.so from devtools) and the internal sqlqa suites is available outside the company, which decides whether Rust's memory safety can be checked against the same stress workload.
- Whether the 47 hang/deadlock/stall/stuck issue titles include memory-corruption causes. Deadlocks and stalls as such are outside what Rust prevents.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- Most of the recent memory-safety fixes, and a deadlock, came within days of the team's own large refactors, not from old C++ code. Both 'thread pool' bugs came from the adaptive worker pool. The PX UAF and self-deadlock came from the RPC framework removal. This matters both ways: Rust's type rules (Send/'static closures, owned JoinHandles) would have caught these handoffs, and a whole-codebase rewrite is the largest refactor of all. (evidence: The adaptive worker pool 91f80b549/bd7c96490 (2026-05-14) introduced do_add_worker; 40fcfa901 (05-18) fixed a UAF and 328253969 (05-26) a double free in that code. The RPC framework removal ee560bb45 (2026-06-12, 1,142 files) added the sync_call SQC path; cb5a24ce5 (06-16, whose body names that refactor) fixed the deadlock; bc31e03f4/b4ad89151 (06-22/23) fixed the resulting UAF; eda242c9a (06-22) fixed the request-arena UAF. Found with git log -S'do_add_worker' and git log -S'ser_buf' -- src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp.)
- The crash reports come from a short burst during the Windows/macOS/Android porting and Sanity-QA push, not a steady stream, and the rate is falling. The two 'still open' crashes the survey leans on (#920, #528) were built before their matching fix commits and are probably fixed but not closed. (evidence: Of 90 crash-type issues, 65 were filed in 2026-03/04 (when 461 of all 840 issues were filed), 58 by one account, and 51 name Windows, macOS or Android. After that: 7 in Jun, 5 in Jul, 2 in Aug, 0 in Sep. 6 titles are duplicates. #920 BUILD_TIME is Jun 18, before b4ad89151 on Jun 23. #528 was built Apr 9 23:40, before 5aa611c00 at 16:27 UTC that day.)
- The main build is Bazel, not CMake. It defines NDEBUG explicitly, which settles open question 1: OB_ASSERT bounds checks are off in production. It also compiles all C/C++ with -Wno-everything and -fno-strict-aliasing, so the compiler warnings that would flag UB-prone code are turned off. (evidence: bazel/seekdb_build_config.bzl:32-37 and 66-69 ('-fno-strict-aliasing', '-Wno-everything', with a comment that warning tightening is follow-up work); lines 86 and 135 ('NDEBUG'); .bazelrc:9 'build --compilation_mode=opt'. The CMakeLists.txt:99-101 error message calls CMake 'The compatibility CMake build'.)
- There is no standard sanitizer (ASan/TSan/UBSan) and no data-race detector in the tracked build. The only memory checker (Sanity) needs internal devtools binaries. Concurrency relies on ATOMIC_* macros applied to plain fields, so nothing checks which fields are shared between threads. Rust's Send/Sync and Atomic* types would cover exactly this gap, which the survey did not weigh. (evidence: git grep for 'fsanitize|ENABLE_ASAN|ENABLE_TSAN|__SANITIZE_ADDRESS__' finds nothing. CMakeLists.txt:232-239 requires ${DEVTOOLS_DIR}/lib64/libsanitypass.so and include/sanity/sanity.h. src/oblib/lib/atomic/ob_atomic.h:41 `#define ATOMIC_LOAD(x) __atomic_load_n((x), __ATOMIC_SEQ_CST)`: 3,051 ATOMIC_* uses vs 26 std::atomic. eda242c9a had to make a plain done_ flag atomic.)
- Counter-evidence the survey left out: seekdb treats running out of memory as a normal error it recovers from, under per-tenant and per-label limits. Stable Rust's Vec, Box and String abort the process when an allocation fails. A port needs fallible-allocation APIs (try_reserve) or custom collections on every allocating path, not just labels passed through by hand. Build time is similar: e2b7c7a8f's fix was for duplicated template instantiation, and Rust generics have the same monomorphization cost, so 'Rust would help build times' is not supported. (evidence: `ret = OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED` appears 4,570 times in src/. OB_ISNULL on allocation results is used throughout (for example the b4ad89151 diff, which checks OB_ISNULL(ser_buf.get())). ENABLE_500_MEMORY_LIMIT is defined in bazel/seekdb_build_config.bzl. The e2b7c7a8f body: 'Remove cross-TU duplicate template instantiation'.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's numbers are mostly reliable: 20 of 24 claims confirmed on re-count, and the whole-tree line count, the defensive-pattern counts, the Sanity build details, the sql-nio unsafe count and the unit-test deletion all reproduce exactly or within pattern noise. Every cited hash exists and matches its description. Its interpretation is weaker, and it leans toward overstating the pain in three ways. First, some of its 'safe Rust would prevent' list does not belong there: d08a76bce was a VSAG config/logic bug caught as an error, and 54123f13e sits in code that would still be unsafe Rust. Second, the '439 MAGIC guards', '31 comparators' and '10,581 hand-tuned SIMD calls' figures are inflated by unrelated or generated code. Third, the two 'still open' crash issues it cites were most likely fixed by commits it already lists. It also misses the most useful pattern in the evidence: the memory bugs cluster right after the team's own big ownership refactors, and the crash tracker is a Mar-Apr porting burst that has since died down, not a steady stream. It read only the CMake build and so missed the Bazel settings that answer its NDEBUG question and show warnings are turned off entirely. Use its counts as given. Treat its classification of which bugs Rust removes as an upper bound of about 17-18 fixes. Re-weigh the conclusion as: modest direct memory-bug evidence, a strong refactor-risk signal, no race tooling, and some Rust counter-costs it did not list (fallible allocation, compile time, still-needed crash and memory-accounting tooling).


## AREA: coupling
**What it is:** How seekdb's modules depend on each other: #include edges at module, subdirectory and file level, link-level edges (a header in one directory whose code is defined in another), and global state that ties modules together (GCTX, server_service<T> slots, get_instance singletons, GCONF, THIS_WORKER). Together these decide whether the C++-to-Rust move can go one piece at a time or must go as a whole.

**Size split:** Scope: every git-tracked file under src/ and rust/ with extension .h .hpp .cpp .cc .c .ipp .inc .y .l .rs. Total: 6,670 files, 2,783,609 lines. Hand-written C/C++: 6,546 files, 2,338,379 lines. Hand-written Rust (rust/sql-nio): 23 files, 8,448 lines. Generated code checked into git: 32 files, 88,107 lines. These are src/share/ob_errno.{h,cpp}, src/oblib/lib/ob_errno.h, src/share/mysql_errno.h, the sysvar outputs of src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py (ob_system_variable_init/alias/meta, ob_sys_var_class_type.h, ob_system_variable_factory.{h,cpp}), src/oblib/grpc/standbyservice*.pb.*, src/share/geo/ob_vector_tile.pb-c.*, src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_*_bp_func.cpp, rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h, and the split compile parts ob_str_datum_funcs_compilation_N.cpp and ob_expr_str_cmp_func_part_N.cpp. Data tables: 3 files, 320,504 lines (src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp 276,043; src/oblib/common/timezone/ob_timezone_info.cpp 39,989; src/oblib/lib/charset/ob_ctype_utf8_tab.h 4,472). Vendored zstd 1.3.8 (src/oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8/zstd_src): 47 files, 25,154 lines. Code that came from libeasy/libev (src/oblib/easy): 19 files, 3,017 lines. Some headers are generated at build time and are not tracked: 42 include lines point at share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema*.h/.ipp, which live under build/generated. Method: a python one-off over `git -C <wt> ls-files src rust` that sorted files by path regex, looked for generator markers in the first 30 lines ("DO NOT EDIT", "generated by"), and found files that lack an OceanBase/seekdb license header (that check found zstd and easy). Correction to the given facts: the 143 tracked .inc files are mysqltest include scripts (tools/obtest/include 62, tools/deploy/mysql_test 46, tools/obtest/t 35), not C++. No quoted C++ include targets a .inc file.

**Summary:** Counting only #include lines, seekdb is close to layered. Across 17 top-level modules, 25,259 resolved include lines form an acyclic graph except for 4 lines in 3 files, which tie sql, storage, rootserver and pl into one cycle. All 4 lines came from commit d51422b54 on 2026-08-31. The repo's own boundary checker reports the same 4 violations, but no CI workflow runs it. That layering is recent (Bazel/module_check work from 2026-08) and is kept by convention only. It is also partly cosmetic: the src/query/api and src/data_plane/api roots moved headers but not code. 109 classes declared under query/api are defined only in src/sql, and 74 storage files use them (the pushdown filter, ObExpr and ObEvalCtx). 59 data_plane/api classes are defined only in storage, and 87 sql files use them (ObTxReadSnapshot, ObDatumRow). So at link level sql and storage call each other, and the table-scan parameter carries raw sql::ObExpr and filter-executor pointers into storage. One level down, each big module is a single cycle: 18 of 19 subdirectory groups in src/sql and 30 of 33 in src/storage. At file level, include cycles are small (29 cycles covering 73 files), because 5,556 forward declarations hide the real dependencies. Every .cpp is heavily tied to the base libraries: the median .cpp pulls in 557 project headers, 443 headers reach at least half of the 2,936 .cpp, and lib/ob_define.h reaches 2,856. Global state is widespread: 1,224 server_service<T>() lookups over 122 types (MTL is gone), 892 GCTX uses, 683 GCONF uses and 623 ::get_instance() calls. Lower layers also call back into the SQL engine at runtime through inner SQL (ObMySQLProxy/ObISQLClient/ObMySQLTransaction). Only a few narrow, C-like seams exist: the Rust sql-nio C ABI (33 functions plus 4 callbacks), the extern "C" parser boundary (ParseNode), logservice (its code reaches only oblib and share), and inner SQL as a SQL-text boundary. My conclusion: base libraries, logservice, parser and network could move piece by piece. The sql+storage+query/data_plane+pl group (about 1.83M lines, including the 276k-line dictionary) has to move as one unit, or its scan and filter boundary has to be redesigned first.

**Structure call (surveyor):** Redesign at one boundary, and module-level translation elsewhere. Rust crates must not depend on each other in a cycle, while modules inside one crate may. The subdirectory cycles inside src/sql (18 of 19 groups) and src/storage (30 of 33) therefore do not force a redesign by themselves: each module can become one crate with modules that mirror its directories. Three things do force a choice. (1) sql and storage call each other at link level through the api roots (C4). The table-scan parameter passes raw sql::ObExpr, ObPushdownOperator and ObPushdownFilterExecutor pointers into storage, and storage evaluates them (C5). So the two can be separate crates only after that boundary is redesigned, for example by passing storage a filter description instead of the SQL engine's objects. The alternative is one large crate of about 1.83M lines. (2) The global service slots (server_service<T>, 1,224 uses over 122 types) and GCTX (892 uses) map mechanically onto Rust statics (OnceLock), but that keeps the nullable, lifecycle-guarded pattern (151 null checks, 113 SERVER_MODULE_SCOPE). Removing it means passing a context struct, which touches every call site. (3) The oblib/lib containers are header-only templates (C16), so no C ABI can hand them to the remaining C++. A bottom-up start must keep the C++ versions alive until the last C++ caller is gone. Files that force the call: src/data_plane/api/data_plane/access/ob_tablet_scan.h, src/query/api/query/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.h with src/sql/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.cpp, src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h, src/share/ob_server_struct.h.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I partly agree. Parts I agree with: (1) The subdirectory cycles inside src/sql (18/19) and src/storage (30/33) do not by themselves force a redesign, because a crate's internal modules may form cycles. (2) Storage really does run SQL-engine code. Non-virtual ObPushdownFilterExecutor::execute_skipping_filter (sql/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.cpp:1451) is called from storage/access/ob_sstable_index_filter.cpp:76. ObExpr::eval is OB_INLINE in query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h:1039, calls through the eval_func_ function pointer (:657) and reads SQL struct fields directly. 544 of 688 storage .cpp transitively include query/api headers. So sql and storage cannot become separate crates as they are. (3) GCTX and the server_service slots map onto statics mechanically; the slots are plain non-atomic pointers (ob_server_runtime.h:127-148). Parts I disagree with or would widen: (a) The group that must move together is larger than stated. Link-level edges also run from sql/storage/rootserver/pl into observer through query/api declarations implemented in src/observer: the vector index code (src/observer/vector_index, 25,210 lines, used by e.g. sql/das/iter/ob_das_hnsw_scan_iter.cpp and storage/ddl/ob_ddl_pipeline.cpp) and ObInnerSQLConnectionAccess (static functions in observer/ob_inner_sql_connection.cpp, used by 12 sql, 3 storage and 4 rootserver files). So observer is not a clean top layer, and 'unit 12 = observer' does not stand at link level. (b) The redesign choice is not only 'pass storage a filter description'. Storage can own a trait or function-pointer interface that sql implements, and eval_func_ already works this way. The cheap-looking alternative, pulling the expression/filter runtime into a lower crate, is costly: ob_pushdown_filter.cpp's include closure holds 230 sql headers and ob_expr.cpp's 124. (c) The bottom-up start is better supported than the survey shows. By the same heuristic, share, oblib/common, oblib/rpc and logservice have 0 classes whose member definitions live only in a higher module, and oblib/lib's single hit (ObArrayHashMap) is a same-name class in storage/fts. It is also harder than the survey shows, because upper modules subclass base-layer classes (ObTimerTask 72, ObDLinkBase 72, ObIKVCacheKey/Value 16/17, ObITask 24 and ObIDag 9 in data_plane, ObIReplaySubHandler 14, ObICheckpointSubHandler 15, AppendCb 8, ObFuncExprOperator 313). A base class moved to Rust cannot be subclassed from C++, so every such class needs a vtable bridge or must stay in C++. That is a larger drag than header-only templates alone.

**Size split check (verifier):** Reproduced. `git ls-files src rust` filtered to .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.inc/.y/.l/.rs gives 6,670 files and 2,783,604 lines by wc -l (the survey's 2,783,609 is 5 lines off, probably files without a trailing newline). Rust: 23 .rs files, 8,448 lines. Data tables: ob_ik_dic.cpp 276,043 + ob_timezone_info.cpp 39,989 + ob_ctype_utf8_tab.h 4,472 = 320,504. zstd_src: 47 source files, 25,154 lines. src/oblib/easy: 19 files, 3,017 lines. 42 include lines point at untracked build/generated inner_table headers (27 + 8 + 7). The 143 .inc files are all mysqltest scripts under tools/, and no src file includes a .inc. The generated-code bucket matches within about 2k lines (85,750 for my pattern set without ob_sys_var_meta.*). One inconsistency: it counts the tiny split wrappers ob_str_datum_funcs_compilation_N.cpp and ob_expr_str_cmp_func_part_N.cpp (25-48 lines each, just a DEF_*_INIT macro call, not generator output) but leaves out the matching ob_datum_funcs_compilation_0..7.cpp. The effect is under 1k lines. Hand-written C/C++ at about 2.34M of 2.78M (84%) stands.

**Units of work:** At module level, after the 4 violating include lines are fixed, 17 modules form a DAG. At link level these units remain: (1) oblib/easy, (2) oblib/lib, (3) oblib/common, (4) oblib/rpc together with the existing rust/sql-nio, (5) oblib/grpc, (6) objit, (7) share, (8) logservice, (9) one group of sql + storage + query + data_plane + pl (1,833,562 lines) that stays together unless the scan/filter boundary is redesigned, (10) standby, (11) rootserver, (12) observer. That is 12 units, one of which holds about 66% of the code. The parser (src/sql/parser plus parse_node.h) and the sql-nio network layer can move separately because they already have C boundaries. Inside a unit, the natural translation unit is a group of files sharing a path stem (header + .cpp + .ipp): 3,825 groups in 326 directories (sql 1,301, storage 801, share 426, oblib/lib 392, observer 267). They must be matched by class/basename across directories, not by path, because 73 api headers and ObPL are implemented in another directory. The 29 file-level include cycles (73 files) each have to move as one batch.

**Public-surface tests:** All 793 .test files are mysqltest scripts that drive the server through SQL, so they test the whole process, not one module. tools/deploy/mysql_test has 283: test_suite/geometry 42, vector_index 22, fork_table 18, fts_index 15, subquery 11, expr 11, type_date 10, global_index 7, trx 5, storage 3, and 93 in t/. tools/obtest/t has 500, grouped by directory: primary_standby 156, storage 63, rootservice 42, trans 33, quick 29, minor_freeze 26, table_redefinition 23. Every DML or query test crosses the sql->storage scan boundary (C5), and the storage, minor_freeze and trans groups also exercise storage/logservice/transaction together. Only 3 tracked gtest files exist (2 in deps/oblib/unittest, 1 in tools/ob_error). No test imports internals, so changing module boundaries cannot break a test. It also means no test can check one migrated module in isolation: a hybrid C++/Rust build can only be checked through the full server.

**Hard parts:**
- SQL expression objects cross into storage as raw pointers. ObVTableScanParam in src/data_plane/api/data_plane/access/ob_tablet_scan.h holds const sql::ExprFixedArray*, sql::ObPushdownOperator* and sql::ObPushdownFilterExecutor*, and storage calls methods on them (src/storage/access/ob_sstable_index_filter.cpp:76 -> src/sql/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.cpp:1451). Storage and sql alias the same objects, so there is no single owner, and lifetimes span both modules.
- Global service slots: template<typename Service> struct ObServerServiceSlot { inline static Service *service_; } in src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h. They are bound and unbound at runtime by src/observer/omt/ob_server_runtime_controller.cpp (83 BIND_SERVICE) and src/standby/standby_module.cpp. Rust needs either OnceLock/ArcSwap statics per type or explicit context passing through 1,224 call sites.
- GCTX (src/share/ob_server_struct.h) is a zero-initialized singleton (MEMSET(this, 0, ...)) of raw pointers to the schema service, SQL proxies, config manager and SQLite pool, used 892 times. It has no clean equivalent in safe Rust without redesign.
- Inner SQL goes back up the stack: ObMySQLProxy/ObISQLClient (src/oblib/common/mysqlclient) -> ObInnerSQLConnection (src/observer/ob_inner_sql_connection.cpp) -> sql -> storage, called from rootserver, share and storage. A migrated lower module still needs a way to call up into whichever language the SQL engine is in at that time.
- Header-only template containers and hash maps in src/oblib/lib/container and src/oblib/lib/hash (ObSEArray, ObIArray, hash maps; 25,749 header lines against 1,042 .cpp lines) are the common types in every api signature (ObIArray appears 290 times in the api headers). They cannot sit behind a C ABI, so every FFI seam needs its own conversion layer.
- Macro frameworks used in every module: OB_FAIL (100,009), USING_LOG_PREFIX (2,347 files), OB_UNIS_VERSION/OB_SERIALIZE_MEMBER (1,991 uses, which fix on-disk and wire formats), TO_STRING_KV (2,676). A per-file translation has to reproduce these before any file compiles.
- Unity builds (216 groups over 2,804 sources; cmake/Utils.cmake:84, and :119 to undef USING_LOG_PREFIX): a file may compile only because an earlier file in its group included what it needs. Translating file by file has to rebuild each file's real imports.
- Classes are split across directories: 73 api headers have their .cpp in sql or storage, and ObPL (src/sql/pl/ob_pl.h) has members defined in both src/pl and src/sql. File-based work units must be keyed by class, not by path.
- FFI conventions any seam must follow, as seen in rust/sql-nio: C++ passes an empty ObString as nullptr plus length 0, so Rust must not call from_raw_parts on it (commit 076eb309b). Reverse callbacks from Rust into C++ (ob_sql_sock_handler_on_* in rust/sql-nio/src/ffi_types.rs:53) are needed, and the crate has 186 `unsafe` in 9,074 lines. Each new seam carries a similar share of unsafe code.

**Pain instances:**
- Commit d51422b54 (2026-08-31) quietly brought back 3 cross-domain includes and 1 upward include (src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_sub_coord.cpp, src/storage/ddl/ob_tablet_slice_writer.cpp, src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_insert_dag.cpp). The boundary is guarded by 4,399 lines of Python in tools/module_check that no CI workflow runs. In Rust, a dependency cycle between crates fails the build, so this checker would only be needed inside a crate.
- src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h: nullable global service slots lead to defensive code at the call sites: 151 null-check lines on server_service<T>() results, 113 SERVER_MODULE_SCOPE guards on g_server_modules_ready, and check_server_runtime_ready() returning OB_SERVER_RUNTIME_NOT_READY. Rust would remove these only if services are passed as owned references or a context. A Rust registry of global slots would keep the Option checks.
- Commit 40fcfa901 (2026-05-18), 'fix worker use-after-free and add missing lock in th_worker': src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp called workers_.add_last(&w->worker_node_) on a shared intrusive list without holding workers_lock_. Rust's Send/Sync rules would reject mutating shared state without the lock. The file was later deleted in 71937e637.
- Commit 6bd81a07b (2026-02-03), 'fix rpc mempool use-after-free issue', in deps/oblib/src/rpc/obrpc/ob_local_procedure_call.h (path since removed). The error path after deliver() touched a request whose memory the callback path could already have freed; the fix drops the return value (IGNORE_RETURN). I inferred the ownership mismatch from the diff; the commit message does not describe it.

**Open questions:**
- What is the exact link-level dependency graph? The class-name match here needs to be confirmed with nm/objdump on the per-module object libraries (for example, how many undefined symbols in storage objects resolve in sql objects). build_release/ was off limits.
- Does any .cpp compile only because of its unity group, relying on includes pulled in by an earlier file? This needs a build with OB_ENABLE_UNITY=OFF.
- How many runtime cycles run through virtual dispatch (ObISQLClient/ObIExecutor, ObNewRowIterator, ObDatumRowIterator, the callbacks from logservice to storage), which neither the include graph nor the definition match can see?
- The generated inner-table schema headers (build/generated/share/inner_table/*) were not inspected. Which modules depend on them beyond the 42 include lines counted?
- Does the embedded form use the same composition root (ObServerRuntimeController) and the same global slots? I did not trace the embedded entry point.
- How costly is redesigning the sql/storage scan boundary (C5)? For example: how many storage files call methods on ObExpr, ObEvalCtx or the filter executors, and could a smaller expression IR replace them without a performance loss?

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- Link-level edges into observer: parts of query/api are implemented in src/observer and called from sql, storage, rootserver and pl, so observer is part of the link-level cycle and does not sit cleanly on top. (evidence: A class-declared-in-X, defined-only-in-Y scan over all module pairs gives 27 query->observer classes. Examples: ObPluginVectorIndexAdaptor (query/api/query/vector/ob_vector_index_adaptor.h, defined in src/observer/vector_index/ob_plugin_vector_index_adaptor.cpp, used by sql/das/iter/ob_das_hnsw_scan_iter.cpp and storage/ddl/ob_ddl_pipeline.cpp), ObVectorIndexParam (used in 16 sql, 2 storage and 1 pl file), and ObInnerSQLConnectionAccess (query/api/query/session/ob_inner_sql_connection_access.h:51, static functions defined in src/observer/ob_inner_sql_connection.cpp, used in 12 sql, 3 storage, 4 rootserver and 1 pl file). src/observer/vector_index is 33 files and 25,210 lines.)
- Upper modules subclass base-layer classes, so a base class moved to Rust would need a C++-side vtable bridge for every subclass, or has to stay in C++. This limits a bottom-up start more than templates do. (evidence: git grep for `public <Base>` in src: ObTimerTask 72 and ObDLinkBase 72 (intrusive list nodes, oblib/lib), ObLink 15, ObIKVCacheKey 16, ObIKVCacheValue 17 (share), ObITask 24 and ObIDag 9 (data_plane/api, subclassed in storage), ObIReplaySubHandler 14 and ObICheckpointSubHandler 15 (share/log/ob_log_base_type.h:236,255), AppendCb 8 (logservice/ob_append_callback.h:54), ObFuncExprOperator 313 (query/api, subclassed by sql expressions).)
- The base layers are clean at link level too, not only at include level. The survey checked link-level edges only for the api roots, so it missed this evidence for its bottom-up units. (evidence: The same definition-location heuristic over all modules: classes declared only in share (1,240), oblib/common (322), oblib/rpc (47) or logservice (133) headers have 0 cases where the member definitions live only in a higher module. oblib/lib has one hit, ObArrayHashMap, which is a different class with the same name in storage/fts/dict/ob_ft_dat_dict.h:39. The only non-api upward pairs are sql->pl 77 (sql/pl headers implemented in src/pl), sql->observer 1 (ObSqlEndTransCb) and data_plane->observer 1 (ObSessionParamSaved).)
- Storage shares struct layout with the SQL engine, not just function calls. A cross-language split at the scan boundary would need identical #[repr(C)] layouts for ObExpr, ObEvalCtx and the datum/vector frames on both sides. Pulling the expression runtime below storage drags a large slice of sql with it. (evidence: ObExpr::eval is OB_INLINE at query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr.h:1039. It dispatches through `EvalFunc eval_func_` (:657) and reads eval_info fields directly, so storage compiles SQL inline code. Storage calls ->locate_datum_for_write( 28 times, ->eval( 21, ->locate_expr_datum( 11 and ->locate_batch_datums( 10. 64 storage files name ObExpr, ObEvalCtx or filter/operator types, and 544 of 688 storage .cpp transitively include query/api headers. The include closure of sql/engine/basic/ob_pushdown_filter.cpp holds 230 sql headers, and sql/engine/expr/ob_expr.cpp's holds 124.)
- logservice is less independent than the survey says. The repo's own boundary checker places it in the same domain as storage, and at runtime it calls back into storage/observer/rootserver through C++ virtual callbacks. (evidence: module_layer_check.py output: 'domains: ... data_plane=src/logservice, data_plane=src/storage'. ObILogHandler::append (logservice/ob_log_handler.h:60) takes share::SCN, palf::LSN& and an AppendCb* callback object. AppendCb is subclassed 8 times, ObIReplaySubHandler 14 and ObICheckpointSubHandler 15 across storage (12 files), observer (4) and rootserver (3). logservice's own .cpp pull in 41 share and 211 oblib/lib headers, so it can move only after, or together with, bridges for those types.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey can be trusted for its counts. I re-ran nearly every number with an independent resolver, git grep or git blame. The module cycle, the 4 offending lines and their commit, the file-level SCCs, the closure sizes, the global-state counts, the depmap_c.py resolution rate and the size split all reproduce exactly or within a few percent. The errors are small: engine->optimizer is 73 lines, not 49; nio.h is included from 10 files, not 8; the serialization-macro file count is 678, not 619; the fan-in list skips a few headers; get_instance 'callers' are spellings, not classes. The one real factual error is C24: the ObPL regex caught static calls as definitions, so the claim that ObPL is implemented in both src/pl and src/sql is false, although the header-in-sql, code-in-pl split is real. The conclusions are sound in direction, but the link-level analysis was run only on the query/api and data_plane/api to sql/storage pairs. That missed the query/api classes implemented in observer (vector index and inner SQL access), which pull observer into the cycle. It also missed the heavy cross-module inheritance of base-layer classes (timers, intrusive lists, KV cache, DAG tasks, log replay/checkpoint handlers). That inheritance makes a bottom-up, piece-by-piece start harder than the survey's 'base libraries could move piece by piece' suggests, even though the base layers are clean at link level. Use the numbers as they are. Before any migration decision, widen the 'must move together' group to include parts of observer, and add the inheritance bridging cost to the bottom-up plan.


## AREA: runtime
**What it is:** The runtime is the layer under SQL and storage that owns threads, memory accounting and allocators, disk IO, the hand-off of network requests to SQL worker threads, timers and the background DAG scheduler, the int-return-code error convention, crash and signal handling, and configuration parameters. In this branch it is already single-tenant and single-node: the TG thread-group framework, MTL tenant lookups and the RPC framework were deleted, and the MySQL network front end is the existing Rust crate rust/sql-nio (mio, no async runtime) called from C++ over a C ABI.

**Size split:** Measured with git ls-files per group, filtered to .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.inc/.def, piped to xargs cat | wc -l (run under bash). Groups: threads/workers (src/oblib/lib/{thread,thread_local,task}, src/observer/omt, src/share/{rc,resource}, src/share/ob_thread_pool.h, ob_occam_thread_pool.h, ob_shared_timer.h, src/oblib/lib/{worker,runtime}.*) 79 files 16,310 lines; memory (src/oblib/lib/{alloc,allocator,resource,rc,objectpool}) 88 files 21,763; locks+queues (src/oblib/lib/{lock,queue}) 40 files 7,926; IO (src/share/io, src/oblib/lib/{file,restore}, src/share/ob_local_device.*, src/share/ob_io_device_helper.*) 27 files 15,050; network glue (src/oblib/rpc, src/observer/ob_srv_network_frame.*, ob_srv_deliver.*, hand-written src/oblib/grpc/ob_grpc_*) 68 files 7,304; DAG scheduler (src/storage/scheduler, src/data_plane/api/data_plane/scheduler) 12 files 8,594; logging (src/oblib/lib/oblog) 28 files 9,192; signals (src/oblib/lib/signal, src/observer/ob_signal_handle.*) 9 files 1,050; config (src/share/config + parameter attr/macro) 25 files 6,576. HAND-WRITTEN TOTAL: 93,765 lines. GENERATED: 31,169 lines = protobuf/grpc src/oblib/grpc/standbyservice*.pb.* 4 files 12,345 + errno src/share/ob_errno.h, src/share/ob_errno.cpp, src/oblib/lib/ob_errno.h 3 files 18,824 (headers say 'DO NOT EDIT ... generated from ob_errno.def', generator src/share/gen_errno.pl). DATA: 3,095 lines = src/share/ob_errno.def 1,914 (1,546 error codes) + src/share/parameter/ob_parameter_seed.ipp 1,181 (289 parameters). VENDORED: src/oblib/easy 19 files 3,017 lines (leftover libeasy headers incl. libev ev.h 838 lines; no .c files remain). Area total 404 files 131,046 lines. Not counted but adjacent: src/observer/mysql (MySQL command processors) 66 files 14,372 lines; existing Rust rust/sql-nio 8,448 .rs lines + include/nio.h 534 lines.

**Summary:** The runtime is about 94k hand-written lines plus 31k generated, 3k data and 3k vendored, and recent commits have already made it simple: the TG thread-group framework (8d49c42e7), multi-tenancy/MTL (03b513560, 3609383cd) and the RPC framework (ee560bb45) are gone. What is left is a plain single-process design: every thread goes through lib::Thread, a Rust sql-nio reactor pushes requests into one priority queue served by a pool of blocking ReqWorker threads, 58 ObTimer objects share one ObTimerService, one DAG scheduler runs compaction/DDL work, and disk IO uses libaio on Linux with condvar waits. That thread and IO model maps directly onto Rust std threads, Mutex/Condvar and channels; nothing in it needs async, and the existing sql-nio crate already uses mio with plain threads. Memory is the part that does not keep its shape: ob_malloc returns NULL at the server or per-ctx hard limit (after first trying to evict KV cache), callers turn that into OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED at 4,976 sites, every container insert returns int (7,135 push_back calls), and most objects live in ObArenaAllocator arenas whose free() is a no-op. Stable Rust collections abort on OOM and the Allocator trait is nightly-only, so a port has to choose between try_reserve-style fallible APIs with custom arena-aware collections, or a global allocator that blocks like today's glibc malloc hook (which already retries forever every 10ms). The sql-nio crate already takes the try_reserve route. Per-thread ambient state (THIS_WORKER, the CURRENT_CONTEXT stack of Flow objects, thread-locals in 126 files, and context macros that read and write the caller's local ret) plus 122 global service slots bound at runtime are what make a line-by-line translation non-idiomatic; in Rust these become explicit context arguments or scoped guards. The error convention is the easiest part: 80,001 `if (OB_FAIL(...)) {` blocks, 89% with empty bodies since 8a8d6f5a8 removed 55,206 LOG_WARN lines, become `?`. But about 6,000 uses of error codes as normal outcomes (OB_ITER_END, OB_ENTRY_NOT_EXIST, OB_HASH_NOT_EXIST, OB_EAGAIN) should become Option or enum results, and the 1,546 codes must stay stable because 142 of 283 CI tests check errors. Two-phase init (is_inited_ in 1,071 files) and locks declared next to the data they guard (at least 546 declarations in 284 files) are defensive patterns Rust would remove, but moving fields under Mutex<T> is a per-class restructuring. Intrusive lists, manual refcounts, raw parent/child DAG graphs, hazard-version reclamation and siglongjmp-based crash recovery need unsafe code or a redesign. The bug history has fixes of exactly the kinds Rust's ownership and Send/Sync rules prevent: a worker list changed without its lock, a worker double free, and arena-lifetime use-after-frees.
