All CI tests are end-to-end mysqltests, so they survive an internal rewrite as long as error codes, parameter names and DEBUG_SYNC point names are preserved.

**Structure call (surveyor):** Split verdict. The process/thread model, timers, IO manager thread layout, signal threads, configuration table and the network hand-off can keep their structure: they are OS threads, queues, condvars and a parameter table, and map onto std::thread, Mutex/Condvar, channels and a declarative macro (or build script) for the 289 parameters, with no need for an async runtime (sql-nio already runs on mio with plain threads). The error convention also keeps its structure mechanically (the OB_FAIL chains become `?`), though error-codes-as-outcomes should become Option/enum. Memory management and the ownership model built on it force a redesign: fallible allocation everywhere (NULL -> OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED, int-returning push_back), polymorphic ObIAllocator where free() may be a no-op, arena-owned object graphs, a thread-local stack of memory contexts driven by macros that write the caller's ret, per-thread THIS_WORKER state, 122 mutable global service slots, locks separate from data, intrusive lists, manual refcounts and a raw-pointer DAG graph. Translating these one-to-one gives unsafe-heavy Rust with raw pointers everywhere; idiomatic Rust needs an explicit context/allocator parameter, arena lifetimes or index-based graphs, Mutex<T>-wrapped state, and a decided OOM policy (fallible try_reserve-style APIs and custom collections for query memory, plus a global allocator that blocks or aborts for everything else). Files that force the call: src/oblib/lib/rc/context.h, src/oblib/lib/allocator/page_arena.h, src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_malloc_allocator.cpp with src/oblib/lib/resource/ob_resource_mgr.cpp, src/oblib/lib/worker.h, src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h, src/data_plane/api/data_plane/scheduler/ob_dag_scheduler.h, src/share/io/ob_io_define.h.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the split verdict, with three changes. (1) The memory half still forces a redesign, but for a partly different reason. The survey's central example (ob_malloc returning NULL at a server-wide limit enforced in ObMemoryMgr::update_hold) is not the default runtime. Linux and macOS builds default to jemalloc (CMakeLists.txt:213-214, deps/external/CMakeLists.txt:17-19, ob_malloc.cpp:147-151), and there ob_malloc/ObMallocAllocator/the malloc hook call je_malloc directly with no limit check (ob_malloc.h:118-125, ob_malloc_allocator.cpp:67-70, malloc_hook.cpp:163-164). What stays is the int/NULL-return convention at about 5k sites, module budgets that return NULL (ob_block_alloc_mgr.h:38-49; memstore, KV cache and vector limits from commit 38bd9c622), polymorphic ObIAllocator with no-op free (page_arena.h:1020), the thread-local context stack with ret-writing macros (context.h:65-67), intrusive lists and manual refcounts. Those still rule out an idiomatic one-to-one translation. But it means a Rust port can do what the C++ already does: jemalloc as the global allocator, aborting on true OOM, with budgets as explicit fallible allocator objects only where query memory is bounded. That is less work than the 'fallible everywhere' reading suggests. (2) The thread side keeps its structure more easily than the hard parts imply. Custom mmap'd stacks are used only on Linux (thread.cpp:84-118); macOS, Android and Windows already use pthread_attr_setstacksize, which std::thread::Builder::stack_size covers. (3) The thread/timer side has more mechanisms than the survey names: ObTimeWheel, ObDedupQueue, ObUniqTaskQueue, ObAsyncTaskQueue, ObDynamicThreadPool, ObMapQueueThreadPool, ObOccamThreadPool, ObLightyQueue, and ObIndependentDag run by ObDDLDagThreadPool (src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_dag_thread_pool.h:29). They can keep their shape, but a port should merge them into a few Rust primitives rather than translate each one. The signal half is smaller than stated because do_with_crash_restore is dead code, so siglongjmp is only in memory_dump.cpp. The network hand-off, errno table and parameter table keep their structure as the survey says.

**Size split check (verifier):** Reproduced exactly with the survey's own method: 404 files and 131,046 lines, of which 93,765 hand-written, 31,169 generated, 3,095 data and 3,017 vendored. Every group matches. I also used git grep -c '' to rank files, and that confirmed the generated protobuf/grpc files are the four largest in the network area. The generated/data/vendored classification is sound: 'DO NOT EDIT' appears only in the standbyservice pb files and the errno outputs, and ob_net_easy.cpp is a hand-written replacement for a few libeasy helpers. The split is narrower than the structure call it supports. It leaves out the fallible, allocator-parameterized containers the memory argument rests on: src/oblib/lib/container (32 files, 12,685 lines) and src/oblib/lib/hash (31 files, 14,106). It also leaves out src/share/cache (22 files, 8,356; the KV cache washer and hazard-version reclamation named in hard_parts), src/oblib/lib/stat plus wait_event (16 files, about 3,359; latch ids), src/oblib/lib/utility (45 files, 15,377; includes the OB_FAIL/OB_SUCC macros), and the process entry points src/observer/ob_server.cpp (2,910), main.cpp (889) and src/share/ob_debug_sync.cpp (1,004). If no other area counts these, the runtime is closer to 170k hand-written lines than 94k.

**Units of work:** The natural translation unit is the header/implementation pair: 236 of them across the 93,765 hand-written lines, from git ls-files over the runtime groups with extensions stripped and sort -u. They fall into 14 directory groups that can be ported and tested as units: (1) thread primitives and timers: src/oblib/lib/{thread,thread_local,task}, 44 files; (2) worker runtime and the place where modules are created: src/observer/omt, src/share/{rc,resource}, src/oblib/lib/worker.*; (3) core malloc: src/oblib/lib/alloc; (4) allocator family: src/oblib/lib/allocator, 36 files; (5) object pools: src/oblib/lib/objectpool; (6) locks and queues: src/oblib/lib/{lock,queue}; (7) IO manager: src/share/io; (8) files and local device: src/oblib/lib/{file,restore}, src/share/ob_local_device.*; (9) C++ side of the network hand-off: src/oblib/rpc, src/observer/ob_srv_*, 13 files that call nio_*; (10) DAG scheduler: 12 files; (11) errno: port the generator gen_errno.pl and regenerate from ob_errno.def instead of translating 18,824 generated lines; (12) logging: src/oblib/lib/oblog; (13) signals: 9 files; (14) config and parameters: 25 files plus the 289-entry table. Groups 3, 4 and 2 have to be designed first, because the allocator/OOM policy and how context gets passed determine the signatures of every other unit and of the 2,498 files that use ObIAllocator.

**Public-surface tests:** CI runs only tools/deploy/mysql_test: 283 .test files (t/*.test plus test_suite/*/t/*.test, per .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py:248-268). tools/obtest holds 500 more .test files, but no CI script refers to them. No C++ runtime unit tests are tracked except deps/oblib/unittest/lib/alloc/test_jemalloc_hook.cpp and deps/oblib/unittest/lib/allocator/test_malloc_backend.cpp; unittest/ contains only .gitignore files. Every CI mysqltest goes through the runtime end to end (sql-nio reactor -> ReqQueue -> ReqWorker -> memory context -> error codes), so all 283 cover it implicitly. Tests that target it more directly: 71 open extra connections ('connect ('), e.g. t/connection.test; 34 use query/transaction timeouts, e.g. t/largetimeout.test, t/trx_timeout.test, t/trx_timeout_bug.test; 43 set 19 distinct parameters with ALTER SYSTEM SET (vector_memory_limit 47 times, _enable_defensive_check 10, debug_sync_timeout 9), e.g. test_suite/config_test/t/config.test; 12 use protocol compression; 3 use SSL; 11 trigger freeze/compaction/DAG work, e.g. test_suite/ddl/t/dump_ddl_mem.test and test_suite/fork_table/t/fork_table_cow.test; 142 check error codes with --error. None exercises out-of-memory: no test expects -4013. Nine files depend on internal DEBUG_SYNC point names through ob_global_debug_sync (e.g. BEFORE_TABLET_FULL_DIRECT_LOAD_MGR_CLOSE, FORK_TABLE_BUILD_DATA); 174 DEBUG_SYNC points exist in 74 source files. These tests survive a rewrite only if error codes and messages, parameter names and those hook names are kept.

**Hard parts:**
- Fallible allocation as the normal path: 4,976 OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED sites and int-returning container ops (src/oblib/lib/container/ob_array.h:304); stable Rust Vec/Box/String abort on OOM and the Allocator trait is nightly-only (assumption from Rust docs), so ports need try_reserve discipline or custom collections such as allocator-api2/hashbrown-style containers.
- Memory accounting and limits inside the allocator, with cache eviction on the failure path: src/oblib/lib/resource/ob_resource_mgr.cpp:54-86 and :201-254, src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_malloc_allocator.cpp; a Rust #[global_allocator] cannot return an error to the caller, only NULL -> handle_alloc_error.
- Polymorphic allocator where free() may do nothing and objects are placement-new'd into arenas without destructors: src/oblib/lib/allocator/page_arena.h:975-1029, src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_iallocator.h; Rust needs arena lifetimes ('a references, bumpalo-style) or indices.
- Thread-local memory-context stack and for-loop macros that assign the caller's ret: src/oblib/lib/rc/context.h:46-73, :700-760.
- Per-thread worker state reached from anywhere: THIS_WORKER (src/oblib/lib/worker.h, Worker::self_ __thread, 396 uses), ObMallocHookAttrGuard thread-local memory attr (src/oblib/lib/alloc/malloc_hook.cpp:79), ObPageManager::thread_local_instance (src/observer/omt/ob_th_worker.cpp).
- Mutable global service slots bound and cleared at runtime: src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h:126-148 (122 types, 1,243 call sites); OnceLock does not support unbinding, so this needs explicit context passing or atomic pointer slots.
- Intrusive lists and CONTAINER_OF: ObDLinkBase (71 files), ObDList (77 files), src/observer/ob_srv_deliver.cpp repost(); worker list in src/observer/omt/ob_server_runtime.h (WList workers_).
- Raw parent/child DAG graph with per-node locks and documented free-ordering rules: src/data_plane/api/data_plane/scheduler/ob_dag_scheduler.h:108-154, :594; src/storage/scheduler/ob_dag_scheduler.cpp (4,782 lines).
- Manual dual refcounts on IO results/requests and condvar waits: src/share/io/ob_io_define.h:268-300 (ObRefHolder), :405-432, :481-511; src/share/io/ob_io_define.cpp:661.
- Hand-written lock-free reclamation (hazard versions, QSync, retire station): src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.cpp, src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_hazard_ref.h, ob_retire_station.h, ob_qsync.h; would need crossbeam-epoch or unsafe.
- Crash recovery with sigsetjmp/siglongjmp across frames (undefined behaviour to skip Rust destructors this way): src/oblib/lib/signal/ob_signal_utils.h:57-82, src/oblib/lib/alloc/memory_dump.cpp:45,69.
- Custom thread stacks from an mmap'd guarded allocator, which std::thread::Builder cannot supply: src/oblib/lib/thread/thread.cpp:84,118, src/oblib/lib/thread/protected_stack_allocator.cpp.
- C++ objects living in Rust-allocated storage during a mixed period: ObSqlSockSession placement-new'd into memory sized by nio_start (src/oblib/rpc/obmysql/ob_sql_nio_server.cpp static_assert alignof <= 16; src/oblib/rpc/obmysql/ob_sql_sock_handler.cpp on_connect).
- Platform ifdefs in runtime code (Linux libaio vs emulation, Windows OVERLAPPED, macOS kqueue/ulock): src/share/ob_local_device.cpp, src/oblib/lib/thread/ob_thread_hook.cpp; _WIN32 572 hits in 246 files across src.

**Pain instances:**
- 40fcfa901 (2026-05-18) 'fix worker use-after-free and add missing lock in th_worker': workers_.add_last on the intrusive worker list ran without workers_lock_ (then src/observer/omt/ob_tenant.cpp acquire_more_worker) and a worker could be reaped before start (deps/oblib/src/lib/thread/ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp). A Mutex<List> that owns the list would not compile without the lock.
- 328253969 (2026-05-26) 'fix: worker double-free and memory hold rollback blocked by limit check': reap_workers() could OB_DELETE a worker still being started in ob_simple_thread_pool.ipp do_add_worker (ownership split between starter and reaper), plus the hold rollback with negative size wrongly failed the limit check in ob_resource_mgr.cpp update_hold/update_ctx_hold.
- eda242c9a (2026-06-22): ObCreateIndexArg.index_schema_ used the request arena, which was freed when the CREATE INDEX request ended while the async DDL task still ran (arena lifetime use-after-free; a borrow tied to the arena lifetime would be rejected); same commit fixed a result lost because async_call filled a local copy.
- b4ad89151 (2026-06-23) 'fix(px): own SQC serialize buffer to avoid UAF' in src/sql/engine/px/ob_dfo_scheduler.cpp: a buffer's owner ended before its user.
- 6bd81a07b (2026-02-03) 'fix rpc mempool use-after-free issue' in deps/oblib/src/rpc/obrpc/ob_local_procedure_call.h (code since deleted with the RPC framework).
- 54123f13e (2026-06-10): kvcache hazard-version retire freed nodes still used by readers because Node::tenant_id_ was never set (src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_hazard_version.cpp, ob_kvcache_map.cpp). This is a logic bug inside hand-written reclamation; Rust would only help by replacing it with a vetted epoch library, not by the borrow checker alone.
- Defensive code Rust constructors/types would remove: is_inited_ checks in 1,071 files with OB_NOT_INIT 4,714 / OB_INIT_TWICE 1,016 hits; OB_INVALID_ARGUMENT 10,384 hits (many are NULL-pointer argument checks); ObINodeWithChild::check_with_lock() marked '// defense check' (src/data_plane/api/data_plane/scheduler/ob_dag_scheduler.h:127); 'free_task must be called together with task_list_.remove, otherwise task will be double freed' (same file :594); 'do not hold dag_net_map_lock_, otherwise deadlock' (:880).

**Open questions:**
- Whether the memory limit holds under MALLOC_BACKEND=jemalloc: the jemalloc branch of ObMallocAllocator::realloc skips update_hold. jemalloc hooks or extent limits configured elsewhere were not checked.
- How many threads a running server actually has, and of which kinds; this needs a live process (thread list or virtual table), not a read-only look at the source.
- Whether the embedded form always runs as a separate server process (run/seekdb.clients, wait_no_client, _Exit in src/observer/ob_server.cpp:1670-1680) or must also load into a host process. This decides whether a Rust port may own signal handlers, the global allocator and panic=abort.
- How much vendored C/C++ code (zstd, ICU and others) depends on the glibc malloc hook for memory accounting. A Rust #[global_allocator] does not see C allocations unless the hook is kept.
- Which of the 500 tools/obtest tests still run against seekdb, since CI does not use them.
- Whether the 206 latch ids and wait-event statistics (ObLatchIds, ObWaitEventIds) are user-visible through virtual tables that tests or users depend on. If so, a Rust Mutex<T> port has to keep that instrumentation.
- Whether ReqWorker latency and fairness (100ms poll, adaptive expansion, mclock IO scheduling in src/share/io/io_schedule) can be matched and checked without a benchmark run. This cannot be settled read-only.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The default allocator backend is jemalloc, and on that path the process-wide memory limit and KV-cache washing are not enforced. Memory limits now live in per-module budgets. This changes the OOM-policy analysis the survey builds its redesign case on, and it answers the survey's open question 1: the limit does not hold under jemalloc. (evidence: deps/external/CMakeLists.txt:17-19 builds seekdb_jemalloc on Linux/macOS non-ASAN; CMakeLists.txt:213-214 sets OB_HAVE_BUNDLED_JEMALLOC; ob_malloc.cpp:147-151 picks JEMALLOC when MALLOC_BACKEND is unset; no tracked script sets MALLOC_BACKEND; ob_malloc.h:118-125 and ob_malloc_allocator.cpp:67-70 call je_malloc/je_realloc without update_hold; ob_resource_mgr.cpp:123-124 alloc_cache_mb also bypasses it; module budgets in ob_block_alloc_mgr.h:38-49 (14 files); commit 38bd9c622 'Refine jemalloc integration and module memory limits (#1262)'.)
- CI runs 272 configured mysqltest cases, not all 283. Two of the survey's examples of runtime-targeting tests are among the 11 excluded, so they give no CI coverage. (evidence: .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py:196-230 reads runtime_configs.psmall.test-set from tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml (272 entries), and discover_cases returns only configured names (:293-296). Excluded: config_test.config, ddl.dump_ddl_mem, fork_table.fork_database_check_names, fork_table.fork_table_sstable, geometry.geometry_ddl_mysql, geometry.geometry_index2_mysql, geometry.st_bestsrid_mysql, update.update2, vector_index.create_table_with_hybrid_vector_index, view.check_privilege, window_function.sqlancer_const_prop_cast.)
- On Linux, Rust code in the process already allocates through the C++ malloc override. The 'which global allocator' question for a mixed C++/Rust period is therefore already settled by the hook. Under jemalloc, a NULL reaches Rust's handle_alloc_error and aborts; under obmalloc, the hook retries forever. A Rust port that installs its own #[global_allocator] must keep the hook or remove it. (evidence: malloc_hook.cpp overrides malloc/free/realloc/memalign/malloc_usable_size, and malloc_hook_extended.cpp:72-195 overrides calloc/aligned_alloc/posix_memalign/valloc/pvalloc. src/observer/CMakeLists.txt links sql_nio and malloc_hook into seekdb (lines 20, 36, 51, 97, 103). git grep global_allocator over rust/ = 0 hits.)
- Many more scheduling mechanisms exist than ObTimer and one DAG scheduler. Each is a separate thread-and-queue design that a port must reproduce or merge, and the survey's thread-type count leaves them out. (evidence: ObTimeWheel (5 files, src/share/ob_time_wheel.cpp), ObDedupQueue (4), ObUniqTaskQueue (4, src/observer/ob_uniq_task_queue.h), ObAsyncTaskQueue (6), ObDynamicThreadPool (ob_dynamic_thread_pool.cpp:245), ObMapQueueThreadPool, ObOccamThreadPool (src/share/ob_occam_thread_pool.h), ObLightyQueue (5), ObIndependentDag plus ObDDLDagThreadPool (src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_dag_thread_pool.h:29). Raw ob_pthread_create threads: ob_base_log_writer.cpp:102, ob_ringbuf_log_writer.cpp:212, ob_server_runtime.cpp:512.)
- Latch and wait-event instrumentation is no longer visible to users. A Rust Mutex<T> port does not need to carry the 206 latch ids, which answers the survey's open question 6. (evidence: src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py:3058 '__all_virtual_latch # removed', :3121 '__all_virtual_system_event # removed', :7440 'GV$LATCH # removed', :7510 'V$LATCH # removed'. No test under tools/deploy/mysql_test queries latch, system_event or session_wait views.)

**Verifier's trust note:** For raw counts and code structure, the survey can be trusted. Almost every count reproduced exactly, or within a few percent, with a different method (line and distinct-file counts instead of -c sums, a Perl pass instead of awk, a function list pulled from nio.h). Every cited path and line number I opened says what the survey claims, and the six bug-fix commits exist with the stated contents. Its weak spot is the memory section, which matters most for the decision. It presents the obmalloc path (server hard limit, KV-cache washing, a malloc hook that retries forever) as how the server behaves, but Linux and macOS builds default to jemalloc, where none of that runs and limits are per-module budgets. The redesign verdict for memory still holds because of the int/NULL-return convention, arenas, context macros and intrusive structures, but the reasoning and the proposed OOM policy have to be restated from the jemalloc default. Smaller problems: several counts come from loose regexes (nio_* calls 61 vs 37, O_DIRECT 76 vs 54, lock declarations 546 vs about 460, context macros 175 vs about 49 ret-writing uses); the CI test count is 272, not 283, and two cited example tests are not run; the sql-nio 'already fails softly' claim is overstated; do_with_crash_restore is dead code; and the size split leaves out containers, hash and the KV cache that the argument depends on. Two open questions can be settled from the source. The embedded mode is a flag on the seekdb executable (ob_command_line_parser.cpp:364; the only shared library, liboceanbase.so, is a Linux pretest target), and latch ids are no longer user-visible. Use the survey's numbers and structure description as they are, but re-derive the allocator/OOM section before it feeds the migration decision.


## AREA: gap-data-compat-conflict
**What it is:** The startup check that decides whether a seekdb binary will open an existing data directory: src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.{h,cpp} compares etc/seekdb.data_version.bin with DATA_CURRENT_VERSION (src/oblib/common/ob_version_def.h:53). Around it sits everything else the server persists, which this survey checks against that gate, against the v1.4.0 release, and against any promise of upgrade or logical export.

**Size split:** Hand-written, the gate itself: 4 files, 730 lines (ob_data_version_mgr.cpp 368, ob_data_version_mgr.h 105, ob_version_def.cpp 164, ob_version_def.h 93). Hand-written persisted-format definitions surveyed: palf log_*header* 8 files / 1,462 lines; src/storage/slog + slog_ckpt 48 files / 10,238; storage/tx/ob_tx_log.{h,cpp} 2 files / 2,080; super block + server runtime meta 4 files / 874; src/share/storage (SQLite meta.db) 17 files plus ob_config_storage.{h,cpp}, 3,392 lines in total; vsag adaptor + snapshot stream 3 files / 1,980; S2 adapter 4 files / 1,343; FTS code excluding the dictionary 71 files / 10,526. Generated: the SQLite CREATE TABLE statements are generated into ob_inner_table_schema_misc.ipp, which is not tracked, by src/share/inner_table/generate_inner_table_schema.py (3,417 lines) from ob_inner_table_schema_def.py (14,510 lines). Data: src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp, 276,043 lines. Vendored third-party: none tracked. vsag and s2geometry arrive as prebuilt packages listed in deps/init/*.deps (for example devdeps-vsag-abiv1-1.1.0 and devdeps-s2geometry-0.10.0 on el8). Commands: git -C <wt> ls-files <paths> | xargs wc -l, and git ls-files ... | wc -l.

**Summary:** The gate is real and strict. validate_or_init_current_version returns OB_NOT_SUPPORTED when the stored version is not exactly DATA_CURRENT_VERSION (1.4.0.0). load_from_file also rejects the 1.3.0-era file layout (header version 1 vs 2). It runs in ObServer::init → init_config, before IO, storage, super block, slog replay, clog and tablet load, so on a mismatch no sstable, slog or clog byte is parsed. It has three gaps. SQLite meta.db is opened, __all_sys_parameter is created if missing and all parameters are loaded before the check. A missing version file is taken as a fresh install without checking whether store/ or clog already hold data. And the check compares only a constant that did not change when formats did. v1.4.0 has the identical gate and the same 1.4.0.0 value. It is not an ancestor of HEAD, but it is the master merge-base 977053bf1 plus one deps-only commit. After that point, slog sub-types 2/3/4/9/10/11/13 and the DDL clog types 0x20/0x40 were removed (da23ae158, d51422b54) without a version bump, so "same data version" does not mean "same bytes". The core headers (palf, tx log, micro block, TableType, ObCompressorType, record header, tablet meta header) have zero source diff between v1.4.0 and HEAD. The project has already dropped in-place upgrade on its own: the upgrade machinery was deleted in 3609383cd (2026-06-26), 1.4.0 refuses 1.3.0 data, standby restore requires equal versions, and no doc, README, release note, package hook or binding promises data migration. The package hooks only carry seekdb.cnf across an upgrade, and the bindings return a generic SEEKDB_INTERNAL_ERROR. The derived artifacts (vsag snapshots, S2 cell ids, FTS token rows) are rows inside gated storage whose bytes come from outside libraries or tokenizers with no version tag. All of them can be rebuilt through SQL (DROP/CREATE INDEX, DBMS_VECTOR.REBUILD_INDEX). A logical export/import path exists (SELECT INTO OUTFILE, LOAD DATA, LOCK/UNLOCK TABLES accepted as no-ops for mysqldump), but public tests barely cover it: 0 OUTFILE tests, 1 LOAD DATA test, 0 mysqldump tests, 0 restart tests.

Persisted artifact | gated? | file:line
- etc/seekdb.data_version.bin | this file is the gate (magic 0xBEDE, format 2, value must equal 1.4.0.0; a missing file means fresh install) | src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.h:91-94; ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:50-70, 88-94, 128-132
- store/sstable/meta.db, SQLite (__all_sys_parameter, __all_merge_info, __all_tablet_meta_table, __all_tablet_local_checksum with BLOB b_column_checksums, __all_column_checksum_error_info, __all_deadlock_event_history, __all_rootservice_job) | NO, read before the gate, no schema version | src/observer/ob_server.cpp:1698-1716 vs 1717-1719; src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py:93-230
- Parameter persistence (__all_sys_parameter rows in meta.db) | NO | src/share/config/ob_config_storage.cpp:47-72, 85, 219; ob_config_manager.cpp:114-125
- Server super block (block_file slots 0/1, header copied with MEMCPY) | YES | ob_server.cpp:1134; src/storage/ob_super_block_struct.cpp:60, 72
- Server and local slog | YES by version value, but the sub-types changed after v1.4.0 with no bump | ob_server.cpp:1155; src/storage/slog_ckpt/ob_server_checkpoint_slog_handler.cpp:254-256; ob_local_storage_checkpoint_slog_handler.cpp:812-815
- clog/palf (log headers, tx log, DDL clog) | YES; DDL types 0x20/0x40 removed after v1.4.0 with no bump | ob_server.cpp:1180; v1.4.0:src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_clog.h:41-42
- sstables, tablet meta, table store, tx data | YES | ob_server.cpp:797, 811, 1155
- vsag snapshot bytes (LOB rows) | YES as rows; the format belongs to vsag (use_old_serial_format:true) and differs by platform | src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp:633, 1276, 1338; deps/init/oceanbase.android.arm64.deps:19
- S2 cell ids in spatial index rows | YES as rows; values come from s2geometry 0.10.0, no version tag | src/sql/das/ob_das_domain_utils.cpp:166-204
- FTS doc-word/word-doc rows and the IK dictionary inner tables | YES as rows; values depend on the tokenizer and dictionary | ob_das_domain_utils.cpp:39-43, 339-354; ob_inner_table_schema_def.py:2462-2486
- PL user-defined-type serialized objects | has its own per-object check against DATA_CURRENT_VERSION | src/pl/ob_pl_user_type.cpp:422
- run/seekdb.pid, run/seekdb.clients, run/sql.sock, run/sql.pipe, run/seekdb.startup | NO, short-lived files, but their names are shared with the bindings | src/observer/main.cpp:670; ob_server.cpp:640, 651; rust/sql-nio/src/reactor.rs:19, 21; seekdb-bindings lib/src/seekdb.c:519-523
- etc/dump.config, etc/nic.rate.config | NO, optional text inputs | src/observer/ob_dump_task_generator.cpp:35; ob_server.cpp:2533
- seekdb.cnf (package config) | NO, kept across package upgrades by the install hooks | tools/macpkg/launchd/profile/preinstall; v1.4.0:tools/systemd/profile/pre_install.sh.template

Conclusion: not required (a version bump plus logical export is enough).

**Structure call (surveyor):** The gate itself is small (4 files, 730 lines of plain file I/O and a comparison) and translates one-to-one into idiomatic Rust (std::fs plus a parsed struct). What matters for the whole migration is what it allows. Because the binary already refuses any other data version before storage, slog or clog are parsed, the Rust build can bump DATA_CURRENT_VERSION and redesign the on-disk formats (storage blocks, clog/palf, OB_UNIS field orders, ObNumber and CRC encodings) instead of copying them byte for byte. Three conditions: move the check ahead of the meta.db open, or keep meta.db as plain SQLite, which is free with rusqlite; close the missing-file hole; and keep behavior (not bytes) equal for vsag, S2 and tokenizer output so query results match. Files that force the call: src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:50-70, src/oblib/common/ob_version_def.h:53, src/observer/ob_server.cpp:1698-1719.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the direction, with caveats. The gate is small, plain file I/O (ob_data_version_mgr.cpp: open, read, ObRecordHeader, STRTOK_R/sscanf at 249/252, placement new at 175/275; the survey cites 257-258, which is off). It maps directly to Rust std::fs plus a parsed struct. The code already has no multi-version reader: besides the gate (ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:61), strict DATA_CURRENT_VERSION equality is enforced in ob_medium_compaction_info.cpp:330/438, ob_trans_service_v4.cpp:93, ob_tx_table_define.cpp:86, ob_macro_block_meta.cpp:204/223/471/499/542 and ob_pl_user_type.cpp:422. So bumping the version and redesigning formats matches how the project already works; 1.4.0 already refuses 1.3.0 data. The caveats are stronger than the survey says. (1) The missing-file hole is not only hypothetical. v1.0.0/v1.0.1 wrote etc/observer.data_version.bin (v1.0.1:deps/oblib/src/common/ob_tenant_data_version_mgr.h:162, renamed in 581c57990). At HEAD, such a directory takes the fresh-install branch (ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:88-94, 58-60), and check_need_initialize (ob_server.cpp:410-433) sets need_initialize=false because block_file and clog exist, so storage parsing starts. (2) meta.db holds more than parameters, for example the standby role state saved as a config value (src/standby/control/standby_state_store.cpp:172-205), and it is opened before the gate (ob_server.cpp:1713-1715). (3) The 'no byte compatibility' freedom applies between releases, not inside one. If the port ships mixed C++/Rust binaries, the slog, clog and sstable codecs on each side of a boundary must agree byte for byte in that release. (4) The export must run on the old C++ binary, and the installers restart the new binary straight away (tools/macpkg/launchd/profile/postinstall), so the safety of the cutover depends on code that has already shipped.

**Size split check (verifier):** All counts reproduced with git ls-files | xargs wc -l / cat | wc -l. Gate: 4 files, 730 lines (368+105+164+93). palf log_*header*: 8 files, 1,462. slog + slog_ckpt: 48 files, 10,238. ob_tx_log.{h,cpp}: 2,080. Super block + server runtime meta: 4 files, 874. src/share/storage has 17 files and 3,075 lines, or 3,392 with ob_config_storage.{h,cpp}. vsag: 1,980 = ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp 1,417 + .h 166 + ob_plugin_vector_index_serialize.cpp 397 (that file has no .h). S2: 1,343 = ob_s2adapter.{h,cpp} 544 + ob_geo_to_s2_visitor.{h,cpp} 799. FTS without the dictionary: 71 files, 10,526. ob_ik_dic.cpp: 276,043. generate_inner_table_schema.py: 3,417. ob_inner_table_schema_def.py: 14,510. ob_inner_table_schema_misc.ipp is not tracked; it is listed in .gitignore:183 and in src/share/BUILD.bazel:1423 as a generated output. Macro counts per directory (storage 102/140, share 201/251, sql 304/396, oblib 33/41, standby 20/20) reproduced with git grep -h -o. No vsag, s2geometry or sqlite sources are tracked. Minor: run/seekdb.startup is never referenced in src/ or rust/; only the bindings use it (seekdb.c:521).

**Units of work:** Without byte compatibility, which the evidence supports: (1) the gate, 1 unit (ob_data_version_mgr.{h,cpp} + ob_version_def.{h,cpp}, 4 files, 730 lines), translated directly, plus fixing the meta.db-first order and the missing-file hole; (2) a new cutover unit that is not a translation: a tested logical export/import path (OUTFILE/LOAD DATA/mysqldump round-trip over every column type, index kind and generated column); (3) three units where behavior must stay equal: vsag adaptor and snapshot stream (3 files, 1,980 lines, likely kept as FFI to C++ vsag), S2 adapter (4 files, 1,343 lines, FFI or a verified port), FTS tokenizers (71 files, 10,526 lines, plus the 276,043-line dictionary data). With byte compatibility, add one codec unit per format family, each needing golden data directories from a named C++ commit: palf headers (8 files), slog + slog_ckpt (48 files), tx log (2 files), super block + runtime meta (4 files), SQLite meta.db storage (17 files), plus the sstable/tablet codecs owned by the storage areas.

**Public-surface tests:** The gate and persistence across restart: 0 public-surface tests. No mysqltest file restarts the server (git grep for restart/shutdown_server in tools/deploy/mysql_test/*.test = 0), and CI (.github/workflows/seekdb.yml) runs only mysqltest slices. The only test mentioning data_version is the legacy OceanBase obtest tools/obtest/t/rootservice/zhoulan_dataversion_reboot.test (cluster bootstrap, not the seekdb gate). Logical export/import: SELECT INTO OUTFILE 0 files; LOAD DATA 1 file (test_suite/fts_index/t/load_data.test); mysqldump 0; SHOW CREATE TABLE 34 files. Rebuild paths for derived data: DBMS_VECTOR rebuild_index 8 files (e.g. test_suite/vector_index/t/rebuild_vector_index.test, fork_table/t/fork_table_vector.test); fulltext 24, spatial index 16 and vector index 24 .test files check tokenizer, S2 and vsag behavior through query results, not on-disk bytes. All counts: git grep -l -i <pattern> -- 'tools/deploy/mysql_test/*.test' | wc -l. These tests use only SQL, so they survive a Rust rewrite unchanged; none of them would catch a persistence regression.

**Hard parts:**
- Startup order: meta.db (SQLite) is opened, its table is created and every parameter is read before the version check (src/observer/ob_server.cpp:1698-1719; src/share/config/ob_config_storage.cpp:47-85). A Rust build that changes the SQLite schema has to move the check first or migrate the schema in SQL.
- Missing-version-file hole: ENOENT is treated as a fresh install and nothing is cross-checked against existing store/ or clog data (src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:58-60, 88-94; ob_server.cpp:410-433 runs later). A Rust cutover must close this or old C++ data directories would slip past the check.
- Same version, different bytes: slog sub-types and DDL clog types were removed after v1.4.0 without a bump (da23ae158, d51422b54). Any byte-compatibility target would have to name a commit, not a version, and would need golden data directories written by that commit.
- If byte compatibility were kept, some serializers copy whole C++ structs with MEMCPY, so the format is whatever compiler layout and padding happen to be: src/storage/ob_super_block_struct.cpp:60,72; src/storage/blocksstable/ob_simplified_sstable_macro_block_header.cpp:99,113; src/storage/memtable/ob_memtable_mutator.cpp:110-137. Rust would need #[repr(C)] mirrors with the exact padding.
- The serialization byte contract is spread across macro field lists in .cpp files. Macro counts per directory: src/storage 102 OB_SERIALIZE_MEMBER* / 140 OB_UNIS_VERSION*, src/share 201/251, src/sql 304/396, src/oblib 33/41, src/standby 20/20. Not all are persisted; the sql ones are mostly in-process plans.
- vsag snapshots are streamed through C++ std::streambuf subclasses with callbacks (src/observer/vector_index/ob_plugin_vector_index_serialize.cpp:31-240) into vsag's Serialize(std::ostream&) (src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp:1276,1338). Rust needs a C++ shim for as long as vsag is the library.
- Derived-data behavior must stay equal even without byte compatibility: S2 coverings come from C++ s2geometry 0.10.0 (src/share/geo/ob_s2adapter.cpp), and FTS words from the IK tokenizer and 276,043-line dictionary (src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp). A Rust S2 or tokenizer that differs changes query results in the 16 spatial and 24 fulltext mysqltest files.

**Pain instances:**
- src/storage/ob_super_block_struct.cpp:60,72 serialize and deserialize ObServerSuperBlockHeader with MEMCPY(this) / MEMCPY(this, buf), so the on-disk layout is whatever the C++ compiler lays out; Rust would force an explicit encoding.
- src/storage/memtable/ob_memtable_mutator.cpp:126-137 copies MEMCPY(this, buf + pos, min(sizeof(*this), meta_size_)), defensive partial copying of a struct to survive size changes between versions.
- src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:170-175 and 257-258 place ObDataVersion into an arena with placement new and never destroy it; lines 241-252 parse the file with STRTOK_R and sscanf into fixed char buffers ('%63s %lu %c', ob_data_version_mgr.h:66-69). This is hand-written defensive C-string parsing that owned Rust types and str parsing remove.

**Open questions:**
- Whether a real v1.4.0 data directory actually starts on HEAD: it passes the gate (same 1.4.0.0), but it depends on whether v1.4.0's bootstrap checkpoint already truncated the now-removed slog sub-types (2,3,4,9,10,11,13) and DDL clog types (0x20/0x40), and on whether the persisted create_status value equals 1. This needs a runtime test.
- Whether 'refuse any other data version' is a product decision or a side effect of the 2026-07 cleanup commits. There is no release note or doc, and embedded/pip users would only see SEEKDB_INTERNAL_ERROR after an upgrade.
- Whether SELECT INTO OUTFILE + LOAD DATA, or mysqldump, round-trips every seekdb type and DDL feature (VECTOR, GEOMETRY/SRID, JSON, LOBs, generated columns, fulltext/vector/spatial index DDL, sys packages). The public tests do not check this.
- Whether packaging still builds at HEAD: cmake/RPM.cmake, cmake/DEB.cmake and package/deb/seekdb-build.sh reference tools/systemd templates deleted in 45cbbe9e1. Bazel packaging may have replaced this path; this could not be checked read-only.
- Whether meta.db content other than parameters (__all_tablet_meta_table, checksums, merge info) must survive a cutover or can be regenerated.
- If the migration ships intermediate releases that mix C++ and Rust modules in one binary, those modules must share one on-disk format inside a release. That is a separate constraint from reading older releases' data, and depends on how the work is split.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The two oldest public releases slip past the gate entirely. v1.0.0 and v1.0.1 stored the data version in etc/observer.data_version.bin, not etc/seekdb.data_version.bin. HEAD treats their data directories as fresh installs: it writes 1.4.0.0 and goes on to parse their super block, slog and clog. So the missing-file hole already applies to real user data. It is not only a theoretical cutover risk. (evidence: git grep at v1.0.0/v1.0.1: deps/oblib/src/common/ob_tenant_data_version_mgr.h:162 TENANT_DATA_VERSION_FILE_PATH = "etc/observer.data_version.bin"; renamed in 581c57990 (2026-01-09, 'change SeekDB name'). v1.1.0 already uses seekdb.data_version.bin. HEAD: ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:88-94 (ENOENT skipped) and 58-60 (writes the current version); ob_server.cpp:410-433 (block_file exists and clog non-empty -> need_initialize=false). Neither v1.0.1 nor v1.1.0 opens meta.db in ob_server.cpp, so HEAD would create it fresh.)
- Strict version equality is also built into persisted objects, not just the startup file. These checks were added in the same July 2026 cleanup. This backs the 'no byte compatibility needed' reading: there is no multi-version reader to keep. It also means a version bump alone already makes old tablets' medium-compaction info and serialized tx descriptors unreadable, even if the gate were bypassed. (evidence: src/storage/compaction/ob_medium_compaction_info.cpp:330 (is_valid) and 435-439 (deserialize -> OB_NOT_SUPPORTED); src/storage/tx/ob_trans_service_v4.cpp:93-96; src/storage/tx_table/ob_tx_table_define.cpp:86-89; src/storage/blocksstable/ob_macro_block_meta.cpp:204,223,471,499,542. git log -S shows fed273f33 (2026-07-22), 3e877d774 / 8efd8d156 (2026-07-29/30), cbcea2b91 (2026-07-30).)
- When the version is the same but the bytes differ, some of the removed log types do not fail cleanly. An unknown DDL clog type is turned into OB_EAGAIN and replay retries forever. An unknown slog sub-type fails with OB_ERR_SYS. A second enum value was also removed after v1.4.0 without a version bump. So a v1.4.0 data directory on HEAD could hang in clog replay rather than refuse to start. That matters for the survey's open question 1 and for any golden-directory test plan. (evidence: src/storage/ls/ob_ls_ddl_log_handler.cpp:276-288 (default -> OB_NOT_SUPPORTED, then 'ret = OB_EAGAIN' after LOG_ERROR). ObDirectLoadType DIRECT_LOAD_DDL = 1 exists at v1.4.0:src/storage/ddl/ob_direct_load_type.h:29 and is gone at HEAD src/data_plane/api/data_plane/ddl/ob_direct_load_type.h:27-31 (removed with d51422b54). This enum is serialized in DDL logs (src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_clog.cpp:73-122).)
- The logical export has to be done by the old C++ binary before it is replaced, and every install channel replaces and restarts the binary with no pre-upgrade step. So the export code that matters is in releases that have already shipped (v1.4.0) and can no longer be fixed. The test the migration needs is a round trip from a C++ v1.4.0 export into a Rust import. It also needs a startup message that says what to do: today, bindings and pip users see only SEEKDB_INTERNAL_ERROR. (evidence: tools/macpkg/launchd/profile/postinstall (launchctl bootout/bootstrap of com.seekdb.server right after install, data at /opt/seekdb/var/seekdb/data); v1.4.0 tools/systemd/profile/pre_install.sh.template and post_install.sh.template (only seekdb.cnf kept); seekdb-bindings lib/src/seekdb.c:590-664 (spawns the installed binary, returns SEEKDB_INTERNAL_ERROR on failure); lib/include/seekdb.h:18-21.)
- The tree already contains a restart and persistence harness, and the artifact table misses some ungated state. tools/obtest has 509 .test files that use obN.reboot, including a seekdb-specific fork-table restart test. None of them run in CI, but they are a starting point for persistence and cutover regression tests. The 0-restart-test count holds only for mysqltest. Ungated artifacts missing from the table: the standby role state, kept as a config value in meta.db; run/telemetry.json, which is written and read back and whose TELEMETRY_VERSION changed 5->6; and the SQLite WAL side files. (evidence: git grep -l -i -E 'restart|reboot' -- 'tools/obtest/t/*.test' = 498 of 509; tools/obtest/t/fork_table/fork_table_restart_recovery.test:10 'ob1.reboot;'; .github/workflows/seekdb.yml runs mysqltest only; 10 obtest files mention data_version. src/standby/control/standby_state_store.cpp:172-205 (SERVER_ROLE_STATE_CONFIG via config_manager.save_config into __all_sys_parameter); src/share/ob_telemetry.cpp:58,61,1299,1314; src/share/storage/ob_sqlite_connection.cpp:284 (journal_mode=WAL).)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey can be trusted to a high degree. I reproduced every count: file and line totals, commit ancestry (692/1, merge-base 977053bf1), the zero-diff list of persisted-format headers, the test counts and the macro counts. Every behavior claim about the gate, its startup order, the meta.db exception, the missing-file hole, the slog and DDL-clog removals, the runtime-meta legacy field, standby restore, bindings and packaging matches the code. The only errors are small line offsets (1134 vs 1135; main.cpp 808/820-822 vs 809/823; 257-258 vs 272-275). C13 and C23 needed factual corrections. C13 overstates 'never a seekdb 1.x upgrade chain': v1.1.0 still carried OceanBase compatible-version scaffolding for 1.0.x/1.1.0. C23 counts the #define as a use. The public_surface_tests text wrongly says only one obtest file mentions data_version, and it ignores the in-tree obtest reboot harness. The main weakness is in the assessment, not the facts. The survey treats the missing-file hole as a cutover risk, but it already applies to v1.0.0/v1.0.1 directories. It does not see that strict version equality is also built into persisted objects, which supports its own conclusion. It does not note that same-version byte drift can hang DDL clog replay instead of failing. And it understates that 'not required' covers reading older releases only: mixed C++/Rust releases still need byte agreement, and the export depends on C++ code that has already shipped. With those caveats, its one-line conclusion stands: byte compatibility is not required; a version bump plus logical export is enough.


## AREA: gap-judge-reach-map
**What it is:** This area maps which registered units the 272 configured mysqltest cases actually reach. The cases are listed in tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml under psmall test-set, run by .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py after init.sql and init_user.sql. The units are 61 physical operators, 527 expression names, 89 statement resolver classes, 34 rewrite rules, the inner tables and views, system variables and parameters. The area also covers the coverage build switches and any output order that depends on pointer addresses.

**Size split:** Hand-written test inputs: 283 .test files with 95,255 lines. The 272 configured .test files alone have 91,961 lines. There are also 46 .inc files with 2,924 lines. Command: git ls-files 'tools/deploy/mysql_test/*.test' | xargs cat | wc -l, and the same per extension.

Expected output: 305 .result files with 933,203 lines; the 272 configured ones have 912,659 lines. These are recorded from runs of the C++ binary and checked in.

Data: tools/deploy/mysql_test/test_suite/geometry/t/default_srs_data_mysql.sql (5,177 lines, sourced by 38 geometry cases), plus dataset .inc files such as vector_index/include/wine_dataset.inc and iris_dataset.inc.

Harness: init.sql 54 lines, init_user.sql 6, mysqltest_config.yaml 284, .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py 696, tools/deploy/mysql_test/psmalltest.py 2,806.

Registries surveyed (hand-written C++): src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator_reg.h 552, src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp 395, src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_operator_factory.cpp 1,232, src/sql/resolver/ob_resolver.cpp 686, src/sql/rewrite/ob_transformer_impl.cpp 983, src/share/parameter/ob_parameter_seed.ipp 1,181.

Generator input (data kept as code): src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py has 14,510 lines. The inner-table C++ it generates at build time is not tracked. src/share/system_variable/ob_system_variable_init.json has 10,979 lines. All counts come from wc -l.

Vendored third-party code: none in this area.

**Summary:** The judge reaches far less than the unit counts suggest, and a read-only pass can only bound that reach.
- **Operators:** 40 of the 272 configured .result files carry EXPLAIN tables; the figure of 42 counted two a_trade files whose column names start with OPERATOR_. Those 658 plan tables show 36 of the 61 registered physical operator types. Statement shapes suggest 4 more are executed (REPLACE, ON DUPLICATE KEY, window functions, and the VALUES operator under every EXPLAIN). 8 more are plausible but unproven, and 13 have no evidence at all, including recursive CTE, SAMPLE, SELECT INTO, JSON_TABLE, join filter and stats gathering.
- **Statements:** 44 of 89 resolver classes are reached. Of the 45 unreached, 5 cannot be produced by either grammar, and 2 (CreatePackage and CreatePackageBody) are hit only when system packages load at startup.
- **Expressions:** 207 of 527 registered names show any evidence. Array/map (4 of 35), JSON (6 of 33), date/time (14 of 62) and vector index internals are largely unreached; names such as hour(, month( and unix_timestamp( appear in none of the 283 .test files.
- **Rewrite rules:** only 9 of 34 show their effect in any plan or outline, one more is named only by a NO_UNNEST hint, and 24 show nothing.
- **init.sql gate:** init.sql matters more than its 54 lines suggest. It turns on recyclebin, which is off by default, so every DROP goes through the recycle bin. Its 12 set_tp lines switch on self-checks read through OB_E, which is compiled into release builds too: EXPLAIN plans are regenerated from their own outline, rewrite convergence and expression formalization are checked, the new sort path is forced, NLJ/SPF batches are forced to group size 4, and the fast NLJ range generator is cross-checked. One tracepoint (509) has no reader at all.
- **Coverage:** a measured coverage run is close to ready. The clang source-based flags (cmake/Env.cmake:67-74) and the constructor that writes the profraw file (src/observer/main.cpp:19) still exist. Only the Bazel migration commit 4cb6fb268 blocks them (CMakeLists.txt:147 FATAL_ERROR, build.sh:241), so a one-day measured check is realistic.
- **Pointer-address order:** I found no sort or hash iteration keyed by pointer value whose order reaches EXPLAIN text or result rows. Every pointer-keyed map in these directories is lookup-only, feeds another map, or is dead code.
- **What does bind a port:** the judge compares exact text. That text includes 568 plan tables with floating-point EST.ROWS/EST.TIME, query-block names printed as %08X hashes, std::sort tie order, 2,688 expected-error directives, and 10,107 of 11,908 SELECT...FROM statements with no ORDER BY, which depend on C++ hash-table output order. Those, not pointer addresses, constrain how a port may be structured.

**Structure call (surveyor):** **The registries:** these translate one-to-one into Rust enums with match tables. That covers the REGISTER_OPERATOR macro table (61 entries), the REG_OP list (526 classes), the resolver switch (89 classes, 5 of them dead) and the fixed 34-rule list. Their current C++ form (macro-generated template traits, placement new, dynamic_cast checks) is a mechanism Rust replaces rather than something to preserve.

**What the judge forces:** the judge compares exact text, and that forces structure-preserving translation, or a relaxed judge, for the parts that produce the text:
- the cost model and filter ranking behind 568 plan tables with EST.ROWS/EST.TIME
- the %08X query-block-name hash in outline data
- std::sort tie order wherever ties can reach output
- the C++ hash-table output order behind 10,107 SELECTs with no ORDER BY
- the OB_E self-check hooks that init.sql turns on.

A redesign of the optimizer or of hash execution would fail the current judge unless plans and rows are first normalized.

Files that force the call: src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp, src/sql/optimizer/ob_log_join.cpp, src/sql/resolver/dml/ob_sql_hint.cpp, src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_sort.h, src/sql/optimizer/ob_explain_log_plan.cpp, and the 40 plan-bearing .result files under tools/deploy/mysql_test.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I partly agree. The registries do map one-to-one onto Rust enums with match tables: 61 REGISTER_OPERATOR lines in ob_operator_reg.h, filled at static-init time and dispatched through dynamic_cast checks in ob_operator_factory.cpp:187-300; 528 REG_OP lines for 526 classes in ob_expr_operator_factory.cpp; the resolver switch in ob_resolver.cpp (89 classes, 5 unreachable from the grammar); and the fixed 33+1 rule list at ob_transformer_impl.cpp:455-487. PHY_TABLE_ROW_STORE and PHY_MULTI_PART_TABLE_SCAN can be dropped. I also agree that the exact-text judge forces structure-preserving translation, or a normalized judge, for the parts that produce text. The survey weights those parts wrongly, though. (1) std::sort tie order barely matters inside the optimizer. reorder_filters_exprs breaks ties by original_pos (ob_logical_operator.h:305-313), and print_join_tables_in_hint sorts by name. The tie-order risk that matters is in result rows from the SORT operator: OB's own ObAdaptiveQS/radix sort and std::sort in ob_sort_op_impl.cpp, with init.sql tracepoint 1200 forcing which path runs (ob_optimizer_util.cpp:6989). (2) Most unordered SELECTs are single-table shapes, about 7,500 of about 10,060. Their order comes from storage and PX scan order, not hash tables, so the hash-function constraint covers only about 300 statements. (3) The plan tables also bind layers outside the optimizer. EST.ROWS comes from storage estimation (ob_access_path_estimation.cpp:165/569 into src/storage/access/ob_table_estimator.cpp and ob_index_sstable_estimator.cpp). The Outputs & filters blocks print expression trees with implicit casts (369 'cast(' output lines) and internal names (495 lines with T_FUN_/T_OP_/column_conv/lnnvl), which binds type deduction and the expression printer. (4) Only 40 cases carry plan tables. So masking EST.ROWS/EST.TIME and rowset= in those 40 files is cheap, and would free the cost model far more than the survey implies. Everything else in the survey's forcing list stands: the %08X qb-name hash at ob_sql_hint.cpp:389, the OB_E hooks, and the 568 cost-bearing tables.

**Size split check (verifier):** Most figures reproduce with my own commands. Tracked mysql_test: 283 .test files with 95,255 lines, 46 .inc files with 2,924, and 305 .result files with 933,203. The 272 configured results have 912,659 lines (exact). The 272 configured .test files have 91,945 lines by `cat | wc -l`, against the survey's 91,961; the 16-line gap is probably files without a trailing newline. Harness: init.sql 54, init_user.sql 6, mysqltest_config.yaml 284, mysqltest_for_seekdb.py 696, psmalltest.py 2,806. Registries: ob_operator_reg.h 552, ob_operator_factory.cpp 395, ob_expr_operator_factory.cpp 1,232, ob_resolver.cpp 686, ob_transformer_impl.cpp 983, ob_parameter_seed.ipp 1,181. Generator inputs: ob_inner_table_schema_def.py 14,510 and ob_system_variable_init.json 10,979. default_srs_data_mysql.sql has 5,177 lines. It is sourced through import_default_srs_data_mysql.inc by 39 configured geometry cases, not 38. The generated inner-table C++ is indeed untracked; src/share/inner_table holds only .py/.cpp loaders and whitelists. Two omissions: the survey does not note that 54 def_sys_index_table blocks sit alongside the 384 def_table_schema blocks, or that ob_inner_table_init_data.py is a second generator input. The split between hand-written inputs, recorded output, data and generator input is sound.

**Units of work:** Reach table per unit family. "EXPLAIN" means shown in a plan table; "inferred" means the statement shape is present but the operator is not shown; "init.sql gate" means reached only because init.sql runs.

| Unit family | Reached | Not reached |
|---|---|---|
| Physical operators (61) | 36 in EXPLAIN; 4 inferred (REPLACE, INSERT_ON_DUP, WINDOW_FUNCTION, VALUES); 8 plausible but unverified (PX_MULTI_PART_INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE, PX_MULTI_PART_SSTABLE_INSERT, DDL_BLOCK_SAMPLE_SCAN, MERGE_INTERSECT, EXPAND, TOPK) | 13, including TABLE_ROW_STORE, which nothing can reach |
| Resolver classes (89, from 93 dispatch sites) | 44 by tests; 2 only at startup (CreatePackage, CreatePackageBody) | 38 by tests; 5 impossible from either grammar |
| Expression names (527) | 201 in test SQL; 6 only in EXPLAIN | 320 |
| Rewrite rules (34) | 9 with visible effect; 1 named only by a hint | 24 |
| Virtual tables (143) | 11 named | the rest |
| information_schema views (62) | 11 | 51 |
| oceanbase views (69) | 16 | 53 |
| mysql views (8) | 0 | 8 |
| System tables (74 + 28 history) | 12 named, plus 2 history tables | the rest |
| System variables (776) | 37 | the rest |
| Config parameters (289) | 9 | the rest |

The init.sql gate in each family:
- **Operators:** no operator is gated as such, but init.sql forces the NLJ/SPF batch group size to 4 and the new sort path, so it changes which code paths inside operators run.
- **Resolvers:** Deallocate and GetDiagnostics appear only inside init.sql's uncalled exec_sql body.
- **Expressions:** none.
- **Rewrite rules:** every rule's loop runs for each DML, and init.sql adds the convergence and expression-formalize checks after each pass.
- **Inner tables:** __all_virtual_core_all_table and __all_virtual_core_column_table are analyzed by init_user.sql:5-6 and also named by 1 test.
- **System variables:** _nlj_batching_enabled is set only by init.sql (a no-op), and recyclebin is effectively gated because init.sql turns it on.
- **Config parameters:** 6 are set only by init.sql.

Natural unit of work for the judge: one mysqltest case (272), grouped into 28 suite directories plus top-level. On the code side the units are one operator type (Spec + Op + logical op, 61), one expression class (526), one resolver class (89), one rewrite rule file (34 files under src/sql/rewrite/ob_transform_*.cpp), and one virtual table class under src/observer/virtual_table.

Pointer-address order sites (file:line; none reaches EXPLAIN text or result rows):

| Site | What happens |
|---|---|
| ob_raw_expr_copier.cpp:336 | Iterated; used at ob_log_plan.cpp:11156-11157 to fill a map |
| ob_raw_expr_replacer.cpp:377 | Iterated; used at ob_log_plan.cpp:9438/9440 to fill a map |
| ob_transform_utils.cpp:7953 | Iterated into an array; the function has no callers |
| ob_exec_context.cpp:376 | Iterated for cleanup only |
| ob_shared_expr_resolver.cpp:79/87 | Pointers mixed into a hash used for lookup; iterated only in the destructor (:98) |
| ob_logical_operator.cpp:255-313, ob_column_index_provider.cpp:70-120, ob_range_graph_generator.cpp:1279-1371, ob_transform_const_propagate.cpp:2242/2279, ob_sql_utils.cpp:1839-1893, ob_stmt_expr_visitor.cpp:128/143, ob_aggr_expr_push_up_analyzer.cpp:433-475 | Lookup only |

Proposed one-day measured check:
1. In a scratch worktree, re-enable WITH_COVERAGE: remove the guard at CMakeLists.txt:147, or pass the -fprofile-instr-generate / -fcoverage-mapping / -DWITH_COVERAGE flags as Bazel copts and linkopts.
2. Run the 272 cases with .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py, which writes <base_dir>/seekdb.profraw.
3. Merge with llvm-profdata, then report per function with llvm-cov.
4. Map the covered functions to the unit families above.

**Public-surface tests:** The 272 configured cases are the public-surface tests for this area: SQL scripts over the MySQL protocol, with no internal imports.
- **Size:** 38,763 statements parsed from 91,961 .test lines plus sourced .inc files, checked against 912,659 lines of recorded .result output.
- **Resolvers:** reached across 248 cases (Select), 226 (Insert), 250 (CreateTable), 72 (CreateIndex), 40 (AlterTable, 319 statements) and 40 (Explain, 409 statements).
- **Plan checks:** concentrated in executor.basic (152 plan tables), subquery.subquery (42), vector_index.sparse_vector_index_vsag_query (41), fts_index.simple_query (39), geometry.geometry_filter_mysql (39), dist_nest_loop_simple (38), executor.full_join (36) and topk (28).
- **Expressions:** exercised mainly by the geometry suite (47 of 65 GIS names), expr.*, type_date.*, vector_index.* and ai_function.ai_prompt.
- **Setup:** init.sql and init_user.sql run before the cases and change behaviour for all of them: recyclebin on, 12 tracepoint self-checks, 6 parameters.
- **Not configured:** tools/obtest/t holds 500 more .test files in a separate harness, and 11 tracked mysql_test cases are left out of the config.

**Hard parts:**
- Operator registration built from macros and template specializations: src/query/api/query/engine/ob_operator_reg.h:68-100 (REGISTER_OPERATOR counts its arguments and generates ObOpTypeTraits<N> specializations at :75), with a table filled at static-init time in src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp:305-334.
- Spec and operator objects are placement-new'd after a 16-byte-aligned child-pointer array in the same allocation, with a manual destructor call and free on failure: src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp:187-212 and :219-252. In Rust this needs an arena plus indices, or a separate Vec of children.
- Code generation downcasts ObLogicalOperator to the registered logical class with dynamic_cast and reports a mismatch at runtime: src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp:278-300. This is inheritance-based dispatch that a Rust enum turns into a compile-time check.
- The expression registry builds a throwaway object in a lambda to read its name and type into global NAME_TYPES/OP_ALLOC arrays: src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_operator_factory.cpp:431-445, 528 REG_OP lines covering 526 classes.
- Pointer identity used as a hash input and map key during expression dedup, copy and replace: src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_shared_expr_resolver.cpp:67-87 ((uintptr_t) of child exprs), src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr_copier.cpp:112-207, src/sql/resolver/expr/ob_raw_expr_replacer.cpp:335-465. Rust needs stable arena ids or pointer-equality wrappers; iteration order was checked and does not reach output.
- Exact-output parity inside the optimizer: floating-point filter ranks (src/sql/optimizer/ob_logical_operator.cpp:2200-2238), EST.ROWS/EST.TIME printed in 568 plan tables, query-block names as %08X hashes (src/sql/resolver/dml/ob_sql_hint.cpp:360-395), std::sort tie order (src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_sort.h:41).
- Self-check hooks the judge depends on, read through OB_E from optimizer, rewrite and engine code: src/sql/optimizer/ob_explain_log_plan.cpp:143, src/sql/rewrite/ob_transformer_impl.cpp:359 and :896, src/sql/rewrite/ob_query_range_define.cpp:776, src/sql/engine/join/ob_nested_loop_join_op.cpp:69. A port has to provide equivalent switches or init.sql loses its checks.
- Inner-table and system-view schemas are generated at build time by Python from src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py (14,510 lines; 143 virtual tables, 139 views), so the generator needs a Rust output target.

**Pain instances:**
- Commit e66776e2d, found by Sanity: src/observer/virtual_table/ob_table_columns.cpp:603 called databuff_printf with buf_len instead of extra_print_buf_size. This is the DESC / SHOW COLUMNS path, which the configured tests use about 163 times. The same commit fixed use-after-release in src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp and src/sql/plan_cache/ob_ps_cache_callback.h, code every test statement passes through. Bounds-checked slices and owned lifetimes in Rust remove this class of bug.
- src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_sort.h:28-41: ob_sort wraps std::sort with a tracepoint-gated check that the comparator is irreflexive (EN_CHECK_SORT_CMP), because an invalid comparator makes std::sort undefined behaviour. Rust's sort stays memory-safe with a bad comparator.
- src/sql/engine/ob_operator_factory.cpp:289-296: a runtime dynamic_cast check with the message 'please check the registered type is correct' guards against a REGISTER_OPERATOR line naming the wrong logical or spec class. :206-210 and :245-250 destroy and free by hand when initialization fails. Enum dispatch and Drop in Rust remove both.
- src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_operator_factory.cpp registers ObExprMid twice (lines 597 and 599) and ObExprBitAnd twice (523 and 720), which puts duplicate NAME_TYPES entries in a hand-maintained list. An exhaustive match keyed by enum would flag the duplicates.

**Open questions:**
- Which of the 8 plausible operators actually run: the PDML operators under 32 enable_parallel_dml hints, the sstable insert during CREATE INDEX on populated tables, MERGE_INTERSECT, EXPAND for WITH ROLLUP, TOPK under 27 topk hints, DDL_BLOCK_SAMPLE_SCAN. Only a measured run can settle it.
- Whether seekdb resolves the SQL inside a procedure body at CREATE PROCEDURE time. If so, init.sql's exec_sql body does reach the Prepare/Execute/Deallocate/GetDiagnostics resolvers once per instance.
- Whether seekdb runs /*!40101 SET SQL_MODE='' */ (empty_input.test:8) as a SET statement or treats it as an empty query. That decides whether the EmptyQuery resolver is reached.
- Aggregate and window-function kinds (T_FUN_MAX .. T_FUN_APPROX_COUNT_DISTINCT_SYNOPSIS_MERGE in ob_item_type.h) are not in the REG_OP registry and were not enumerated here.
- The method behind the earlier 141/414 figure is unknown. My 207/527 uses 'name(' token matching and undercounts synonyms (substr/substring, ceil/ceiling, lcase/lower) and expressions reached implicitly (casts, column_conv, partition calc).
- Which Bazel target builds the seekdb binary, and whether Bazel's gcov-style '--coverage' toolchain flags work with main.cpp's __llvm_profile_set_filename constructor, which needs -fprofile-instr-generate. Also whether the clang crash that excluded the parser from coverage (cmake/Utils.cmake:139) still happens with the current compiler.
- Which hash function and bucket layout hash group-by, hash distinct and hash join use. The 10,107 unordered SELECTs depend on that output order, so a port needs either the same hash or row-order normalization in the judge.
- How much of the recorded expected output differs between macOS and Linux beyond the masked GIS cases, which matters for choosing the platform a differential judge runs on.
- How long a coverage build takes. A timing build is running in build_release/, so build time was not measured here.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- How the runner behaves shapes any differential judge. Cases are split into 4 slices; each slice runs its share of the config list in order on one shared instance, and a failing case is retried up to 3 times on a freshly destroyed, restarted and init.sql-seeded instance. So a case passes if any of 4 attempts pass, which hides flakiness. Global state also leaks from case to case within a slice: alter system parameters such as vector_memory_limit, debug_sync_timeout and the merge intervals, and recycle bin contents. (evidence: .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py:19 (MAX_CASE_RETRIES = 3), :490-518 (the slice_index::slice_count selection, the retry loop, and prepare_instance after a failure), :157-194 (prepare_instance = destroy + start + wait-ready + execute_init_sql). .github/workflows/seekdb.yml:25 sets MYSQLTEST_SLICES to 4. Comparison is plain mysqltest --result-file with no extra normalization (:326-340).)
- Tests depend on named debug-sync points and on tracepoints beyond init.sql. DEBUG_SYNC is live in release builds. Seven configured cases drive named sync points inside DDL and storage code, and three cases set tracepoint 368 themselves. A port must keep these points, with the same names, at equivalent places, or these cases hang or fail. The survey covered only init.sql's tracepoints. (evidence: src/share/ob_debug_sync.h:225-226 ('TODO baihua: empty macro for release version?', #define DEBUG_SYNC(sync_point) GDS.execute(...)). ob_global_debug_sync values in the tests: FORK_TABLE_BUILD_DATA 46 uses, FORK_TABLE_WAIT_DATA_COMPLEMENT 6, FORK_TABLE_SUCCESS 4, REBUILD_VEC_INDEX_* 5, BEFORE_TABLET_FULL_DIRECT_LOAD_MGR_CLOSE 2, CREATE_INDEX_REPLICA_BUILD 1, in fork_table.fork_table_{chain,cow,ddl,lock,merge,with_index} and vector_index.rebuild_vector_index. set_tp 368 appears at histogram/t/stats_farm.test:13/180 and vector_calc_{gbk,utf8}_mysql.test:8.)
- EST.ROWS in the 568 cost-bearing plan tables comes partly from the storage layer's row estimation, not only from the optimizer's cost model. Plan-text parity therefore binds the storage estimators and memtable-vs-sstable state, which the tests steer with major/minor freezes and merge waits. Structure-preserving translation cannot stop at src/sql/optimizer. (evidence: src/sql/optimizer/ob_access_path_estimation.cpp:165 and :569-669 (process_storage_estimation, add_storage_estimation_task[_by_ranges]); the estimators are in src/storage/access/ob_table_estimator.cpp and src/storage/access/ob_index_sstable_estimator.cpp. Tests force merges through mysql_test/include/index_quick_major.inc and wait_daily_merge.inc, sourced by a_trade_notify, bulk_insert, index_14_hint, two_order_by and others. The survey's own EN_THROW_DS_ERROR reader (ob_access_path_estimation.cpp:1765) sits on this path.)
- For ORDER BY rows with equal keys, the output order is set by the SORT operator's own algorithms, not by any address. It is OB's adaptive quicksort/radix on encoded keys or std::sort with the row comparator, chosen by optimizer heuristics that init.sql tracepoint 1200 overrides. libc++ and libstdc++ implement std::sort differently, which is one likely source of the macOS/Linux baseline drift. A Rust port must reproduce these exact algorithms, or the judge must sort ties. (evidence: src/sql/engine/sort/ob_sort_op_impl.cpp:77-260 (ObAdaptiveQS, aqs_radix, inplace_radixsort_more_bucket), :1459/:1462/:1862 (lib::ob_sort over rows with CopyableComparer); src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_sort.h:40 (std::sort); src/sql/optimizer/ob_optimizer_util.cpp:6967-6991 (the can_sort_opt heuristics, overridden when EN_ENABLE_NEWSORT_FORCE is set).)
- The plan text binds the expression printer and implicit-cast insertion, not just operator names. 'Outputs & filters' prints full expression trees with inserted casts, internal function names and batch sizes. Matching them needs the same type deduction, cast placement and printer format. (evidence: Across tracked results there are 4,005 '<n> - output(' lines. 369 contain 'cast(' and 495 contain T_FUN_/T_OP_/column_conv/lnnvl. rowset=16 appears 3,035 times and rowset=256 563 times (from ObCodeGenerator::detect_batch_size, called at ob_explain_log_plan.cpp:56). Example: subquery/r/mysql/subquery.result:441 filter([lnnvl(cast(t1.c2 = 5, TINYINT(-1, 0)))]).)

**Verifier's trust note:** For raw counts and code paths the survey holds up well. I reproduced the configured-case set, the 658 plan tables and their 36/61 operator mapping, and the dead operator paths. The resolver registry arithmetic and the unreached resolvers also check out, as do the init.sql tracepoint readers and their release-build activity, the coverage switches and the commits that blocked them, and the finding that no pointer-keyed ordering reaches output. Several secondary claims are wrong or overstated, though. Only 4 parameters are init.sql-only, not 6, and tests set 12 parameters, not 9. The inner-table case counts include commented-out lines (__all_table is in 24 cases, not 41). Plan tables come from 10 of 27 suite groups, not 16 of 28. The two ob_item_type.h copies differ in real enum members, not just comments. FASTMINMAX and JOIN_LIMIT_PUSHDOWN visibly fire, so the rewrite-rule reach of 9 is a lower bound. Not all drops go through the recycle bin. The survey also misattributes the order risk: most unordered SELECTs follow storage scan order, not hash-table order, and the real tie-order risk is in the SORT operator. All reach figures come from text matching and are lower bounds, as the survey partly admits. The report can use this survey's operator and resolver tables and its coverage-run proposal, after applying the corrections above. It should not use the survey's weighting of which output-order constraints bind a port. It should also add the runner's retry and shared-instance behavior, the debug-sync dependencies, and the storage-estimation coupling before sizing a 'build the judge first' step.


## AREA: gap-referee-price
**What it is:** This area measures how long the compiler takes to judge the code. It covers the measured C++ clean and incremental build times on this machine, the CI compile and mysqltest times, the current Rust crate's check and build times, and published or public-CI numbers from large Rust codebases. From these it derives an estimate of cargo check and build time for about 1.83M lines as one crate versus 12-40 crates.

**Size split:** Hand-written build and CI config (git ls-files | xargs cat | wc -l): .github/workflows 977 lines/9 files, .github/script 2,065/7, .github/actions 103/1, cmake/ 1,507/10, bazel/ 4,213/27, tools/cmake 431/1, build.sh 529, CMakeLists.txt 330, bazel.py 670, MODULE.bazel 44, src/**/CMakeLists.txt 1,144, src/**/BUILD.bazel 9,851 (20 files). Rust (git ls-files rust): 30 files, 9,216 lines. Of these, 8,448 lines of hand-written .rs (8,420 in src/ plus 28 in build.rs, 272,242 bytes). 534 lines are generated: rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h is cbindgen output that is committed, and CI checks it with git diff. The remaining ~234 lines are manifests and toolchain config. rust/Cargo.lock is untracked because rust/.gitignore:5 ignores it. Data: none in this area. The 276,043-line src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp dictionary does show up in the C++ build as unity group ob_storage_ik_0, which is 278,839 lines. Vendored: none tracked. deps/ tracks only 29 files (external/init/oblib), and deps/3rd is copied or downloaded at build time.

**Summary:** 1. The separate timing build had finished, not still running, by 11:40 (timings.txt ends with DONE). A clean C++ build (build.sh release --make: RelWithDebInfo, -O2 -g, Unity batch 30, lld, no ccache) took 314 s on this Apple M4 Pro (14 cores, 24 GiB). That is about 8 s of configure and about 306 s of make, roughly 7,450 source lines/s, or about 530 lines/s per core.
2. The final lld link takes only about 1.2 s. Per-unit C++ feedback today is 9 s for one standalone .cpp and 37 s for one 27-file, 46,590-line unity group (32 s compile, 3.5 s archive, 1.2 s link).
3. The build log has no per-group timings. The build ends on the src/sql rewrite, resolver and optimizer unity groups.
4. In CI (32 nproc, ccache), the Compile step median is 223 s over 19 runs. A true cold cache (0.32% hit) gives make=556 s plus 258 s dependency init. Warm runs are 116-304 s.
5. The four mysqltest slices have median run times of 574, 374, 447 and 370 s, about 29 min of serial test time for the 272 configured cases. Median wall time for a successful run is about 19 min.
6. The Rust sql-nio crate (8,448 lines) checks in 1.2 s or less on its own, which is at least 7,000 lines/s. Its release build takes 10.3 s or less with lto=thin and codegen-units=1, which is at least about 820 lines/s. Its Cargo.lock is gitignored, so each clean CMake build re-resolves 107 packages.
7. Published numbers for large Rust codebases are thin. TiKV reported 15 min dev and 30 min release rebuilds in 2020 for about 2M lines including dependencies. RisingWave reported 2-3 min debug builds on an M1 in 2023. Feldera measured about 25 min release for 100K lines in one crate, against 2m10s with 1,106 crates. Bun published no build times for its ~780K lines in ~100 crates. Public CI shows 7-15 min checks or clippy runs for TiKV and GreptimeDB (~0.8-1.0M own lines on 3-4 vCPU runners).
8. On stable Rust the parallel frontend is still not available (nightly -Zthreads only, compiler-team#1005, June 2026), so each crate is type-checked on one thread.
9. Scaling assumption: about 3,000 lines/s per M4 Pro core for cargo check of own code (range 1,500-7,000). On that basis one 1.83M-line crate needs about 10 min for a clean check (4-20 min). Its release build with the current codegen-units=1 profile would take about 37 min or more, against the C++ baseline of 5.2 min.
10. With 12-40 balanced crates, a whole-workspace check takes about 2-4 min, and a per-crate check takes about 51 s (12 crates) or about 15 s (40 crates).
11. A per-unit cargo check under 60 s is plausible only if no crate is larger than about 90-180K lines. That means src/sql and src/storage must each be split into several acyclic crates. Only under that condition can Step 4 fold into Step 3; with one 1.83M-line crate, Step 4 remains a separate, batched step.

**Structure call (surveyor):** This area has no code to translate, but it rules out copying the build graph one-to-one. The C++ build uses unity-grouped object targets that are linked together, so dependency cycles between directories cost nothing at link time, and a leaf edit comes back in 9-37 s. Rust makes the crate both the unit of compilation and the unit of type-checking. That crate must sit in an acyclic graph, and on stable Rust its frontend runs on one thread. To keep a per-unit check near today's C++ cost, the Rust layout has to be designed around a crate-size budget of about 90-180K lines, which means at least roughly 20 crates, with src/sql and src/storage each broken up. The release profile also has to change from codegen-units=1. Both are redesign decisions, not translation.

Files that force the call: src/sql (983,706 lines) and src/storage (484,206 non-dictionary lines) are larger than the per-crate budget, and rust/Cargo.toml [profile.release] codegen-units=1 / lto=thin does not scale past one small crate.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the direction: a Rust layout has to be a planned, acyclic set of crates, not a one-to-one copy of the CMake targets, and codegen-units=1 with thin LTO in rust/Cargo.toml cannot stay for large crates. Databend's release build (30m37s with 99% sccache hits, driven by a ~181K-line crate built with codegen-units=1) supports that more strongly than the evidence the survey cites. I disagree with how precise the budget is and with parts of the reasoning. (1) The 90-180K-line-per-crate budget depends on a check rate whose lower-end evidence was misread (TiKV's clippy took ~95 s, not 415 s) and whose other data point was misclassified (GreptimeDB's deps were warm). The honest range is roughly 30-250K lines per crate, i.e. about 8-60 crates. (2) The per-unit estimate ignores downstream re-checks after edits to foundation crates, and those are the common case when fixing shared types in oblib and share. (3) The claim that seekdb 'does not have to be acyclic today' misses that the maintained Bazel build already has an acyclic, analysis-time-validated header-level graph: src/oblib/BUILD.bazel, src/sql/sql_runtime_group_deps.bzl, src/sql/sql_runtime_group_oblib_deps.bzl, src/storage/storage_runtime_group_oblib_deps.bzl, and tools/bazel_migration/generate_oblib_group_deps.py. It has 79 oblib and 116 share interface targets. It stops at headers, and final link cycles are still allowed through seekdb_final_link, but it is the natural starting point for crate boundaries, much better than 41 CMake targets or '20 packages'. (4) The C++ template-cost cautions (e2b7c7a8f, the 27 split files) apply to cargo build, not cargo check, so they bear on Step 5 build time, not on whether Step 4 can fold into Step 3.

**Size split check (verifier):** The numbers I rechecked all match: .github/workflows 977/9, .github/script 2,065/7, .github/actions 103/1, cmake 1,507/10, bazel 4,213/27, tools/cmake 431/1, build.sh 529, CMakeLists.txt 330, bazel.py 670, MODULE.bazel 44, src CMakeLists 1,144, src BUILD.bazel 9,851/20. rust/ is 30 files and 9,216 lines, with 8,448 lines of .rs (272,242 bytes), 534 in nio.h and 234 in the rest. The survey calls those 234 lines 'manifests and toolchain config', but they include rust/BUILD.bazel (97) and cbindgen.toml (34). The split leaves out two things. First, 48 Bazel .bzl files under src/ (23,706 lines of generated source and header inventories and per-unity-group dependency maps). Second, a second tracked Cargo project, deps/external (Cargo.toml, a tracked Cargo.lock, src/lib.rs, and 140 lines in cmake/CargoExternal.cmake and Jemalloc.cmake), which builds jemalloc through Cargo on every build. 'Vendored: none tracked' is right, since deps/3rd is untracked. ob_ik_dic.cpp is 276,043 lines and its unity group ik_0 is 278,839 lines.

**Units of work:** For the compile referee, the natural unit is the Rust crate, because it is the smallest thing cargo check can judge on its own. Budget: no more than about 180K lines per crate at the central estimate (about 90K pessimistic) to keep a per-unit check under 60 s. For about 1.83M lines that means at least 11 balanced crates at the central rate and at least 21 at the pessimistic rate. With today's uneven directory sizes (src/sql 983,706 lines), about 20-40 crates is realistic, with src/sql and src/storage each split into several crates. C++ units that exist today and can seed the split: 41 CMake targets, 20 BUILD.bazel packages under src, and 364 compile commands (221 unity groups of up to 30 files each, plus 143 standalone TUs).

**Public-surface tests:** Not applicable for compile price as such. As a verification cost: CI runs 272 configured mysqltest cases from tools/deploy/mysqltest_config.yaml (runtime_configs.psmall.test-set), out of 283 tracked .test files under tools/deploy/mysql_test.
