These are public-surface tests that drive the seekdb binary through obclient/mysqltest (.github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py). By suite: 93 top-level, geometry 39, vector_index 21, fork_table 16, fts_index 15, expr 11, subquery 11, type_date 10, global_index 7, datatype 6, and others. One full pass costs about 1,765 s of serial test time on CI runners (4 slices, medians 574/373/447/370 s). Every Step-5 run of a Rust build pays this on top of compile time. Measured from 10 successful seekdb.yml runs.

**Hard parts:**
- The type-checker cost of a single giant crate: stable rustc 1.98.1 (rust/rust-toolchain.toml) runs the frontend serially, and -Zthreads is nightly-only (compiler-team#1005). One 1.83M-line crate needs an estimated 4-20 min per clean check, and incremental check time on a crate that size is unmeasured.
- Breaking directory cycles into an acyclic crate graph. Bun needed about 100 crates and hit about 16,000 errors, mostly from untangling cycles. seekdb's C++ targets (ob_sql, ob_share, ob_storage, ob_server, merged into oceanbase_static; see noop.log) do not have to be acyclic today.
- Template-heavy code whose C++ compile cost is already managed by hand: extern template and ALONE TUs (commit e2b7c7a8f: 455 s -> 53 s), and the 27 split instantiation files in src/share/datum/ob_datum_funcs_compilation/, src/share/datum/ob_str_datum_funcs_compilation/, src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_cmp_func_compile/ and src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_str_cmp_func_compile/. Rust generics monomorphize in each crate that uses them, and release builds do not share generics by default. The same slow spots will come back and must be handled through crate boundaries or dyn dispatch.
- Boost.Geometry code in src/share/geo (5 files include boost/geometry; objects such as ob_geo_func_symdifference2.cpp.o are 55 MB) has no drop-in Rust equivalent, and it is already a compile-time hotspot (commit 53c8c5423 split it).
- The release profile in rust/Cargo.toml (codegen-units=1, lto=thin, debug=true) serializes LLVM for each crate. Applied to a large crate, that is Feldera's failure mode (about 25 min for 100K lines in one crate).
- The unity-group reference point is 32 s for 46,590 lines (optimizer_0), which is roughly 1,450 lines/s. A Rust crate of the same size must check in a comparable time for the per-unit loop to stay as fast as C++ today.
- Build reproducibility while C++ and Rust are linked together: Cargo.lock is gitignored (rust/.gitignore:5), and cmake/Rust.cmake calls cargo without --locked, so each clean build re-resolves 107 packages. The make-driven cargo call also warns that it could not connect to make's jobserver (full-build.log line 89), which can oversubscribe cores during mixed builds.

**Pain instances:**
- 22b7c24db (#932) 'Fix compilation in non-unity build' adds 167 missing #include lines hidden by unity grouping. The same class is fixed in e45b8ae5c ('fix compile in non-unity', 253 files) and 6f213cece ('fix compilation errors without unity build'). Rust modules with explicit use paths remove this class.
- The unity-build machinery in cmake/Utils.cmake (UNITY_GROUP assignment, SKIP_UNITY_BUILD_INCLUSION, UNITY_BUILD_CODE_AFTER_INCLUDE at lines 38-129) and cmake/Env.cmake:40 (OB_MAX_UNITY_BATCH_SIZE 30) exists only to make C++ compile times tolerable. Cargo's crate model would replace it.
- This one is not removed by Rust, so it is listed as a caution rather than a case for Rust: e2b7c7a8f (455 s unity straggler from duplicate template instantiation) and 53c8c5423 (geo files split for compile time) show that compile-time hotspots come from generic code, and Rust monomorphization has the same cost.

**Open questions:**
- What is the real cargo check throughput, full and incremental, on translated seekdb code, which may be more generic-heavy than sql-nio? This needs a timed pilot of about 50-150K lines in one crate, which I could not run read-only.
- How much memory does rustc need to check or build a single 1.83M-line crate, and does it fit in this machine's 24 GiB?
- How long does an incremental cargo check take after a one-file edit in a very large crate? rustc re-runs expansion and name resolution over the whole crate, so the cost is unmeasured.
- Can src/sql, src/storage and src/share be cut into an acyclic DAG of 20-40 crates, and how deep would the chain be? The coupling area has to answer this; my 5-level and 8-level chain depths are assumptions.
- Which C++ unity groups are slowest? This needs a build with timestamps or -ftime-trace, and I did not start one.
- What cache state and CPU count did the reference CI numbers use? GitHub-hosted ubuntu-latest (4 vCPU) and macos-15 arm64 (3 vCPU) are assumed from GitHub docs, not verified here, and the cache hit state of the TiKV and Databend jobs is unknown.
- When will the rustc parallel frontend reach stable? compiler-team#1005 has a plan but no date.
- Does `cargo test --locked -p sql-nio` in .github/workflows/buildbase/action.yml:23 depend on the CMake build first generating the untracked rust/Cargo.lock?
- Bun has not published cargo build or check times for its ~780K lines in ~100 crates, so the closest recent agent-driven port has no referee price on record.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The Bazel build already has a fine-grained, acyclic, validated dependency graph at the header level for oblib, share, sql, storage and more. It is the best existing evidence for how many crates the code can be split into and whether the split can be acyclic. The survey counted only '20 BUILD.bazel packages' and skipped Bazel. (evidence: The 20 src BUILD.bazel files contain 179 cc_library( and 50 seekdb_semantic_unity_cc_library( calls. 48 src/**/*.bzl files hold 23,706 lines. src/sql/sql_runtime_group_deps.bzl: 'Exact fine-grained dependencies for SQL Unity compile groups ... The group partition is validated at analysis time.' src/sql/sql_runtime_group_oblib_deps.bzl maps each unity group to targets such as //src/oblib:oblib_collections_base. 79 distinct //src/oblib:oblib_* and 116 //src/share:* labels are referenced. The generator is tools/bazel_migration/generate_oblib_group_deps.py. It was last changed 2026-09-16 (915ef6091).)
- The C++ baseline already contains two cargo builds, not one. deps/external builds jemalloc through Cargo (added 2026-09-16 in #1376) with a tracked Cargo.lock, --locked, --jobs 4, MAKEFLAGS cleared, and its cargo-target deleted each time. It costs 54 s locally and 1m11s-2m45s in CI, and it runs alongside the C++ compile. It also shows an in-repo pattern for a locked, reproducible cargo build that sql-nio lacks. (evidence: full-build.log:78 '[  3%] Building jemalloc with Cargo', :123 'Compiling seekdb-jemalloc-sys v0.2.2+5.3.1', :302 'Finished `release` profile [optimized] target(s) in 54.02s'. deps/external/cmake/CargoExternal.cmake:69-75 ('rm -rf', 'MAKEFLAGS=', 'build --locked --release --jobs 4'). The CI setup logs show 'Finished release [optimized] in 2m 40s / 2m 45s / 1m 11s / 1m 31s'.)
- The public Rust CI numbers were misread in ways that move the scaling assumption. TiKV's clippy took 87-102 s on warm deps, and the rest of the 411-420 s step was cargo-deny setup. GreptimeDB's 425-886 s check had warm deps, so it is an own-code rate. Databend's release build was 99% sccache hits yet still took 30m37s under codegen-units=1 and thin LTO. Together these give roughly a 5-10x spread in per-core check rate across codebases, and they show release cost is dominated by large final crates. (evidence: TiKV job logs for runs 35911991256, 35905207943 and 35901267085: 'Cache hit ... full match', 'cargo-deny 0.18.9' about 5 min into 'make clippy', 'Finished `dev` profile ... in 1m 35s / 1m 27s / 1m 42s'. GreptimeDB run 35955581772 job log: 'Cache hit for: v0-rust-check-...', 'Cache Size: ~1530 MB', 134 workspace crates 'Checking'. Databend job 107485147255: 'Finished `ci` profile ... in 30m 37s', 'Cache hits 3009', 'Cache misses (rust) 23'. Databend Cargo.toml [profile.release] has codegen-units = 1 and lto = "thin".)
- The per-unit check estimate treats a unit as one crate checked in isolation. It ignores that an edit to an upstream crate re-checks every downstream crate. In the Step-3/4 loop, fixes to shared foundation types (the oblib and share equivalents) would cost close to the whole chain, about 2-4 min or more, not 15-51 s. This matters directly for whether Step 4 can fold into Step 3. (evidence: Materialize's compile-times post attributes its 45 s to 23 s check gain and its 2m12s to 53 s iteration gain to making 'the heavyweight mz-sql and mz-transform no longer get invalidated'. The survey's own chain times in C19 (122-254 s) are the cost of that cascade.)
- The scaling treats C++ source lines as Rust lines and never states a conversion factor. It also misses that the only Rust unit-test gate is not running in active CI. (evidence: src is 705,539 .h lines against 1,977,438 .cpp lines (26% headers, which Rust would not duplicate). The Bun post reports 535,496 Zig lines becoming ~780,000 Rust lines (1.46x). For tests: `cargo test --locked -p sql-nio` exists only in .github/workflows/buildbase/action.yml:23. Only compile.yml calls it, and compile.yml is disabled ('workflow_call' only, triggers commented out, last run 2026-07-06). That step would also run before rust/Cargo.lock exists on a fresh checkout.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The local measurements can be trusted. I re-derived the 314 s clean build, the 1/9/37 s incremental times, the ~1.2 s link, the 364-command and 2.28M-line inventory, and every CI duration and mysqltest statistic from the raw files and gh api, and they match to within rounding. The mysqltest and CI numbers are solid inputs for the report. The weak part is the step from these numbers to the Rust scaling. Two of the three public anchors for the check-rate assumption were misread: TiKV's 415 s was mostly cargo-deny setup (the clippy itself took ~95 s), and GreptimeDB's deps were warm, not cold. The sql-nio release 'rate' is dominated by dependency codegen and LTO, so the 37 min single-crate release figure has its bound pointing the wrong way. The per-unit check estimate also leaves out downstream re-checks. The qualitative conclusion holds: one 1.83M-line crate is too large for a sub-60 s per-unit check, and codegen-units=1 must go. The specific numbers do not: 3,000 lines/s, 90-180K lines per crate, ~20 crates, and the 51/15 s per-crate checks should be read as uncertain by about 5x, with the downside wider than the survey states. They need the pilot the survey itself asks for. The survey also overlooked the Bazel build's existing acyclic header-level target graph and the second cargo build (jemalloc) already in the baseline, and both bear on the crate-split decision.


## AREA: gap-unit-ledger-and-tokens
**What it is:** A deduplicated ledger of every git-tracked C/C++/grammar/Rust source file under src/ and rust/ (6,690 files, 2,804,701 lines). Each file is tagged as hand-written, generated-and-checked-in, data, vendored or dead. Each of the 328 code directories is assigned to exactly one of 52 unit groups. For each group the ledger gives token sizes (bytes/3.5), the oversize files that must be split, and the one-level include context a translator has to see.

**Size split:** Scope: `git -C <wt> ls-files -z src rust`, filtered to .h .hpp .cpp .cc .c .ipp .y .l .def .map .rs. There are no .inc files under src/ or rust/; all 143 tracked .inc files are under tools/. Lines = newline count, tokens = bytes/3.5, all computed in one read-only python3 -B pass.
TOTAL: 6,690 files, 2,804,701 lines, 98,689,719 bytes, 28.20M tokens.
- hand-written: 6,468 files, 2,314,419 lines, 24.26M tokens. This includes 3 .y + 3 .l grammar files (24,585 lines) and the existing Rust crate rust/sql-nio (23 .rs, 8,448 lines). By extension: .h 3,530 files / 658,687 lines / 6.74M tok; .cpp 2,853 / 1,590,409 / 16.91M; .ipp 40 / 22,582 / 0.26M; .c 9 / 3,333; .cc 7 / 6,375.
- generated-and-checked-in: 27 files, 86,856 lines, 1.08M tok. Sources: gen_errno.pl (share/ob_errno.cpp 15,636, share/ob_errno.h, oblib/lib/ob_errno.h); gen_ob_sys_variables.py (share/system_variable/ob_system_variable_init.{h,cpp}, ob_system_variable_alias.h, ob_sys_var_class_type.h, ob_sys_var_meta.{h,cpp}, sql/session/ob_system_variable_factory.{h,cpp}); oblib/lib/codec/generate_{simd,scalar}_bitpakcing.py (4 files, 24,406 lines); protoc (oblib/grpc/standbyservice.*pb*, 12,345 lines; share/geo/ob_vector_tile.pb-c.*, 651); gen_str_datum_func_parts.py (share/datum/ob_str_datum_funcs_compilation/*); cbindgen (rust/sql-nio/include/nio.h, 534 lines, rust/sql-nio/build.rs:19-22).
- data: 24 files, 343,167 lines, 2.20M tok. storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp alone is 276,043 lines / 1.51M tok. Also oblib/common/timezone/ob_timezone_info.cpp (39,989 lines, about 39K lines of table plus about 1K lines of code), charset tables ob_ctype_utf8_tab.h / ob_uctype.h / ob_ctype_bin_tab.h, sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_to_pinyin_tab.cpp, share/mysql_errno.h, 16 type-deduction .map files (16,875 lines), and 4 .def files (share/ob_errno.def is the input to gen_errno.pl).
- vendored: 68 files, 29,385 lines, 0.35M tok. zstd 1.3.8 (oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8/zstd_src, 47 code files, 25,154 lines), libeasy/libev headers (oblib/easy, 19 files, 3,017 lines), xxhash (oblib/lib/hash_func/xxhash.{c,h}, 1,214). The tzcode copies (localtime.c, private.h, tzfile.h) are also vendored but are counted under dead.
- dead: 103 files, 30,874 lines, 0.30M tok. Dead means not in any inventory list CMake reads, not in an explicit CMake source list, and not reached by #include from any compiled file.
Not in git (generated at build time, so not counted): 50 inner-table schema .cpp files that the inventory names (from share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py, 14,510 lines of Python, and generate_inner_table_schema.py, 3,417); the bison/flex outputs of the 3 grammars; type_name.c; syspack_source.cpp (from 26 .sql files); ob_version.cpp (from ob_version.cpp.in).
Baseline check: the .h/.hpp/.cpp/.cc/.c/.ipp/.y/.l subset under src/ alone is 6,646 files / 2,774,622 lines. The brief's 2,777,368 is 2,746 lines higher, probably because it used a different file filter.

**Summary:** 1. The ledger covers 6,690 tracked code files (2,804,701 lines, about 28.2M tokens at 3.5 bytes per token) under src/ and rust/. They are assigned to 52 unit groups by directory prefix, and each of the 328 code directories lands in exactly one group. This removes the double counting of share/geo, observer/vector_index, the storage retrieval/vector code and the two cast files.
2. Only 6,468 files, 2,314,419 lines and 24.3M tokens are hand-written. The rest is data (343K lines, 80% of it the one IK dictionary file), generated-and-checked-in code (86.9K lines from five generator families), vendored C (29.4K lines, mostly zstd 1.3.8) and dead files (103 files, 30.9K lines).
3. Several C++ outputs are generated at build time and are not in git: the inner-table schema from a 14,510-line Python definition, three bison/flex parsers, the system-package source and type_name.c. A Rust port has to point these generators at Rust output rather than translate what they produce.
4. The natural unit of work is the stem (.h + .cpp + .ipp with the same base name). There are 3,692 hand-written stems with a median of 2.6K tokens. The sizes are very uneven: about 129 stems over 30K tokens hold a third of all hand-written tokens, and 18 are over 100K.
5. 67 files have 4K lines or more (18 have 8K or more); 54 of them are hand-written, holding 397K lines and 4.57M tokens. Almost every one is a single large class per file (ObDDLService has 381 methods over 23,242 lines), so each splits along method-name families into several `impl` blocks. The two cast files are different: they are matrices of hundreds of free or macro-generated functions and split by source type.
6. Context cost: the direct project headers a stem includes (one level, following forwarding headers) are a median of 25.6K tokens, against 2.6K for the stem itself. Summed over all stems that is 129M tokens, 5.3 times the code; the 30-stem sample gives 7.1 times.
7. The full transitive include closure of a typical stem is a median of 2.4M tokens across about 700 files. A translator can never see all of it and needs summaries of the declarations it does not see.
8. The include graph is tightly coupled: 27 header include cycles covering 69 files, 118 forwarding headers left behind by the Bazel split, and a few headers included by hundreds of files (share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h by 499).
9. Most of the 13 unowned directories (45,854 lines) are thinly tested by mysqltest. sql/tablelock and rootserver/dbms_job have zero active test statements, and change_stream and dbms_scheduler have one test each, while sql/engine/cmd runs in almost every test.
10. Internal unit tests are gone at HEAD: unittest/ holds only .gitignore files, and commit 45cbbe9e1 deleted 678 files there. The 792 mysqltest files are therefore the whole regression base for verifying a migration.

**Structure call (surveyor):** The ledger supports structure-preserving translation at the stem level for most of the code: 3,692 hand-written stems, median 2.6K tokens, each mapping to one Rust module, with class hierarchies kept as they are. Three kinds of files force something other than one-to-one translation.
(a) Build-time and checked-in generators must be pointed at Rust output, not translated. This covers the inner-table schema (14.5K-line Python definition), system variables, error codes, codec SIMD bit-packing and protobuf, about 105K lines of output.
(b) The three bison/flex grammars (sql_parser_mysql_mode.y alone is 18,723 lines and 758 rules with C actions) have no bison-compatible Rust target. They need either a redesign onto another parser generator or keeping the C parser behind FFI.
(c) 54 hand-written files of 4K lines or more must be split before one implementer call can hold them. The single-class files split cleanly into several `impl` blocks by method family; the two cast matrices split by source type and are better produced by a macro or generator.
Data files (IK dictionary, timezone table) and vendored C (zstd, xxhash, libeasy) should be converted by script or replaced with crates. Because a stem's full include closure is a median of 2.4M tokens across about 700 files, a translator can only work from summaries of the declarations it does not see. Header cycles (69 files) set the minimum crate boundaries.
Files that force the call: src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.y, src/pl/parser/pl_parser_mysql_mode.y, src/share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema_def.py, src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py, src/share/gen_errno.pl, src/oblib/lib/codec/generate_simd_bitpakcing.py, src/share/object/ob_obj_cast.cpp, src/sql/engine/expr/ob_datum_cast.cpp, src/rootserver/ob_ddl_service.cpp, src/storage/multi_data_source/mds_node.h.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I partly agree. These parts hold: point the generators at Rust output (gen_errno.pl, gen_ob_sys_variables.py, generate_inner_table_schema.py), treat the bison grammars separately (sql_parser_mysql_mode.y: 758 rules, 197 opt_*, 18,723 lines), split the 54 oversize hand-written files, convert data by script, and replace vendored C with crates.
I disagree with four points.
(1) 'Header cycles (69 files) set the minimum crate boundaries' is wrong. The cycles that matter for crates are between directories, not between headers:
- At directory level, one include cycle joins 110 directories and 4,108 live files across sql, storage, rootserver and pl.
- At the level of the survey's own 52 groups, 42 groups form a single cycle and the 10 oblib groups form another. Part of that is caused by the grouping: share-schema contains observer/schema, search-vector-retrieval spans observer + storage + share + sql, jobs-scheduler spans observer + rootserver, and ai-functions spans share + observer + sql.
- At top-level module level the layering is almost clean. Only 4 include edges tie sql and storage together: storage/ddl/ob_tablet_slice_writer.cpp -> sql/engine/ob_batch_rows.h, 2 edges from sql/engine/px/ob_px_sub_coord.cpp to storage/ddl, and storage/ddl/ob_ddl_insert_dag.cpp -> rootserver/ddl_task/ob_ddl_task.h. Cutting those leaves cycles of 57 sql directories (2,408 files), 38 storage directories (1,367), 38 oblib directories (447) and 20 share directories (608).
(2) 'Each stem maps to one Rust module' ignores that 118 forwarding headers plus query/api and data_plane/api keep declarations in one group and the implementing .cpp in another. Examples: query/api/query/engine/expr/ob_expr_in.h is implemented in sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_in.cpp; sql/pl/ob_pl.h is implemented in src/pl/ob_pl.cpp. Rust requires a struct's inherent impl to be in the crate that defines the struct.
(3) 'Class hierarchies kept as they are' needs one design decision made before any stem is translated, because Rust has no inheritance. The tree has 4,364 derived class declarations, 17,225 lines containing `virtual` and 16,392 #define lines.
(4) Hard part (e), 'generated SIMD code maps to std::arch', should be a check for deletion first: the codec library has no users outside its own directory.

**Size split check (verifier):** The totals and category split reproduce exactly. I checked them three ways: a file/line/byte recount, an independent dead-file analysis (running the real emitter to /tmp plus my own include resolver), and a match against all 52 hand-written TSV rows (0 differences in files, lines or tokens). The numbers can be used. The tagging rules have problems that change what counts as translation work:
(1) The hand-written total includes rust/sql-nio (23 .rs, 8,448 lines, 78K tokens), which is already Rust, and the 6 grammar files (24,585 lines), which the structure call says will not be translated one-to-one. Model-translation work is therefore about 2.28M lines, not 2.31M.
(2) Nine checked-in outputs of gen_expr_str_cmp_func.py (425 lines) are tagged hand-written.
(3) The narrative says data holds '16 .map files (16,875 lines) and 4 .def files'. The TSV is actually built from 15 .map files (16,633 lines) and 2 .def files (ob_errno.def, ob_lib_cache_namespace.def). ob_expr_compatiable_promotion.map (242 lines), ob_sql_topic.def (2,262) and ob_sql_category.def (38) are dead. Totals are unaffected.
(4) 'Dead' means unreachable by #include only. Compiled code that nothing calls stays live: the whole of src/oblib/lib/codec (4 generated files, 24,406 lines, plus 5 hand-written files, 2,881 lines) is compiled but no file outside that directory includes its headers, and rootserver/dbms_job's master and executor have no callers.
(5) The 50 build-time files are 47 inner-table files + 1 sqlite virtual table + 2 PL parser outputs.
(6) Stem cap counts are slightly high: 4,137 units at a 20K cap and 3,919 at 30K.
The context numbers (median 25.8K, sum 129.4M, sample ratio 7.1x) reproduce.

**Units of work:** Natural translation unit: the stem (same-directory .h/.cpp/.ipp with one base name). There are 3,692 hand-written stems, median 2.6K tokens. Capping units at 20K tokens by splitting along method families gives about 4,200 units; a 30K cap gives about 4,000. Coarser levels are the 52 unit groups below or the 216 Unity groups in the inventory.

Sample median: stem 4,460, context 30,293 (7.1 times). Transitive closure median: 2.41M tokens.

**Public-surface tests:** The regression base is 792 .test files (283 in tools/deploy/mysql_test, 509 in tools/obtest). At HEAD there are no internal unit tests: unittest/ holds only .gitignore files after 45cbbe9e1 deleted 678 files, and only 2 small tests remain in deps/oblib/unittest. Nothing is left that imports internals. Keyword counts over the 792 files: CREATE TABLE in 666, SET in 451, ALTER SYSTEM in 520, EXPLAIN in 55-65 (depending on pattern), FULLTEXT in 33, MATCH...AGAINST in 12, vector syntax in about 30, ai_* functions or dbms_ai_service in 9 (5 in test_suite/ai_function), GRANT/REVOKE/CREATE USER in 21, LOAD DATA in 1.
Coverage of the unowned directories, with each directory's group:
- sql/engine/cmd (18,401 lines; executors for CREATE/ALTER/DROP, index, user/role/grant, SET, ALTER SYSTEM, LOAD DATA, KILL, vector index refresh, routines) goes to sql-cmd-exec. It runs in nearly every test (CREATE TABLE 666, SET 451, ALTER SYSTEM 520; LOAD DATA only 1).
- sql/monitor (5,534; plan records used by sql/engine/ob_physical_plan.cpp and printer/ob_dml_stmt_printer.cpp, SQL stat records, show trace) goes to sql-core-monitor-printer. Covered through EXPLAIN in about 55-65 tests; SHOW TRACE in 0.
- sql/privilege_check (2,081; privilege checks for every statement and for AI models) goes to sql-cmd-exec. Covered indirectly by all tests; explicit GRANT/REVOKE/CREATE USER in 21.
- sql/executor (1,912; ObCmdExecutor dispatch, execute result, dependency maintenance task) goes to sql-cmd-exec. Covered indirectly by every DDL test.
- sql/tablelock (1,257; LOCK TABLES and GET_LOCK-family executors) goes to sql-cmd-exec. 0 active tests; all matches are commented out in func_group_7.test and yonyou.test.
- observer/change_stream (4,613; reads redo rows and passes them to plugins; the only plugin maintains async vector indexes) goes to search-vector-retrieval. 1 test (vector_index/create_table_with_hybrid_vector_index.test, SYNC_MODE=ASYNC).
- observer/dbms_scheduler (2,890; DBMS_SCHEDULER job master, executor and table operator) goes to jobs-scheduler. 1 test (histogram/stats_farm.test:47-51 calls dbms_scheduler.disable/enable).
- observer/composition (1,505; the legacy text-retrieval merge iterator, installed at src/observer/ob_server.cpp:2094) goes to search-vector-retrieval. 12 tests use MATCH...AGAINST.
- rootserver/dbms_job (1,425; DBMS_JOB master, executor and utils) goes to jobs-scheduler. 0 tests.
- share/text_analysis (839; token stream and analyzer behind the 'space' parser, which is the default per src/oblib/lib/ob_define.h:1444, and the 'beng' parser) goes to fts. Covered by the 33 FULLTEXT tests.
- sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_ai (4,163), observer/ai_service (755) and share/ai_service (479) together form ai-functions (5,397 lines; AI_COMPLETE/EMBED/RERANK/PROMPT, the model-endpoint HTTP client, AI model metadata). 9 tests.

**Hard parts:**
- Single-class files far above one call's size. src/rootserver/ob_ddl_service.cpp (ObDDLService: 381 methods, 23,242 lines, 351K tokens), src/sql/optimizer/ob_join_order.cpp (ObJoinOrder: 286 methods, 14,976 lines), src/sql/rewrite/ob_transform_utils.cpp (368 methods) and src/sql/optimizer/ob_log_plan.cpp (298 methods) must be split into several impl blocks. Their shared member state has to be settled once, before the pieces are translated in parallel.
- Cast function matrices produced by macros. src/sql/engine/expr/ob_datum_cast.cpp has 359 CAST_FUNC_NAME(intype,outtype) functions and the OB_DATUM_CAST_MYSQL_IMPLICIT[ObMaxTC][ObMaxTC] table at line 11498. src/share/object/ob_obj_cast.cpp has 413 free functions and OB_OBJ_CAST at line 10086. Both need macro_rules! or a generator plus a const table, not per-function translation.
- Bison/flex grammars with C actions: src/sql/parser/sql_parser_mysql_mode.y (18,723 lines, 758 rules, 197 of them opt_* rules) with sql_parser_mysql_mode.l, src/pl/parser/pl_parser_mysql_mode.y/.l, and src/sql/parser/ftsparser.y. Their generated .c output is not in git and has no drop-in Rust generator.
- Generators that emit C++ must be rewritten to emit Rust: src/share/inner_table/generate_inner_table_schema.py (3,417 lines) driven by ob_inner_table_schema_def.py (14,510), src/share/system_variable/gen_ob_sys_variables.py (8 output files), src/share/gen_errno.pl (3 outputs from ob_errno.def), src/share/inner_table/sys_package/syspack_codegen.py, and src/share/datum/ob_str_datum_funcs_compilation/gen_str_datum_func_parts.py.
- Generated SIMD code: src/oblib/lib/codec/ob_generated_unalign_simd_bp_func.cpp (12,705 lines) together with ob_sse_to_neon.h, built with -mavx2 -mssse3 (src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt:12-19). This maps to std::arch intrinsics with per-arch cfg, or to a regenerated Rust generator.
- Header include cycles held together by include guards and forward declarations: 27 cycles covering 69 files, the largest being src/storage/multi_data_source/{mds_node.h, mds_node.ipp, mds_ctx.h, mds_table_base.h, compile_utility/mds_register.h, ...} with src/storage/tx/ob_multi_data_source.h, and 7 files in src/sql/pl (ob_pl.h, pl_cache/ob_pl_cache.h, ...). These files must share one crate.
- Template bodies in .ipp files: 40 hand-written .ipp files (22,582 lines), for example src/storage/multi_data_source/mds_node.ipp and src/share/parameter/ob_parameter_seed.ipp (a config definition list). Rust generics and trait bounds must be written out where C++ templates relied on duck typing.
- Dense include graph: a stem's transitive closure is a median of 2.41M tokens (108-1,129 files). src/share/rc/ob_server_runtime.h has 499 includers and src/oblib/lib/ob_define.h has 266 includers (23K tokens). Per-unit prompts therefore need a generated declaration index instead of raw headers.
- Data compiled as code: src/storage/fts/dict/ob_ik_dic.cpp (276,043 lines, 5.3 MB) and src/oblib/common/timezone/ob_timezone_info.cpp (a 39K-line table with about 1K lines of code after line 38,990). The code must be split out and the tables converted by script, for example to include_bytes! or generated statics.
- Vendored C to replace rather than translate: src/oblib/lib/compress/zstd_1_3_8/zstd_src (47 files, 25,154 lines), src/oblib/easy (libeasy with libev's ev.h), and src/oblib/lib/hash_func/xxhash.c. The zstd 1.3.8 on-disk format and version pinning must match whatever crate replaces it.

**Pain instances:**
- src/share/gen_errno.pl:268-274 makes the generated ob_errno.cpp keep `#include <iostream>` as a workaround: 'ob_error.cpp file requires at least 20g memory for release(-O2) compilation and will jam when asan turned on ... it is clang bug'. A Rust const table of error codes would not carry this compiler workaround (assessment, not tested).
- Template instantiation is sharded by hand across files to spread the compile load: src/share/datum/ob_datum_funcs_compilation/ (8 shard .cpp files plus an .ipp) and src/share/datum/ob_str_datum_funcs_compilation/ (3 shards produced by gen_str_datum_func_parts.py). Rust's codegen-units split a crate automatically, so shard files would not be needed; the compile cost itself remains.
- A .cpp file is textually included through headers: src/storage/memtable/mvcc/ob_keybtree.cpp. src/storage/BUILD.bazel:57-60 says 'This .cpp is textually included through the current external Storage header closure' and needs its own _textual_ob_keybtree target. Rust modules have no textual inclusion.
- Include-path aliasing: src/CMakeLists.txt:14-19 keeps three include roots so that 'share/inner_table/...', 'inner_table/...' and bare basename spellings of the same generated headers all resolve. There are also 118 forwarding headers (e.g. src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_operator.h) that exist only to keep old include spellings. Rust module paths are unique, and a `pub use` replaces a forwarding header.
- Commented-out includes leave whole headers dead without any warning. src/storage/tx/ob_trans_factory.h:23 has `// #include "ob_trans_log.h"`, and ob_trans_log.h (412 lines) is included nowhere else. rustc warns about unused items inside the module tree, though not about files outside it (assessment).

**Open questions:**
- Was the deletion of 678 unittest files in 45cbbe9e1 intended? The commit title is an unrelated SQL fix. If it was not, recovering those tests changes the 'tests that import internals' answer.
- Are rootserver/dbms_job's ObDBMSJobMaster and ObDBMSJobExecutor reachable at runtime? They are compiled, but nothing outside the directory references them.
- Do the checked-in generated files (ob_errno.*, the system-variable files, the codec bit-packing files, standbyservice.pb.*) match what their generators produce today? The generators were not run, so drift was not checked.
- Tokens are estimated as bytes/3.5. A real tokenizer may count C++ with long ob_ identifiers differently (possibly closer to 3.0-3.3 bytes per token); this was not measured.
- The one-level context counts whole header files, including comments and inline bodies. Stripping them down to declarations only would lower the 25.6K median; by how much was not measured.
- Includes of build-directory generated headers are not resolved (42 unresolved includes of share/inner_table/ob_inner_table_schema*.h/.ipp), so context is understated for schema-heavy stems.
- The brief's total of 2,777,368 lines is 2,746 lines above my measured 2,774,622 for the same extension set under src/. The filter it used was not identified.
- Platform- and option-dependent sources are all counted as live: ob_win_service.cpp is Windows-only, standby_module.cpp and standby_module_disabled.cpp depend on OB_ENABLE_STANDBY, and malloc_hook is Linux-only. Which configurations a Rust port must cover is a product decision.
- The classification of 13 unowned directories into groups (e.g. change_stream going to search-vector-retrieval because its only plugin maintains async vector indexes) is a judgment call. Other groupings are possible without changing the totals.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The unit groups do not form a dependency order, so they cannot serve as crates or as a translation order. At directory level the include graph has one cycle of 110 directories and 4,108 files, and 42 of the 52 groups form one cycle. At top level, only 4 include edges tie sql and storage (and rootserver) into one cycle. (evidence: Tarjan SCC over resolved include edges of live files. Directory level: 110-directory SCC (sql 57, storage 42, rootserver 7, pl 4). Group level: 42-group SCC plus a 10-group oblib SCC. Top level: storage->sql 1 edge (src/storage/ddl/ob_tablet_slice_writer.cpp -> src/sql/engine/ob_batch_rows.h), sql->storage 2 edges (src/sql/engine/px/ob_px_sub_coord.cpp -> storage/ddl/ob_ddl_direct_load_utils.h and ob_ddl_insert_dag.h), storage->rootserver 1 edge (src/storage/ddl/ob_ddl_insert_dag.cpp -> src/rootserver/ddl_task/ob_ddl_task.h). share, logservice, query and data_plane have 0 upward edges.)
- Many classes are declared in one directory and implemented in another, so the stem is not a self-contained unit. query-api (110 .h, 3 .cpp) and data-plane-api (110 .h, 2 .cpp) are almost entirely declaration headers whose implementations sit in sql, storage and observer groups. Rust needs a struct and its inherent impl in the same crate, so these pairs must be translated together. (evidence: src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_in.h contains only #include "query/engine/expr/ob_expr_in.h"; the implementation is src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_in.cpp. 83 forwarders have a same-stem .cpp, and 53 of those pairs cross groups (query-api->sql-expr 10, data-plane-api->search-vector-retrieval 10, query-api->sql-operators 8, query-api->sql-optimizer-stat 6, data-plane-api->fts 5). By basename, 37 query/api headers and 35 data_plane/api headers have a same-name .cpp in another group.)
- 'Dead' only means no file includes it. Code that is compiled but that nothing calls is counted as live work, including the 24.4K lines of generated SIMD bit-packing that the survey lists as a hard part. (evidence: git grep for any include of lib/codec/ headers, or for __fastunpack, compute_deltas or ObCodec outside src/oblib/lib/codec, returns only oblib_*_inventory.bzl. Yet ob_fast_bp_func.cpp, ob_generated_scalar_bp_func.cpp (oblib_lib_codec_0 group) and ob_fast_delta.cpp, ob_generated_unalign_simd_bp_func.cpp (oblib_lib_simd group) are compiled. The directory holds 32,109 lines, 12 headers of which are already dead. rootserver/dbms_job's ObDBMSJobMaster/Executor likewise have no callers (C24). A symbol-level or linker-based pass (for example --gc-sections output) would give a truer count of live work.)
- Shared C++ conventions (macros, inheritance, templates) need one Rust design before any stem is translated. Their definitions sit deeper than the one-level include context, so the per-stem context figures leave out what the translator most needs. (evidence: Across src/*.h/.cpp/.ipp: 4,364 derived class/struct declarations, 17,225 lines with `virtual`, 7,275 template heads, 16,392 #define lines, OB_UNIS_VERSION 916 uses, OB_SERIALIZE_MEMBER 672, TO_STRING_KV 3,161. OB_UNIS_VERSION is defined at src/oblib/lib/utility/ob_unify_serialize.h:236. Of the 348 hand-written files that use it, only 55 include that header directly, so for 293 files the definition is outside the one-level context.)
- The ledger counts code that is not C++ translation work, and it misses some generator outputs. The survey's point that generators must be pointed at Rust output needs the full list of generators. (evidence: rust/sql-nio (23 .rs, 8,448 lines) is inside the hand-written total and the protocol-mysql row. src/sql/engine/expr/ob_expr_str_cmp_func_compile/gen_expr_str_cmp_func.py writes 9 checked-in files that are tagged hand-written. ob_all_virtual_sqlite_tables.cpp, emitted by generate_inner_table_schema.py (lines 1437-1539 and 1869-1973 write its includes), is missing from the not-in-git list. The PL parser outputs pl_parser_mysql_mode_lex.c and _tab.c are among the '50 inner-table files'.)

**Verifier's trust note:** You can trust the survey's measurements; its readings of what they mean need rework. With an independent method I reproduced:
- the file set, line and byte totals
- all five category totals
- the dead set (exactly, apart from one .def labelled data)
- all 52 hand-written group rows of the TSV
- the one-level context distribution (within about 1%)
- the 30-stem sample, to the token
- the header-cycle, forwarder and fan-in counts
- the test-coverage greps.
The factual errors are small: the 50 build-time files are not all inner-table files, 358 not 359 cast functions, the cap-based unit counts are a little high, the largest header cycle has 8 files not 9, a sixth generator was missed, ob_keybtree.cpp is live, and 'almost every oversize file is one class' is too strong (ob_schema_struct.h has 93 classes, ob_raw_expr.h about 48, ob_rpc_struct.h 149 structs).
The weaker parts are the interpretations. The 2.4M-token 'typical' closure comes from a biased sample; over all stems the median is 1.43M. The claim that header cycles set crate boundaries is wrong: cycles between directories and between groups are far larger, and the survey's own grouping across modules makes them worse. The stem-as-unit model ignores declaration/implementation pairs split across groups. Include-only dead detection misses compiled code that nothing calls, such as the codec library.
Use the ledger's counts and token sizes as they are. Rebuild the grouping, crate plan and dead-code estimate before they feed the cost formula.


## AREA: gap-moving-target
**What it is:** How fast the seekdb C++ tree is still changing, where the changes come from (the obdev mirror of seekdb's internal repo, GitHub PRs, cherry-picks from OceanBase mainline), how much code is still shared with OceanBase CE master, and which parallel branches and open PRs a port would also have to absorb. Every figure comes from read-only git on /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/migrate-to-rust (HEAD 076eb309b, remote refs fetched 2026-09-22) plus live GitHub API calls on 2026-09-24.

**Size split:** CHURN TABLE: C/C++ source only (.h .hpp .cpp .cc .c .ipp .y .l .inc). Merge commits are left out. Commits are deduplicated by patch-id. A "bulk" commit is one that deletes more than 5,000 lines in total. Windows are measured by committer date from 2026-08-25, 2026-06-26 and 2026-03-28 (+08:00).
