Data files (tz log, tpch data, collation tables) 81 files, -280,917. Build/CI/packaging +3,648/-1,769. Rust +712/-1,117. Generated 2 files. Vendored 3 files. Over 90 days: hand-written +281,797/-1,505,894, generated +9,924/-265,812, data -2,382,055, vendored +326/-346, tests +324,776/-4,013,592.
Commands: git log --no-merges --since=2026-03-28 --numstat -M --format=@@%H|%an|%ct|%s, aggregated in python3 read from stdin. Patch-ids from git log -p --no-merges | git patch-id --stable. git diff --numstat -M between the first-parent commit at each window start and HEAD (git rev-list -1 --first-parent --before=<date> HEAD).

**Summary:** The large feature-removal wave ran from 2026-06-11 to 2026-08-25 and is now over. C/C++ source fell from 6.62M lines (2026-06-23) to 2.84M (2026-08-22), then only another 2.4% to 2.77M by HEAD. In the last 30 days the ordinary edit rate in src/ C/C++ is 32 deduplicated commits, +3,895/-3,944 lines, about 0.3% of the tree per month. Three bulk commits removed another 80,040 lines (8a8d6f5a8 log reduction -59,109 on 2026-09-16, d51422b54 DirectLoadMgr, e2dc7eecf drift fix). Cutting is still going on at a lower rate. Open PR #1356 removes obmalloc (-12,094 lines in 227 files), and #1427 rewrites OB_FAIL branches to ASSERT_COND in 130 files. 18 open issues titled "Remove ..." act as the removal backlog, since docs/, AGENTS.md and README* state no roadmap. Raw git numbers overstate churn. Until 2026-08-25 the same change often landed twice, once through the obdev mirror and once as a GitHub PR, and then got reconciled by 38 "fix drift between internal and public master" commits (131 of 869 commits are exact patch duplicates). Large deletions also rode inside commits with unrelated messages (45cbbe9e1, 706253423). obdev is a mirror of seekdb's own internal repo, oceanbase-lite on gitlab.oceanbase-dev.com, not of OceanBase mainline. 25/25 sampled commits have no body beyond seekdb-engineer Co-authored-by trailers, and none has a mainline marker. The mirror stopped on 2026-08-25: all 57 commits on live master since then are GitHub PRs or direct pushes, and internal MRs now arrive as PRs (#1340, #1341). Fixes from OceanBase mainline did flow in, as 103 [CP] commits since 2025-09. That flow peaked at 58 in 2025-12 and has been almost zero since May 2026 (one in June, from 4_6_0_release). Upstream sharing is still high: 22 of 30 sampled hand-written files are near-identical (at least 90% of lines matched) to OB CE master, 90.1% line-weighted, and 75.6% of src C/C++ files exist at the same path in OB CE master, which keeps fixing them (29 commits to the 30 sampled files since 2026-03-28). So a rewrite gives up a fix stream seekdb has mostly stopped using, but one it could still use. Parallel lines a port would also have to absorb: 34 live branches were active in the last 30 days, the largest being feature/plugin (+105,879 code lines), namespace-worker-proxy-v20 (+17,592), feat/embedded-mode (+16,487), feature/webassembly-shell (+11,081), plus the release/1.4.0 maintenance branch. Judgment: the tree is settling but is still being cut.

**Structure call (surveyor):** This area does not decide translation style on its own, but it pushes toward a structure-preserving, file-for-file translation for as long as the C++ tree keeps changing. 75.6% of src C/C++ files still sit at the same path in OceanBase CE master, and about 90% of sampled lines match. A port that keeps the file and function layout can therefore keep replaying both seekdb's own fixes (about 8K lines a month, concentrated in sql/optimizer and sql/rewrite) and any OB mainline fix onto the Rust side. A redesign breaks both links for good. Against that, the mainline stream seekdb actually uses has nearly dried up (1 [CP] commit since May 2026, none since 2026-08-25), so giving it up costs little today but closes an option. A port should also start only after the in-flight structural PRs land (#1356 obmalloc removal, #1427 OB_FAIL to ASSERT_COND, #1351 plugin system), because they change the idioms a translator's rules would depend on. Files that force the call: src/sql/engine/ob_physical_plan.cpp, src/sql/engine/table/ob_table_scan_op.h and src/sql/engine/join/ob_join_filter_op.cpp (the shared files OB master changes most), plus the seekdb high-churn directories src/sql/optimizer, src/sql/rewrite, src/sql/resolver and src/storage/tx.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I partly agree. A port that keeps the file and function layout is the right call while the C++ tree is still moving. The strongest reason is seekdb's own edit stream: about 8K lines a month, with the SQL fixes concentrated in src/sql/optimizer and src/sql/rewrite by one author, all arriving as PRs that must be replayed. The upstream-sharing argument is much weaker than the survey says. The [CP] stream has been close to zero since May 2026. More importantly, most OB master commits on the 'most changed shared files' do not apply to seekdb. Of 21 OB master commits since 2026-03-28 on src/sql/engine/ob_physical_plan.cpp, table/ob_table_scan_op.h and join/ob_join_filter_op.cpp, only 4 have at least 80% of their old-side lines present in seekdb. The rest are OB feature placeholders ('占位'), feature merges, or SPM fixes; seekdb has no src/sql/spm, and ob_physical_plan.cpp has no evolution code, and the cited fa31e32f5 is one of those. Path and line overlap (75.6% same path, about 90% of seekdb lines found in OB) measures shared ancestry, not a usable fix feed: two-way similarity of at least 0.9 holds for only 13-17 of 30 sampled files. Advice to wait for in-flight PRs should be narrowed. #1427 touches 559 of about 99,910 OB_FAIL sites and does not change the idiom a translator keys on. #1356 keeps ob_malloc facades, so the 186 caller files stay the same and only src/oblib/lib/alloc and src/oblib/lib/resource internals go away. #1245 (GIS to plugin) is parked on master.bak with 0 changed files. #1351 (feature/plugin) does matter, but for another reason: it adds its own Rust plugin runtime and extension SDK (34.6K Rust lines under rust/plugin-runtime, rust/extension-sdk and plugins/rust_*), which fixes FFI and crate-layout conventions that any port has to line up with.

**Size split check (verifier):** I rebuilt the churn table from git log --no-merges --numstat -M with patch-id dedup and the same bulk rule. All src rows match exactly for 30, 90 and 180 days, including the second-level sql and storage rows. The net-size snapshots also match when counted a different way (cat-file line counts): 7.18M, 6.62M, 2.84M and 2.77M lines. Four problems with the split. (1) The unittest+mittest row only reproduces if deps/oblib/unittest is included, and the label doesn't say so. (2) The table leaves out src/libtable (removed whole, 180d bulk -36,132), src/plugin (-7,615) and src/diagnose. (3) The 30d figures agree with reality: the table net of -67.7K matches the snapshot diff of -67.3K. The 90d figures still double count after patch-id dedup: the table net of -4.55M against a real shrink of -3.84M leaves about 0.7M lines (~18%) of near-duplicate commits or deletions undone by merges. One example is 977053bf1's -318K tools/obtest deletion, which never reached HEAD. (4) The 30d 'bulk' column contains e2dc7eecf, whose 1,086-file src part (+12,097/-12,053) is whitespace reconciliation, not a cut. The real 30d cuts in src are about 68K lines (8a8d6f5a8 and d51422b54). The split between hand-written and generated code in the net 30d diff is close to my own (2,078 files +3,930/-71,192 vs their 2,074 +3,936/-71,085). The survey's headline rate (about 8K ordinary lines a month) is sound.

**Units of work:** For keeping a port in step with C++, the natural unit is the second-level directory: 18 under src/sql plus 36 loose files, 32 under src/storage plus 91 loose files, and 10 other top-level src modules. After 2026-08-25 the change feed arrives as GitHub PRs: 63 merged since then, with 35 commits touching src C/C++ in 30 days (32 ordinary, 3 bulk). A sync can therefore be run per PR. Parallel lines are separate units: 8 branches differ from HEAD by more than 1,000 code lines (feature/plugin, codex/namespace-worker-proxy-v20, feat/embedded-mode, feature/webassembly-shell, feature/webassembly, feature/assert, codex/palf-log-buffer-redesign, feature/rust), plus release/1.4.0 as a maintenance line.

**Public-surface tests:** n/a as a code area; the relevant facts are how the test surface moves. At HEAD, tools/deploy/mysql_test has 283 .test / 305 .result files (largest suites: geometry 42, vector_index 22, fork_table 18, fts_index 15, subquery 11, expr 11), and tools/obtest has 509 .test / 555 .result. The mysql_test count went 600 (2026-03-09), 593 (2026-06-23), 503 (2026-08-22), 283 (HEAD). Tests that import internals have almost disappeared: unittest+mittest went from 2,030 files to 4 (the 678-file deletion is in 45cbbe9e1). In the last 30 days mysql_test got 5 ordinary commits (+176/-12 lines) and 1 bulk commit. Recent SQL fixes add cases there (e.g. 270b47ea9, 192aedaf6, 402f0e25b touch tools/deploy/mysql_test). Only the public-surface suite survives the churn, but it is itself being cut.

**Hard parts:**
- Keeping up with SQL correctness fixes during a port: in the last 30 days 11 of 16 ordinary src/sql commits hit src/sql/optimizer and src/sql/rewrite (SQLancer-found regressions, e.g. b228105f0, 192aedaf6, 402f0e25b). Each has to be replayed on the Rust side, or those directories frozen.
- The allocator under the code is being replaced: open PR #1356 (codex/remove-obmalloc-upstream, 227 files, -12,094 lines) removes obmalloc for jemalloc, which touches src/oblib/lib/alloc and every ob_malloc caller (186 files at HEAD). A port started before it lands would be porting an allocator on its way out.
- The error-handling idiom is changing: PR #1427 (feature/assert, 130 files) rewrites infallible OB_FAIL branches to ASSERT_COND, and a translator's rules would key on exactly the OB_FAIL/ret pattern.
- Parallel lines that would need re-porting or merging first: feature/plugin (include/seekdb/plugin, plugins/gis, +105,879 code lines), feat/embedded-mode (C ABI, 197 commits ahead), feature/webassembly-shell (260 code files), codex/namespace-worker-proxy-v20 (src/observer/namespace_worker_*.ipp, 223 commits ahead), feature/rust (#1412, a second Rust effort by another author).
- A released version is maintained in parallel: release/1.4.0 (6 ahead, 692 behind master) receives cherry-picked fixes (#1389 '[1.4.0] Fix block stat memtable refresh rowkey boundary'). The C++ code base has to stay alive for released versions during any port.
- History can't be trusted as a change feed. The same change landed twice on the internal-mirror and GitHub lines (131 exact duplicate patches since 2026-03-28), drift-fix commits reconciled the two, and large deletions rode in commits with unrelated messages (45cbbe9e1, 706253423). A sync tool for a port must diff trees, not follow commit messages.
- Upstream divergence differs by module: src/share matches OB CE paths for only 52% of files and src/oblib for 70%, versus 90% for src/sql. The option to keep taking OB mainline fixes is worth the most for sql/ and storage/, and the least for share/ and the seekdb-only modules (src/query, src/data_plane, src/standby).

**Pain instances:**
- 6da784ac1 (twin e66776e2d, #1319): wrong buffer length passed to databuff_printf (buf_len instead of extra_print_buf_size) in src/observer/virtual_table/ob_table_columns.cpp. Rust slices carry their length, so this mismatch can't be expressed.
- 6da784ac1: plan-cache accounting read a cached object after releasing the node write lock, while concurrent eviction could drop its last reference (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_plan_cache.cpp). A borrow tied to the lock guard would have rejected this.
- 6da784ac1: ObPsStmtInfo dereferenced ps_item_, which the prepare path can free first (src/sql/plan_cache/ob_ps_cache_callback.h). This is a dangling pointer across two owners.
- bbfa2d543 (twin a44c7762d, #1166): virtual table row cells pointed into buffers owned by a local diagnose_info that was destroyed before the row was read (src/observer/virtual_table/ob_all_virtual_ha_diagnose.cpp). The borrow checker rejects a returned reference that outlives its owner.
- dd899675c: fallback schema managers were allocated from rotating arenas that a concurrent refresh could reset, fixed by taking schema_refresh_mutex_; the same commit fixes the bound check `count() > MAX_PARALLEL_TASK` to `>=` (src/share/schema/ob_multi_version_schema_service.cpp). Rust helps with the arena race only if arena lifetimes are expressed as borrows; the off-by-one would remain a logic bug.
- 50652b1e9 and 5c2637685: PL interpreter per-call temporaries and SQL string copies were allocated on the long-lived exec allocator, so memory grew in long-running SQL (src/pl/ob_pl_interpreter.cpp). Rust's scope-based drop makes per-call cleanup the default, but a port that keeps a shared bump arena could repeat this.

**Open questions:**
- Is the internal oceanbase-lite repo still an active source of changes after 2026-08-25? Only its migrated MRs are visible (#1340, #1341, 4 open PRs), and the gitlab host can't be reached.
- Where do [CP] commits come from exactly (OB master, 4_6_0_release, or another internal branch)? Only one body (6e53dd10f) names its source branch, and 3 of 6 sampled subjects were found in OB CE master.
- Will seekdb resume taking OB mainline fixes, e.g. for vector index or hybrid search, which were the bulk of the 2025-12 [CP] wave? No stated policy was found in docs/, AGENTS.md, README* or the issues.
- Which of the 18 open 'Remove ...' issues and in-flight PRs (#1356 obmalloc, #1427 ASSERT_COND, #1351 plugin, #1245 GIS to plugin) will land, and when? That decides whether the tree is settled enough to freeze for a port.
- Will the large parallel lines (feature/plugin, feat/embedded-mode, namespace-worker, webassembly-shell) be merged into master before any port starts, or stay long-lived?
- Is the 30-day rate (about 8K ordinary lines a month) representative? It covers only the first month after the switch to GitHub-only development and includes a quieter week (2026-W39).
- Near-duplicate commits (the same change with different conflict resolution) are not caught by patch-id, so the 90- and 180-day ordinary figures still carry some double counting.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- The OB mainline fix stream a rewrite would 'give up' is mostly not usable today, even for files that are shared. Most OB master commits on shared files are placeholders or target code seekdb has removed, so the ongoing cost of cutting off upstream is lower than the sharing percentages suggest. OB master itself is very active: 214 commits in 30 days and 740 in 90. (evidence: For the 21 OB master commits since 2026-03-28 on ob_physical_plan.cpp, ob_table_scan_op.h and ob_join_filter_op.cpp, I checked each patch's old-side lines against git show HEAD:<file>. Only 27b673bed, df974faad, cdb586d85 and 8edead784 have at least 80% present. Commits like d7505c273, b2b1bf47d, e57277511, db3d2baec and f0b296f72 are '占位' (placeholder) commits. fa31e32f5, 8846ff365 and ba7e27bb0 are SPM fixes, and git ls-files src/sql/spm returns 0. gh api commits?sha=master&since= returns 214 (30d) and 740 (90d).)
- Rust is already being written in parallel lines, in three places. The survey counts feature/plugin as C/C++ to be re-ported, but a third of it is Rust. The plugin runtime and extension SDK set an FFI and crate layout that a port would have to match or reconcile. (evidence: git diff --numstat HEAD...origin/feature/plugin: Rust +34,611 (rust/extension-sdk 16,929, rust/plugin-runtime 10,792, plugins/rust_candidate 4,000, plugins/rust_text 1,907, rust/cargo-seekdb 976) against C/C++ +71,176. origin/feature/rust (eef859558, #1412) adds rust/embedding-response and edits rust/sql-nio. HEAD already has rust/sql-nio (23 .rs files, 8,464 lines).)
- Many open PRs are parked on a stale base. 18 open PRs target master.bak (the pre-switch master at b4c1b0655, 2026-08-28), and none has been updated since 2026-09-01. They include 16 of the internal-tracker branch PRs and #1245 (GIS to plugin, 0 changed files). Their sizes are also misleading: #1333 shows +352,476 lines, mostly tools/obtest, which is an artifact of the base. Only 34 open PRs target master. (evidence: gh pr list --state open --json baseRefName,updatedAt, grouped: master 34 (26 updated since 09-01), master.bak 18 (0 updated), vldb_2026 60, release/1.3.0 1. gh api branches/master.bak gives b4c1b0655 at 2026-08-28T07:07:09Z. gh api pulls/1333/files is mostly 1,313 tools/obtest files.)
- The in-flight 'structural' PRs are narrower than presented, so they are weak reasons to delay. #1356 keeps ob_malloc as a facade over jemalloc, so callers don't change. #1427 converts 559 of about 99,910 OB_FAIL sites. (evidence: gh api pulls/1356 body: 'keep source-compatible ob_malloc facades backed by bundled jemalloc'. It removes 39 files, mostly under src/oblib/lib/alloc and src/oblib/lib/resource. gh api pulls/1427 body: 'replace 559 failure branches across 269 source files'. git grep -c -w OB_FAIL over src gives 99,910 matches in 2,784 files.)
- About half the post-switch change stream comes from coding-agent branches, and a few people drive it. That means the edit rate can jump quickly, and the maintainers already have agent tooling that could carry a replay or sync process. The 30d SQL fixes all come from one author. (evidence: gh pr list --state merged --search 'merged:>=2026-08-25 base:master' returns 55 PRs. 28 have agent-style head branches (codex/ 22, work-assistant/ 4, agent/ 2). By author: willlling 18, wangyunlai-seekdb 17, hnwyllmm 5, LINxiansheng 5. All 9 distinct 30d commits in sql/optimizer or sql/rewrite are by willlling.)

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's numbers can be trusted. I reproduced the churn table's src rows, the net-size snapshots, the bulk-commit list, patch-id duplicates, the obdev sample (exact seed reproduction), the [CP] monthly counts, the OB CE path-sharing percentages, branch divergence and PR/issue counts, most of them exactly. The errors I found are small: 53 rather than 58 sync merges, 9 rather than 11 optimizer/rewrite commits, the unittest row silently including deps/oblib/unittest, and a few omitted modules. The main weakness is interpretation, not counting. The upstream-sharing figures are presented as a live fix stream seekdb 'could still use', but most OB master changes to the sampled shared files are placeholders or target features seekdb has deleted, including the cited SPM example fa31e32f5. 'Near-identical' really means 'seekdb's lines are contained in OB's'. The in-flight PRs (#1427, #1356, #1245) are framed as larger idiom changes than they are. 16 of the 18 open internal-tracker PRs, plus #1245, are parked on master.bak. And feature/plugin's Rust content is counted as C++ to re-port. The 30-day rate and the verdict that the tree is 'settling but still being cut' hold. The report should soften the value it puts on keeping upstream compatibility, and should not delay a port on account of #1427 or #1356.


## AREA: gap-oom-policy-conflict
**What it is:** This area is the allocation-failure policy of seekdb: which allocators can hand a NULL (or a memory-limit error) back to a caller in the default jemalloc build, and which of the roughly 4,700 `ret = OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED` sites guard a failure that can actually happen. It covers the ob_malloc/jemalloc glue and malloc hook, the obmalloc context allocators, the module budgets (ObBlockAllocMgr and the FIFO allocators), the SQL work-area manager, the TxShare throttle, the KV cache store, and the existing Rust crate rust/sql-nio.

**Size split:** Hand-written C++ directly in scope: 129 files / 34,491 lines (src/oblib/lib/alloc 33 files / 8,436; src/oblib/lib/allocator 36 / 8,320; src/oblib/lib/resource 5 / 1,635; src/oblib/lib/rc 4 / 1,005; src/storage/throttle 9 / 1,811; src/storage/allocator 14 / 2,098; src/share/cache 22 / 8,356; SQL mem mgr + processor 4 / 2,685; sql/executor/ob_memory_tracker.* 2 / 145). Of these, 26 files / 8,490 lines are obmalloc-only (block_set, object_set, object_mgr, abit_set, ob_ctx_allocator, ob_context_allocator, memory_dump, alloc_struct, alloc_failed_reason, achunk_mgr, ob_resource_mgr, ob_allocator_v2, ob_page_manager), plus the malloc hook (malloc_hook.cpp + malloc_hook_extended.cpp, 674 lines). Generated: the error-code tables that define -4013/-4030/-4080/-11049/-7603 (src/share/ob_errno.cpp, src/share/ob_errno.h, src/oblib/lib/ob_errno.h, 18,824 lines total, 'DO NOT EDIT ... generated from ob_errno.def'; ob_errno.def is 1,914 lines). Data: none. Vendored: jemalloc is not in the tree; it is built from the Cargo crate seekdb-jemalloc-sys =0.2.2 (deps/external/Cargo.toml:10, deps/external/cmake/Jemalloc.cmake). deps/oblib holds 3 files / 308 lines (an out-of-date copy of ob_jemalloc.h plus 2 allocator unit tests). Existing Rust: rust/sql-nio 24 .rs files / 8,464 lines. Commands: `git ls-files -- <dirs> | grep -E '\.(h|hpp|cpp|cc|c|ipp|inc)$' | xargs cat | wc -l` per directory, and `git ls-files <obmalloc file list> | xargs wc -l`.

**Summary:** In the default build (bundled jemalloc on Linux and macOS without ASAN), ob_malloc, ob_realloc and ObMallocAllocator call je_malloc/je_realloc directly and ignore ObMemAttr. The per-context limits, the label accounting and the global limit, all of which live in the obmalloc ObMemoryMgr/ObCtxAllocator path, are never checked. The setters for the global limit have zero callers, so the only failure left under the general allocator is jemalloc itself returning NULL. The memory_limit parameter is documented as deprecated and ignored, memory_budget only sizes things, and no system_memory parameter exists. The real fail points are a short list of module budgets. The clog slice allocators (30% of budget) return -4013 at 5 log_engine.cpp sites. The replay budget (min 5%, 512MB) is turned into OB_EAGAIN back-pressure. The IO allocator budget ends in -4013 for request/result objects and in a retry-until-timeout for IO buffers. The KV cache store has a fixed budget that runs its own synchronous wash and then fails with -4013, and that error can reach a read through put_cache_block. The vector module limit returns NULL/-7603. Four more budgets use their own error codes: the query memory tracker (-11049), the memstore-full check on DML (-4030), the temp-file write buffer pool (OB_ALLOCATE_TMP_FILE_PAGE_FAILED), and SQL work areas, which spill to disk instead of failing. The TxShare throttle for tx-data and MDS only sleeps, and their ObBlockAllocMgr stays at INT64_MAX. About 14 FIFO allocators carry large fixed caps (128MB to 10GB) that are practically unreachable. By my scripted classification, roughly 40 of the 4,737 `ret = OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED` sites (under 1%) sit behind one of these budgets; the rest check a NULL from arenas or ob_malloc that only comes from jemalloc. No mysqltest .result/.test, init SQL or bindings test expects 4013/HY001 (or -4030/-11049/-4080/-7603) or sets a limit low enough to trigger one. Allocation-failure injection (errsim EN_4) exists only on the obmalloc path, and no script selects that backend, so the 4013 handling code is effectively untested. On Linux the malloc hook sends every libc allocation, and so every Rust allocation, to jemalloc; on macOS the jemalloc zone is promoted to the default zone. rust/sql-nio already works the way I recommend: no global_allocator, panic=abort, handle_alloc_error, and try_reserve only where a client or peer controls the size. Recommendation: abort on OOM, plus explicit typed budget checks at 8 named budget owners (clog, replay, IO allocator, KV cache store, vector module, query memory tracker, memstore-full check, temp-file write buffer pool), plus the existing spill protocol for SQL work areas and the sleep-only throttle. Fallible collections everywhere would carry over about 4,700 checks for a failure that does not occur in the default build.

**Structure call (surveyor):** This area does not call for a 1:1 translation. A 1:1 translation would turn about 4,700 `ret = OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED` checks and about 6,470 `OB_FAIL(push_back)` checks into fallible collections. In the default build nearly all of them guard a jemalloc NULL that does not come back, and allocation-failure injection cannot test them. The error model should be redesigned. General allocation aborts on failure (Rust's default, already used by sql-nio with panic=abort and handle_alloc_error). The code that can really run out gets explicit typed budget checks: clog, replay, the IO allocator, the KV cache store reservation, the vector module, the query memory tracker, the memstore-full check, and the temp-file write buffer pool. SQL work areas keep their spill protocol, and the throttle stays sleep-only. try_reserve is kept only where a client, peer or data controls the size, as sql-nio does today. Push-style calls that can fail for non-memory reasons (ObFixedArray capacity, deep-copy assign) stay Result-returning, but as capacity or copy errors, not OOM. The obmalloc backend (26 files, 8,490 lines) and the obmalloc branch of the malloc hook should not be translated. The large fixed FIFO caps (128MB to 10GB) become plain allocations, or bounded queues where the cap is really a queue bound. Files that force the call: src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_malloc.h, src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_malloc_allocator.cpp, src/oblib/lib/alloc/malloc_hook.cpp, src/oblib/lib/resource/ob_resource_mgr.cpp, src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_block_alloc_mgr.h, src/logservice/ob_log_allocator.cpp, src/share/io/ob_io_struct.cpp, src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_store.cpp, src/storage/allocator/ob_vector_allocator.cpp, src/sql/executor/ob_memory_tracker.cpp.

**Structure call check (verifier):** I agree with the direction: abort on OOM for general allocation, explicit typed budget checks at the few real budget owners, and no 1:1 carry-over of ~4,700 NULL checks and ~6,470 push_back checks. The code supports it. On the default Linux/macOS build every general path ends in je_malloc (ob_malloc.h:118-125, ob_malloc_allocator.cpp:62-70, malloc_hook.cpp:161-165). Uncaught std::bad_alloc from operator new already terminates the process (malloc_hook_extended.cpp:215-231, only 5 catch sites). sql-nio already aborts on OOM. Even the obmalloc path has no enforced limit, because limit_bytes_ is written while hard_limit_bytes_ is what update_ctx_hold checks (ob_resource_mgr.cpp:157-165 vs 229-240). Four corrections are needed before this goes into the rulebook. (a) The IO allocator entry is backwards. Request and result objects retry until the IO timeout, and data buffers fail with -4013, because try_alloc_buf_until_timeout has no callers (ob_io_define.cpp:866, 1129, 1189; ob_io_manager.cpp:722-770). (b) The named list misses -4013 sources that are not allocator NULLs, which a 'delete the NULL check' rule would not cover and which must stay as typed errors: hash-partition depth in hash join (ob_hash_join_op.cpp:837-842, ob_hash_partitioning_infrastructure_op.h:1699-1700), KV cache mb-handle pool exhaustion (ob_kvcache_store.cpp:1029-1030), the IVF cache vector limit (ob_vector_index_ivf_cache_mgr.cpp:163-164) and vsag NO_ENOUGH_MEMORY (ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp:61-62). (c) The micro block cache's 4GB FIFO is a real budget with a 3-try sleep loop. It reaches reads through generic allocator references (ob_micro_block_cache.cpp:396-402, 1115-1118). (d) The recommendation only holds for builds with bundled jemalloc. Windows x64 and Android arm64 (build.sh:46-47), and Bazel non-sanity builds, default to obmalloc with no malloc hook. On Windows there is no overcommit, so abort-on-OOM turns a recoverable query error into a process abort. That needs an explicit decision, or a platform policy.

**Size split check (verifier):** The raw counts reproduce exactly with the survey's own method: alloc 33/8,436, allocator 36/8,320, resource 5/1,635, rc 4/1,005, throttle 9/1,811, storage/allocator 14/2,098, share/cache 22/8,356, SQL mem mgr + processor 4/2,685, memory_tracker 2/145, for a total of 129/34,491. The obmalloc list is 26 files/8,490 lines. Generated errno is 396+15,636+2,792 = 18,824 lines, all marked 'DO NOT EDIT ... ob_errno.def', and the .def is 1,914 lines. deps/oblib is 3 files/308 lines. Three corrections: (1) Existing Rust is 23 .rs files / 8,448 lines (22 in src plus build.rs), not 24/8,464. (2) The 26-file 'obmalloc-only' group is not all obmalloc-only. alloc_struct.h/.cpp defines ObLabel, ObMemAttr, ObMallocHookAttrGuard and ObUnmanagedMemoryStat, which every allocation site and the malloc hook use. ob_resource_mgr.cpp's ObMemoryMgr::alloc_cache_mb has a jemalloc branch that the KV cache calls on the default path (ob_kvcache_store.cpp:1150, 1163). (3) ob_context_allocator.h/.cpp in that group is not compiled at all (no source inventory lists the .cpp). The same holds for storage/meta_mem/ob_tablet_meta_mem_mgr.cpp and ob_meta_obj_pool.cpp, which src/storage/BUILD.bazel:49-52 excludes. So a few hundred lines in scope are dead rather than 'leave out while obmalloc exists'.

**Units of work:** 1) The allocator glue: ob_malloc.h/.cpp, ob_jemalloc.h and the malloc hook, 2 hook files plus 3 glue files. This becomes one small unit that keeps a single jemalloc shared by C++ and Rust; no port of the obmalloc branch. 2) The obmalloc backend, 26 files / 8,490 lines: leave it out (keep the C++ while it exists, then delete). 3) General-purpose allocator primitives in src/oblib/lib/allocator (36 files): arenas, slice/vslice/FIFO allocators and the qsync/hazard helpers. The natural unit is one file group per allocator family, about 6 families: page_arena, FIFO, slice/vslice/small, concurrent/LF FIFO, hazard/qsync/retire, and block alloc mgr. 4) Budget owners, one unit each, each translated together with its explicit budget check: ObLogAllocator (src/logservice/ob_log_allocator.*), ObIOAllocator (src/share/io/ob_io_struct.cpp and ob_io_manager.cpp), ObKVCacheStore (src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_store.*), ObVectorAllocator (src/storage/allocator/ob_vector_allocator.*), ObMemTrackerGuard (src/sql/executor/ob_memory_tracker.*), the memstore-full check (src/storage/tx_storage/ob_memstore_freezer.cpp plus the ob_access_service.cpp call), ObTmpWriteBufferPool (src/storage/tmp_file/ob_tmp_file_write_buffer_pool.*), and the SQL work-area manager with its spill signal (src/sql/engine/ob_sql_memory_manager.* and ob_sql_mem_mgr_processor.*). That is 8 units. 5) The throttle framework (src/storage/throttle, 9 files) and the shared memory allocator manager (src/storage/allocator, 14 files): 2 units. Each of the roughly 4,700 OOM sites is not a unit of work. Under abort-on-OOM they disappear as part of translating their host files, so the rulebook entry is one mechanical rule ('NULL-from-allocator check: delete; allocation aborts') plus the named exceptions above.

**Public-surface tests:** None of the 861 .result or 793 .test files under tools/deploy/mysql_test expects -4013/HY001, -4030, -11049, -4080 or -7603, and none sets a memory limit low enough to trigger one (git grep, 0 hits). Memory-related public tests exercise sizing and spilling only: join_many_table.test and join_many_table_single_field.test set ob_sql_work_area_percentage to 100 and then 5 (the spill path); test_suite/fork_table/t/fork_table_vector.test and test_suite/vector_index (all_virtual_vector_index_info) set vector_memory_limit = 0 (automatic). test_suite/inner_table/r/mysql/all_virtual_sys_parameter_stat.result pins the parameter names (memory_budget, memory_limit, kvcache_memory_limit, memstore_memory_limit, vector_memory_limit, query_memory_limit_percentage, _ctx_memory_limit, _storage_meta_memory_limit_percentage, _pdml_thread_cache_size), so dropping or renaming a parameter breaks that result even though no behavior test depends on it. tools/deploy/init.sql (54 lines) and init_user.sql (6 lines) set no memory parameter. seekdb-bindings: 9 tracked test files. lib/tests/test_open_parameters.cc and test_parameter_persistence.cc set and read memory_limit (1G/10G) and expect the value to persist; none expects an OOM error. Only 2 C++ unit tests touch this area (deps/oblib/unittest/lib/alloc/test_jemalloc_hook.cpp, deps/oblib/unittest/lib/allocator/test_malloc_backend.cpp), and both import internals (ob_jemalloc.h, ob_malloc.h). Result: abort-on-OOM breaks no public-surface test.

**Hard parts:**
- The ObIAllocator trait object is the receiver at about 2,500 OOM sites (receiver names 'allocator'/'allocator_'/'alloc'/'get_allocator()'). Arenas that hand out memory tied to a query or statement lifetime (src/oblib/lib/allocator/page_arena.h, src/oblib/lib/rc/context.h) need a Rust arena design (lifetimes, or bump arenas with explicit reset) because allocator_api is unstable.
- Lock-free slice and FIFO allocators built on hazard pointers and quiescent-state reclamation: src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_slice_alloc.h (541 lines), ob_vslice_alloc.h (294), ob_qsync.h, ob_hazard_ref.h, ob_retire_station.h. This is unsafe-heavy code, intrusive block headers included.
- The memstore arena with reference-counted pages retired by quiescent state: src/storage/allocator/ob_fifo_arena.cpp (ObFifoArena::alloc/free, WaitQuiescent).
- The KV cache store: a fixed budget reserved with CAS, a synchronous wash under wash_out_lock_, hazptr-protected handle scans and a qclock retire station (src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_store.cpp:849-1000).
- One allocator shared across the language boundary. C++ and Rust must keep freeing each other's pointers through the same jemalloc. On Linux that works today only because src/oblib/lib/alloc/malloc_hook.cpp and malloc_hook_extended.cpp interpose libc symbols; on macOS it depends on zone promotion in src/observer/main.cpp:645-653. A Rust #[global_allocator] with a different jemalloc prefix would break cross-frees during a mixed period.
- Per-context memory accounting (ObMemAttr ctx_id and label; TrackedAllocator headers in src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_malloc.h:222-359; MemoryUsageTracker resolvers in ob_malloc.cpp:195-211). Rust's GlobalAlloc receives no attributes, so accounting has to come from explicit allocator handles or a thread-local context.
- Variadic-template throttle framework with macro-generated units: src/storage/throttle/ob_share_resource_throttle_tool.ipp and ob_throttle_unit.ipp (ACQUIRE_THROTTLE_UNIT, DEFINE_CUSTOM_FUNC_FOR_THROTTLE).
- The vsag third-party C++ library allocates through ObVsagMemContext (src/storage/allocator/ob_vector_allocator.cpp). The vector budget check must stay reachable from C++ through FFI, and a NULL returned to vsag is handled inside vsag.

**Pain instances:**
- Retry loops on -4013 that spin waiting for memory that the default build never withholds: src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_store.cpp:405-407 and 642-644 (do/while around hazptr protect until the result is not OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED); src/storage/meta_mem/ob_storage_meta_mem_mgr.cpp:374-409 (retries up to 1000 times with 100ms sleeps); the same code duplicated in src/storage/meta_mem/ob_tablet_meta_mem_mgr.cpp:371-404; src/storage/throttle/ob_throttle_unit.ipp:236-248 (sleep 10ms and retry).
- The obmalloc malloc hook's ob_malloc_retry loops forever until allocation succeeds (src/oblib/lib/alloc/malloc_hook.cpp:75-86), so every NULL check behind libc malloc is dead code on that path too.
- Configuration knobs that do nothing in the default build: _storage_meta_memory_limit_percentage becomes set_ctx_limit (src/storage/meta_mem/ob_meta_memory_limit.h:28-35), and _ctx_memory_limit is applied by a duty task (src/observer/ob_server_duty_task.cpp:60-80). Neither limit is read on the jemalloc path. memory_limit is documented as ignored (ob_parameter_seed.ipp:81-84) but is still set and tested by the seekdb-bindings tests.
- Dead limit plumbing: ObMallocAllocator::set_allocator_limit and set_allocator_hard_limit have zero callers (ob_malloc_allocator.cpp:172-196). The ObFixedSizeBlockAllocator template, with its -4080 path, has no users outside its header (src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_fixed_size_block_allocator.h:293-301).
- ObRADatumStore::need_dump reads the ctx allocator's limit and hold (src/sql/engine/basic/ob_ra_datum_store.cpp:1233-1256). Under jemalloc the hold is never updated (update_hold is reached only from ObMemoryMgr::alloc_chunk/free_chunk, ob_resource_mgr.cpp:55-105), so its 80% trigger never fires.
- Zero-size requests are reported as OOM: ob_malloc on the jemalloc path returns NULL and logs OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED for nbyte <= 0 (src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_malloc.h:118-125).
- The 4013 handling paths cannot be exercised in the default build, because errsim EN_4 injection exists only in obmalloc (src/oblib/lib/alloc/ob_ctx_allocator.h:427, ob_context_allocator.h:454) and no tracked script sets MALLOC_BACKEND.
- An out-of-date duplicate header: deps/oblib/src/lib/allocator/ob_jemalloc.h lacks the ENABLE_SANITY branches present in src/oblib/lib/allocator/ob_jemalloc.h (diff of the two files).

**Open questions:**
- Whether any production deployment runs with vm.overcommit_memory=2 or a single request large enough to make je_malloc return NULL. That is the only case where abort-on-OOM would turn a query error into a process abort; it cannot be settled read-only.
- Whether anyone runs MALLOC_BACKEND=obmalloc in production. If obmalloc must be kept, the ctx limits (META_OBJ_CTX_ID, _ctx_memory_limit) become real fail points again and join the list of named budgets.
- Which allocator is behind the 6 allocator_-> sites in src/storage/blocksstable/ob_micro_block_cache.cpp (around lines 399-402, 500-503, 681-683, 697-699): the 4GB cache FIFO or a per-query unbounded ObFIFOAllocator.
- Whether a -4013 from the KV cache reservation reaching a read through put_cache_block (ob_micro_block_cache.cpp:442-446) happens in practice, and whether the Rust design should fall back to an uncached copy instead of failing the read.
- On macOS, whether allocations made before inner_main promotes the jemalloc zone (static constructors, early Rust code) end up freed through a different zone, and whether je_zone_register alone already makes jemalloc the default zone.
- How vsag behaves when ObVsagMemContext::Allocate returns NULL after the vector limit is hit (a third-party library, not in this tree).
- Whether ObStorageMetaMemMgr and ObTabletMetaMemMgr are both live; the files are near-duplicates, each with its own 4GB meta_cache_io allocator.
- Whether the memory virtual tables (__all_virtual_memory_info, ctx memory info) must stay meaningful. Under jemalloc the ctx hold is never updated, so a Rust port must decide whether to rebuild per-ctx accounting (MemoryUsageTracker) or drop it.

**Missed by the survey (verifier):**
- Several supported build targets do not use jemalloc. Windows x64, Android arm64 and Bazel non-sanity builds have no bundled jemalloc, so their default backend is obmalloc with no malloc hook. On Windows, commit limits make NULL returns real, and Rust allocations go to the system heap rather than the C++ allocator. This limits 'NULL never comes back' and 'one shared jemalloc' to Linux and macOS CMake builds. (evidence: deps/external/CMakeLists.txt:17-19 (Jemalloc.cmake only for Linux OR APPLE); ob_malloc.cpp:147-152 (#else -> OBMALLOC); src/oblib/lib/CMakeLists.txt:34-41 and observer/CMakeLists.txt:17-22 (no malloc_hook on WIN32/ANDROID/APPLE); build.sh:31-47 (--android, 'Windows x64 uses build.ps1'); bazel/seekdb_build_config.bzl:207-214 (OB_HAVE_BUNDLED_JEMALLOC only under sanity); seekdb-bindings lib/src/port.c:171 (CreateProcessA))
- The obmalloc path has no enforced byte limits either, so keeping obmalloc would not add named budgets. The survey's open question assumes the opposite. set_ctx_limit writes limit_bytes_, but enforcement reads hard_limit_bytes_, which nothing sets. ObMemoryMgr::hard_limit_ and AChunkMgr::hard_limit_ are never set. obmalloc fails only on an OS mmap failure after its sync-wash retry, or on errsim. (evidence: src/oblib/lib/resource/ob_resource_mgr.cpp:157-165 vs 201-240; ob_ctx_allocator.h:89-109 (set_hard_limit has no callers); achunk_mgr.cpp:311-326 and achunk_mgr.h:185, 349-361 (8GB soft limit only triggers cache reuse; hard limit INT64_MAX); git grep -P 'set_hard_limit\(|set_ctx_hard_limit\(' shows only definitions)
- Some -4013 errors are logical failures, not allocator NULLs, and are user-visible on the default build. They must survive the rewrite as typed errors, but they are not among the 8 named owners. (evidence: src/sql/engine/join/ob_hash_join_op.cpp:837-842 ('we report 4013 instead of 4016, and remind user to increase memory'); src/sql/engine/basic/ob_hash_partitioning_infrastructure_op.h:1699-1700 (part_shift_ >= 64); src/share/cache/ob_kvcache_store.cpp:1029-1030 (mb_handles_pool_ empty -> -4013); src/observer/vector_index/ob_vector_index_ivf_cache_mgr.cpp:145-164 (vector limit -> -4013); src/oblib/lib/vector/ob_vsag_adaptor.cpp:61-62)
- Some in-scope files are not compiled, which skews the counts, the pain list and the open questions. ObTabletMetaMemMgr and ObMetaObjPool are excluded from the build, which answers the 'both live?' question and removes the second 4GB meta_cache_io cap and the duplicated retry loop. ob_context_allocator.cpp is in no source inventory, so its errsim hook and the ObContextAllocatorV2 code are dead. IO's try_alloc_buf_until_timeout is also dead. (evidence: src/storage/BUILD.bazel:49-52; storage_source_inventory.bzl lists only ob_storage_meta_mem_mgr.cpp (line 334); git grep ob_context_allocator.cpp returns nothing outside the file itself; git grep try_alloc_buf_until_timeout finds only ob_io_define.cpp:1189 and ob_io_define.h:478)
- Budgeted FIFOs are handed down as generic ObIAllocator references. So receiver-name matching undercounts budget-backed sites, and the '8 named sites' boundary is not a set of call sites. The micro block cache's 4GB FIFO flows into IO callbacks and the cache put path, and it has its own sleep-retry loop. The tx replay path turns -4013 into OB_EAGAIN and retries. Both are behaviors the rulebook has to decide on. (evidence: src/storage/blocksstable/ob_micro_block_cache.cpp:396-402 (3 retries, 100ms*i sleeps; ob_micro_block_cache.h:292-293), 444-445, 1037-1039, 1115-1118 (get_allocator returns the 4GB allocator_); src/storage/tx/ob_tx_replay_executor.cpp:697-702 (rewrite -4013 as OB_EAGAIN); 78 comparison sites (git grep -P '(==|!=)\s*OB_ALLOCATE_MEMORY_FAILED'))

**Verifier's trust note:** The survey's numbers can be trusted: every count I rechecked with a different method matched, except the sql-nio file total (23/8,448, not 24/8,464) and a few small tallies (ObBlockAllocMgr instances, FIFO sites, memory-tracker call sites). Most behavior claims also match the code, including the jemalloc routing, the unused global limit setters, the ignored memory_limit, the KV cache wash-then-fail, the throttle that only sleeps, the spill protocol, the malloc hook and zone promotion, and the empty public test surface. There are two real mechanism errors. The IO allocator semantics are reversed. The ctx-limit explanation is wrong, and the correct version (limits are not enforced on obmalloc either) makes the survey's case stronger. Its only mysqltest search for the bare code 4013 used git grep -E with \b, which does not work here. A -P recheck confirmed the conclusion, but that check was vacuous as run. The main gaps are scope, not accuracy. The analysis silently assumes the Linux/macOS CMake build and does not address Windows, Android and Bazel non-sanity, which default to obmalloc. The list of real fail points misses several non-allocator -4013 errors, and its site estimate is a lower bound. Its recommendation (abort on OOM plus explicit budget checks) is sound for the jemalloc builds and can go into the rulebook. First, the IO entry needs fixing, the logical -4013 errors need adding, and there needs to be a stated decision on non-jemalloc platforms.


## COMPLETENESS CRITIC
The fifteen areas cover every top-level module under src/ (sql 983,706 lines, storage 760,249, oblib 382,226, share 271,801, observer 132,632, rootserver 104,311, pl 42,811, logservice 32,705, query 30,639, data_plane 16,157, standby 14,899, objit 2,486; HEAD 076eb309b). They also cover the cross-cutting questions: idioms, coupling, runtime, deps, build, pain and the test judge. The structure call is well supported: every core area independently finds that ownership and memory need a redesign while the outer code can be translated file by file, and the verifiers mostly corrected numbers rather than conclusions. Five things are still weak. (1) Some claims conflict and nobody has settled them. On data compatibility, storage says there is no such constraint, tx-log says v1.4.0 is the baseline, and tests-judge says the binary refuses other versions. I checked: src/oblib/common/ob_data_version_mgr.cpp:61 returns OB_NOT_SUPPORTED when the persisted data version differs from DATA_CURRENT_VERSION, a check added on 2026-07-26/27. On memory, oblib, idioms and pain treat out-of-memory as a recoverable error, but the runtime verifier found that jemalloc is the default backend. I also found no mysqltest .result file that expects -4013/HY001. (2) The verification cost has no Rust-side scaling assumption, and the digests do not include the C++ clean-build baseline. (3) Nobody has measured which of the planned units the 272 CI cases actually reach, beyond expression function names. (4) Sizes overlap between areas (share/geo, observer/vector_index, the storage vector and retrieval directories). About 45K lines belong to no area: sql/engine/cmd, sql/monitor, sql/privilege_check, sql/executor, observer/change_stream, observer/dbms_scheduler, rootserver/dbms_job, and the AI-service code. Nobody gave token sizes per unit, and the model plan needs them. (5) Nobody measured things outside the source tree that bear on the verdict. The tree changes fast: 504 commits and +2.2M/-3.9M lines in src/ over the last 90 days, 151 of those commits by the 'obdev' mirror account. Test assets sit in sibling repos: seekdb-bindings lib/tests and python/tests, seekdb-async/tests, shell-e2e, and unittest/wasm on the wasm branch. The wasm32 branch is live: 366 files changed.

Gaps filled (areas prefixed gap-): data-compat-conflict, judge-reach-map, referee-price, unit-ledger-and-tokens, moving-target, oom-policy-conflict

Gaps not yet filled:
- external-judge-assets: tests-judge looked only inside the worktree. It found no CI coverage of restart and replay, the embedded C API, the binary prepared-statement protocol, multiple clients, wasm/OPFS, or performance. Public-surface test assets for exactly these gaps exist in sibling repos that no area inspected. They could widen the judge and seed parity scenarios, and would reduce or reshape the 'build the judge first' task. The judge also runs only on OceanBase-internal Linux CI, so the report needs to know whether it can run on the user's macOS machine.
  brief: Read-only. Inspect: /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/seekdb-bindings/lib/tests (test_parameter_persistence.cc, test_two_clients_threads.cc, test_one_client_process.cc, test_open_parameters.cc) and python/tests/seekdb_test.py; /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/seekdb-async/tests (a Rust mysql_async client); /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/shell-e2e (driver.mjs, fixtures.mjs); unittest/wasm on origin/feature/webassembly-shell (77 files; use git show and git ls-tree, no checkout); /Users/colin/repo/sysbench; the logs in /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/dogfood-runs and /Users/colin/seekdb-dev/deployment-runs. For each asset give: the number of cases; whether it goes only through the public surface (C API, MySQL protocol, JS shell) or imports internals; what it covers that the 272 mysqltests miss (restart and parameter persistence, embedded mode, concurrent clients, prepared statements, crash recovery, wasm); whether it runs on macOS; and its runtime if logged. Also: (a) search this repo and the siblings for where the unit tests deleted in 45cbbe9e1 went (for example references to 'seekdb_test'); (b) check whether an obclient/mysqltest binary exists on this Mac (which mysqltest obclient, ~/seekdb-dev/seekdb-bins) so .github/script/seekdb/mysqltest_for_seekdb.py can run locally; (c) note any recorded performance numbers (sysbench outputs, VLDB benchmark artifacts, dogfood timings) usable as a performance baseline. Output: a table of assets and a list of parity scenarios they already cover.
- cpp-islands-and-wasm32: The report treats vsag, S2, boost.geometry (share/geo), ICU regex and possibly the bison parser as C++ that stays behind a C ABI. The search area says the GIS boundary sits at ST_-function level, but its verifier found share/geo also reached from casts, range extraction, table scan, DML, aggregates, SRS loading and a serialized MBR type. So the permanent C++ residue and its ABI are unsized. The live wasm32 target on origin/feature/webassembly-shell (5 commits, 366 files, +22,080/-1,112, pthread builds, sql-nio built for wasm32-unknown-emscripten) adds constraints: usize is 32 bits, the Rust target is Tier 2 and needs nightly, and every kept C++ island must also build under emscripten. No area sized these for a whole-Rust seekdb.

Compare the geo result with the 'ST_-function level' claim. Output per island: lines kept in C++, ABI function count, callback count. Say which patches fix 64-bit or pointer-size assumptions (for example sizeof(ObDatum)==12, the pointer/int64 union in the micro block header), threads and futexes, IO/OPFS, or mmap.
